# Developer notes

Monster Girl Quest! Online keeps its first name inside: the `Multiplayer` install folder, `Multiplayer.dll`, the `MGQParadox.Multiplayer` projects, `MGQ_Multiplayer`, the `MGQ_Mp` modules and the DLL's `mp_` functions. The `mgqmp` strings in join and world codes, key derivation labels and player ids must never change, since every world and player id on the relay derives from them.

## Layout

```
MGQ-Online.slnx                       Visual Studio solution
Directory.Build.targets               puts vswhere.exe on the PATH, which the NativeAOT link needs
GameScript/Multiplayer.rb             Ruby, what every way of playing together shares; loads the others
GameScript/Multiplayer/Scripts/       Ruby, the other scripts, see the table below
GameScript/Tests/                     Ruby, tests of the scripts outside the game, run.rb runs them all
MGQParadox.Multiplayer/               C# NativeAOT project -> Multiplayer.dll, and the package
MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests/         xUnit tests of the DLL, 32-bit like it
MGQOnline.Bridge/                     C# library: the bridge between the scripts and C#, see Bridge below
MGQOnline.Bridge.Native/              C# library: the bridge's four exports, which the DLL and the battle server core export
MGQOnline.Bridge.Tests/               xUnit tests of the bridge and its exports
MGQOnline.Data/                       C# library: the events database over SQLite, linked in statically, see Events database below
MGQOnline.Data.Tests/                 xUnit tests of the events database, 32-bit like the DLL
MGQOnline.Data.Generator/             C# console tool, run by hand: builds the events database from the untranslated game; sorts, names and exports for the tools
MGQOnline.Data.Generator.Tests/       xUnit tests of the generator
MGQOnline.Game/                       C# library: the game-side rules leaving the scripts, see Game rules below
MGQOnline.Game/Battles/Live/          the live battle's session and the guest's stream, see Live battles below
MGQOnline.Game/Coop/                  co-op battles: matchmaking, the host's intake, enemies, roster and level, see Co-op battles below
MGQOnline.Game/Raid/                  the raid pool ledger and its reports, see Raid boss pools on the relay below
MGQOnline.Game/Duels/                 the duels' and team duels' handshake, see Duels below
MGQOnline.Game/Teleports/             the teleport requests between party members, see Parties below
MGQOnline.Game/Npcs/                  the NPCs a party shares on a map, see NPCs in a party below
MGQOnline.Game/Trade/                 the trade session between two players, see Trades below
MGQOnline.Game/Chat/                  the chat's channels, tabs, whispers, commands and lines, see Chat below
MGQOnline.Game/Hud/                   the edge arrows' places and pictures and the raid bars, see Edge arrows and HP bars below
MGQOnline.Game/Overview/              the World overview's texts, see World overview below
MGQOnline.Game.Tests/                 xUnit tests of the game rules
MGQOnline.ServerCore/                 C# NativeAOT library -> MGQOnline.ServerCore.so (.dll on Windows), the battle server's core, see below
MGQOnline.ServerCore.Tests/           xUnit tests of the battle server core through its exports
fixtures/bridge/                      golden Marshal dumps Ruby wrote, and the script that wrote them
fixtures/wire/                        golden cases of the battle line codec, and the scripts that write them
fixtures/catchup/                     golden cases of the catch-up experience, the row rules and the catch-up engine, recorded from the scripts
fixtures/mods/                        golden mod keys, written from the relay's modKey
fixtures/story/                       golden story parts, written from the relay's partOf
fixtures/world/                       the answers of the DLL's world rule topics, which its tests and the script tests share
fixtures/ui/                          golden cases of the interface's text rules, and the scripts that write them
Relay/                                the relay server, see Relay/README.md
BattleServer/                         the battle server, the game's battles without Game.exe, see BattleServer/README.md
package/Multiplayer/                  static files shipped as-is
package/Multiplayer/Data/events.db    the events database, built by the generator from the untranslated game and committed
package/Framework/                    the Mod Framework's release the package ships, git-ignored, see Build and test
Shipping/                             publish output, git-ignored
docs/DEVELOPER.md                     this file
docs/GUIDE.md                         the player guide, which README.md links to
docs/CLASSIC_WORLDS.md                the player guide's page of Classic Worlds
docs/RAID_WORLDS.md                   the player guide's page of Raid Worlds, its boss list from battles_raid_bosses.rbx
docs/images/                          the guide's and README's screenshots
.github/workflows/release.yml         tests, builds and attaches the zip and the battle server core on release
.github/workflows/scripts.yml         tests the scripts on every push
.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml the form players report bugs with
```

**Scripts.** `Multiplayer.rb` is a Patch folder mod that loads the other scripts from `Patch/Multiplayer/Scripts/` itself (`load_scripts`), each once, in the order of its `SCRIPTS`, which the table below follows. How it loads is `MGQ_Multiplayer::Stages`. Under the Mod Framework, which ships with it (see Build and test) and which reads its tag from `Patch/Multiplayer/Mod.ini` (`tag=Title`) before it ever reached the website, it is a Title mod in three stages: the framework runs it as the game starts running; it starts the DLL, loads `BOOT_SCRIPTS` (the log, the hooks, the hotkeys and the look) through `Mod_Framework::Mods.load_part`, which counts the mod as in use, and adds the title's *Multiplayer* command (`Mods.title_command`). Picking it loads `WORLD_SCRIPTS`, the world screens that list, create and join worlds (`Stages.load_world`, also through `load_part`), and opens the world screen; a Discord invite into a world and the framework's actions after a restart (`multiplayer.rejoin`, `multiplayer.open`, `multiplayer.invite`) load them first too. The rest of `SCRIPTS` loads in its `Mods.on_enter` block as the player enters the game: New Game, a save's load, or a world, whose `MGQ_MpWorld.start` calls `Mods.enter` right before the world's save loads, or the title right before the world's New Game, never before the world screen goes back to the title, which the framework would make a restart once the game was entered. A player who backs out of the question such a New Game asks first (Luka's hero list, for instance) leaves a title the framework counts as entered with the world's mods, so the game starts again into the world screen (`MGQ_MpWorld.restart_after_backing_out`, `Stages.entered?`, the action `multiplayer.open`), and Continue or another world never runs with them. The framework runs that block inside its entering, which lets the hooks the battle scripts put on `SceneManager.run` set up, and writes how the game closes through `Run.on_close`. A script of the world screens that joins a later script's registry, such as `world_mods.rbx` the open world's messages, waits for `Stages.when_loaded`. Every hook sleeps (`MGQ_MpHooks.sleep_while`) in a game entered without the mod, such as one turned off on the title's Mods screen after its title part loaded. Without the framework, as on the battle server, the game's loader runs `Multiplayer.rb`, which loads every script at once and follows `SceneManager.run` itself. They are `.rbx` files because the loader recurses into folders: as `.rb` files it would load them a second time, in alphabetical order. A script may only use what loaded before it at load time; later ones are reached at run time (`defined?`). The order also decides how the battle scripts' hooks wrap each other, since they install them as the game starts running, the last loaded first. A script that is missing or fails to load is logged to the in-game log, and the others load all the same. The Russian translation brings its own `Patch.rb`, which loads only `Patch/*.rb`, reads each as it is (a byte order mark breaks the file) and catches no error, so `Multiplayer.rb` holds ASCII only and catches the errors of its own load (`load_failed`), which it writes into the log at once, since neither the start's settling nor the frame hook may have run, and shows in the game's message box.

**Hooks.** A game method that more than one script follows, such as `Scene_Map#update_scene`, `Spriteset_Map#update` and `#dispose`, `Game_Map#update`, `Graphics.update`, `SceneManager.run`, `Scene_Title#start` and `#update` or `Game_Interpreter#execute_command`, is wrapped once, by `core_hooks.rbx`; each script registers a block with `MGQ_MpHooks.before` or `.after` under its own name instead of wrapping the method itself. The blocks run on the object the method runs on, with its arguments, in the order nested wraps would: before blocks from the last script loaded to the first, after blocks from the first to the last. So `battles_pvp.rbx` still puts the shared data back before `world.rbx` closes the world as the title screen starts. A failing block is logged once and the others run on. A hook that changes what a method does or returns is a wrap of that script's own, made with `MGQ_MpHooks.around` from a block that gets the object, the arguments and the original, registered under the script's name as well, so a script loaded again replaces its wrap instead of wrapping the method once more. Only what the registry cannot express stays a plain alias guarded by a `hookable?`: `world.rbx`'s redirect of `File` and `Dir` into a world's save folder and its `Bitmap#initialize`. Every wrap runs the game's method alone while `MGQ_MpHooks.sleep_while`'s block says the mod sleeps. `MGQ_MpHooks.plain` names the game's own body of a hooked method and `hooked` lists a class's hooked methods, which the story replay's interpreter runs without any script's hooks. Holding the player is wrapped once too: a script registers with `MGQ_MpHooks.hold_player`, and while any block says so the player stands still (`Game_Player#movable?`) and the menu stays shut (`Scene_Map#update_call_menu`), as during a held encounter or the leader's story scene.

| File | Module | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `Multiplayer.rb` | `MGQ_Multiplayer` | The foundation every mode shares: `Stages` (how the mod loads, see Scripts above), `Log` (the scripts' lines, which go into the DLL's log of the day through the bridge, see Logs below), `load_scripts` (the other scripts), `Bridge` (the bridge to C#, see [The bridge in the scripts](#the-bridge-in-the-scripts)), `Link` (the DLL's older `mp_*` functions, each named once with its signature in `Link::EXPORTS`; `Link.read` takes a state the DLL writes into a buffer, and when the text grew between the size probe and the copy it asks again with a larger buffer, up to `READ_TRIES` (4) times, before it returns ""), `Background` (keeps the game running while another window is in front, and has `Input` report no buttons meanwhile), `Key` (F-keys the game's `Input` does not know), `Capture` (takes the buttons away from the game while any screen of the mod reads them, each holding them until it gives them back), `Discord` (the bridge client, see below). |
| `core_log.rbx` | `MGQ_MpLog` | The scripts' lines in the in-game log. A script's module extends it and sets `LOG_TAG`, which starts its lines: `log` writes one, `log_once` only the first time for its key, for what would otherwise fail every frame. |
| `core_hooks.rbx` | `MGQ_MpHooks` | The game methods several scripts follow, each wrapped once, with the blocks the scripts register (see Hooks above). `Multiplayer.rb` registers its `Graphics.update` and `SceneManager.run` blocks once the scripts loaded. |
| `core_game_access.rbx` | `MGQ_MpGame` | What the mod reads and writes of the game that the game keeps private: `get` and `set` take a field by its key of `FIELDS`, `call` a private method of `METHODS`. A field the game no longer has is logged once by its name, so a game update that renames one is followed in this one script. The game's classes get no accessors of the mod's own, which another mod's could clash with. The scans over every field of an object (a battle's windows, an action's result) stay where they are. |
| `core_hotkeys.rbx` | `MGQ_MpHotkeys` | The hotkeys the player binds: the action wheel (B), the emote wheel (E), the chat (T), the World overview, in a world and in single player (F11), accepting and declining the notification box's first invite (Y, N), the party box's size (Tab, which in a raid boss battle also crops the boss's HP bar to its gauges, see [HP bars](#hp-bars-ui_raid_barrbx)), the Leader HUD's size (H), joining a battle nearby in a Raid World (J), and the bug report (F3, see `ui_bug_report.rbx`). `code` reads the key from `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini` (`key_wheel`, `key_emotes`, `key_chat`, `key_overview`, `key_accept`, `key_decline`, `key_party_box`, `key_leader_hud`, `key_join_battle`, `key_bug_report`, Windows key codes), else the default; `pressed?` reads it with `MGQ_Multiplayer::Key`, and `label` names it for the texts. A press while a confirm box of the mod is open (`confirm_open?`, `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.open?` at run time) is read and dropped by `pressed?`, logged, so nothing opens under the box and the box's Y never answers an invite. `pressed?` logs a press with what it is for (`log_press`), and every script that refuses a hotkey logs why through `log_refusal` (under `keys:`, with the key, its name and the reason); neither logs while the player types (`$mgq_text_input`), where the key is a letter they typed. With Mod Config Remake installed, `register` adds a key binding per hotkey in the group *MGQ Online Hotkeys* (`GROUP`; every other row of the mod goes to *MGQ Online Settings*, `MGQ_MpUi::OPTION_PREFIX`, but the rows for single player, which go to *MGQ Online SP*, `MGQ_MpUi::SP_PREFIX`) (`:keybind`, with `:value` and `:on_change` going to Player.ini, since `$game_system.conf` lives in each save). |
| `ui.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi`, `MGQ_MpUi::Style`, `MGQ_MpUi::Motion`, `MGQ_MpUi::PlayerColor`, `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm`, `Sprite_MpConfirmBox`, `Window_MpInfo`, `Sprite_MpListBox`, `Sprite_MpMessageBox` | What the screens and boxes share (see [Shared UI](#shared-ui-uirbx)): `Style` (the flat boxes' colors, font sizes, padding, timings and outlined and plated texts), `Motion` (a sprite's fade and slide), `PlayerColor` (a player's color by who they are), `Confirm` (the yes/no box over any scene); `MGQ_MpUi.wrap` breaks a text into lines that fit, at spaces and inside a word that is too long, for the chat and the windows; `MGQ_MpUi.cut` shortens a text to a width with an ellipsis, keeping a tail such as the overview's type and difficulty; `MGQ_MpUi.grouped` writes a whole number with its thousands grouped by commas; `MGQ_MpUi.within` keeps a value between two bounds; `MGQ_MpUi.draw_icon` draws an icon of the game's icon set at any side; `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` tells the pixels a strip of the mod takes at the top of the map's screen, such as a raid boss's bar, which the notification box and the party box move down by; `FRAMES_PER_SECOND` (60) converts frames and seconds; `Z` names the depth of every sprite of the mod on the map (`:arrows` 190 under `:lines`, `:confirm` 1050 over every box, `:typing` 1100 over it, `:layout` 1200 topmost); `Window_MpInfo` is the window of lines across the top of the world screen, and the base of the save copy's notice; `Sprite_MpListBox` is the large box of headings and items (see The list box under [Worlds (DLL)](#worlds-dll)), `Sprite_MpMessageBox` the box in the middle of the screen that tells the player something. |
| `ui_theme.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::Theme`, `MGQ_MpUi::Theme::Skinned` | The interface presets, Monarch (the default), Legacy and Game's Default, which set the style's box colors in place, and the window skin painted in them that every window, the game's too, wears (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). Loads right after `ui.rbx`. |
| `ui_layout.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::Layout` | The floating boxes the player moves and resizes with the left Alt and the mouse, each kept in `Player.ini`, never smaller than its least size (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). Loads right after `ui_theme.rbx`. |
| `ui_text_box.rbx` | `MGQ_MpUi::TextEdit`, `MGQ_MpUi::TextBox` | The text boxes: `TextEdit` is the editor of a text being typed, `TextBox` draws a box being typed into, and `MGQ_MpUi.wrap_spans` breaks a text into lines that keep every character's place (see Text boxes under [Worlds (DLL)](#worlds-dll)). |
| `core_actors.rbx` | `MGQ_MpActors`, `Game_MpActor` | Characters of another game: `Builds` writes and reads a character's build as plain numbers, `Items` its equipment, and `Game_MpActor` rebuilds it as a real character of this game (see [Team](#pvp-battles-battles_pvprbx) and Rebuilt characters); its `name` is "Alice (\<player\>)" for the battle's messages, `owner` the player and `base_name` the character alone, which the co-op status window draws in the owner's color. PvP battles build their enemy side on it, co-op battles the party members' characters. |
| `core_async.rbx` | `MGQ_MpAsync` | The world running on behind every other screen while a world is open, the live map behind menus without the mod's HUD (`Spriteset_Map#mgq_mp_hud?`), and a log line with the memory the game uses at every switch of screens (see [The world never pauses](#the-world-never-pauses-core_asyncrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpWorld` at run time. |
| `overworld_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` | The open world's messages: what each game tells the others, the other games by seat, and the registry the scripts after it join for their messages (see [On the map](#on-the-map)). Every script sends through `tell`, shows its notices through `notice`, and reads the player's id and seat from `Me.id` and `Me.seat`. Reaches `MGQ_MpWorld` at run time. |
| `ui_wheel.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWheel` | What the action wheel and the emote wheel share: the arrows held point like a joystick (`held`), one to its side, two to the diagonal between them, and nowhere while none is held, read past the capture with `Capture.press?`, and steadied for the open wheel (`steady`: one arrow left of a diagonal still points to the diagonal for `GRACE_FRAMES`, since two arrows are seldom let go in the same frame); and how a wheel spreads its choices over the eight directions (`places`: choice i of n on direction round(i × 8 / n), so every count is mirror-symmetric). A wheel keeps its pick while they point nowhere. |
| `ui_actions.rbx` | `MGQ_MpActions` | The action wheel on the map and what the scripts after it offer between players (see [Actions](#actions-ui_actionsrbx)). It names no later script: each adds its choice to the wheel's ring by order (`wheel_choice`: the party 10, duels 20, trades 30, leaving the party 40, the chat 50, the bug report 60) or fills its middle (`wheel_center`: the overview), lists the choices of a player's menu for the World overview and the chat (`peer_options`, `own_options`), says the line above the player's own head (`own_line_from` for a line of its own, `own_doing_from` for what the line joins, with the moment it lapses for a countdown, see [Actions](#actions-ui_actionsrbx)), tells that its screen lies over the map (`cover`), and registers its `Offers` (`offer`): `peer_option` and `own_options` for the World overview's menus, `call_of` for its rows and `notice_of` for the notification box. `MGQ_MpCoop::Offers`, `MGQ_MpCoopTeleport::Offers`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather::Offers`, `MGQ_MpCoopChoices::Offers`, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin::Offers`, `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel::Offers` and `MGQ_MpTrade::Offers` are those registered. Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `ui_chat.rbx` | `MGQ_MpChat` | Chat: the chat box, the bubble above the sender and the chat log, on the map and in battles (see [Chat](#chat-ui_chatrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `ui_emotes.rbx` | `MGQ_MpEmotes`, `Sprite_MpEmoteWheel` | The emote wheel on the map (see [Emotes](#emotes-ui_emotesrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`. |
| `overworld.rbx` | `MGQ_MpOverworld` | What the player sees of the other players of the open world: the ghosts, their name labels in their `PlayerColor`, the own ping and the status line (see [On the map](#on-the-map)). Reads `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoop` | Parties: `Party` (who is in the player's party, invites, the leader), and the gate that hands the party's messages to the scripts registered for them (see [Parties](#parties-cooprbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop_scope.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` | Whom the player shares the map and the story with: the party in a Classic world, everyone on the map in a Raid World; the map's gate (`MGQ_MpCoop.route_map`) and its sends (`MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map_to`) (see [Party or map](#party-or-map-coop_scoperbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopEvents`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather` and `MGQ_MpCoopStory` at run time. |
| `coop_squad.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopSquad` | A party's squads: each player's share of the Frontline and the Backline, the followers shown, the formation screens' cut and the Party Followers option (see [Squads](#squads-coop_squadrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `coop_events.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopEvents` | The events of a party: sorts every event page by what it does, and opens chests for the whole party (see [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Hands gathering messages to `MGQ_MpCoopGather`; reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpCoopCastle`, `MGQ_MpStoryState` and `MGQ_MpCoopStory` at run time. |
| `coop_gather.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopGather` | Gathering the party for the leader's story scenes, holding the scene until it came, bringing a player over to a teleport they asked for, and a Raid World's follow, which says whether the telling moves the world's story on (`moves`) (see Story scenes together under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpActions` and `MGQ_MpStoryState` at run time. |
| `coop_teleport.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopTeleport` | Teleports between a party's members: at once to the leader, to anyone else once they allow it (see Teleports between members under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpCoop`, `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopGather` at run time. |
| `coop_scene.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopScene` | The leader's story pictures, screen effects and hidden character, shown to the members on the leader's map and put back once the story ended, and the player's own scene (`MGQ_MpCoopScene::Own`), whose changes coop_events.rbx, coop_gather.rbx and world_story.rbx take back where the mod ends it early or moves the player away (`cut`, `leave`) (see The story's scene under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpCoopEvents`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather` and `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` at run time. |
| `coop_npcs.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopNpcs` | The NPCs a party shares on a map: the Map Owner moves them and the other members follow (see [NPCs in a party](#npcs-in-a-party-coop_npcsrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions` at run time. |
| `story_state.rbx` | `MGQ_MpStoryState` | The story's state model, which the party's story and a Raid World's story share: the switches, variables and self switches read and written as raw data, what is each player's own (`personal_switch?`, `personal_variable?`, by the table of `story_keys.rbx`, and in a Classic party the member's own choices and the map's chests), a story joined with the player's own values (`mix`), written for a message and read back (`full`, `story_lists`, `pack`, `unpack`, `decode_full`, `decode_delta`), and the story's skills, companions and items granted (`learn`, `join`, `bring`, `knows?`, `roster_ids`, `story_count`) (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)). It repairs a save the 0.6.9.2 chest path left stuck once, at its first load in this build (`repair_check` after `extract_save_contents`, `repair` after the map's update until the DLL could decide): `story.repair` (`SaveRepair` over the events database's `repair_pairs`, in a world the player's own keys alone) names the switches whose shut chest proves them on, which are turned on with a log line each and no notice; `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_repaired` marks the save done, a new game at once. Logs under the tag of `coop_story.rbx`; reaches `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope`, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents` and `MGQ_MpCoopChoices` at run time. |
| `story_keys.rbx` | `MGQ_MpStoryKeys` | The story's keys as the multiplayer shares them: the table the DLL builds (a Raid World's story mapping, a Classic party's table), the one hook on the game's writes that the telling's scene and the story's requests share, the event each write came from, and the guard of the multiplayer's own writes (see [The write hook](#the-write-hook-story_keysrbx)). Loads after `story_state.rbx`. |
| `story_replay.rbx` | `MGQ_MpStoryReplay` | Replays a run another player's game ran in the receiving game: the receiver's own interpreter, without the mod's hooks, bound to the run's map and event, the world and work keys in a view only the replay sees, the presentation and the opener's play shut, the inputs from the opener's record (see The replayer). |
| `coop_story.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopStory` | The story a party plays in a Classic world: members borrow the leader's switches, variables and self switches and get their own back afterwards, built on `MGQ_MpStoryState` (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)). Reaches `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions` at run time. |
| `coop_castle.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopCastle` | The Pocket Castle in a party: which pages are its residents, each player's own talks (see The Pocket Castle under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). Reads `MGQ_MpStoryState`. |
| `world.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorld` | Worlds: the Multiplayer command on the title screen, the world screen, each world's folder with its saves and system save, and whether the open world is a Raid World (`raid?`, `companion_sharing`) (see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)). |
| `world_mods.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldMods` | A world's required mods: this game's copies against the relay's mod catalog and the creator's hashes, the download and restart, and the creator's mod settings while in the world (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)). |
| `world_story.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldStory` | A Raid World's story on the relay's tracks: the player placed, their story asked for, what the relay hands written straight into the player's story once they are free, their tellings, the teller they watch, their level, the Great Decision's routes and the return to it (see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)). Builds on `MGQ_MpStoryState` and `MGQ_MpStoryKeys`; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and the bridge; reaches `MGQ_MpCoopChoices`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather`, `MGQ_MpWorldFreeze` and `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` at run time. |
| `world_catchup.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` | A Raid World's catch-up: a player behind on their part's checkpoint, their story held, the level gates, the story's rewards, removals, key items and story chests by story position, companions by level and the world's shared companions (see [Catch-up](#catch-up-world_catchuprbx)). Hands the DLL a snapshot of the player's story, party and items and carries out the actions its catch-up engine answers (`catchup.*`, `CatchupTopics`); reads `MGQ_MpStoryState` and `MGQ_MpWorldStory`; `coop_events.rbx` asks it at run time. |
| `world_catchup_exp.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldCatchupExp` | A Raid World's catch-up experience: base, job and race experience grown for each character below the cap the world's story unlocked before its latest one, see *Catch-up experience*. |
| `world_opening.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldOpening`, `Game_MpOpeningInterpreter` | A new game's opening in a Raid World whose story is past it: the start map's autorun event runs its setup in an interpreter of its own without its scene, as the multiplayer's own writes, the player goes where it ends, and only then is the relay's track written into the story. `world_story.rbx` and `coop_events.rbx` ask it at run time. |
| `world_gates.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldGates` | A Raid World's gated areas (`AREAS`): no move enters one from outside (`Game_Player#reserve_transfer`, which event transfers, the relay map, follows and teleports all go through) while the world's story lacks one of its needs, the screen fades in again after a move kept back, and a choice leaves out an option whose commands transfer there (`gated_choice`, reading a map event's direct transfer in its map folder as the game's `command_201` does). The one area is the Realm of Chaos behind the Central Tartarus (map 1836 and every map below it), needing the ten chains the game asks before v1143 = 24 (map 1287, event 233), since the God of Chaos plays the bad end (ce:9051) without them. Reads `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope`. |
| `world_difficulty.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldDifficulty` | A Raid World's difficulty: every game in it plays on it from entry, load, new game and the relay's push on, the game's own difficulty choice and the Reaper's change set it instead, and the fights with values of their own keep them (see [Raid World difficulty](#raid-world-difficulty-world_difficultyrbx)). Reads `MGQ_MpWorld`; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `world_save_distribution.rbx` | `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution`, `Scene_MpStartSave` | A world's starting save: the creator picks one of their saves on the game's own save screen, and new players fetch it before entering the world (see [Starting save](#starting-save-world_save_distributionrbx)). Called by `world.rbx` at run time. |
| `world_text.rbx` | `Scene_MpText`, `Window_MpTextEdit`, `Window_MpTextInput` | The text screen: names and passwords, typed on the keyboard or picked from the game's letters with a gamepad. |
| `world_screen.rbx` | `Scene_MpWorlds`, `Window_MpWorldList`, `Window_MpWorldDetail`, `Window_MpChoice`, `Window_MpWorldForm` | The world screen and its windows: the list, a world's details, the forms that create a world, change one or add a hidden one to the list, the mod picker over the forms, and the choices in the middle. The picker lists MGQ_MpWorld::ModPick, which reads and writes the mods as the world keeps them (! required, ? essential), in the list box of ui.rbx. |
| `world_save_export.rbx` | `MGQ_MpSaveExport`, `Window_MpSaveExportNotice` | Copies a world's latest save, an autosave included, with its thumbnail into the first free slot of the player's own `Save` folder; the player's own system save stays as it is. The world screen offers it on a world played on this PC, the game's menu while a world is open (*Copy to my game*, after the game's own extra commands, greyed out until the player saved in the world, with a notice of the slot). It writes through `Files.unmapped`, since `Save/` stands for the world's folder while one is open. |
| `coop_choices.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopChoices`, `Window_MpStoryChoices`, `Scene_MpStoryChoices` | The story's choices a member makes for themselves, the leader's offer to sync the story and the screen of choices (see [The story's choices](#the-storys-choices-coop_choicesrbx)). Loads after `world_screen.rbx`, whose `Window_MpWorldForm` its screen draws with. |
| `battles.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattles` | The rules every multiplayer battle shares, PvP and co-op alike: no ero offers, no Give Up, and no swapping the Backline in where the battle has none (a PvP battle or duel whose host chose *Frontline Only*, a team duel); `begin(kind)` sets them and keeps the game's own settings, `finish` puts those back. `MGQ_MpBattles::Mode` is what a kind of live battle does differently from a duel between two players (whose party the host's is, who else plays, what happens before the start, at each command phase, when a player or the host leaves); `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop::Mode` and `MGQ_MpBattlesTeam::Mode` register theirs with `MGQ_MpBattles.mode`. |
| `battles_raid_bosses.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidBosses` | The story's boss troops by troop id: the story milestone each belongs to, the milestone's cap, and whether the troop is its fight's last phase (`at`, `boss?`, `last_phase?`; never on a map of `SKIRMISHES`, where Carmilla and Elizabeth withdraw from the Sabasa Attack before their Vampire Castle fights), the story's bosses outside the Level Cap milestones as milestones of their own, the one-time bosses outside the story that count too (`OPTIONAL`: the six of the Chaos Domain), each boss's name (`milestone_key`), the story's section it belongs to (`SECTIONS`, `OPTIONAL_SECTION`, `sections`, `section`), tier and the factors its tier gives its enemies' stats (`TIERS`, `TIER_BOSSES`, `tier`, `key_tier`, `factor`, `tier_stats`) and the title its HP bar shows (`TITLES`, `title`), and whether the battle running is a boss battle (`battle?`, the table or switch 22, which the game's events turn on around every story boss battle). Data only; loads before `battles_coop.rbx`. |
| `battles_coop.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop`, `Game_MpAlly` | Co-op battles: who joins, the party every game shares, the allies other players command, and the status window's names in their players' colors (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Drives `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin` | Hotjoining in a Raid World: a random encounter joins a co-op battle running on the map (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Loads after `battles_coop.rbx`, which calls it at run time, and before `battles_sync.rbx`, so its command phase hook wraps the live battle's. |
| `battles_coop_join.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesJoin`, `Sprite_MpJoinPrompt` | Joining a battle by choice in a Raid World: the prompt below the player's character near a battle that takes players in, and the request to its host (see [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). Loads after `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`, which takes the player in. |
| `battles_raid_pool.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidPool` | A Raid World's raid bosses in the game: every story boss battle fights the boss's pools at the relay, its enemies' stats raised by the boss's tier, reporting what it dealt as its actions end and at each command phase and taking the pools' HP at each command phase and every few seconds while it waits for commands, and a player near a boss battle that takes nobody in may fight the same boss in a side battle (see [In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)](#in-the-game-battles_raid_poolrbx)). Loads after `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`. |
| `battles_raid_freeze.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidFreeze` | A Raid World's lost raid boss fight: its record at the battle command, the undo of what its defeat changed, and its resume onto the battle's spot (see [Frozen fights](#frozen-fights-battles_raid_freezerbx-world_freezerbx-ui_raid_freezerbx)), with the sites of the story's raid boss fights from the DLL's events database (`raid.sites`). Loads after `battles_raid_pool.rbx`. |
| `world_freeze.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldFreeze` | The world's list of frozen fights, carried in the world's story as the list `f`, and the keys they hold (see [Frozen fights](#frozen-fights-battles_raid_freezerbx-world_freezerbx-ui_raid_freezerbx)). `world_story.rbx` calls it as it takes, compares and writes the story. Loads after `battles_raid_freeze.rbx`. |
| `battles_raid_tiers.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidTiers` | The raid bosses outside Raid Worlds, in a Classic world with *Raid Bosses* and in single player: each boss battle only gets its tier's stats, nothing kept or shared (see [Raid bosses outside Raid Worlds (`battles_raid_tiers.rbx`)](#raid-bosses-outside-raid-worlds-battles_raid_tiersrbx)). Loads after `battles_raid_pool.rbx`, whose rules and helpers it reads. |
| `battles_coop_level_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpCoopLevelSync` | Level Sync in a co-op battle: a character above the battle's level, the highest of the party leader's characters in it, fights with the stats of that level, and its equipment loses half as much as the level takes; its stats come back once the battle ends. Every game syncs to the level the host sent. |
| `battles_sync.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync` | Live battles, for any mode: `join` makes the next battle live, `battle_started` marks it running, `finish` ends it and closes the link; `Waiting` is the box a battle waits in, with whom it waits for and the seconds (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)). Knows nothing of PvP battles, and names neither co-op battles nor team duels: it asks the running battle's mode (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.mode`, see `battles.rbx`). |
| `battles_sync_wire.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` | How a live battle's messages are written and read: plain values as tab-separated tokens. |
| `battles_sync_recorder.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Recorder` | The host's side of the stream: what its battle shows, as events for the guests. |
| `battles_sync_playback.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Playback` | The guest's side of the stream: the host's events, played on the guest's battle. |
| `battles_sync_live.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Hooks`, `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Live` | The hooks into the game's battle, installed as the game starts running, and the steps host and guest differ in. |
| `battles_served.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesServed` | Co-op battles the battle server hosts: the starter hands a gathered battle to the server and every player plays it back over the battle's channel; the starter stands in for the server in the world's room; the same script hosts on the server (see [Battles on the battle server](#battles-on-the-battle-server-battles_servedrbx)). |
| `battles_balance_pvp.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBalancePvp` | The balance of a PvP battle, between `begin(battlers)` and `finish`: skills use their formulas for monsters (`MONSTER_FORMULAS`), max HP times `HP_RATE` (damage formulas still read the game's max HP), one action's damage or healing on a target counted in full up to `DAMAGE_KNEE` of its max HP and kept below `DAMAGE_LIMIT`, at least `DAMAGE_RATE_FLOOR` of a hit taken by a character whose physical, magical or sure-hit damage rate is lower, evasion and reflection up to `AVOID_CEILING`, at least `ELEMENT_FLOOR` of an attack past a nullified or absorbed element, and a defense wall that takes `WALL_SHARE` of max HP off a hit. Constants, since both games must agree. |
| `battles_pvp.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp` | PvP battles, which duels start: the team, the rebuilt characters. Drives `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `battles_pvp_backline.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Backline` | The Backline in a PvP battle between two players (see [Backline](#backline-battles_pvp_backlinerbx)): the *PvP Backline* option, the other side's characters outside its Frontline, the Frontline each game tells the other, and the commands a swap costs. Its `Mode` is the `:duel` mode of `MGQ_MpBattles`. |
| `battles_duel.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel` | Duels: challenges like party invites, and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp`'s battle over the world's room (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `battles_team.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBattlesTeam` | Team duels: the sides once the duel starts, the other side's characters, and who commands the characters of a player who left (see [Team duels](#team-duels-battles_teamrbx)). Driven by `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel` and `MGQ_MpBattlesSync`. |
| `trade.rbx` | `MGQ_MpTrade` | Trades between two players on the same map: offers like party invites, both offers and their confirmations, the commit through the relay, applying and recovering a trade, and the save after it (see [Trades](#trades-traderbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync` and `MGQ_MpActions`. |
| `ui_trade.rbx` | `MGQ_MpTradeUi`, `Scene_MpTrade`, `Window_MpTradeOffer`, `Window_MpTradeCommand`, `Window_MpTradeBag`, `Window_MpTradeGold` | The trade screen: both offers as lists the Offers command scrolls, the bag by the game's categories, the gold's digits, confirming and cancelling. |
| `world_overview.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldOverview`, `Sprite_MpWorldOverview` | The World overview (F11 or the wheel's middle): its box, tabs and Players page, and the place, level and story every state tells (see [World overview](#world-overview-world_overviewrbx)). Registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `world_overview_pages.rbx` | `MGQ_MpWorldOverview`, `Sprite_MpWorldOverview` | The overview's World, Quests and Unlocks pages, laid out with a head, a list and a reading pane; the quests read from the game's events, the raid bosses from `MGQ_MpRaidPool.pools` and `MGQ_MpRaidBosses`, the unlocks from `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.unlocks`. Loads after `world_overview.rbx`. |
| `world_overview_mods.rbx` | `ModPages`, `MGQ_MpWorldOverview` | The pages other mods add to `ModPages` (which neither side owns: whichever loads first defines it), each a tab after the own pages with a head, a list and a pane with controls and buttons (see [World overview](#world-overview-world_overviewrbx)). Loads after `world_overview_pages.rbx`. |
| `ui_party_box.rbx` | `MGQ_MpPartyBox`, `Sprite_MpPartyBox` | The party box at the top right of the map, built from `MGQ_MpWorldOverview.party_rows`, in a Raid World from `raid_party_rows` with the others of `map_rows` below, or outside a party the map box from `map_rows`, so it loads after the overview, and its size; each row with its state icon, its name in its `PlayerColor` (dim with the away icon while the connection is down), level and ping in fixed columns; it moves down by `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`. |
| `ui_edge_arrows.rbx` | `MGQ_MpEdgeArrows`, `Sprite_MpEdgeArrow` | The arrows at the screen's edge toward the players on the map whose ghost is off the screen (see [Edge arrows](#edge-arrows-ui_edge_arrowsrbx)). Hooks `Spriteset_Map` on its own; reads `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`, `MGQ_MpOverworld`, `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` and the open screens at run time. |
| `ui_raid_bar.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidBar`, `Sprite_MpRaidBar` | The raid bosses' HP bars, also those of `battles_raid_tiers.rbx`: the boss's title and, for each of its enemies, its name over a gauge, across the top of a battle that fights its pools, and on the map the title with the names over thin gauges above each event that starts a boss battle, or the battle's strip at the top while none shows (see [HP bars (`ui_raid_bar.rbx`)](#hp-bars-ui_raid_barrbx)). Loads after `ui_party_box.rbx` and before `ui_notices.rbx`. |
| `ui_raid_freeze.rbx` | `MGQ_MpRaidFreezeUi`, `Game_MpFreezeStandIn` | The stand-ins of frozen fights on the map, sprites alone: the action press facing the boss's stand-in, the frozen event's own start blocked (see [Frozen fights](#frozen-fights-battles_raid_freezerbx-world_freezerbx-ui_raid_freezerbx)). Loads after `ui_raid_bar.rbx`, whose bars draw above the boss's stand-in. |
| `ui_single_player.rbx` | `MGQ_MpSinglePlayer` | The Mod Config group *MGQ Online SP* (`MGQ_MpUi::SP_PREFIX`) in its order, Leader HUD, Raid Bar on Map, Raid Bar in Battle and Raid Bosses, each kept by its own script, and the lossless restart after a change (see [Single player (`ui_single_player.rbx`)](#single-player-ui_single_playerrbx)). Loads after `ui_leader_hud.rbx`, whose rows it adds. |
| `ui_leader_hud.rbx` | `MGQ_MpLeaderHud`, `Sprite_MpLeaderHud` | The Leader HUD at the top left of the map: the Formation Leader's face (Large) or map sprite (Small), name, base level, HP and MP bars and the base, job and race levels' progress, Large or Small, on or off in the Mod Config (see *The Leader HUD* under the interface presets and layout). Loads after `ui_raid_bar.rbx` and before `ui_notices.rbx`, whose box moves below it. |
| `ui_notices.rbx` | `MGQ_MpNotices`, `Sprite_MpNoticeBox` | The notification box at the top left, on the map and in menus, below `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`. Invites: what the registered `Offers` name (`notice_of`), such as party invites, duel challenges, trade offers and teleport requests, from anywhere, read from the states every frame, so each stays exactly as long as it stands (a full party's invite drops out). Messages: what other scripts show for a moment (`message`, 3 s by default, `drop`), such as a party member meeting enemies, below the invites and without keys; outside a world too, such as a Discord invite taken during a game, while one shown in a world goes when the world closes. `MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:accept` (Y) accepts the first invite (a challenge only on the map), `:decline` (N) declines it: `MGQ_MpCoop::Party.decline` (`party_decline`, the inviter drops the player from `invite_to`) or `MGQ_MpBattlesDuel.decline` (`reason=no`, the challenger drops them from `challenge_to`), and the box hides a declined invite until the inviter sends a new one, an accepted one for at most 10 s (`@answered`, `ACCEPT_HOLD_FRAMES` 600) or until the inviter's game turns the accept down (`release`, which `trade.rbx` calls when the other player declines an accepted trade), so a second press accepts nothing while a slow inviter still shows the invite, and an accept that did nothing, as in a menu, shows the invite again. Loads after `ui_leader_hud.rbx` and before `ui_bug_report.rbx`; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |
| `ui_bug_report.rbx` | `MGQ_MpBugReport`, `MGQ_MpBugReport::Screenshot`, `Scene_MpBugReport` | The in-game bug report: its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:bug_report`, F3) or the wheel's *Report a Bug* takes a screenshot (`Screenshot`: `Graphics.snap_to_bitmap`, whose address `ui.report.snap` hands the DLL, which walks RGSS3's pointers to its pixels in the game's process, `ScreenshotWalk`, and keeps them in `BugReports`; after the wheel faded out) and opens a form drawn by `world_screen.rbx`'s form window: at once in a menu, after the battle, else once the player is free on the map, never on the title or game over screen (`REFUSED_SCENES`). `mp_report_send` sends the title and text with the public facts (`facts`, to which the DLL adds *Last error* when the logs go along, see `Reports/`), the private details (`details`) and, as the player chose, the screenshot and the logs; the notification box tells how it went (`mp_report_state`). The relay files the title, the text and the facts as a public GitHub issue; the screenshot, the logs and the details reach the developer only. Loaded last; registers with `MGQ_MpOverworldSync`. |

**`MGQParadox.Multiplayer/`** builds `Multiplayer.dll`: C# compiled with NativeAOT into a native 32-bit DLL, because the game is a 32-bit process. Players need no .NET installed.

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `Exports/` | The functions the game scripts call, one partial class over ten files, which keep the namespace `MGQParadox.Multiplayer` (a namespace `Exports` would hide the class). `Exports.cs`: `mp_start` (which also logs the crashes Windows recorded, `CrashEvents`), `mp_keep_running`, `mp_check_for_update`, `mp_newer_version`, `mp_update_rollback`, `mp_dev_build` (1 for a development build, which a bug report names) and `mp_memory_usage` (`ProcessUsage`). `Exports.Pvp.cs`: `mp_set_player_name`, `mp_host`, `mp_join_invite`, `mp_join_clipboard`, `mp_receive_invite`, `mp_cancel`, `mp_copy_code`, `mp_state`, `mp_status`, `mp_send` and `mp_receive`. `Exports.World.cs`: `mp_player_id` for the player; `mp_world_open`, `mp_world_close` and `mp_world_send` for worlds; the world room goes through the bridge (see [Worlds](#worlds-dll)). `Exports.Directory.cs`: `mp_dir_refresh`, `mp_dir_watch`, `mp_dir_open` and `mp_dir_list`. `Exports.Trade.cs`: `mp_trade_commit`, `mp_trade_cancel`, `mp_trade_state`, `mp_trade_done`, `mp_trade_pending` and `mp_trade_pending_list` (see Trades under [Worlds](#worlds-dll)). `Exports.Raid.cs`: `mp_raid_story_fetch`, `mp_raid_story_headers` and `mp_raid_companions_add`, a Raid World's shared companions (see [World story on the relay](#world-story-on-the-relay-raid-worlds)). `Exports.RaidBosses.cs`: `mp_raid_bosses_fetch`, `mp_raid_boss_report` and `mp_raid_boss_state` (see [Raid boss pools on the relay](#raid-boss-pools-on-the-relay)). `Exports.Battle.cs`: `mp_battle_serve`, `mp_battle_open`, `mp_battle_send`, `mp_battle_receive`, `mp_battle_state` and `mp_battle_close` (see [Battles on the battle server](#battles-on-the-battle-server-battles_servedrbx)). `Exports.Input.cs`: `mp_typing` for the keyboard (what is typed reaches the text box in C# straight, see [Keyboard](#keyboard)). `Exports.Reports.cs`: `mp_report_snap`, `mp_report_drop_snap`, `mp_report_send` and `mp_report_state` (the in-game bug report, see `ui_bug_report.rbx`). Nothing may throw out of them. |
| `UpdateCheck.cs` | Asks the relay's `GET /v1/release` once per session, on its own thread, for the release the games should run (`current`) and names it whenever it is not the DLL's version, an older one too (a step back, `ReleaseOffer.Rollback`); while the relay names none or cannot be reached it names none. Development builds (`0.0.0-dev`) never ask. The scripts then ask the Mod Framework for it (`MGQ_MpWorld::Update`, see Updates below). |
| `Network/Transport/` | What every connection between games is made of: `Message` (header lines and a payload), `IFrameChannel` (whatever carries whole frames), `FrameCipher` (their encryption), and `Link` with `LinkState` (the connection that stays open after a PvP exchange). |
| `Network/Relay/` | The relay as the games reach it: `Relays` (the relays by id, and the rooms a token leads to), `RelayFrameChannel` (a PvP room), `RelayWorldChannel` (a world room), `WebSocketMessages`. |
| `Network/Pvp/` | PvP battles: `Session` with `SessionState` (hosting, joining, the first exchange, the state the script reads) and `JoinCode`. |
| `Network/World/` | Worlds: `WorldSession` with `WorldState` (the connection to a world room), `WorldCode`, `WorldCipher`, `WorldKeys` (auth key and player id), `Player` (who plays) and `PlayerIni` (Player.ini, see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)), whose topics are in `SettingsTopics.cs` beside `IniFile.cs`; the directory: `WorldDirectory` and `DirectoryClient` (the list and its actions, and the story mapping, `DirectoryClient.Story.cs`), `WorldLock` (the token locked with the password), `StartingSave` (a world's starting save, zipped and encrypted), the last three sealing with `SealedBox` (an AES-GCM box with an HKDF key); `RaidStory` (a Raid World's shared companions, which the relay's keyed story keeps beside the tracks; the story itself plays on the tracks through `StoryTopics.cs` and `StoryTrack` of `MGQOnline.Game`); `BattleSession` (a battle the battle server hosts and its channel, `DirectoryClient.Battles.cs`); `RaidBosses` (a Raid World's boss pools at the relay, `DirectoryClient.Bosses.cs`). No folder is named `Link` or `Directory`, since a namespace of that name would hide the class `Link` and `System.IO.Directory`. |
| `../Relay/` | The relay server itself, see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md). |
| `Windows/GameWindow.cs` | Keeps the game running while another application is active, see [Background](#background), and hands typed keys to `Keyboard`. |
| `Windows/AltMenuGuard.cs` | When Alt may not open the game window's menu, in which the game would wait: for Alt pressed alone, while a text screen wants the keyboard, and for an Alt press that began while one did. |
| `Windows/Keyboard.cs` | What the player types while a text screen wants it, see [Keyboard](#keyboard). |
| `Windows/Clipboard.cs` | The join code and world ids on the clipboard. |
| `Windows/CrashEvents.cs` | The crashes of the game Windows recorded in its Application event log since the previous game session started, the newest 3 logged once per start on a thread of its own, never under Wine; the previous start is kept in `Logs\Multiplayer crash check.dat` (`MarkFile`). A crash in native code ends the game before anything can log it. |
| `Windows/ProcessUsage.cs` | The memory and the handles the game uses, one short line for `mp_memory_usage`, which `core_async.rbx` logs at each switch of screens. |
| `Windows/NativeMethods.cs` | The Windows functions the DLL needs to find itself. |
| `PlayerName.cs` | Cleans the name a player typed, for PvP and worlds alike. |
| `Mods/` | A world's mods: `CatalogMod` (an entry of the relay's catalog), `ModHash`, `ModOption` (a Mod Config option an admin's game read), `PatchScripts` (the scripts of the Patch folder, which the check, the mod picker and the details read) and `ParkedLeftovers` (the mods an older release left in the game folder's `Parked mods`, put back as the DLL starts). |
| `Reports/` | The in-game bug reports: `BugReports` (the screenshot kept for the report, and sending one report at a time to the relay on a thread of its own, which files it as a GitHub issue), `ReportFiles` (the report's files as the relay commits them: `report.md`, the screenshot, and on request the newest log of each type written since the game started, `errors.txt` and `mods.txt`, text files gzipped), `LastError` (the public fact *Last error* of a report that sends the logs: whether the newest error of an `errors.txt` written since the game started was raised in the mod's scripts, a Patch folder script such as another mod, or the game, by the top line of its trace) and `Png` (the screenshot as an 8-bit RGB PNG). `BugReportRequest` behind `ReportTopics` holds the request as `ui_bug_report.rbx` keeps it today, for the script to read once binding 18 drew its screen on the framework: the hotkey and the wheel refused where a report cannot open (the player types, the form is open, the title screen, one is sent, one waits, no name), the screenshot at the press or once the wheel faded out (`WheelFrames` 13), the form at once in a menu or on a free map, a waiting report forgotten on the title screen and a form another scene took the place of, sending the form's report with its facts and details cut to the relay's lengths, the sending followed every 30 frames (`PollFrames`) and its outcome a notice once the notification box shows, and what waits for the box. `report.tick` runs a frame up to a screenshot that is due (`true` answers: snap now, then `report.snapped`, which runs the frame's rest and answers whether the form opens); `report.on_map`, `report.wheel_refusal`, `report.wheel`, `report.take_opening`, `report.send` and `report.closed` follow; sounds, chat lines, the box's messages and the hotkey's refusals come as the events `report.sound`, `report.chat_line`, `report.notice` and `report.hotkey_refused`. The screenshot stays the script's (`ui.report.snap`); `mp_report_snap` keeps nothing and goes with the old exports. The golden cases, `fixtures/hud/report.json`, were recorded from the script of 3ee61503 by `ruby fixtures/hud/make_report_fixtures.rb`; `BugReportRequestTests` replays them. |
| `Log.cs`, `ScriptLog.cs`, `ModFolder.cs` | `Multiplayer <day>.log` in the game folder's `Logs` folder, one per day named after the day the game process started on, which the DLL finds two folders above its own, with the scripts' lines too (`ScriptLog`, the topics `log.write` and `log.flush`); past 4 MB it keeps its newest 2 MB (`MaxBytes`, `KeptBytes`, `CutToNewest`). |

**`package/Multiplayer/`:** the player `README.txt`.

**Updates.** The Mod Framework finds, counts and installs the updates of every mod, this one's too: MGQ Online is the website's mod `mgq-online`, tagged Title. The relay alone tells which release the games must play (`UpdateCheck`, `GET /v1/release`): once another one is the one, newer or a step back, the scripts keep worlds and PvP battles shut (`MGQ_Multiplayer.outdated?`), and *Multiplayer* on the title screen, a Discord invite into a world and the entry again after a restart ask the framework for it instead of opening the world screen (`MGQ_MpWorld::Update.ask`: `Mod_Framework::Mods.require("mgq-online", version, :exact => step back, :reason, :action => "multiplayer.open")`). The framework shows the question, has its helper install the release once the game closed and starts the game again, whose title opens the world screen (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Stages::OPEN_ACTION`). The title's commands wait while it asks (`Update.asking?`). Without the framework the player reads the update message. A player whose game cannot start runs the framework's `Update.bat`.

## Build and test

You need the .NET 10 SDK and the Visual Studio workload **Desktop development with C++**, whose linker NativeAOT uses and whose compiler builds SQLite into the DLL (see [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata)).

Publishing the DLL project assembles the complete release layout in `Shipping/` at the repository root. Copy its content into a game folder to install or update the mod there:

```
Patch/                            !Mod_Framework.rb  Multiplayer.rb
Patch/Multiplayer/                Multiplayer.dll  Manifest.txt  README.txt
Patch/Multiplayer/Data/           events.db
Patch/Multiplayer/Scripts/        *.rbx
Patch/Multiplayer/Mod_Framework/  the Mod Framework's copy
```

- **The Mod Framework ships with the mod**, the release `FrameworkVersion` in `MGQParadox.Multiplayer.csproj` names: its loader as `Patch/!Mod_Framework.rb`, which sorts before every mod's script, and its files as the copy inside the mod's folder, `Patch/Multiplayer/Mod_Framework/`, which the loader finds and picks unless a newer copy of the framework is installed (see the framework's own developer notes). A publish on this PC copies the framework's publish output from its checkout beside this repository (`..\MGQ-Modding-Framework\Shipping\Patch`) into `package/Framework/Patch/` (ignored by Git); the release workflow downloads that framework release's zip instead. Without it the publish stops with an error.

- **Publish, not build:** only a publish runs NativeAOT, so a plain build gives no usable DLL.

  ```powershell
  dotnet publish MGQParadox.Multiplayer -c Release -p:Version=0.1.0
  ```

- **Release publishes** also zip it: `bin/Release/MGQ-Online-<Version>.zip`.
- **Every publish replaces `Shipping/`.** Close the game before copying it over an install: `Multiplayer.dll` is locked while the game runs.
- **Logs:** one log, `Multiplayer <day>.log` in the game folder's `Logs` folder, which every mod writes its logs to, holds the DLL's lines and the scripts' (what the mod did, decided and refused, and errors) in one timeline, each script line marked `[script]` (`ScriptLog.Mark`). Each day has one, stamped with the day the game started on (`YYYY-MM-DD`, local time), so a session past midnight stays in its log and every game started that day appends to the same file (the DLL opens it shared for that); each session starts with the DLL's "--- multiplayer ... started ---" line and the scripts' "starting" line. The scripts' `Log.write` stages each line with its time of day as `log.write` into the frame's batch (see [The bridge in the scripts](#the-bridge-in-the-scripts)), so a line can land a few lines after a DLL line of the same frame; the lines written before the handshake wait and follow it. Before a bug report reads the log (`Log.hand_over`) and as the game's scenes end (`MGQ_Multiplayer.run_game`, an `around` on `SceneManager.run`: a quit returns from it, the window closing throws a `SystemExit` out of it and an error throws before the game's error box; each calls `MGQ_Multiplayer.closing`, the closing line once per ending) the scripts send what they staged at once and call `log.flush`, which writes the queue on the calling thread with how often the last line repeated. The scenes' end is the last moment the scripts run: the game's Main section (the WF-RGSS Exit-EX plugin) then ends the process with `ExitProcess` (`Exit.quit`, WFExit.dll), so Ruby runs no `at_exit` (kept as a last resort) and `AppDomain.ProcessExit` may never come in the NativeAOT DLL; as the game closes they also flush the story's last writes first and call `world.drain`, which waits until the world room sent them (`WorldSession.CloseWait`, 3 s at most), and a world left for the title screen closes its story before its room (`MGQ_MpWorld.leave`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.close`) for the same reason. While the mod stays off (the DLL missing, turned off or refusing the handshake) the scripts write their lines, the reason among them, into that file themselves (`Log::OWN_FILE`). A line repeated is counted, told at 10, 100, 1000 and 10000 repeats while it goes on and in all once another line comes or the game closes (`Log.Repeats`). There is no line limit; once the log passes 4 MB it keeps its newest 2 MB from a whole line on, behind a line that says the earlier ones were cut (`Log.MaxBytes`, `KeptBytes` and `CutLine`), since a bug report sends the newest lines. As the DLL starts (`mp_start`), it deletes every log of the mod but the day's (`Log.DeleteOtherDays`): those of earlier days, the scripts' own `Multiplayer InGame` logs and per-session logs of older builds and the unstamped ones of the oldest; only the files actually deleted are counted in the log, and other mods' logs stay. A bug report takes only the newest log of each type written since the game started (`ReportFiles.LogType`: a log's name without its day or session start), so earlier sessions' logs and those of earlier days stay out. The DLL logs the world's messages as one line about every minute (`WorldTraffic`: messages and kilobytes in and out, by kind, and the seats they came from), plus each message it could not send, and the scripts log their hooks as one line per script with how many methods it follows and wraps (`MGQ_MpHooks.report`, after loading and every frame), plus each one registered again or failing.
- **Two games on one PC** can play each other: both go through the real relay as they would between two homes.
- **A relay on this PC:** run `node Relay/node/server.js` (port 8080, `ADMINS` for admin ids, `MGQ_RELAY_DB=<file>` to keep worlds, saves, mods and trades in a SQLite database instead of memory) and start the game with the environment variable `MGQMP_RELAY=ws://127.0.0.1:8080`; the DLL then talks to that relay instead of the live one, which lets a build with relay changes be tried before the relay is deployed.

### Tests

`MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests` covers the DLL without the game: the join code, its relay room and codes of other versions, messages, their encryption (another token, a changed, repeated, missing or returned frame), the link (messages both ways, pings keeping a quiet link open, goodbye, silence, oversized messages) and whole exchanges between two sessions, including the party both sides name and what hosting and joining say when the relay finds no host, cannot be reached, turns the game away as too old or is unknown. Everything meets at a relay inside the test process (`TestRelay`), which never reaches the real one; like `relay.js`, it closes a peer that sends more than 60 messages a second after a burst of 240 with 4003, in world rooms and PvP rooms alike, and counts those closes (`TooFastCloses`). It builds into an executable that runs itself:

```powershell
dotnet run --project MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests
```

The tests run 32-bit like the game and need the x86 .NET 10 runtime (`C:\Program Files (x86)\dotnet`); with `DOTNET_ROOT` pointing at the x64 install they need `DOTNET_ROOT_X86` pointing at the x86 one.

`GameScript/Tests` covers the scripts outside the game, one `*_test.rb` file per area (the loader, the shared hooks, the open world, parties, battles, worlds, the hotkeys and the boxes on screen), with stand-ins for the parts of RGSS and the game each script touches. Each file runs in a Ruby process of its own, since each brings its own stand-ins; `support.rb` gives them `check` and `load_script`, `world_support.rb` the open world's stand-ins that the overworld, duel and overview tests share, `battle_support.rb` the live battle's that the co-op, hotjoining and team duel tests share, `bridge_support.rb` the bridge to C# (see [The bridge in the scripts](#the-bridge-in-the-scripts)), and `world_room_support.rb` the DLL's world room on a fake bridge: it records what the scripts hand over and plays its events (`peer_told`, `message_from`, `take_party`) into the scripts' mirror, without the room's rules. With Ruby 3.3 or later:

```powershell
ruby GameScript/Tests/run.rb        # -v shows every check
```

The *Scripts* workflow runs them on every push, and the *Release* workflow before it builds a release. They run on a newer Ruby than the game's (1.9), so they do not catch what only that one lacks. Drawing, input and the game's own battle and event engine stay untested: those need the game.

## Bridge (`MGQOnline.Bridge`)

The managed core of the bridge between the scripts and C# (the refactor's `0-bridge.md`): a net10.0 library, AOT-clean and BCL only, for the win-x86 DLL and the battle server's linux-x64 core alike. It holds no statics with state; everything hangs off a context. The DLL reaches it through the four exports of `MGQOnline.Bridge.Native`, the scripts through `MGQ_Multiplayer::Bridge` (see [The bridge in the scripts](#the-bridge-in-the-scripts)).

- **Codec** (`Ruby/`): `RubyMarshal.Read` and `Write` take Marshal 4.8 as a `RubyValue` tree: `RubyNil`, `RubyBool`, `RubyInteger` (a `BigInteger`), `RubyString` (bytes and the encoding Ruby marked them with: UTF-8, US-ASCII as `Integer#to_s` gives it, or binary), `RubyArray` and `RubyHash` (in Ruby's order; keys compare as Ruby's `eql?`). **Strict mode**, the default, takes only those, with String or Integer keys (switch and variable ids), and refuses anything else by name (`RPG::Item cannot cross the bridge.`), symbols, floats, other encodings and an Array that holds itself included. **Full mode** reads game data: `RubyObject` (also Structs), `RubySymbol`, `RubyFloat`, `RubyUserData` (a dumped class such as Table, as its bytes), `RubyUserObject`, `RubyRegexp`, `RubyModule`. A full-mode read may go through a `MarshalPruner`, which leaves out the fields a reader never needs (read past, as `RubySkipped`), builds its own value of each object as it finishes (a `RubyBox<T>`, which also stands where the data links back to the object) and seals objects nothing links into, so the read lets go of their contents at once: the game's 49 MB CommonEvents.rvdata2 reads into about 650 MB whole, more than the game's 32-bit process or the battle server core's 256 MB heap take. The writer writes plain values only, as Ruby 3.3 on Windows does: integers outside RGSS3's 31-bit Fixnum range as Bignums (1.9.2 would read a larger `i` wrong), repeated Hash keys as links. Wrong kinds and refusals throw `RubyValueException`.
- **Host and handshake:** `BridgeHost` is what the four exports call, one per process in the export layer. `Open` (mp_bridge_open) compares the scripts' version with `BridgeHost.Version` (BRIDGE_VERSION), refuses another one, and otherwise makes a `BridgeContext`, lets the host's setup register its topics, and answers `{"bridge"=>N, "build"=>..., "context"=>id, "topics"=>[...]}`. `Call`, `TakeReply` and `Drain` route to a context by its id; `Close` drops one. A handshake that opens a context closes the older ones, since F12 restarts the scripts, which shake hands again, but not the process.
- **Dispatcher** (`BridgeContext.Call`, mp_call): runs the topic's `BridgeHandler` with the request read in strict mode (no request bytes means nil) and answers in the `BridgeEnvelope`: `{"ok"=>true, "value"=>...}`, nothing at all (0) for a nil value, or `{"ok"=>false, "error"=>code, "text"=>...}` with `unknown_topic`, `bad_request` (a refused request, a wrong kind, or a handler's `BridgeRequestException`) or `internal` (any other exception, or a reply that cannot cross). It holds the core's only catch of handlers and logs the first failure of each topic and code. A reply larger than the caller's buffer is **parked**: the call returns the size needed negated, `TakeReply` (mp_reply) hands it out into a larger buffer, and the handler never runs twice. A call that comes while a reply is still parked drops it and logs that. A handler may build on another area's topics in-process with `BridgeContext.Ask(topic, request)`, which runs that topic's handler and hands back its plain value, outside the envelope (a failure throws to the asking handler, whose envelope reports it).
- **Batch:** every context has the topic `batch` (`BridgeContext.BatchTopic`), which takes what the scripts staged during a frame as `[[topic, payload], ...]` and runs each handler in order, dropping their replies. It checks every pair first, so a broken batch runs none (`bad_request`); an entry that fails does not stop the ones after it. It answers nothing when every entry ran, else the failed entries in order as `{"topic"=>..., "error"=>code, "text"=>...}`. A batch inside a batch is refused.
- **Events** (`BridgeContext.Events`, a `BridgeEventQueue`): first in, first out. A status topic (`Coalesce`) keeps only its latest event, at the place of that event; past the limit (1024 message events by default) the oldest message events are dropped, logged once until the next drain. `Post` refuses a payload that cannot cross. All of it is for the game thread; network threads hand their work to a frame step (`EveryFrame`).
- **Drain** (`BridgeContext.Drain`, mp_drain): sets `Frame`, the core's clock, from the frame passed (rules read it, never a wall clock), runs the frame steps, then writes every queued event as one Marshal Array `[[topic, payload], ...]`, 0 when there is none. One that does not fit is parked the same way and handed out by the next drain, which runs no frame step.
- **Counters:** per topic (`Counters`, the drain's under `(drain)`): calls, bytes in and out, the longest call and errors, logged one line per topic and reset every 36,000 frames (`CountersEveryFrames`, 10 minutes) or when the frame goes back.
- **Log:** the core writes through `IBridgeLog` only, never to a file: the DLL passes its file log (`GameBridge.FileLog`, into `Multiplayer <day>.log`), the battle server core turns each line into a `log` event (see [The battle server's core](#the-battle-servers-core-mgqonlineservercore)).
- **Exports** (`MGQOnline.Bridge.Native/BridgeExports.cs`): `mp_bridge_open` (`lpl`: version, reply buffer, size), `mp_call` (`lpplpl`: context, topic null-terminated, request, its length, reply buffer, size), `mp_reply` (`lpl`: context, reply buffer, size) and `mp_drain` (`llpl`: context, frame, buffer, size), Stdcall `UnmanagedCallersOnly`, over the one host `BridgeExports.Install` set; without one, or when an exception gets past the host (logged), they answer 0, as a stand-in DLL does. NativeAOT exports a referenced assembly's entry points only when the publishing project names it, so a native library exports them with `<UnmanagedEntryPointsAssembly Include="MGQOnline.Bridge.Native" />` and a `[ModuleInitializer]` that installs its host, which NativeAOT runs as the runtime starts, before the first export runs. **Trap:** NativeAOT runs a module initializer only for an assembly it compiled code of; a library whose own assembly nothing but the initializer reaches roots it (`<TrimmerRootAssembly>`), or the exports answer 0 for good. The DLL does both in its csproj and `GameBridge.cs` (with its build, `UpdateCheck.ModVersion`, and its topics in `GameBridge.RegisterTopics`) and keeps its 88 old exports beside them, which reach its assembly; `MGQOnline.ServerCore` does the same with its own log and topics. On x64 the Stdcall is ignored, so the same source serves both.
- **Tests:** `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Bridge.Tests` (64-bit, any .NET 10 runtime). The codec's golden dumps are in `fixtures/bridge/codec/`, written by `make_fixtures.rb` there; run it again only on Ruby for Windows, whose Marshal writes the Fixnum borders as RGSS3 does. The contract fixtures, `fixtures/bridge/<topic>/<case>.request.marshal` and `.reply.marshal` (an empty reply file for no reply), are written by `fixtures/bridge/make_topic_fixtures.rb`: the C# tests check that the core answers each request with the reply's bytes, the script tests that the scripts send the request's bytes and read the reply.

### The bridge in the scripts

`MGQ_Multiplayer::Bridge` in `Multiplayer.rb` is the scripts' side. Requests, replies and events are plain values (nil, true, false, Integer, String in UTF-8, US-ASCII or binary, Array, and Hash with String or Integer keys); the core refuses anything else, such as a Symbol, a Float or a game object, and the call then answers nil and logs why.

- **Handshake:** `MGQ_Multiplayer.start` calls `mp_start`, then `Bridge.start`, which loads the four exports (`Win32Backend`) and shakes hands with `Bridge::VERSION`. Only a handshake that succeeds sets `MGQ_Multiplayer.available?`, which every mode and screen asks before it starts, and only then do the background hook and the update check start. A missing DLL, one that lacks the exports (an older build), a stand-in that answers 0 (JoiPlay) or a DLL of another bridge version keeps the mod off, with one line in the in-game log (`bridge: the mod stays off, ...`). `Bridge.has?(topic)` tells whether the DLL named a topic in the handshake.
- **Calling:** `Bridge.call(topic, request = nil)` dumps the request, calls `mp_call` and loads the reply's `value`; no reply (0) is nil. An error reply gives nil and is logged once per topic and code (`bridge: <topic> failed with <code>: <text>`), as is a script error around the call. `Bridge.call!` raises `Bridge::Error` instead, for the few callers that must know, and while the bridge is closed.
- **Staging:** `Bridge.stage(topic, payload)` keeps what a script hands over during a frame, such as writes. `Bridge.frame`, in `Multiplayer.rb`'s `Graphics.update` hook, sends everything staged as one `batch` call, logs each failed entry once, then calls `mp_drain` once with the bridge's own frame counter (not `Graphics.frame_count`, which goes back when a save loads) and hands each event to the handlers `Bridge.on(topic) { |payload| ... }` registered, in order; an event nobody takes and a handler that fails are logged once, and the other handlers still run. `Bridge.frame_number` tells the frame the last drain passed, which times C# counts in frames come as, such as the party invite's `invite_ends`. `Bridge.flush` sends what was staged at once, outside the frame, such as the log's last lines as the game closes. Nothing is staged while the bridge is closed.
- **Buffers:** one reply buffer (`REPLY_SIZE`, 4 KB) and one drain buffer (`DRAIN_SIZE`, 16 KB), allocated once at the handshake and grown for good when the DLL parks a larger reply (`mp_reply`) or drain (the next `mp_drain` of the frame). A reply is loaded at once and the slice never kept, since the next call writes into the same buffer behind Ruby's back.
- **Backends:** `Win32Backend` in the game; `FiddleBackend` on the battle server, which loads the core library through Fiddle (`dlopen` and the four functions with Fiddle's types for the same signatures, no call yet) and calls it as `Win32Backend` does; the game never makes one, since RGSS3 has no Fiddle. Any object with `open`, `call`, `reply` and `drain` works, which is how the tests plug in.
- **Adding a topic:** in C#, register a handler on the context, `context.Register("area.name", (context, request) => ...)`, in the host's setup (`GameBridge.RegisterTopics` for the DLL), reading the request with `AsHash`, `AsInt`, `AsText` and the like (a wrong kind answers `bad_request`, as does a `BridgeRequestException`) and returning a `RubyValue` or `null`; post events with `context.Events.Post` (`Coalesce` for status topics). In Ruby, call it with `Bridge.call` or stage it with `Bridge.stage`, and take its events with `Bridge.on`. Cover the handler with xUnit, and the script with a `FakeBridge` (`GameScript/Tests/bridge_support.rb`), whose `answer(topic) { |request| ... }` blocks stand in for the handlers; it round-trips every request and reply through Marshal and the core's plain-value check, so a test fails where the game would refuse a value, and it runs the `batch` topic as the core does. A topic whose bytes both sides must agree on gets a case in `fixtures/bridge/make_topic_fixtures.rb`.
- **What changed in the worlds:** the topics of worlds and mods share one `WorldServices` per context (the one `PlayerIni`, the world directory, the scripts of the Patch folder and `WorldChanges`). `WorldChanges` posts at most one event `worlds.changed` per drain, an Integer of bits (`List`, `Details`, `Mods`, `Settings`, `Parking`, `Screen`, `Overview`), marked by a topic (`Mark`) or by a revision that moved since the last drain (`Watch`), so the scripts ask for rows, details or steps only after a change; `MGQ_MpWorld::Changes.take?(bits)` takes them, true at the first look.
- **Tests:** `GameScript/Tests/bridge_test.rb` covers the handshake gating `available?`, calls and errors, one batch per frame, the drain's dispatch, the buffers growing and the export signatures against `BridgeExports.cs`; `MGQOnline.Bridge.Tests` covers the exports through their unmanaged entry points.

## The battle server's core (`MGQOnline.ServerCore`)

The bridge's C# side on the battle server (`BattleServer/`): a NativeAOT shared library with the four exports and nothing else (`Bridge`, `Bridge.Native`, `Data` with SQLite linked in and `Game`, no network, no file writes, `InvariantGlobalization`, so it loads nothing from `/usr/lib` once a battle's sandbox hid it). `ServerBridge.cs` installs its host with the build from its informational version (`0.0.0-dev` unless `-p:Version` stamps one) and registers its topics; each area adds its own there as it moves. Besides `batch` it answers `core.runtime`: `{"heap_limit"=>bytes, "server_gc"=>false, "latency"=>"Batch", "sqlite"=>"3.54.1"}`, what the GC runs with and the SQLite it links, and the topics both hosts answer (`SharedTopics`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)).

- **Events database** (`ServerEvents`): the core opens `Patch/Multiplayer/Data/events.db` (`EventSchema.GamePath`) under the game folder at the first question and keeps it for the life of the process, one child per relay connection, with `EventStore.ServerCacheKib`; immutable and read-only, so a sandboxed child (the folder is its root, `FSIZE` 0) writes no journal or temporary file. `Core.open` hands the core the game data's fingerprint right after each handshake (`core.game_data`, `MGQ_MpWorld::GameData.fingerprint`, a String; nothing when MGQ Online is not loaded), against which `EventDataGate` checks the file; each outcome (opened, a problem, the parts that differ) is logged once.
- **Story index** (`StoryIndexTopic`, `story.index`, asked by `BattleServer/lib/story_index.rb`): `{"keys" => [{"key", "kind"}], "events" => [{"key", "share"}]}` in; `SelfSwitchIndex.Derive(EventStore, ...)` over the store, refused as a bad request (the relay keeps the index it has) when the store is foreign, the fingerprint was not handed over, or the store differs from it in the maps or the common events (`ReadParts`); the derive's time is logged in milliseconds (about 30 ms over 3.06). The answer carries the names of the story's keys too, from `StoryNamesTopic` (`story.names`, its own topic): a read of the game folder's whole data (`StoryNames.Of(GameData.OfFolder(...))`), whose names are the translation's, since the events database holds none; the generator's `names` file is meant to replace that read.
