- **Log:** `EventLog` turns every line into a `log` event of the open context, which the battle server writes into its log (a sandboxed child may write no file). Lines from before the first handshake, such as a refused one, wait for it, up to 64.
- **GC:** workstation and non-concurrent (one thread less per battle child against the sandbox's `NPROC`), with a heap limit of 256 MB baked in (`HeapHardLimit` in the csproj, as `System.GC.HeapHardLimit`). The GC reserves about five times its limit as address space, about 1.3 GB, which must fit the sandbox's 6 GB beside the game the child shares. `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit` still overrides it, in hexadecimal *without* `0x` (NativeAOT reads `0x10000000` as no limit).
- **linux-x64:** NativeAOT cannot build across systems, so `MGQOnline.ServerCore/build.ps1 [-Version x]` builds it in Docker, in the pinned `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0-noble-aot` image (Ubuntu 24.04, glibc 2.39; the library needs `GLIBC_2.34` and links only `libc`, `libm` and `ld-linux`), into `MGQOnline.ServerCore/bin/linux-x64/MGQOnline.ServerCore.so`. `container-build.sh` is what runs inside: it copies the five projects without their `bin/` and `obj/`, publishes, and prints the size, sha256, needed libraries and exports. The *Release* workflow's `server-core` job runs the same script on Ubuntu after the mod's build and attaches `MGQOnline.ServerCore.so` to the release; the mod zip does not hold it.
- **win-x64**, for a battle server on Windows during development: `dotnet publish MGQOnline.ServerCore -c Release -r win-x64` gives `MGQOnline.ServerCore/bin/Release/net10.0/win-x64/publish/MGQOnline.ServerCore.dll`.
- **On the battle server** (`BattleServer/lib/core.rb`, `server.rb`): `BATTLE_CORE_LIBRARY` names the library. `Core.library` checks it once as the server starts and logs it; none, a file that is not there, or game scripts without `Bridge::FiddleBackend` (an MGQ Online older than the bridge) leave the core out with one line. MGQ Online's scripts ask the core for the rules they share with the game (the ids they make up, the squad rules and math, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)), so a server whose core does not answer (`Core.ready?`: the bridge is open in the process that hosts) refuses each MGQ Online battle the relay hands over with `refused`, code `core` and why (`Core.why_missing`), rather than hosting it half; the starter then hosts it, and does not ask again in that session. In fork mode each battle's child does it in `enter_child`: `Core.load` (`dlopen` through `FiddleBackend`) after the fork and before `Sandbox.enter`, since the chroot hides the library's path, and `Core.open`, the handshake and so the first call, after the sandbox closed, so the runtime starts under its limits. A library that cannot be loaded or does not shake hands is logged, and the child refuses its battle. Nothing calls the core in the parent before a fork: a child of a process whose runtime started hangs at its first garbage collection, since a fork copies no runtime thread. In thread mode (Windows, `BATTLE_FORK=0`) the server loads and opens it once as it starts. `Core.open` hands the `log` events of every drain (each frame of a battle) to MGQ Online's log, which `Online` writes into the server's (`[mgq] core: ...`). `Online.install`'s stand-ins for the old exports stay as they are.
- **Tests:** `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.ServerCore.Tests` (64-bit, any .NET 10 runtime) calls the exports over the installed host: the handshake's build and topics, the log leaving through the drain, and `core.runtime`; `StoryIndexTopicTests` derives the index from a tiny events database written under a game folder (the events that read or call a shared key, a map named personal, the time logged) and covers its refusals (the maps differing while another part may, no database, no fingerprint handed over, a row without its key), and with `EVENTS_SOURCE_DIR` and `BATTLE_GAME_DIR` that the untranslated copy's database passes the installed game's `GameData.ini` fingerprint and derives with the installed game's names; `core_library_test.rb` also checks that the real library runs the SQLite of the checked-in amalgamation, and `core_check.rb` that each sandboxed child does. `BattleServer/Tests/child_test.rb` covers which library a child takes, the order of loading, sandbox and handshake with a stand-in library, the game data's fingerprint a child hands the core after the handshake, the battle a child without the core refuses, and the mods a child reads of its setup before the core opens; `BattleServer/tools/security_test.rb` (by hand on the server, as root) also has the core read the events database inside the sandbox and finds no journal or temporary file beside it; `core_library_test.rb` runs the real one when `BATTLE_CORE_LIBRARY` names it (the win-x64 `.dll` on Windows). `BattleServer/tools/core_check.ps1` runs `core_check.rb` as root in a privileged Ubuntu 26.04 container with `ruby3.3` from apt, as on the server: it forks two children whose parent holds the battle server parent's address space (1229 MB), prepares each with `server.rb`'s own `enter_child`, calls `core.runtime`, runs a batch of 1000 entries, drains and prints a table; the *Release* workflow runs it before it attaches the library.

## Events database (`MGQOnline.Data`)

The reader and writer of the events database (the refactor's `MGQ Online events database research.md`): a net10.0 library, AOT-clean and BCL only, over an own thin P/Invoke layer of SQLite, for the win-x86 DLL and the battle server's linux-x64 core alike.

- **P/Invoke layer** (`Sqlite/`): `SqliteNative` imports the C API functions it needs from `e_sqlite3` (Cdecl, blittable). `SqliteConnection.Open(fileName, mode)` opens a file, `:memory:` (`InMemory`) or a `file:` URI with query parameters such as `immutable=1`, read-only or read-write-create; `Execute` runs SQL without rows; `Prepare` hands out a `SqliteStatement` prepared once per connection and reused for the same SQL (`Bind`, `Step`, `Int64`, `Text`, `IsNull`; parameters count from 1, columns from 0), which its caller disposes of to reset it before the same SQL is prepared again. SQLite's refusals throw `SqliteException` with its result code and message. A connection is not thread-safe; the store that owns it holds a lock.
- **Static SQLite:** `native/sqlite/sqlite3.c` and `sqlite3.h` are the public-domain amalgamation 3.54.1 as released (`sqlite-amalgamation-3540100.zip`, SHA3-256 `92281c86e2957f9b9305890f9ac04204caa7b677cc868dc326d942689df28e30`), checked in untouched; to move to another release, replace both files from sqlite.org's amalgamation zip. No compiled library is ever checked in: `native/StaticSqlite.targets`, which the DLL and the server core import, compiles the amalgamation in every NativeAOT publish (target `BuildStaticSqlite`, before `LinkNative`, again only when the source or the targets change) into `MGQOnline.Data/obj/sqlite/<rid>/` and lists it as a `NativeLibrary` with `e_sqlite3` as a `DirectPInvoke`, so the imports become static calls and no SQLite library ships beside the binary. Windows compiles with `native/build-sqlite.cmd` (`cl /O1 /Os /MT` under the `vcvarsall.bat` that vswhere finds, as NativeAOT's own `findvcvarsall.bat` does), other systems with `clang -O2 -fPIC` and `ar`. The flags (`SqliteDefines`) leave out extensions, WAL, JSON, deprecated functions, shared cache and the progress callback, keep temporary tables in memory (a sandboxed battle child may write no file) and the default page cache at 64 KB. The win-x86 DLL grows by about 520 KB and gains no dependency but the CRT's `api-ms-win-crt-time` API set; the DLL logs `the events database reader runs sqlite 3.54.1` as it starts.
- **Schema** (`Events/EventSchema.cs`, `Version` 2): the game's events by id, numbers only, no name or text in any column; application id `0x4D475145` ("MGQE") and `user_version` = the schema version in the file's header, page size 4096. Map ids count a thousand per map folder, pages count from 0. Tables: `meta` (schema, generator and SQLite version, game version, source, fingerprint, input hashes, the kept and the text codes), `fingerprint_parts`, `counts`; `maps`, `map_encounters`, `map_events`; `lists` (one per map page, common event and troop page, with `count_all` as the game runs it, `count_kept` and `shape`; indexed by site only where it has one), `pages` (keyed by site: the six conditions, move type and the derived page flags), `common_events`, `troops`, `troop_members`, `troop_pages`; `commands` (only `KeptCodes`, the codes any rule reads: `ord` among the kept commands, which a translation leaves, and `idx` in the list the game runs, `p0`..`p5` numbers and `text_kind`; keyed by list and `ord` with no index by `idx`, since a list's commands are read whole and found by place in memory); the per-list rows `list_reads` (with `ReadSources`), `list_writes` (with `value`, the constant a Control Variables command sets), `list_self_switches`, `list_calls`, `list_battles` (with `battle_no`, `preamble_idx` and `side_switch`), `list_transfers`, `list_tilesets`; `site_alternatives`, `scene_entries`, `chests`, `repair_pairs` (version 2); `switches`, `variables`, `items`, `enemies`. Views: `battle_sites` (every battle against a troop by its id on a map page or in a common event, with its place, indent, preamble, side switch and whether its map is a cutscene map; troop pages hold none), `site_callers` (the map pages that reach each common event through calls, through other common events too) and `defeat_writes` (the common events the game's post-defeat processing, common event 3000, runs, each with the ranges it writes by Control Switches and Control Variables on any branch). The rows are records of the same names (`MapRow`, `ListRow`, `CommandRow`, `BattleSiteRow`, ...) and `EventTables` holds each table's columns, key, write and read in one place.
- **Shape** (`ListShape`): the 24-bit hash `h = (h * 31 + v) & 0xFFFFFF` over the code, the indent and every whole number parameter of a list's kept commands, so it stays a Fixnum on RGSS3's 32-bit Ruby and a list whose texts alone were translated keeps it, while a changed branch, call, troop or variable changes it. `fixtures/events/hash_cases.json` (written by `ruby fixtures/events/make_hash_cases.rb`, the scripts' hash) holds the cases both sides test.
- **Writer** (`EventDatabaseWriter`): `Build(rows)` writes an `EventRows` (every table's rows in any order, with `EventBuildInfo`) into an in-memory database in one transaction, each table in the order of its key, journal off; `Write(rows, path)` then writes it out with `VACUUM INTO`, so the same rows give the same bytes (with the same SQLite).
- **Store** (`EventStore`): `Open(eventsPath, mirrorPath?, cacheKib)` opens the file as `file:...?immutable=1&mode=ro`, so it takes no lock and writes no journal (a read-only game folder, Wine, a sandboxed battle child), with `cache_size` 256 KB in the DLL (`DllCacheKib`) or 4 MB on the server (`ServerCacheKib`), temporary tables in memory, no memory mapping and `query_only` without a mirror; `Over(connection)` reads a database `Build` made. A missing file, one that is no events database or one of another schema version makes the store foreign: it opens without throwing, `Problem` tells why, and every question answers nothing. The questions are by site, never a whole table: `ListOf(map, event, page)`, `CommonList`, `TroopPage`, `List`, `Matches(list, countAll, shape)`, `Commands(list)` (the last 64 kept), `IdxOfOrd`, `OrdOfIdx`, `RowsOf(list)` (the per-list rows, the last 512 kept), `CallersOf(common)`, `MapHasFile`, `MapEvents`, `Page`, `Pages(map)`, `Chests(map)`, `Common`, `SwitchFlags`, `VariableFlags`, `Item`, `Enemy`, `Troop`, `TroopMembers`, `Alternatives`, `SceneEntries`, `TransfersInto`, `BattleSites(troop)`, `SiteCallers(common)` (the view's walk started from that one common event's calls), `DefeatWrites()`, and `Meta` (`EventStoreMeta`). For the derived index of the story mapping it also answers `ListsTouching(switches, variables)` (the lists that read one of the keys, other than as a common event's own switch, or set a range holding one: one question over `list_reads` and one over `list_writes` per kind), `ListsCalling(commons)`, `MapIds()`, `CommonIds()` and `CutsceneMaps()`, and `MapEvent(map, event)`. One connection per store under a lock.
- **Rules over the store** (`MGQOnline.Game`): `EventStoreSource` is the rules' `IEventSource` over a store, each question by its site: a page's or common event's kept commands (`EventStoreSource.CommandOf`, a jump naming its label's `ord`, no script keys), a list's keys from its rows (`ListRows.Of(ListRowSet, runningMap)`, which puts a page's transfers back to the numbers their commands hold and leaves out battles and transfers by variable; a common event's without its own switch, `WithoutTriggerSwitch`), callers, and scratch flags by id; it reads nothing ahead. `EventSorter` runs over it as over `GameDataSource`; `EventCommandIndex` (`TroopsOf`, `WritesOf`, `VariableSetsOf`) and `EventChain.VariableSetsOf` take a stored list by its `ListKeys` (`PageKeys`, `CommonKeys`), since a database list's commands carry no script keys and a list passed as commands loses its script writes; `SelfSwitchIndex.Derive(EventStore, keys, events)` derives the index by the store's questions (`ListsTouching`, the callers' closure without the player's own common events, `ListsCalling`) and applies the whole-event rows as the walk does. `EventDataGate.Check(store, gameFingerprint)` compares the store's `meta.fingerprint` with the game data's (`GameDataCompare.Differing`) and `RefusalFor(parts)` tells why the tables of some parts may not be read (a foreign store, a game data not handed over, or one of those parts differing, such as `GameDataCompare.MapsPart` and `CommonEventsPart`), so a mod that changes another part leaves them readable; `GatedEventSource` is a source as far as a gate lets it be read (the map events' pages while the maps match, the common events while those match), so a rule meets a list of a differing part as it meets a mod's. `RaidSiteIndex` finds the frozen fights' story sites of a troop over the store by `RaidRules` (a battle on a page of an excluded map or of one that turns on an arena switch is none, nor one in a common event only such pages reach, as the generator's `RaidSites` reads them) and the map pages that reach a site's common event. The battle server's core derives the story index from the store (see [The battle server's core](#the-battle-servers-core-mgqonlineservercore)), the DLL answers its topics from it (In the DLL, below).
- **In the DLL** (`MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics`): `GameEvents.Current` opens `Patch/Multiplayer/Data/events.db` under the game folder (`EventSchema.GamePath`) with `EventStore.DllCacheKib` at the first question and keeps it for the session; its `Source` is an `EventStoreSource` behind a `GatedEventSource` of the gate against the game data's fingerprint, which the scripts hand over once per session before their first question about events (`events.open {fingerprint}`, `MGQ_MpWorld::GameData.fingerprint`; answers `usable`, `problem` and `differing`, and the outcome is logged once). Before the fingerprint came, without the file or with one of another schema nothing of the database is read: every page and common event is unknown, so the scripts hand its commands over, which still sort. `events.sort`, `events.index` and `story.leaves_part` find a map event's page or a common event only while the scripts hold its list as the database does: `size` (`count_all`) and `shape` (`ListShape`, which the scripts compute once per list object, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.shape`) both match, else `unknown`; an `index` (a live place in the game's list) becomes the kept command's `ord` (`OrdOfIdx`; a command the database does not keep, such as a text line, moves no story on); the command index and the chain read a stored list's keys from its rows. A common event's list and a list handed over carry the running map and event (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.running`), inside whose map folder the chain follows a transfer, as for a page. `events.kinds` takes each page as [event, page, size, shape] or, for those the first call answered nil, as [event, page, commands] in one more call (`page_kinds`, `sort_pages`). `events.index` also takes a map's pages at once (`map`, `pages` as for `events.kinds`) and answers `indexes`, each page's answer or nil, which the raid bar asks for the troops of the map's pages and the frozen fights for what a page writes. The topics of `EventTableTopics` read rows for the scripts: `events.chests {map, rows}` (each row [event, page, size, shape]; the letter of a chest page in a new game, false for another page, nil for one held otherwise), `events.pages {map, rows}` (`scene_page`, `runs_nothing`, `fades_in`, `sets_encounters`), `troops.members {troops}` (each enemy as [enemy, x, y, hidden]), `raid.sites {troops}` (the frozen fights' tables for those troops, as `MGQ_MpRaidFreeze.tables` reads them: `sites` (site, index, indent, preamble's index, cutscene map and battle number), `callers`, `alternatives` among the troops asked, `scenes`, `scene_maps` and `defeat` with `events`, `switches` and `variables`, every one empty while the maps, common events or troops differ; and `RaidRules`' `excluded_maps` and `preamble_codes` always) and `files.exists` (a path inside the game folder, found in the archive's index or loose, never read). The DLL no longer reads the game's data files for events.
- **Relay mirror** (`MirrorSchema`, `Version` 1, `StoryMirror`): `Patch/Multiplayer/Data/story.db` (`MirrorSchema.GamePath`), the game's copy of the relay's story mapping (`GET /v1/story-mapping`), written only by the DLL, in no manifest and never shipped, so an update that replaces `events.db` leaves it. `Open` attaches it to the store's connection as `relay` when given its path and makes its tables where missing: `mirror_meta`, `story_keys` and `story_events` (each row's key, its place in the relay's order and its options in typed columns), `story_index_events`, `story_index_commons`, one `catchup_<name>` per catch-up table of the relay (`MirrorSchema.CatchupTables`: `ord`, the relay's story order; `row_id`, its key; typed columns; its site with the page from 0; the relay's command index `idx`, not yet checked against the events database, `idx_checked` 0) and `other_rows` for a table the mirror has none for; every row keeps its JSON. A file of another version (`user_version`) gets the tables anew, empty; a torn file (SQLite's "not a database" or "corrupt", at the attach or later) is deleted and made anew, never repaired; a mirror that cannot be attached (a folder the game may not write) leaves the store without one (`MirrorProblem`). `StoryMirror.Write(answer, stamp, time)` empties every table and fills it from an answer with rows in one transaction under the store's lock, `mirror_meta` last (`schema_version`, `relay`, `game_fingerprint`, `rev`, the `tables` the answer held, `index_built`, `index_stale`, `idx_checked`, `fetched_utc`), never by a file swap, since a file attached cannot be replaced on Windows. `Read(stamp)` gives the answer back as the relay gave it (`MirrorRead`), or nothing: a mirror of another schema, relay (scheme, host and port) or events database fingerprint is emptied, and one holding a row whose text does not read (SQLite keeps no checksum per cell) is made anew; its note says why.
- **Generator** (`MGQOnline.Data.Generator`): a 64-bit JIT console tool, run by hand on the developer's PC, never by a publish or CI (which has no game data). `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Data.Generator -- build "<MGQ Paradox src>" <events.db>` reads the untranslated 3.06 (its `Game.rgss3a`, through `GameFolderFiles`) and writes the file; the options `--game-version` and `--source` (default `3.06`, `untranslated`) name what it read in `meta`. It reads with a pruner of its own (`Source/SourcePruner.cs`), wider than the DLL's: every kept command's parameters (flags and texts too, which the shape and `can_escape` read), page conditions with item and actor, move type, event positions, map tilesets and encounters, troop members and page conditions, the map lists' names, and of the named parts of the database whether each entry holds a note, features or effects; the names and texts the flags are read from (switches', variables' and common events' names, items' types and descriptions, enemies' notes) it reads into flags and never writes. Its rows (`Build/EventBuilder.cs`, `ListBuild.cs`): a common event's list as the game runs it (145 - Module.rb drops the marks it starts with and puts one mark first carrying the id its object holds, which differs from its place for about 4,900 of them; common events and troops are keyed by place, as the game finds them, `leading_marks` keeps the marks dropped); list ids in a fixed order (map pages by map, event and page, then common events, then troop pages); the kept commands with `ord` and `idx`, a jump's label as its `ord` (-1 for none) and the `CommandClassifier`'s text kind; the per-list rows from `ListRows` (a read's `ReadSources`, a common event's own switch as `TriggerSwitch` when it starts by itself, a continuation line's writes on its script command); every battle numbered (`battle_no`) with its troop or variable, flags, `preamble_idx` (`RaidRules.PreambleStart`) and side switch; transfers with their side switch; tileset changes; maps under a root named イベント (or the translations' Events and Event) as `cutscene`; the fingerprint (`GameFingerprint`, world.rbx's rule after the marks and with the two skills the game appends, which have effects); `counts`; and `input_hashes`, each file read with its SHA-256 and size and `DataEx.rvdata2` as present or absent. Once every list is read, `Build/DerivedBuild.cs` derives the rest over the lists kept in memory (`BuiltGame`, which is also the sorter's `IEventSource`), each rule in the untranslated game's words and the translation's alike: `IdFlags` reads a switch or variable as scratch by coop_events.rbx's TEMPORARY_NAMES with システム専用 (never a switch of the story's band 1001-2000), the affection variables from 3000 on one per actor, an item of type 2 as a key item, the job or race an item unlocks by world_catchup.rbx's tag with 【転職用】 (the untranslated tag of the translation's [Job]), and an enemy's level by its note's `<逃走レベル n>`; `ChestFlags` ports story_rewards.rb's chest flags (switches only chest-like pages turn on, or that a chest notes itself opened in and nothing else reads; a page giving a key item or material, 【貴重品】 or 【素材】, notes none); `CommonFlags` the player's own common events (2, 3, 5, 114), the battlefuck events (those that count a battlefuck's win or loss, 6999 and 7000, their callers up to each talk, and the scenes only they call) and the Final Chapter's (named 大いなる決断 or Great Decision, from 9000 on, or branching on a route variable 1141-1143); `PageFlags` sorts every page with `EventSorter.KindOfPage` in a new game (switches off, variables 0, the player's own switches and variables those of `ClassicStoryTable.Build` with the chest flags, the side switches while the sides differ) into `kind_new_game`, a chest's letter into `chest_letter` and `chests` (a snapshot: no live page may be sorted by it), and sets `scene_page` (a condition switch the post-defeat processing writes), `runs_nothing` (only ends and comments), `has_fade_in` and `sets_encounters` (its last Change Encounter; NULL for none); `RaidSites` ports raid_freeze_data.rb for every troop, not only the boss troops: a battle against a troop by id is a story site unless its page is on an excluded map (`RaidRules.ExcludedMaps` 154, 576) or turns on an arena switch (87, 97), or its common event is reached by no map page but through those; `site_alternatives` pairs troops fought for one page in branches of the two side switches (each with the side that picks the other, from the first page they pair on); `scene_entries` names for each story site page on a cutscene map the field events whose transfers lead into its map, over other cutscene maps too (a transfer of a map from 1000 on lands in that map's folder, a script's `forced_transfer` where it names), each with the switch around its first transfer on the way, and the first transfer into the map (a page entered from no field event gets no rows). `check <game folder> <events.db>` builds again and compares byte for byte (exit code 1 when it differs); `fingerprint <game folder>` prints the fingerprint and its parts; `compare <events.db> <other game folder>` counts per kind of list those of another copy, such as a translation, that are the same, differ in text only (same shape), in numbers only (same count) or in both, naming the sites: the release's check against a new translation. For the tools: `sort <events.db> <out.jsonl> [--state new|<values.json>]` reads a database whole (`Tools/DatabaseSource.cs`, an `IEventSource` over the kept commands, a jump naming its label by `ord`, and each list's keys from its rows with the command places as `ord`) and writes every map page and common event as the event checker's sorting check reads them (`OfflineSort`, the shape of the checker's earlier `sort_events.rb`: one JSON object a line, `id` `map:<map>:<event>/<page from 1>` or `ce:<id>`, `name` empty, `trigger`, a common event's `switch`, `kind` and `marks` by the Ruby sorter's names), sorted by `EventSorter` in a Classic party's game (`ClassicGameState`: a new game's values, or the switches on and variables set of a file `{"switches": [ids], "variables": {"id": value}}`, the player's own switches and variables those of `ClassicStoryTable.Build` with the database's counts and chest flags); it knows no Pocket Castle residents, which coop_castle.rbx tells apart by their scripts' texts in the game, so it sorts those pages as their commands are. `export <events.db> <table> <out.json>` writes one table or view as an array of rows, each an object by column name, one a line, ordered by their values (the event checker's chest flags from `switches` and chests from `chests`). `names <translated game folder> <out.json>` writes the names of the story's keys as the website's names file holds them (`StoryNames`: `switches`, `variables` and `maps` by id, `events` by `"map.event"`, `companions`), from a translated copy since the database holds no names; it is meant to lie next to the battle server's events database for `GET /v1/admin/story/names` once the server reads it. `ordinals <events.db> <game folder> <rows.json> <out.json>` rewrites the `index` of the relay's `joins` and `removals` rows (`StoryOrdinals`) from a command's place in the game folder's data file (a common event's counting the marks its file had before the game's own) into its `ord` in the database, a script's continuation line read as its script, each command matched as the nth of its code and first two numbers in its list: a seed file of admin edits becomes the same file with the ords, a table as the relay's admin route lists it becomes the batch of changes for `POST /v1/admin/story/edit` (Relay/README.md, "Join and removal indexes as ords"); a row whose command it does not find keeps its index and is named, and the exit code is then 1. The build fails on a command code or a script root of a map page or common event the story replay does not class (`Build/ReplayChannels.cs` over `Build/ScriptRoots.cs`, see The replayer). A build of 3.06 takes about 10 seconds and writes 38 MB (12 MB zipped); the shipped file is that build (Shipping, below).
- **Repair pairs** (`Build/RepairPairs.cs`, table `repair_pairs`, `EventStore.RepairPairs()`): the pairs the one-time repair of saves the 0.6.9.2 chest path left stuck completes ("Issue 73 chest effects.md", E2). A gift page (it gives items, weapons, armors or gold, itself or in the common events it calls) whose self switch K, once on, proves its switch S on: S turned on in the same block as K, before or after it, or at the top of an "if S is OFF" of K's block, with nothing between that leaves the block (exit, break, jump, a battle, game over, the title). Gate 1: nothing else turns K on (another page of its event, a common event a page of its event calls, a script by K's key or as the running event's, or a script of the event that writes a self switch its text does not name; `ScriptTexts.SelfSwitchesOf`). Gate 2: every list that turns S off (Control Switches, or a script that sets S) also resets K's map (`reset_selfswitch_map` with its maps or a spread range of local numbers, or `Game_SelfSwitches.new`). Gate 3: no game script or database entry uses S as a switch (`SwitchUses`, over `Data/Scripts.rvdata2`: `$game_switches[...]` with a literal or a constant, a band the scripts index by a constant base up to the next base, the switch module's ranges, the awaken band of the actors that hold anything; an enemy's action that asks for a switch, a note or a damage formula that reads one). On the TEST install this gives 19 pairs: the 18 of the model's scan on its 13 switches, and Tamamo's farewell page 1650:1 (A, 2708), which the rule proves the same way.
- **JIT hosts** (the test projects and the generator): they reference `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` only for its native `e_sqlite3` library, which the same imports load at run time, so the tests run the package's SQLite (3.53.3), not the amalgamation's build.
- **Shipping:** the mod ships `package/Multiplayer/Data/events.db` as `Patch/Multiplayer/Data/events.db` (the `DataFile` item of `MGQParadox.Multiplayer.csproj`, listed in `Manifest.txt`; a publish without the file stops with an error). It is the generator's build of the untranslated 3.06 (`EVENTS_SOURCE_DIR`, `MGQ Paradox src`), committed as built (`binary` in `.gitattributes`, no Git LFS while regenerations stay rare); CI never generates it, since it has no game data. Build it again and commit it whenever the game data, the schema or a rule of the generator changes: `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Data.Generator -- build "<MGQ Paradox src>" package/Multiplayer/Data/events.db`, then `check` with the same arguments, which must say byte-equal. The release's tests are its gate: the shipped file opens in the 32-bit run as the DLL opens it and holds this code's `EventSchema.Version`, the untranslated 3.06 and its pinned fingerprint (`ShippedDatabaseTests`); it was written by this generator's version or a later one (`ShippedFileTests`), and with `EVENTS_SOURCE_DIR` a build gives it byte for byte. The helper's update adds the file to an older install and replaces it with each release; the relay mirror `story.db` beside it is in no manifest, so the update never removes it. The battle server's core reads the same file in its game folder, so every release installs it there with its scripts (`BattleServer/tools/deploy_release.sh`, see [BattleServer/README.md](../BattleServer/README.md), Deploying a release).
- **Tests:** `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Data.Tests`, 32-bit like the DLL (the x86 runtime, see [Tests](#tests)): a row written with bound values reads back, a reused statement starts from its first row, SQL SQLite refuses throws its message, and a missing file opened read-only is refused, not created; a tiny game's rows (`TinyRows`) round-trip through a file table by table and answer the store's questions in memory, two builds give equal bytes whatever order the rows came in, another schema version, a missing file and a file that is no events database make foreign stores, a read-only file is read with nothing written beside it, a mirror is created with its tables, and `ListShape` gives every shape of `hash_cases.json`. The relay mirror (`StoryMirrorTests`): the relay's answer of `fixtures/story/mirror.json` (written by `node fixtures/story/make_mirror.mjs` through the relay's own `StoryMapping.forGames` over a sample of every seed table) reads back as it came after the store was opened again, with its rows' sites, pages from 0 and story order in the typed columns; a mirror of another relay or other game data is dropped; a file of another schema gets the tables anew; a torn file, or one holding a row whose text does not read, is made anew; and a place the game cannot write leaves the store without a mirror, still answering. `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Data.Generator.Tests` (64-bit) builds the game rules' `TinyGame` into a database in memory and reads it back: a common event starts with exactly one mark, the id its object holds, whatever marks its file had; texts leave the shape alone and a branch's switch does not; a jump names its label's `ord`; each script keeps its kind; a script's assignment is one read and one write; Control Switches to on turns its range on; a battle gets its side, preamble and number; troops and cutscene maps; the fingerprint equals a golden string Ruby's `Zlib.crc32` wrote; two builds give equal bytes; and per derived rule (`DerivedRowsTests`): scratch, story band and affection flags from names, key items, unlocks and enemy levels from texts, chests in a new game with the chest flags that keep them chests, the common events' and the pages' flags, the three views, the side switches' alternatives and the scene entries over a second cutscene map and a forced transfer; and the story replay's gate: an unclassed command or script root fails the build where a troop's page does not, a closed command's write must be a work key, and a script's roots leave out local and loop variables, members, symbols, strings, regular expressions and comments. With `EVENTS_SOURCE_DIR` naming the untranslated copy and `BATTLE_GAME_DIR` the installed game, it checks that the untranslated copy's fingerprint equals the installed game's and the one the running game wrote to `Patch/Multiplayer/GameData.ini`, and that the story replay's gate classes both copies, whose only key a closed command writes is v57. The parity tests against the Ruby tables the database replaced (the frozen fights', the chest flags, the battlefucks, the unlocks and the enemy levels) passed a last time before those tables were deleted (2026-10-28, build step 12); the chests of a new game's sort differed from the generated CHESTS by 52 only there, mostly chests behind a branch a new game skips, which the relay's `party_chests` hold, and 7 only sorted. The tools' tests (`ToolsTests`): the sort writes each page of the kind the build found and in the checker's shape, follows the branches a values file holds, and the database read whole gives each list the keys the build read from its whole commands; a table exports one row a line and a name the database has no table for writes no file; the names file holds the names by id and by map and event with the companion count; and a relay row's index becomes the ord of its command, one moved by text lines, a script's continuation line and a common event's place in its file alike, while a place no command stands on is named. With `EVENTS_SOURCE_DIR`, the sort of the untranslated copy's database gives every one of its 97,471 map pages the kind the build found from the whole lists.

## Game rules (`MGQOnline.Game`)

The game-side rules that leave the scripts, one folder per area: a net10.0 library, AOT-clean, BCL, `MGQOnline.Bridge` and `MGQOnline.Data` only and without state in statics, for the win-x86 DLL and the battle server's linux-x64 core alike. The DLL answers the scripts with it through bridge topics (`MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/`), each area's added as it moves. Rules whose scripts also run on the battle server answer through `SharedTopics` (`Topics/`), which both hosts register (`GameBridge.RegisterTopics` and `ServerBridge`), so a script asks them the same way in the game and on the server.

- **Ids** (`Ids/`): `RandomId.Of`, the ids the scripts make up (`MGQ_MpCoop.random_id`: a battle's, a raid pool battle's, a party's, a trade's, a teleport's nonce), through `id.random` (`length`, `radix` in, exactly that many digits out, leading zeros kept). They come from a `Random` of each context, never the game's `rand`, whose stream the replay seeds of battles follow (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)): an id drawn from it, also by the battle server's host, would shift every seed after it.
- **Squads** (`Squads/`): `RaidSquad`, a Raid World's squad settings and their text (see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)), and `SquadMath`, the split of the Frontline and the Backline, a Raid World's squads, the order of a battle's players and their places (`Arrange`) and the companions a team past its cap leaves out (see [Squads](#squads-coop_squadrbx)), through `SquadTopics`. Their golden cases, `fixtures/squad/squad.json`, were recorded from `coop_squad.rbx`, `arrange` of `battles_coop.rbx` and `squad_of` of `battles_served.rbx` before the rules left them; the script tests answer the topics through stand-ins checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/rules_support.rb`).
- **The interface's models** (`Ui/`, see [Shared UI](#shared-ui-uirbx)): `UiTopics.Register` puts a `UiHost` on each context, which takes the frame's tick (`ui.tick`, a Hash of each model's name and its input, staged by `MGQ_MpUi::Tick`) and hands every `IUiModel` the input put under its name, all names checked first; a model posts what changed as an event of its own, with the sounds to play under `cues` (`UiCue`: `ok`, `cancel`, `cursor`, `buzzer`). `UiButtons` reads the game's buttons as the scripts pack them (`Tick.buttons`): went down, went down or repeats, held, for confirm, cancel and the four arrows. Models time by `UiHost.Now`, the last drain's frame.
- **The layout** (`Ui/Layout/LayoutModel.cs`): the boxes registered with their least sizes and how they resize, where `Player.ini` keeps each (`Parse`: four whole numbers split by commas, as Ruby's `split(",")` and `strip` read them), each box's last default and the screen, the drag (`Grab` 8 pixels resize, else move; the sides stop at the least size and the screen, `Span`) and the double click (`DoubleClickTicks` 24 by `UiHost.Now`, `DoubleClickReach` 6). `Fit` and `OnScreen` place a box. The model `layout` takes `[Alt, button held, it went down, mouse x, y or nil, screen width, height, offers]`. The golden cases are `fixtures/ui/layout.json`, steps played through `ui_layout.rbx` before it moved (`fixtures/ui/make_layout_fixtures.rb` with the `ui_layout.rbx` of commit f67e1fd); the script tests' `FakeLayout` fits a box as `LayoutModel` does, without the drags.
- **The confirm box** (`Ui/ConfirmModel.cs`): one box at a time (`ui.confirm.ask` answers its id, nil while one is open; `ui.confirm.close`), the model `confirm` of the tick (`[id, accept, decline, cancel, confirm, left or right]`): nothing reads in its first tick, the accept hotkey answers yes before the decline hotkey, cancel and confirm (the pick, no at first), left or right move the pick; it posts `ui.confirm` with `picked`, and `answer` with `by` (`accept`, `decline`, `cancel`, `confirm`) and the sound.
- **Wheels** (`Ui/Wheels/`): `WheelPointer` points like a joystick (`Held`) and steadies it (`Steady`: one arrow left of a diagonal still points to the diagonal for `GraceTicks`, 8), `WheelPlaces.Of(count)` spreads the choices over the eight `WheelDirection`s, `index * 8 / count` rounded half away from zero as Ruby's `Float#round` does. `WheelModel` is a wheel's state, the action wheel `wheel` (a middle choice, -1, which a direction without a choice picks; confirm reports `take` and the choice closes it) and the emote wheel `emotes` (none picked at first, a direction without a choice keeps the pick; confirm closes it with `take`). Its tick input is `[true or why it may not show, hotkey, cancel, confirm, arrows (`WheelArrows`), choices]`, its event `ui.wheel` (`wheel`, `open`, `selected`, `places` when opened, `take`, `reason`, `cues`). The golden cases are `fixtures/ui/wheel.json`, played through `ui_wheel.rbx` before it moved (`fixtures/ui/make_wheel_fixtures.rb` with the `ui_wheel.rbx` of commit 8fc9bc6); the script tests' `FakeWheels` answers places from it.
- **Text** (`Ui/Text/`): `FontGlyphs` keeps one font's width per code point as the scripts measured it (a character never measured counts as the font's size, so a line breaks early rather than overflowing, and `UiText` asks for it once with the event `ui.glyphs.wanted` at the next drain); key 0 is the font of cells, every character one wide. `TextLayout.Cut`, `Wrap` and `Spans` are `MGQ_MpUi.cut_measured`, `wrap_measured` and `wrap_spans` by the sum of the widths, counted by code point, with Ruby's ASCII-only white space (`split(" ")`, `\s`, `rstrip` with NUL) and the cut's halving as Ruby halves, so a text whose trailing spaces make a shorter start fit better cuts where Ruby cuts it. The topics `ui.glyphs` (define a font: `key`, `name`, `size`, `bold`, `widths`; or add `widths` to one), `ui.text.cut` (`[key, width, text, tail, new widths]`), `ui.text.wrap` and `ui.text.spans` (`[key, width, text, new widths]`). The golden cases are `fixtures/ui/text.json`, written from the scripts' own rules by `ruby fixtures/ui/make_fixtures.rb` (`text_harness.rb` reads the three methods out of `ui.rbx` and `ui_text_box.rbx` and measures with a table of widths); `GameScript/Tests/ui_text_fixtures_test.rb` checks the scripts against them on every run.
- **The text box** (`Ui/Text/TextEditor.cs`, `EditModel.cs`): `TextEditor` is the editor of a text being typed, by code point: typing at the cursor (Enter, Escape and Tab only reported, Backspace erases, a control character does nothing, the longest text and the allowed pattern refuse), the keys that type nothing (`EditKeys`: left, right, up and down a line in a box of several, Home, End, Delete), the blinking (`BlinkTicks`), the part a box of one line shows (`Visible`, a mask for a password), the first line a box of several shows (`FirstLine`) and scrolling it (`ScrollLines`). `EditModel` is the one box open, the model `edit` of the tick (`[id, keys or -1, take typed, lines to scroll, Alt held]`): each tick it takes what was typed from `ITypedText` (the DLL's `Keyboard`), leaves out the opening key's character (`echo`) and what Alt types (`alt_codes`), types until Enter or Escape and keeps the rest for the next tick, a locked box only Escape, follows the keys and posts `ui.edit` when anything changed: the text, the cursor, `shown`, what typing did, the keys and characters that came, and the view, `{"line" => [first, count, cursor x]}` or `{"area" => [spans, first line, cursor's line, cursor x]}`, in the font and width `ui.edit.view` named. Characters the font never measured count as its size until the scripts measured them. An allowed pattern is the Ruby source of one character's class, read with ECMAScript's ASCII classes as Ruby's `\d` reads. The golden cases are `fixtures/ui/edit.json`, played through the scripts' own editor before it moved (`fixtures/ui/make_edit_fixtures.rb`, with the `ui_text_box.rbx` of commit b20ddf0).

- **Raid pools** (`Raid/`): `PoolLedger`, a Raid World's boss pools as the game knows them, which the DLL's `RaidBosses` fills with what the relay told (fetches, answers, the world room's pushes) and the player's own reports, and the battle server's core with what the relay tells the server (`online.rb`'s `BossPools` hands it over on the game's thread, before the scripts read) and the server's own reports: the share left now as the relay's `leftOf` counts it (the relay's share a minute, `TakeRegen`; a clock that runs back takes nothing away), a standing pool's least share (`MinLeft`), each own report counted at once and waiting for its answer per pool, word older than those or than a newer push not taken, and a push's defeat or reset told once (`PoolChange`). `PoolReport` reads a report's text as the relay takes it. `RaidFight` is the battle that fights a boss's pools, as `battles_raid_pool.rbx` fought it: the moments of the battle (`FightMoment`: its start, a command phase, an action's end, the wait for commands, a turn's end, its end, a takeover, a role that changed), the report of what each own enemy lost since its HP was last taken or reported (`seq\tdelta\tmax\tkilled`, the share with ten decimals), the pools' HP and kills, the enemy refused or built anew, the takeover of a guest, and the slots of 2 s between an action's reports and 5 s between the wait's syncs, on the ledger's clock. Each host has its way to the pools (`IRaidRelay`): the DLL's `RaidBosses`, and the core's `PoolOutbox`, whose reports wait until the server's scripts send them. `PoolTopics` (both hosts, through `SharedTopics`): `raid.pools`, `raid.pool.heard`, `raid.pool.reports`, `raid.fight.open`, `raid.fight.turn`, `raid.fight.close`, and `raid.fight.export` and `raid.fight.import`, which hand a served fight's count, side battle and pools (each share with ten decimals) from the starter's ledger to the server's, and the events `raid.boss_defeated` and `raid.boss_back` at each drain. Its golden cases, `fixtures/pool/ledger.json`, `relay.json` and `turns.json`, were recorded from `battles_raid_pool.rbx` before its ledger and its turns left it and from the relay's `leftOf` (`ruby fixtures/pool/make_fixtures.rb`, `make_turn_fixtures.rb`); `GameScript/Tests/pool_support.rb` answers the topics from a stand-in held against them.
- **Served battles** (`Battles/Served/`): `ServedSetup`, the setup a served battle's starter hands the battle server (see [Battles on the battle server](#battles-on-the-battle-server-battles_servedrbx)): `Write` makes the battle line from the facts the starter's script collected, `Read` reads it back as `read_setup` did (a field of the wrong kind left out, a line of another format none), `Refusal` refuses it as `served.rb`'s `check` did, given whether the troop has enemies in the server's database. `ScriptsFingerprint` chains Ruby's `Zlib.crc32` over the scripts, each name, a line feed and its text without carriage returns. `SetupTopics` (both hosts, through `SharedTopics`): `served.setup.write`, `served.setup.read`, `served.setup.check` and `served.fingerprint`. Their golden cases, `fixtures/served/setup.json`, were recorded from `battles_served.rbx` and `served.rb` before they moved (`ruby fixtures/served/make_setup_fixtures.rb`), with the fingerprint's test vector; `fixtures/served/front.json` (`make_fixtures.rb`) holds the starter's front, which `BattleSessionTests` replay.
- **Ruby's texts** (`Ruby/`): `RubyNumbers.ToI`, `ToF` and `FloatToS` read and write numbers as Ruby 3.3's `String#to_i`, `String#to_f` and `Float#to_s` do, quirks included (white space, "0d", single underscores between digits, a signed "-0x" hexadecimal float, a NUL ending the text, `to_f` cleaning a text with more after its number into a 60-byte buffer, "1.0e-05", "1.0e+16", "Infinity"), and `RubyAscii` has Ruby's ASCII-only character classes (`\w`, `isspace`).
- **Battle lines** (`Battles/Wire/`): the `Wire` codec of `battles_sync_wire.rbx` (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)). `WireWriter.Line` writes `WireValue`s (nil, true, false, Integer of any size, Float, Symbol, String, battler, action, database entry, equipped item, Color, Tone, list and action result) as Ruby writes them; `WireReader.Parse` reads a line as Ruby does, with the same refusals (512 KB, 8 deep, unbalanced brackets, no UTF-8: null), `]` and `}` closing either bracket, and nil for a token that names nothing the reading game has. What the game has comes in a `WireGame`: its symbols (Ruby's `Symbol.all_symbols`), the fields of its `Game_ActionResult` and the size of each database table, which Ruby hands over when a battle area moves. The writer also refuses what Ruby would write as a broken line (a tab in a battler reference or action kind) or cannot hold (a lone surrogate, a channel that is no finite number). Floats do cross in battle lines (camera places, sounds, pictures), so the codec writes Ruby's float text exactly.
- **Telling** (`Telling/`): `TellingMachine`, how a story event that starts goes on with the player's telling (its next event in the frame its last one ended, an event that runs by itself on the map the telling moved the player onto within `ContinueFrames`, else a new telling) and the relay's telling lock a new telling takes through an `ITellingLock` (`StoryTrack` in the game): its first command holds until the relay answered and the event ends once the relay refused it (see [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx) and [Party or map](#party-or-map-coop_scoperbx)). The DLL answers it as `telling.start`, `telling.command`, `telling.end`, `telling.moving` and `telling.arrived` (`StoryTopics`). Its golden cases, `fixtures/telling/cases.json`, were recorded from `coop_events.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/telling/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the topics through a stand-in checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/telling_support.rb`).
- **Story pages** (`Telling/StoryPages.cs`): a story's pages as coop_events.rbx kept them: the teller's page ids, the pace while the player skips (`SkipSendFrames`) and the page kept meanwhile, and the viewer's pages heard (`MaxHeard`), those the teller moved past (`MaxDone`), the catch-up (`CatchUpPages`), the page held with its reading time (`ReadFrames`, `ReadFramesPerCharacter`, `MostReadFrames`) and a Classic leader's stale page (`StaleFrames`), by the frame of the call; the script reaches them through `PageTopics` (`pages.show`, `pages.passed`, `pages.flush`, `pages.telling_starts`, `pages.hear`, `pages.done`, `pages.forget`, `pages.next`, `pages.over`, `pages.ended`), which both hosts answer and which read and write the lines in Base64 as the wire carries them. Its golden cases, `fixtures/pages/cases.json`, were recorded from `coop_events.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/pages/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the topics through a stand-in checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/pages_support.rb`, `pages_fixtures_test.rb`).
- **Story calls** (`Telling/GatherCalls.cs`): the calls of a story and their frames as coop_gather.rbx kept them: a leader's call and its wait (`GatherFrames`, `CallLapseFrames`), a follow, a teleport the player asked for (`AskedLapseFrames`), the leader's hold (`HoldFrames`, `CallFrames`, `NamedMissing`), the bans kept for the map a call leads to, the follow a Raid World keeps after the arrival and the offers to follow a teller (`FollowOfferFrames`), by the frame of the call; the script reads them through `GatherTopics` (`gather.called`, `gather.come_to`, `gather.followed`, `gather.ban`, `gather.frame` once per frame, `gather.take`, `gather.hold`, `gather.holding`, `gather.offer`, `gather.follow_offer`, `gather.accept`, `gather.decline`, `gather.forget`, ...), which both hosts answer, and moves the player. Its golden cases, `fixtures/gather/cases.json`, were recorded from `coop_gather.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/gather/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the topics through a stand-in checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/gather_support.rb`).
- **Story scene** (`Scene/`): `SceneQueue`, the teller's changes of the story's scene as coop_scene.rbx kept them, merged as they come and split into the messages they go out in, with the skip's pace by the frame of the call, and `SceneChecks`, what a viewer refuses of another game's changes before the game's own facts are asked (a change it has no such change for, arguments the game cannot take, a name that is no plain file name, a change bound to the teller's map while the player is on the way); the script reads them through `SceneTopics` (`scene.change`, `scene.flush`, `scene.clear`, `scene.take`, and `scene.check` for a battle's playback), which both hosts answer, with each change's arguments as their `Wire` line. Its golden cases, `fixtures/scene/cases.json`, were recorded from `coop_scene.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/scene/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the topics through a stand-in checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/scene_support.rb`, `scene_fixtures_test.rb`).
- **Catch-up experience** (`Catchup/`): `CatchupExperience`, the target, the factor, the grown experience and the factor's text of [Catch-up experience](#catch-up-experience-world_catchup_exprbx). Its golden cases, `fixtures/catchup/exp.json`, were recorded from `world_catchup_exp.rbx` before the rule left it; the script tests answer the topics from them (`GameScript/Tests/rules_support.rb`), so a test leans only on answers C# was checked to give.
- **Story choices** (`Story/Choices/`): `ChoiceTable`, the story's choices and their outcomes as coop_choices.rbx kept them, and `StoryChoices`, the rules of [The story's choices](#the-storys-choices-coop_choicesrbx): which choices a story carries a player past (`Passed`, `Prompts`, `Missed`), what the screen offers (`Offered`, `BothEndings`), what an outcome brings and sets (`Note`, `Outcome`), which keys are the player's own (`Personal`), the catch-up's skips and the leader's offers (`SkipReason`, `OfferRefusal`, `Lapsed`), through `ChoiceTopics`, which both hosts answer. Its golden cases, `fixtures/choices/cases.json`, were recorded from `coop_choices.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/choices/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the topics through a stand-in checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/choices_support.rb`).
- **Catch-up tables** (`Catchup/`): `CatchupTables`, the relay's catch-up tables read with the story mapping (`StoryTable.Catchup`, Relay/README.md "Catch-up tables"): the rows that replaced the scripts' generated tables and the rules, counts and companion values of `world_catchup.rbx`, which `CatchupEngine` decides over. `MGQOnline.Game.Tests` reads the rules from the relay's seed rows (`Relay/seed/story`) and the generated tables from `fixtures/catchup/generated`, both written by `Relay/seed/story/make_seed.rb`; the seed's reviewed rows, lists of admin edits, stay out of these tests, whose recorded cases were decided over the generated tables. `StoryRows` decides how the rows stand against a story as `world_catchup.rbx` does (`StoryValues`: the switches and self switches on and the variables set, a variable never set holding no mark): which rows a story is past (`Passed`, `PassedAny`), which key items a later row took back (`TakenSince`), which joins a removal takes back and which are visits (`TakingBack`, `Temporary`, `Visiting`), which removal comes after which join (`After`) and where a player moves on to (`NextPart`, `WorldParts`). Its golden cases, `fixtures/catchup/rows.json`, are recorded from the script over the generated tables by `fixtures/catchup/make_rows.rb` (`ruby fixtures/catchup/make_rows.rb`), from `fixtures/catchup/engine.rb`, the script's decisions kept as a frozen Ruby engine.
- **Catch-up engine** (`Catchup/`): `CatchupEngine` and `CatchupLook`, the catch-up of [Catch-up](#catch-up-world_catchuprbx) over the tables the DLL holds (`CatchupTopics`: `catchup.keys`, `catchup.check`, `catchup.note`, `catchup.hold_join`, `catchup.unlocks`), one engine per bridge context holding the save's notes. Its golden cases, `fixtures/catchup/engine.json`, are every call the script tests make (`GameScript/Tests/world_catchup_test.rb`), answered by `fixtures/catchup/engine.rb`, the script's decisions as they stood before they left it, over the same tables and notes; `ruby fixtures/catchup/make_engine.rb` records them again. The script tests answer the topics from that engine, so a test leans only on decisions C# was checked to make.
- **Story parts** (`Story/`): `StoryParts.Of`, the part a story is in, as the relay's `partOf` counts it; the scripts ask it through `story.part`. `fixtures/story/parts.json` (`node fixtures/story/make_fixtures.mjs`) holds the relay's parts, which the relay's tests check too.
- **Story keys and tracks** (`Story/`): `StoryKey` reads and writes the tracks' keys (`s12`, `v1001`, `ss7.39.A`, `f868`); `StoryTable` holds a `StoryClass` byte per switch and per variable (kept, one-way, scene, chest, count, the whole world's, written by the defeat, a Classic side switch), the self switch rules and the indexed events, and finds a key's class as the relay's `TrackRules.kindOf` does; `StoryMapping.Read` builds it from the relay's story mapping, `ClassicStoryTable.Build` from the lists a Classic party keeps each player's own. `StoryTrack` is the player's side of the tracks protocol (see [World story on the relay](#world-story-on-the-relay-raid-worlds)). `fixtures/story/classes.json` (`node fixtures/story/make_classes.mjs`) holds the kinds the relay gives a mapping's keys; `StoryTrackRelayTests` plays two games' tracks against the relay's own `StoryTracks` through `fixtures/story/track_harness.mjs` in Node, skipped without Node.
- **Mod keys** (`Mods/`): `ModKey.Of`, the key mods are compared by, as the relay makes it (see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)); the DLL's mods check, a world's session and the scripts use it, and `fixtures/mods/keys.json` (`node fixtures/mods/make_fixtures.mjs`) holds the relay's keys, which the relay's tests check too. `OverviewText` (`Overview/`) writes the World overview's story and title texts (see [World overview](#world-overview-world_overviewrbx)). `ModsText` reads a world's mods as written (`Names`, `Required`, `Essential`, see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)); its golden cases, `fixtures/mods/text.json`, were recorded from `world.rbx` by `ruby fixtures/mods/make_mods_text.rb` before the rule left it. `ModCheck` checks a world's required mods against the catalog, this game's copies and the creator's hashes (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)). `SharedSettings` and `SettingNotices` hold the rules and the notices of a world's shared mod settings. `SessionMods` decides which of the player's mods the session of a world that turns other mods off loads and says it (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods)).
- **Event data** (`Events/Data/`): `GameData` reads the game's event data as the game loads it: through `GameFolderFiles`, from `Game.rgss3a` when the folder has one and it holds the file (the untranslated game keeps its data there; `Rgss3aArchive`), else from the loose files. Maps 0-999 are `Data/MapNNN.rvdata2`, maps from 1000 on lie in folders of a thousand, `Data/Map/Data/MapNNN.rvdata2` for 1000-1999, `Data/Map2/Data/` for 2000-2999 (`GameDataPaths.Map`, as the game's `DataManager.map_file_name`), each folder with its own `MapInfos.rvdata2`. It gives typed `GameMap`, `MapEvent`, `EventPage` (conditions, trigger, list), `CommonEvent`, `Troop`, `MapInfo` and `GameSystemNames` (switch and variable names), read through a pruner (`GameDataPruner`) that keeps only the parameters of the commands a rule reads (`EventCodes.KeepsParameters`: branches, switches, variables, self switches, calls, labels, items, transfers, battles, scripts) and leaves out the dialogue: the common events (10,007, 1.19 million commands) take about 0.7 s and 54 MB (64-bit), every map's events about 0.7 s more; the maps are read one at a time and kept by their caller.
- **Event sorter** (`Events/`): `EventSorter` sorts a page or a list (`KindOfPage`, `KindOf`, `MarksOf`, `MayTell`, `IsScene`, `IsStoryCommand`, `ChestLetter`) as [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx) tells, over an `IEventState` (switches, variables as `to_i` reads them, and the player's own switches and variables) and an `IEventSource`. The rules read the game's events only through `IEventSource`: the map events' pages and the common events, what each list reads and writes by itself (`ListKeys`), which common events call one, and whether a switch or variable is scratch (today by its name, `GameSystemNames.IsScratchSwitch`); `GameDataSource` answers it from `GameData`, keeping the common events' keys and the last 8 maps read, and `EventStoreSource` from the events database (see Rules over the store in [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata)); the DLL's topics make the sorter, the command index and the chain over their source for each request. The DLL reads its events from the events database (see In the DLL in [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata)), which it opens on a thread of its own (`EventTopics.ReadAhead`) as a world opens (`world.me`, see [The world room](#the-world-room-worldroomtopics)), so the first event start finds it open; a sort that comes meanwhile waits for the open, and one that failed is logged. `ListKeys` is built from `ListRows`, the rows of one list with each command's place (reads with where they are read, `ReadSources`; writes with whether they turn switches on, whether a script makes them and the constant a variable is set to; self switches with whether they turn on; calls; battles; transfers to a map by a constant), the form the events database stores. `RaidRules` holds the hand-written raid rules the generator reads battles by (the boss switch 22 and the preamble's commands, `PreambleStart`; the excluded maps 154 and 576, the arena switches 87 and 97, `TurnsOnArena`, and the post-defeat processing, common event 3000), until the Raid Designer redesign owns them; `EventSorter.OwnCommonEvents` lists the player's own common events. The rules read numbers only: `CommandClassifier` turns each list's texts into numbers where the list is read (the game data's reader and `events.sort`'s handed-over lists): a self switch's letter A to D into 1 to 4, a label into the number of its name, a jump into the place of its label (-1 for none), a script into its `TextKind` (talk, travel, choice with its variable as its first number, novel, other) and every script text into the switches and variables it names (`ScriptKeys`, which `ListKeys` reads); it also finds the innermost branch on a switch around a command (`EnclosingSwitch`). The **event command index** (`EventCommandIndex`, over `ListKeys`) tells what a list does through the common events it calls, every branch counted: the troops it fights (each once in the order met, leaving out the common events that turn a given switch on, as the Colosseum's) and the switches, variables and self switches it sets; the DLL answers it as `events.index` (the list named as for `events.sort`, `skip_turning_on`; `troops`, `writes` with `s`, `v` as [first, last] and `ss`). The raid bar (`ui_raid_bar.rbx` `troops_in`) and the raid freeze (`ui_raid_freeze.rbx` `list_writes`) still walk the lists themselves: both carry raid rules (the Colosseum's and the Hades rematches' exclusions, which writes hold a frozen fight) that wait for the Raid Designer redesign. Its golden cases (`fixtures/events/`, written by `ruby fixtures/events/make_fixtures.rb "<MGQ Paradox Translated>\Data"` on the scripts of the commit that recorded them) are every list the script tests sorted (`script_cases.json`, which also answer the script tests' sorts, `rules_support.rb` `answer_events`) and 640 sorts of 320 of the game's pages and common events in a new game's state and a mixed one (`game_cases.json`, by site alone, no game content), which the tests sort again from the events database the generator builds of the game folder `BATTLE_GAME_DIR` names, those whose commands and names the folder holds as the recording's did (a hash of them); the rules' other tests run over a database of `TinyGame` too (`EventDatabases`), and over the game folder's the derived index and the troops of every page and common event equal those the walks of the game's lists find. The event checker sorts every page outside the game with the generator's `sort` (see Generator in [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata)).
- **The world room** (`World/`): `WorldRoom` keeps the other games of the open world, the player's state, the inbox each frame, the ping and the connection's problem, the party (`Party`) and the message gates, fed by a host (`IWorldRoomHost`: sending, the log, and the scripts' events) with inbox entries, the scripts' frame and the frame number; `WorldFields` reads and writes the key=value messages as the scripts do. See [The world room](#the-world-room-worldroomtopics).
- **Worlds** (`Worlds/`): `WorldEntries` (the world screen's entries and their order), `GameDataCompare` (two games' data fingerprints compared, and the parts that differ named), `WorldSaves` (a world's latest save among its files) and `ModPick` (the mod picker: the installed mods and those a world names, each unlisted, listed, required or essential, the text kept within the relay's 16,000 characters counted by code point; the DLL keeps the open picker, topics `modpick.open`, `modpick.act` and `modpick.close` in `ModPickTopics`, listing the scripts in Patch without those every player has, `SessionMods.SharedKeys`, and `MGQ_MpWorld::ModPick` shows its answers). Their golden cases, `fixtures/worlds/*.json`, were recorded from `world.rbx` before the rules left it (`fixtures/worlds/make_fixtures.rb`, run on that version of the script); the script tests answer the topics through stand-ins checked against them (`GameScript/Tests/local_worlds_support.rb`), and the DLL's `LocalWorld` reads and writes world.ini against `ini.json`. `WorldScreenModel` is what the world screen shows of a world and why: its row of the list (`RowOf`: the name, a favourite's after `* `, the players online of the seats, the colour key `raid`, `featured`, `favourite` or `normal`, pale once the directory no longer has it), the details' panels and cells (`Panels`, uncut texts with colour keys, targets and chips), the choices (`Actions`), the list box's players and mods (`PlayerLines`, `ModLines`, `ModMarks`), the played text, the list's line and the screen's texts; it reads the world and a `ScreenFacts` the DLL gathers (the player, the game data compared, the mods check, the shared settings' line, the mods it turns off, the hidden worlds added). Its golden cases, `fixtures/screen/screen.json`, were recorded from `world_screen.rbx` before the model left it, through `fixtures/screen/screen_prototype.rb`, a copy of those methods that asks a facts object what they asked the other scripts (`ruby fixtures/screen/make_fixtures.rb`); the script tests answer the `screen.*` topics through the same prototype (`GameScript/Tests/screen_support.rb`), checked against the file as it loads.
- **Forms** (`Forms/`): the form engine and the world screen's forms (see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx), the form engine), checked against `fixtures/forms/world.json`. `RubyAscii.Strip` and `Downcase` strip and lower-case texts as Ruby's `strip` and RGSS3's ASCII-only `downcase` do.
- **Tests:** `dotnet run --project MGQOnline.Game.Tests` (64-bit, any .NET 10 runtime). The golden cases are `fixtures/wire/encode.json` and `decode.json`, written from the real script by `ruby fixtures/wire/make_fixtures.rb` (Ruby 3.3, with the stand-ins and the reading game's facts in `wire_harness.rb`); `GameScript/Tests/wire_fixtures_test.rb` checks the script against them on every run, so a change of the script fails there until the cases are written again and the C# codec follows. The cases come from Ruby 3.3 only: RGSS3's Ruby 1.9.2 may read odd number texts (underscores, a cleaned long mantissa) differently, which no game writes, and has not been compared.

## Conventions

- **File headers.** Every `.cs`, `.rb` and `.rbx` file starts with a header block listing the file name and a changelog, newest entry first. Entries by the same author on the same day are grouped. Only changes after the first commit are recorded; before that, a file carries just `Created`.
- **Documentation comments.** Every type and member is documented: XML comments in C#, YARD-style comments in Ruby. The summary is mandatory, parameters and return values whenever they apply, and remarks only where they add something. Keep them short, and always describe the current state.
- **Inline comments.** Kept to a minimum, only where the code cannot say something itself: a constraint, a trap, or the failure a line prevents.
- **Names.** Magic strings and numbers get a named constant.
- **Language.** Code, comments and documentation are English.

## Releasing

1. Push your changes.
2. On GitHub, go to **Releases → Draft a new release**, create a tag like `v0.1.0`, write the notes, and click **Publish**. Players read them as they update, so write them as something a player reads, not as a commit log.
3. The *Release* workflow runs the tests, builds the mod and attaches `MGQ-Online-0.1.0.zip` to that release (its tests fail while the committed events database is older than the schema or the generator, see [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata), Shipping). Keep that zip name: the website's import of the release takes it, and older releases' updaters look for `MGQ-Online-*.zip`. If the run failed, fix the cause on `main`, then start **Actions → Release → Run workflow** with the release's tag.
4. Import the release on the website as a new version of the mod `mgq-online`, which the Mod Framework updates from.
5. Deploy the battle server: the release's scripts and events database into its game folder (`BattleServer/tools/deploy_release.sh`) and the core library attached to the release (see [BattleServer/README.md](../BattleServer/README.md)).

## Runtime

- **Nothing blocks the game.** The DLL's threads run the network; every export returns at once.
- **Nothing throws into the game.** Every export and the DLL's threads catch everything; an exception escaping them would end the game.
- **The connection.** Hosting waits in a room of the relay, and joining enters it; the first frames swap what each game hands over (a PvP battle's team) and then stay open as the link, which carries the modes' own messages (`mp_send`, `mp_receive`). The link pings after 2 s without another frame, counts as `closed` after a goodbye and as `dropped` after 10 s of silence, which a running game never lets happen. `mp_state` describes it: `state`, `code`, `invite`, `error`, `opponent`, `link`, `role`, `party`, `ignored`, then the payload. `mp_status` describes it without the payload, for the checks the scripts make many times a second.
- **Update check:** `MGQ_Multiplayer.start` calls `mp_check_for_update` once per session, and the `Graphics.update` hook polls `mp_newer_version` every 30 frames until it answers (`MGQ_Multiplayer::UpdateCheck`), then asks `mp_update_rollback` whether that release is a step back, which the update offer names as the version to play (`ROLLBACK_TITLE`, `ROLLBACK_TEXT`). Once a version comes back, `MGQ_Multiplayer.outdated?` is true, which `MGQ_MpWorld.available?` and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.available?` both require to be false: the title screen's Multiplayer command greys out (`MGQ_MpWorld.add_title_command`) and `Scene_Title` shows `MGQ_MpWorld::UpdateNotice` (two lines below the translation's version and Discord's own notice, which takes up to two lines from y=24), and F11 on the map answers with `MGQ_Multiplayer::UPDATE_MESSAGE` instead of opening the PvP lobby (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp.on_map`). Different mod versions never speak the same protocol on purpose, so this keeps an outdated game from confusing a friend's newer one rather than failing mid-exchange.

### Worlds (DLL)

A world is a lasting place several games play in at once, up to 32. Its **world code** (`mgqmp2;<token>;<relay>;<seats>`) never changes, and whoever holds it may enter the world; the game keeps it in the world's folder, which `WorldCode.IdOf` names by a hash of the token, so the folder never gives the code away.

- **The directory.** The relay lists every public world, and the hidden ones the asking player joined (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md), world directory). `mp_dir_refresh` fetches the list with the player's key on a thread of the DLL's own, and `mp_dir_list` hands it out as it stands: `state` and `error`, then one tab-separated line per world (`world`, id, seats, players online, creator's id, when last active, creator's name, name, starting save `none`, `pending` or `ready`, 1 when hidden, 1 when players choose their start, 1 without a password, 1 when featured, 1 when only games with the same data may enter, the creator's game data, the mods it needs, its description, the creator's hashes of required mods outside the catalog, its mod settings, its type `classic` or `raid`, how a Raid World shares companions `off`, `story` or `all`, a Raid World's difficulty from -2 to 4, empty when it sets none, 1 when it turns other mods off, else 0, by its type when the relay does not tell (1 for a Raid World), and a Raid World's squad settings as text (`RaidSquad`: whether Luka may be replaced as 1 or 0, the formation size 4 to 8 or 0 for the game's own, the frontline in regular fights and in raids, 1 to 4 each, such as `1 6 2 1`), empty when it keeps none, and 1 when a Classic world's story bosses fight as raid bosses, else 0 (`ListedWorld.RaidBosses`, from the relay's `raidBosses`, which a Raid World never has); fields added later go at the end, and the game script reads a line without the last five as a Classic world sharing none that allows other mods and has no raid bosses) and per player (`member`, id, 1 when online, name). For builds from 0.6.8 on the relay lists every world without its mods, mod hashes and settings (`modsCount` instead), which `GET /v1/worlds/<id>` hands out (`DirectoryClient.World`; `ListedWorld.ModsUnread` when the list left them out); the list then names such a world with the three fields empty and a line `unread` after it, except for the world opened last: `mp_dir_open` (`WorldDirectory.Open`) names it, its mods are read on a thread of their own at once and again with each list, and the list carries them. The world screen opens the world the cursor is on while it waits for the player, and the world being entered, which stays opened in the world (the shared settings read the one world, see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)) and treat a world still `unread` as one the list lacks. Before entering or editing a world still `unread`, the screen reads it as an action (`mp_dir_read`, `WorldDirectory.Read`, *Reading the world's mods . . .*) and goes on once the list carries its mods; the details say *Reading . . .* for its mods meanwhile. The DLL reads the relay's JSON by hand, since NativeAOT leaves out what serializing by reflection needs. Every request of the DLL to the relay, and its world room's WebSocket, names what the game plays in the header `X-MGQ-Features` (`raid`, `DirectoryClient.Features`), and its version in `X-MGQ-Version` (`DirectoryClient.VersionHeader`: `UpdateCheck.ModVersion`, the informational version without the commit, such as `0.6.4`, or `0.0.0-dev` for a development build), which the PvP room's WebSocket names too; the relay keeps games that do not name `raid` out of Raid Worlds. It turns a game away with "Update MGQ Online to play online." on the world directory's and mod catalog's routes (the Raid World admin routes included), the trades, bug reports, Raid World story and boss routes, or a world room, without `X-MGQ-Features` (builds before 0.6.0), and on one of these or a PvP room when its `X-MGQ-Version` is no version or below its `MIN_VERSION`, a request naming none counting as 0.6.4 (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md), Versions); only `0.0.0-dev` always passes. The World Admin tool names itself `0.0.0-dev` too, so `MIN_VERSION` never applies to it. A world room gets 400 with the code `update`, which released games from 0.5.1 to 0.6.4 take as final with "The relay turned the request down. Update the mod." and newer ones with "Update MGQ Online to play online.", a PvP room 426 with the text in `X-MGQ-Refusal`, any other route 426 with the text as `error`.
- **One action at a time.** The directory runs one action at a time on a thread of its own and refuses another while one runs (`WorldDirectory`: create, find, unlock, unlock with a world code, edit, set the mod settings or the game data, delete, ban, fetch the starting save, read a world's mods); `ActionNow` tells `state` (`idle`, `busy`, `done`, `failed`), `kind` and `error`, `ResultNow` the world code, the world's id, its `name`, `start` and `choose`, and `ClearAction` makes room for the next. Only the DLL starts them: the world screen's steps (`WorldEntering`, see [Worlds](#worlds-worldrbx)) and the shared mod settings (`SharedSettingsSync`), each claiming the action it starts (`DirectoryTickets`), so neither takes the other's answer. The scripts reach the directory through `mp_dir_refresh`, `mp_dir_watch`, `mp_dir_open` and `mp_dir_list` alone. The world screen's small flows run in the DLL too (`screen.do` with `favourite`, `copy_id`, which puts a hidden world's id on the clipboard for its creator, `forget` and `delete_saves`), each answered with what to say and `back`. No export may take more than 16 arguments: the game's Win32API refuses to load one with more (`loader_test.rb` checks it).
- **Game data for the World Admin tool.** As the title screen first opens in a session, `GameData.write_file` writes the fingerprint to `Patch\Multiplayer\GameData.ini` (`fingerprint`, and `written` in seconds since 1970), after the mods changed the game's data, so the tool reads an installation's data from the folder it is set to.
- **Game data.** `MGQ_MpWorld::GameData.fingerprint` is a format number and one CRC32 per part of the database (actors, classes, skills, items, weapons, armors, enemies, states, troops, common events, maps) over the ids that exist: entries with a note, features or effects (`used?`, reading the fields the data file holds through `MGQ_MpGame`, since the game's `features` and `effects` add the note's and need its analysis), troops with members, common events with commands, and the map ids. It reads no name, since the translations name entries the untranslated game leaves unnamed (format 1 counted named entries, and the Russian translation named some); the untranslated game, the English and the Russian translation give the same one. Under the Mod Framework the title has loaded only the Framework and Title mods, so the data is compared only once the world's mods loaded: `MGQ_MpWorldMods.enter_game`, right after `Mods.enter` and before the world's save loads or its New Game starts, measures it again (`GameData.measure_entered`, which leaves `GameData.fingerprint`, the one the DLL keeps and served battles carry, as the title measured it, since the battle server measures its data without the world's mods and refuses a setup whose `data` differs) and asks `worlds.data_check` (`WorldEntering.DataChecked`, with the world's directory id) against the world's `data` from the list. A game that differs reads `EnterRules.DataWarning` in the notification box, naming the parts that differ, and plays on: nothing keeps it out and nothing restarts, since going back to the title from there would start the game again, the same for every world type. The creator's game makes its data the world's instead (`mp_dir_set_data`, the relay's `edit` route, which takes `data` from the creator only), started without waiting for its answer while no directory action waits for one, so after a mod update the others are compared with the creator's game as it is now. A new world is made without `data` and without `strict` (the relay keeps both, and a `strict` from an earlier version is read by no rule): its creator's first entry sets it. A world without `data`, or whose `data` has another format (`GameDataCompare.OtherFormat`, such as format 1), is not compared until its creator enters. The world screen's details compare nothing; *Your game* says that the data is compared as the player enters. The DLL compares (`GameDataCompare` in `MGQOnline.Game`); `worlds.game_data`, `worlds.data_differing` and `worlds.data_text` keep this game's fingerprint and compare with another for the scripts (`GameData.differing`, which keeps each answer).
