- **Cancel.** Every screen of the mod that closes asks `MGQ_MpUi.cancel?`: the game's cancel button past the capture, or the numpad's 0 read from Windows with Num Lock on and off (`NUMPAD_CANCEL_KEYS`, 0x60 and Insert), since the game takes it only with Num Lock on; `Key.triggered?` reads those keys at every `Input.update` (`Key.read_watched`), watched from the start (`Key.watch`). A text box asks `numpad_cancel?` alone, as the game's cancel button is X too. `Key.pressed?` takes a key nobody asked about in the frame before as let go, counting frames at `Graphics.update` (`Key.next_frame`), since a game window's cancel runs `Input.update` twice in a frame; so the numpad's 0 that closed one screen still closes the next.
- **Text boxes.** Every text box of the mod types through `MGQ_MpUi::TextEdit` (ui_text_box.rbx), the scripts' side of the box C# keeps (`EditModel`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)): one box is open at a time, which `TextEdit.new` opens (`ui.edit.open`, with the longest text, the characters it takes as a Ruby pattern of one character, the mask of a password, and the chat's filters) and `close` closes. C# takes what is typed straight from the DLL's keyboard in each frame's tick, so the owner reads no typed text itself: it hands the frame's keys over with `follow(keys, scroll)` where it read the keys before (left and right as the game's buttons, up and down only in a box of several lines, Home, End and Delete from Windows, and the lines the mouse wheel scrolls), and acts a frame later on `take`: what typing did (`:enter`, `:escape`, `:tab`, `:typed`, `:erased`, `:refused`), the keys and characters that came, which tell a keyboard from a gamepad, and whether the box changed, which redraws it. `edit(text, cursor)` replaces the text at once and in C# with the frame's batch, `lock` keeps a box to Escape alone (the chat's help tab), `type_now` types the game's letters a gamepad picks at once (`ui.edit.type`). The box's event (`ui.edit`) mirrors the text, the cursor, the blinking and what the box shows. `MGQ_MpUi::TextBox` draws a box being typed into on any picture, a sprite's or a window's: `draw_line` a box of one line, `draw_area` one of several, each with the cursor in the color of the text, from the part C# picked; the first draw in a place tells C# the font, the width and the lines (`view_for`, `ui.edit.view`), later draws ask nothing. The owner draws the background and what the box shows while nobody types. `MGQ_MpUi.spans` breaks a text into lines that keep every character's place (`wrap_spans` measured here for a font C# does not cut by). The chat box, the world screen's forms, the mod picker's name, the bug report's form and the text screen's box all use them; a new box should too.
- **The list box.** `Sprite_MpListBox` (ui.rbx) is a large box of headings and items drawn like the World overview, above every window; `Sprite_MpListBox::View` holds its lines, the item picked and the scroll. The world screen opens it with a world's players (online, then offline), its mods (each required and essential one marked, then the player's mods it turns off) or its whole description; the players' and mods' lines come from the DLL (`screen.box`, `WorldScreenModel.PlayerLines` and `ModLines`), the description is broken to the box's width in the script, from the world's choices, from the details (`Window_MpWorldDetail#targets`: the right arrow moves the cursor onto them, `update_focus`) or with a click (`target_at`).
- **The left side.** The worlds scroll in `Window_MpWorldList`, which draws a scrollbar on a sprite of its own; the commands sit in `Window_MpWorldCommands` below it. `MpWorldListPane` stands for both towards the screen and lets the player pick one of the two as a whole first (`pick`, the cursor drawn around the window through `MGQ_MpBoxCursor`), so the commands are reached without moving past every world: up and down pick, confirm moves into the picked window onto the row the cursor left there (`enter`), cancel moves back out (`back_out`), and cancel while a window is picked, or the commands' *Back*, leaves the screen. The pane reads those keys itself (`update`), a frame after it took the input.
- **Required and essential mods.** A mod written as `?Name` in a world's mods is essential (`MGQ_MpWorld.essential_mods`): no script is looked for, which suits mods of data files; it shows in green while the game data check finds this game matching the world's, and in gold while it differs or cannot be compared. A mod written as `!Name` is required (`required_mods`). The DLL reads the marks (topic `mods.text`, `ModsText`), and `MGQ_MpWorld.mods_text` keeps each text's answer. `installed_mod?` looks for a script of that name among the `.rb` files of the `Patch` folder, which the DLL lists in UTF-8 with each one's key (topic `mods.installed`, `PatchScripts.List`, since the game's Ruby reads file names in the system's code page), read again as the world screen opens. Names and file names are compared by their mod key (`MGQ_MpWorld.mod_key`, topic `mods.keys`): `ModKey` in `MGQOnline.Game`, the name in lower case without a final `.rb`, white space, underscores and hyphens, made exactly as the relay's `modKey` makes it (JavaScript's `toLowerCase` and `\s`), since the relay keeps the catalog by those keys; `fixtures/mods/keys.json`, written from the relay's function, holds both to it; `on_enter` checks them before the game data check, by their files' hashes where the relay's catalog or the creator knows them (see [World mods](#world-mods-world_modsrbx)), else by file name only.
- **Hidden worlds by id.** *Add a hidden world* has the DLL look the id up (`WorldDirectory.Find`, the world's lock, which names it) and keeps it in `Player.ini` as `added.<id>=1`, the oldest first (`MGQ_MpWorld::Added`, through the DLL's `WorldLists`). Every refresh first hands those ids to the DLL (`mp_dir_watch`), which asks the relay for them with the list (`ids`), so the world shows with its details before the player ever entered it; entering then goes the way of any listed world. *Remove from my list* forgets an id whose world was never entered, and only the 50 ids added last are kept (`WorldLists.MaxAdded`, the relay's `maxListedIds`), so the ids of deleted worlds do not crowd new ones out. Whoever holds a hidden world's id therefore sees its details, but still needs its password to enter.
- **The password locks the token.** Making a world makes a new token and locks it with the password (`WorldLock`: PBKDF2-SHA256, 600,000 iterations, then AES-GCM; the DLL derives PBKDF2 itself (`Pbkdf2Sha256`), since .NET's goes through a BCrypt pseudo-handle no Proton release supports; locks of released versions have 200,000), so the relay only ever keeps the lock. An empty password locks it too, and marks the world `open` in the directory, so the games open its lock with the empty password without asking. Opening a world fetches its lock, which comes with the world's name, seats, starting save state and whether players choose their start, so a hidden world opens by its id alone, and opens it with the password; a wrong password fails the tag. The world's id is `Relays.WorldRoomOf(token)`, which the opened token must match.
- **Proving the token.** Every connection sends the auth key (`WorldKeys.AuthKeyOf`: HKDF of the token, apart from the frames' keys and the room id), whose hash the directory keeps. It also sends the player's key and name, which the DLL takes from `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini` once the scripts ask it to tell the worlds who plays (`player.share`); the relay turns the key into the id everyone sees (`WorldKeys.PlayerIdOf`, which `mp_player_id` hands out), and only the creator's key may delete the world or remove a player.
- **The starting save.** `WorldDirectory.Create` takes the starting save's files as name and path pairs, paths relative to the game's folder (`ModFolder.GamePathOf`). `StartingSave.Seal` zips them and encrypts the zip with AES-GCM under a key from the token (HKDF, apart from the auth key) before the world is made, so a save that cannot be read, or sealed is larger than the relay's 8 MB (`StartingSave.MaxSealedBytes`), leaves no world behind; the relay deletes a world whose save has not come 10 minutes after it was made. The world is made with `start: true`, which keeps everyone out until the upload arrived; a failed upload deletes the world again. `WorldDirectory.FetchStart` downloads it with the auth key and unpacks it into the folder, plain file names only, at most 8 files and 64 MB.
- **No host.** `mp_world_open` takes a seat in the world's room at the relay, whose room id is the world's id. Once every seat is taken, the next game waits and tries again, with `error` saying the world is full; it waits the same while the starting save is still on its way or the relay took too many tries from its address, which the relay names in the `X-MGQ-Refusal` header. The player's key goes in the `X-MGQ-Player` header, never in an address. A world the directory lacks, a token that no longer fits, a player the creator removed, a world with as many players as it may, or a Raid World the relay keeps the game out of (400 `raid_unsupported`, which needs a newer mod) ends the session for good (`failed`), as does a close code 4009 (removed, or with the reason `replaced` when the same player entered from another game) or 4010 (deleted) from the relay. A 400 with the code `update`, the relay turning a game too old for it away, ends it for good with "Update MGQ Online to play online." (`Relays.UpdateNeeded`), a 400 with another code or none with "The relay turned the request down. Update the mod."; a 426 ends it for good too, with the text the relay names in `X-MGQ-Refusal`, else `Relays.UpdateNeeded`. A `replaced` close that arrives while a connect of this game's own, cut when the world was left or entered again, could still take a seat (until a whole connect timeout after the cut, since the relay may hold a request past the connect's own timeout, `Connection.SupersededUntil`, checked as the close arrives) connects again instead; a later one ends the session as a real replace.
- **Coming back by itself.** A cut connection, a silent relay (60 s) or the relay's 2-hour limit per connection leads back into the room, after 2, 5, 10, then every 30 s, the delays starting over after a connection that lasted 30 s. Meanwhile `state` is `reconnecting`, and the game plays on. The seat may be another one afterwards.
- **The ping.** The writer sends the relay a text `ping` on connecting and every 5 s (`PingInterval`), which also keeps the connection open, and the relay answers `pong` without waking the room. The time from the last ping to its pong is the ping, `ping` in milliseconds in `Describe` while the connection is open; a ping whose pong never came is given up at the next.
- **Pacing.** The writer sends at most 50 messages a second after a burst of 200 (`WorldSession.MessagesPerSecond`, `MessageBurst`, through `SendPace`), counting frames, chat texts and pings, so it stays below the relay's 60 a second after 240 (`LIMITS` of `Relay/core/relay.js`), which closes a faster connection with 4003. It delays messages and never drops them. Each spell of pacing logs "world writer pacing its sends to 50 a second with N messages queued" as it starts and "world writer paced N messages over S s, longest wait M ms, largest backlog B" once the queue is empty, and a backlog above 250 (`SendPace.LateBacklog`) logs one "world writer falling behind" warning per spell; a 4003 close logs "the relay closed the world connection: this game sent too fast".
- **One key per sender.** `WorldCipher` derives each game's key from the token and a salt the game picks per connection and sends in front of every frame; the sender's seat and the target seat, which follows the counter, are authenticated, so a frame passed on under another seat or to a game it was not meant for fails. A sender's counters only have to grow, since frames it sends to one seat skip the others. Salts of games that left, or whose seat another game took, are refused from then on. One cipher lasts for every connection of an open world (`Renew` picks a new salt per connection), so frames read before a break are refused again after it.
- **The inbox.** `WorldSession` queues what the room brings as inbox entries: `kind=seat` with `seat` and `others` after every (re)connect, `kind=in` or `kind=out` with the `seat` that came or went, `kind=message` with the sender's `seat` and the text, `kind=chat` with the `name` of an admin and the line the relay said for them, `kind=story` with the `rev` a Raid World's story has now (see [World story on the relay](#world-story-on-the-relay-raid-worlds)), `kind=difficulty` (see below), `kind=squad` with `value` a Raid World's new squad settings as text (`WorldSession.TakeSquad`, from the relay's frame `squad <luka> <formation> <front> <raidFront>` after an edit that changed them; released DLLs log it as unknown and drop it), `kind=raid_bosses` with `value` 1 or 0 for a Classic world's raid bosses (`WorldSession.TakeRaidBosses`, from the frame `raid_bosses <1|0>` after an edit that changed them; released DLLs drop it as unknown too), and `kind=mods_rule` with `value` 0 or 1 and the world's `mods` (see [Turn off other mods](#turn-off-other-mods)). The world room (see [The world room](#the-world-room-worldroomtopics)) takes them each frame. `mp_world_send` sends to one seat, or to every other game with -1, and refuses while the game holds no seat. `Describe` gives `state` (`idle`, `connecting`, `open`, `reconnecting`, `failed`), `seat`, `others`, `ping` and `error`, which the room looks at every 30 frames.
- **The relay reads the chat.** The chat's `chat.send` topic mirrors a line of the world's chat to the relay as the text frame `chat <line>` (`WorldSession.Say`, through the writer's queue like a frame, `RelayWorldChannel.SendText`), which the relay keeps for the world's admins and passes to nobody; the line itself still goes to the others sealed. The relay's `chat <name>` + tab + `<line>` text frame, what an admin said in the World Admin tool, becomes the `chat` inbox entry (`TakeChat`); a DLL that does not know the frame logs and ignores it, as it does any text it does not know.
- **Trades.** `mp_trade_commit` (world, trade id, partner's id, both offers as one line) hands a trade to the relay on a thread of its own (`WorldTrades`): the SHA-256 of the offers, and the offers sealed (`TradeSeal`: AES-GCM with a key derived from the world's token, the trade id bound in), so the relay compares hashes and never reads the offers; then it asks the relay about once a second until the trade is committed or cancelled, 150 s at most. `mp_trade_state` reports it (`trade`, `state`: sending, waiting, committed, cancelled or failed, `reason`, `error`), `mp_trade_cancel` cancels a trade the relay has not committed, and `mp_trade_done` marks it applied. `mp_trade_pending` fetches the committed trades this player has not marked done, which `mp_trade_pending_list` hands out unsealed, one `trade=<id>	<offers>` line each after the state. The relay refuses a commit with 429 while the player has 20 open trades, or opened 100 within a day that ended cancelled; the game takes that as a failed commit. Sealing needs the open world's token, which `WorldSession` keeps for the world it opened (`OpenWorld`), also while it reconnects.
- **A world code in the wrong place.** A world code given to a PvP join says where worlds are entered.

### The world room (`WorldRoomTopics`)

The open world as the player's game takes part in it lives in the DLL: `MGQOnline.Game`'s `WorldRoom` (see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)), which `WorldRoomTopics` registers on each bridge context over `WorldSession.Current`. Its timers count the bridge's frames from the drain, never the clock; only the time the player entered their map (`since`) is this computer's clock, since other games compare it with theirs.

- **What the scripts hand over:** `world.me` once it changed, staged before every frame's `Graphics.update` (`MGQ_MpOverworldSync.before_frame`): `in_world`, the player's `state` without the fields the room adds (every value as text, nil while on no map), `raid`, and `story_map`, the map the story takes the player to (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.follow_taken`); as `in_world` turns true and whenever the map in `state` changes after, the DLL reads the event data ahead (see Event sorter in [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)). `world.routes` once the scripts registered their routes: `[[field, gate], ...]`, the gate `party`, `map` or nil. `world.reset_since` after a save loads or a new game starts. `world.since` (a call) answers when the player entered a map, stamping it now for a map the room has not seen them on yet, which the Map Owner order asks the moment the player arrives. `world.drain` (a call) waits until the room sent what was queued, `WorldSession.CloseWait` (3 s) at most, as the game closes.
- **Each frame** (the frame step, before the drain's events are taken): outside a world it forgets every game, the seat and the party and takes nothing. In a world it takes up to 256 inbox entries (`MaxEntriesPerFrame`; the rest wait in `WorldSession`'s inbox), counts the away windows and the invite, tells the others the player's state when it changed (the scripts' fields with the player's `id` and `name`, `since`, the `ping` told and the party's fields), and every 30 frames looks at the connection: the seat, the ping (told the others only once it moved by 20 ms and a quarter of the one they know) and the problem the bottom left line shows ("Reconnecting . . .", "Connecting . . ." or the error, "Not connected: ...").
- **The other games** (`Peer`): a game's first state makes it known, with a notice "\<name> joined the world." only after the first 300 frames in the world (`SettleFrames`), since every game tells a newcomer at once. After every (re)connect (`kind=seat`) every game is told everything and each of the others is kept for 900 frames (`RejoinFrames`), in which they tell back; a game that went (`kind=out`) is kept as long and is the same player when its id tells again, on any seat, even while the relay still holds its old seat; another player telling from the seat of one away replaces them. When another game comes in (`kind=in`), it is told everything at once. Shared names are numbered by id from the second on, "Name (2)", with ASCII-only case folding as RGSS Ruby's `downcase` ("Élise" and "élise" stay two names).
- **The party** (`Party`, from `coop.rbx`): invites (900 frames, 3600 for one naming a player from afar), joining, the leader admitting a player whom the invite reached (it stands for everyone it reached until it runs out, the leader stops it or the party is full), turning away with `kick=late` or `kick=full` (four players at most), removing with `kick=1`, leaving, the leader (the maker by the id's first 8 characters, else the lowest id), and forgetting a party nobody joined or is left in. Its actions are calls that answer the party's view: `party.invite` (an id named from afar, or nil), `party.stop_inviting`, `party.join`, `party.decline`, `party.leave` and `party.remove` (each a seat).
- **The gates:** a message that is no state goes to the first route registered whose field it carries, the routes of the rules in C# before the scripts', past its gate: the party's gate takes only an admitted member's message naming the player's party, the map's gate only a known player's who stands on the player's map, or whose `mmap` is that map or the story's, and drops one whose `mto` names only others. `kick` and `party_decline` are the party's own routes in C#. The relay's pushes become routes without a sender, marked as the relay's: `chat` (with `name`), `story_rev`, `boss_key` (with `left`, `max`, `defeated`, `by`), `difficulty_value`, `squad_value`, `raid_bosses_value`, `mods_rule_value` (with `mods`). The story tracks' frames never reach the room: `WorldSession` keeps them apart for `StoryTopics` (see [Story tracks](#story-tracks)).
- **What the scripts get** (drained events, each posted after the views it depends on): `world.status` (`seat`, `problem`, `ping`, `map`, `since`), `world.labels` (seat to name), `party.view` (`id`, `leader` as a seat, -1 for the player or nil, `members` and `member_ids`, `admitted`, `admitted_me`, `inviting`, `invite_ends` as the frame it runs out at, `targets`, `inviters` as the seats whose invite reaches the player, `may_invite`, and `parties`, each party's id to its size and leader), then `world.peer` (`seat`, `state`, `member`), `world.peer_away`, `world.peer_moved` (`seat`, `from`), `world.peer_left`, `world.route` (`field`, `seat` or nil, `fields`, `body`, `relay`) and `world.notice`. Views go out only once they changed.
- **The areas over the room:** a rule in C# takes the room's messages only through `WorldRoom.Take(field, gate, handler)`, the one registry of C# routes: they are checked after the party's own and before the scripts' (`world.routes`), pass the same gates and are dropped with the same once-only log lines, so a field a rule in C# takes never reaches a script (`world_room_support.rb` drops such a message too, `CSHARP_FIELDS`). An area steps each frame with `context.EveryFrame`, which runs after the room's step on the drain's frame. Every area's topics over the room are registered from `WorldRoomTopics.Register`, each from a file of its own in `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/` (`DuelTopics`, `TeleportTopics`, `NpcTopics`, `SquadViewTopics`, `CoopRoomTopics`, `ChatTopics`, and `TradeTopics` with the relay's way); the topics both hosts answer come from `SharedTopics.Register`.
- **The chat** (`chat.send`, `ChatTopics`): `{channel, text, map, seat}` for the `global` chat (mirrored to the relay first; a line that comes too fast is turned down, `said` 2), the `map` chat (`ch=say` with the map), the `party` chat (`pchat` with the party's id) and a `whisper` to a seat; it answers `sent` and `said`.
- **The battle server** keeps no world room: it fills the scripts' mirror itself (`Peers.take`, `Peers.remove` from the starter's word, `battles_served.rbx`), so `MGQOnline.ServerCore` needs none of these topics.
- **Tests:** `MGQOnline.Game.Tests` replays `fixtures/peers/cases.json`, the scenarios `fixtures/peers/make_fixtures.rb` recorded from the scripts before these rules moved (it ran on that commit's scripts and cannot run on later ones), frame by frame; `MGQParadox.Multiplayer.Tests` covers the topics over a connection that never connects.

### Keyboard

RGSS only knows buttons, so the text boxes get what is typed from the DLL. While `mp_typing` is on, `GameWindow` hands the window's `WM_CHAR` characters and `WM_KEYDOWN` presses to `Keyboard`, and the open text box in C# (`EditModel`, through `GameBridge`'s `ITypedText`) takes what came since the last take in each tick its owner puts in: how many keys went down, the characters, Enter, Backspace and Escape among them as `\r`, `\b` and `\e`, and the clipboard's text in place of Ctrl+V. Windows makes `WM_CHAR` only when the message loop translates key presses; until the first one arrives, `Keyboard` works the characters out from the presses itself (`ToUnicodeEx`, which leaves the keyboard's state alone), which combines no accents typed as dead keys. The in-game log does not tell which way it went, the DLL's `Multiplayer` log does ("the game window gets typed characters"). While typing is on, `Link.typing` also sets `$mgq_text_input`: mods whose hotkeys read Windows directly, as Party Sheet of the MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection does, check it, since `Capture` only reaches the game's `Input`.

**Mouse wheel.** `GameWindow` hands `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` turns to `MouseWheel`, and `mp_mouse_wheel` takes the whole notches since the last call (delta / 120, up positive, a part notch kept for the next call). `MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse.wheel` reads it once a frame (`Key.frame`, which counts on the title screen too, where `Graphics.frame_count` raises) and gives every caller of that frame the same value; turns nobody asked for in the frame before are dropped, so a box that just opened never scrolls by them; 0 while another window is in front, while the player arranges boxes, or without the export. Scrolling lists include `MGQ_MpUi::ScrollingWindow` (the wheel while the window takes the input or has no cursor, a scrollbar sprite in its right padding); every scrolling box draws its bar with `MGQ_MpUi::Style.draw_scrollbar`.

### Background

RGSS pauses a game whose window is not in front: RGSS301.dll keeps the last `WM_ACTIVATEAPP`'s flag and its frame loop waits while it says inactive. A live battle cannot wait for either player, so `GameWindow` subclasses the game's window (class `RGSS Player`) and tells RGSS the game is always active; `Multiplayer.rb` turns it on at start (`mp_keep_running`). The keyboard only reaches the window in front, but gamepads reach every game, so `Background.guard_input` has `Input` report no buttons while another window is in front. It wraps `Input` through `core_hooks.rbx` once the first scene starts, since the game's plugins, the gamepad one among them, load after the Patch folder, and `Capture` reads the buttons past it while it holds them (`Capture.reading?`). Once the last screen gives the buttons back, `Input` reports none until the next `Input.update` (`Capture.released?`), so the press that closed it, such as the Escape that closed the chat box, never reaches the game too, as when a battle's wait took it for leaving the battle. Each `Input.update` asks Windows once whether the game is in front, since the game's `Graphics.frame_count` raises until the game first sets it.

When the game closes, the in-game log's last line says how (`closing_text`): "the game closes", "it was quit" for `exit`, or the error that ended it, so a log that stops without one tells a crash. A second `SceneManager.run`, which only F12 starts, is logged as a reset.

## Discord (optional)

The [Discord Rich Presence](https://github.com/Pauliinchen/MGQ-Paradox-Discord-Rich-Presence) mod, when installed, shows the connection on the player's profile and brings Discord's invites. The two mods only meet in its `MGQ_Discord::Bridge` (version 1), which `MGQ_Multiplayer::Discord` checks for (`available?`) and otherwise leaves alone; the join code on the clipboard works without it.

- **Every 30 frames** (`Discord.tick`, a `Graphics.update` hook) the script hands over the connection when it changed: `Bridge.hosting(party, join_code)`, `Bridge.connected(party, friend)` or `Bridge.idle`. The Discord mod then shows `Waiting for a friend (1 of 2)` with the join code as the invite's join secret, or `Playing with <Friend> (2 of 2)`, in a party both games name after a hash of the join code's token (`party` in `mp_status`).
- **Invites** the player accepted in Discord come back through `Bridge.take_invite`. A world code goes to `MGQ_MpWorld::Invite`: the title screen opens the world screen, which adds the world to the list by its id (the DLL keeps the invite and tells the world's directory id, topics `worlds.invite` and `worlds.invite_take`), waits for it like an entry after a restart for mods, and opens its lock with the code instead of the password (the DLL's steps take the invite as the screen opens, `screen.do` open, and drop a code that failed once); during a game the notification box says the title screen opens it. Under the Mod Framework, whose way back to the title starts the game again once the player entered, the invite asks the framework to carry it through that restart (`Mod_Framework::Mods.carry_after_restart` with `multiplayer.invite` and the join secret), whose title takes it anew (`Stages.invite`), and the box says the world opens once the player goes back to the title. Anything else goes to `mp_receive_invite`, where they wait until the game joins with them (see [Flow](#pvp-battles-battles_pvprbx)). While the game hosts, the DLL ignores them and counts them (`ignored` in `mp_status`), and the PvP battle screen or the map says so once each: Discord also hands over joins the player did not mean, such as after a friend's request to join, and joining would end the hosting that the player's own invites lead to.
- **The player's name** on Discord (`Bridge.player_name`) goes to `mp_set_player_name` before every host or join, so the friend sees it; without the Discord mod it is "A friend".
- **Status fields:** `battles_pvp.rbx` registers `Bridge.add_status`, which adds `pvp_battle_with=<Friend>` for the Discord mod's first line; `overworld_sync.rbx` adds `mp_world`, `mp_world_id`, `mp_world_size`, `mp_world_max` and `mp_world_invite` while a world is open (the seats from the world code's last field; the world code as the invites' join secret, left out for a world from before the directory), and `coop.rbx` adds `mp_party`, `mp_party_size` and `mp_party_max` while the player is in a world's party of two or more. The Discord mod shows `Playing on World <World> (3 of 8)`, taking turns with `Currently in a Party! (2 of 4)`.

**Dialogue boxes.** The [Battle Dialogue](https://github.com/Pauliinchen/MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection/tree/main/Battle_Dialogue) mod of the MGQ-Paradox-Mod-Collection, when installed, shows every battle's messages in boxes at the screen's sides instead of the message window, so neither side of a live battle waits for a key. The guest's playback never calls it: it adds each message the host's battle showed to the game's message and names its speaker from the stream in `$game_message.speaker`, which that mod reads; before a new message starts it lets the game move the earlier one on (`Scene_Battle#wait_for_message`), unless the guest has to catch up (`Playback.message`). Without it, a live battle's messages stay in the message window, which moves on by itself after 90 frames on both sides.

## Worlds (`world.rbx`)

**Flow.** *Multiplayer*, below *Continue* on the title screen (`Window_TitleCommand#make_command_list`), opens `Scene_MpWorlds`, laid out like Mod Config Remake: the worlds at the left, the chosen one's details at the right (who made it, players online of its seats, whether it is hidden, and everyone who ever joined, those online marked), then *Create new world*, *Add a hidden world*, *Change your name* and *Back*. The list is every world the directory shows the player plus the worlds on this PC it no longer has, which come last once the directory's list arrived (`Entry#gone`); among either, Raid Worlds first (drawn in red, `MGQ_MpWorld::RAID_COLOR`, also above favourites and in place of a featured world's gold; one the directory no longer lists keeps its colour by its `world.ini`), then favourites (marked `*`), then the featured worlds (drawn in gold, `MGQ_MpWorld::FEATURED_COLOR`; only the relay's admins make them, with the admin tool outside this repository), then those played last, then by name (`WorldEntries` in `MGQOnline.Game`, which the screen's model in the DLL joins the list with, which sorts names by their ASCII letters in lower case and bytewise, as the game's Ruby did, and keeps worlds alike in every part of the order in the list's order); it is fetched when the screen opens and every 10 s, and keeps the cursor on the world or command it was on. The DLL holds this model (`WorldScreen`, `ScreenTopics`): `screen.open` and `screen.close` start and stop the refresh, a frame step that fetches the list every 600 drained frames while no directory action runs; `screen.list` hands out the list joined from the directory's list (`WorldDirectory.ListNow`), the folders on this PC and the favourites, each row with its key (the directory's id, or `folder:` and the folder's), its drawn texts and colour, the listed world's fields and the folder's world.ini; `screen.details`, `screen.actions` and `screen.box` answer by a row's key, `screen.world` one world of the list with its mods (for the screen once a world's mods were read), and `screen.texts` the hint, the hint after a world was created or added and what the screen says while each action runs. The scripts ask for the rows only once `worlds.changed` names `List` (the directory's `ListRevision` moves as a fetch starts or ends or the opened world's mods were read, and every change of a world on this PC or a favourite marks it), and draw a world's details again only when the cursor moves to another or `List`, `Details` or `Mods` changed; `Window_MpWorldList` draws each row as the DLL wrote it. A world offers *Enter the world*, *Mark as a favourite*, *Delete my saves of it*, and for its creator *Remove a player*, *Delete the world for everyone* and, for a hidden world, *Copy the world id*. *Enter* asks for the password only while the world has one and this PC's folder of the world does not hold its world code (`Entry#password_known?`; the details' *Password* row then says *Known* instead of *Needed*, `Window_MpWorldDetail#password_text`). While a directory action runs, the screen says what it waits for and holds the input (`@resume_form` false while a form's action runs, so its button cannot send it twice). After *Create the world* or *Add the world* the cursor goes onto that world as soon as the fetched list shows it (`MpWorldListPane#select_world`, `select_asked`: into the worlds box, unless the pane no longer takes the input because the player moved on), and the info line says "\<name\> was created. Confirm to enter it." (`ENTER_HINT`).

**Forms.** While the list points at *Create new world* or *Add a hidden world*, the right side shows its form (`MGQ_MpWorld::WorldForm`, drawn by `Window_MpWorldForm`; see the form engine below), and confirming moves the cursor into it; Cancel goes back to the list and keeps what was filled in. Up and down move between rows, left and right between the two fields on one row, and the lines at the top show the hint of the field the cursor is on. A switch (`:switch`, drawn and turned by `Window_MpWorldForm` for the world screen and the story's choices alike, `draw_switch`, `turn_or_move`) shows its outcome between arrows; confirm turns it onward, an arrow turns it unless it points at the other field of the switch's row, and one that cannot be used buzzes. *Create new world* has *Name*, *Password* (may be empty, as in the other form), *Max Players* (digits only, 2 to 32, 4 at first), *Hidden*, the switch *World type* (Classic or Raid, fixed once created), for a Raid World the switch *Sharing companions* (Off, Story companions, or Story companions and battle recruits), below it the checkbox *Shared save* beside the switch *Difficulty* (the game's seven names, NORMAL at first), and below them *Save* beside the button *Set formation limits*, whose row comes off the description (`RAID_DESCRIPTION_LINES`); for a Classic world on the same two rows the checkboxes *Shared save* and *Player's choice* and below them *Save* (*Save* is greyed out until *Shared save* is ticked, whose `needs` is a block; opens the save screen, see [Starting save](#starting-save-world_save_distributionrbx); a Raid World never sends the choice, `from_save?` and `choose?`), each field shown only for its type (a field's `Shows` rule takes it out of the view while it is not part of the form), all in the panel *World*, which keeps the form on one page; *Mods*, the checkbox *Shared Mod Settings* and the checkbox *Turn off other mods* in *Game data* (ticked for a Raid World and unticked for a Classic one as the type changes, until the player ticks or unticks it, `FormField.Follows`); the description, a box of several lines, in *Description*; and *Create the world*. A field's `group` names its panel, and `Window_MpWorldForm#places` lays the panels out below each other, as `Window_MpWorldDetail` draws a world's details; both share `MGQ_MpWorldPanels`. *Edit the world*, offered to a world's creator and the relay's admins, opens a third form in place of the details (`WorldForms.OpenEdit`, kept as `@forms[:edit_world]` only while it is open) with what may change after a world was made: *Max Players*, for a Raid World the switch *Difficulty* below it (first *Per player* for one that sets none, which sends nothing), *Mods*, *Turn off other mods* and the description. **Requests.** Sending a form has the DLL build the request from the form it holds (`screen.do` with `create`, `edit` or `find`; `WorldEntering.Requests.cs`, its rules in `WorldRequests` of `MGQOnline.Game`, checked against `fixtures/requests/edits.json`, which `fixtures/requests/make_fixtures.rb` recorded from the screen before they moved): a new world goes with the starting save's files the script names (`MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.files_of`), no game data (its creator's first entry sets it, see *Game data*), the creator's mod hashes and, with *Shared Mod Settings* ticked, the settings' marker; an edit sends the seats, the description, the mods, the creator's mod hashes (its creator only), and a Raid World's difficulty and squad rules, a Classic world's raid bosses and *Turn off other mods* only when the form changed them, since the relay tells every player of each, then the mod settings when its creator changed *Shared Mod Settings*, to the relay's `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/edit`; removing a player and deleting a world go the same way (`ban`, `delete`). What follows comes as steps the screen plays: `place_start` (copy the starting save into the new world's folder, answered with `placed`), `fresh_form`, `close_form`, `select` (the cursor onto the world made or added), `resume_form`, `say` and `back`; the DLL makes the new world's folder, marks it a favourite, adds a found world to the list and tells what an edit changed (`WorldRequests.ChangedText`, `SharedText`, `SettingsFailedText`); a world's folder takes the new seats into its code the next time the world is entered from the list (`World#describe`). *Add a hidden world* has *World id* (32 characters of 0-9 and a-f, pasted with Ctrl+V) and *Add the world*, which adds the world to the list; Enter in the id box sends the form like the password form's (`WorldForms.SendsOnEnter`, the view's `sends_on_enter`: only while the form has one text box, so a form that gains another is sent from its button). Sending a form that is not complete names the first field that is wrong and moves the cursor there. The scene is the same object when the text or save screen returns, so the forms keep their values and the cursor goes back to the field it left from.

**The form engine.** The DLL holds the world screen's forms (`MGQOnline.Game/Forms/`): `Form` is a form being filled in, its pages (the main one and, for a world form, `limits`, the squad rules, which share its values), the fields (`FormField`: key, kind, label, row, side, longest text, the characters taken as `FieldChars` `digits` or `hex`, which the scripts map back to a `TextEdit` rule, group, lines, hint, choices, label width, a number's range) and the rules that once were blocks (`FormRule`: a switch's outcome, a ticked checkbox, a fact the form knows such as `discord`, and `Not`; `Shows`, `Needs`, `EmptyOk` and `Follows`, the checkbox that follows a rule after each turn of a switch until the player ticks it); `Turn`, `Tick`, `Set`, `Check` (tidied text and why it is refused: a number in range, a world id lower-cased, a password as typed, other texts stripped), `Problem` (the first field that keeps a page from being sent) and `Tidy`. `WorldForms` makes the five forms of the world screen from the DLL's rules (`WorldFormRules`, from `WorldCode`, `WorldDifficulty` and `RaidSquad`) and adds their values up (`ValuesOf`: type, sharing, difficulty, squad rules, starting save, player's choice, raid bosses, other mods off), which the requests take. `FormTopics` keeps the open forms per context by slot (`new_world`, `join_hidden`, `rename`, `password`, `edit_world`): `form.open` (kind and what it opens with: `name`, `discord`, `world`, `own`), `form.view`, `form.set`, `form.turn`, `form.tick`, `form.check`, `form.send` (the problem, or the page tidied) and `form.close`; a view is the title, the fields shown with whether each can be used, the values, the sums and `sends_on_enter`, handed out only with an action that may change it, never per frame. `MGQ_MpWorld::WorldForm` is the scripts' copy: it reads values from its copy, hands a changed value over (`form.set`, a typed text at most once a key, since its value changes no rule; any other value has every page take its view anew at the next read), and gives the fields as `Form::Field` view structs, equal by key, kind and place. The story's choices and the bug report keep a `MGQ_MpWorld::Form` of their own until they move onto the engine. The golden cases, `fixtures/forms/world.json`, are recorded by `ruby fixtures/forms/make_fixtures.rb` from `fixtures/forms/form_prototype.rb`, the Ruby forms as world.rbx held them, which the script tests also answer the `form.*` topics with (`GameScript/Tests/form_support.rb`, checked against the cases as it loads).

**Rules.** What a world's settings may be comes from the DLL, and the scripts keep no copy: `world.rules` (`WorldTopics`) hands out the seats (`WorldCode`: 2 to 32, 4 for a new world), the difficulties with their names and the default (`WorldDifficulty`), the Formation sizes with their names, the Frontlines and the default squad rules (`RaidSquad`, in `MGQOnline.Game`); `squad.read` and `squad.write` read and write squad rules as world.ini, the list and the relay write them (four numbers separated by single spaces), and `world.code` tells and changes the seats in a world code. The directory refuses seats outside what a world code holds and squad rules that do not parse before it asks the relay (`WorldDirectory.Create` and `Edit`, a failed action the screen tells), so the form check is not the only guard. `fixtures/world/rules.json` holds their answers, which the DLL's tests check and the script tests answer from. The squad topics (`SquadTopics`) are among those both hosts answer, so the battle server's core answers them too, with `squad.default` (the default rules) and `squad.served` (a served battle's setup's `[name, value]` pairs, the defaults filled in, nil for none or rules out of range), which `MGQ_MpBattlesServed.squad_of` and `default_squad` ask; `fixtures/squad/squad.json` holds what `squad_of` answered before the rule left it.

**Entering.** The world screen's way into a world is a step machine in the DLL (`WorldEntering`, `EnteringTopics`), which the screen plays: `screen.enter` (the entry's `id`, `folder`, `name`, `gone` and `me`) starts it, `screen.answer` hands back the player's answer or what an effect did (`yes`, `show_mods`, `back`, `password` with `text`, `password_back`, `from_creator`, `take_start`, `from_beginning`, `from_own`, `own_save` with `index`, `own_copied` with `ok`, `start_failed` with `text` and `placed`, `installed` with `ok`, `text` and `restart`), `screen.step` follows a directory action the steps started once the `Screen` bit of `worlds.changed` came, and `screen.do` runs `open` (as the screen opens: the world the game started again for, `me` and this game's fingerprint, and the Discord invite, which it takes and adds to the list), `look` (while it waits for the list to enter such a world: the list's `state`, whether the screen is `idle`, whether the player is `named` and the `entries`), `read_for_edit` and `close`. Each answers `steps`, plain Arrays the screen plays in order (`Scene_MpWorlds#play_step`): `say`, `buzz`, `back`, `confirm` (what it is about and the commands of the confirm window), `choices` (the start window's), `password`, `leave_form`, `busy` and `idle`, `entry` (the world the list holds), `read` (the world's mods were read: the screen takes it anew, `MGQ_MpWorldScreen.world`), `edit`, `install` (the world's mods for the scripts to install through the Mod Framework), `forget_installed`, `restart` (the world's id, for the framework to start the game again and enter it), `exit`, `save_screen`, `copy_own`, `start` (the world as `worlds.local` describes it, whether its save was just placed, whether the list holds it and whether the list had arrived) and `discard`; `screen.do` also tells `waiting`. The DLL keeps the entry's state (the invite codes, the rejoin, whether a mod installed runs already) and answers `worlds.data_check` once the world's mods loaded (see *Game data* under [Worlds (DLL)](#worlds-dll); the data is no longer compared on the way in, whose golden data cases record the question and the refusal it replaced) and asks the other areas' topics in-process (`BridgeContext.Ask`: `mods.check`, `mods.forget`, `worlds.invite_take`); the rules are `EnterRules` in `MGQOnline.Game`, whose golden cases (`fixtures/entering/rules.json`) `ruby fixtures/entering/make_fixtures.rb` recorded from `world_screen.rbx` and `world_save_distribution.rbx` before they left them. The screen keeps the windows, the forms, the save screen, the copy of a save, `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.check`, `MGQ_MpWorldMods.enter_game` (the Mod Framework's entering with the world's session), `MGQ_MpWorldMods.enter_world` and `MGQ_MpWorld.start`. Creating, editing, finding, deleting and removing a player still run in the screen (`start_action`, `follow_action`).

**Typing.** A text box is typed into in place: confirming it takes the keyboard (see [Keyboard](#keyboard)) and shows a caret; Enter keeps the text once it passes the field's check (`form.check`, `Form.Check` in C#: not empty, Max Players in range, a whole world id), otherwise the reason shows and the typing goes on; Escape puts the text back; characters the field does not take buzz. The form takes the cursor again only once OK and Cancel are let go, so the Enter that ended the typing does not start it again. A button or direction pressed in a frame no key went down, as only a gamepad does (directions by their first frame, since a held arrow key reports its direction without going down again), moves the typing to `Scene_MpText` with the game's letters shown; so does confirming a text box while the game's window is not hooked (`Background.running?`), since the keyboard cannot reach the game then. What the text screen hands back passes the same check.

**Names and passwords.** The first visit asks for the player's name unless the Discord mod knows it (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Player`: the name chosen for Multiplayer, else the one on Discord; PvP battles pass it on too). `Scene_MpText` shows the text in a box, as stars for a password, and takes the keyboard: Enter confirms, Escape leaves, Backspace removes the last character. What came in one frame is drawn once with one sound, the buzzer when a character was refused (`type_text`, `Window_MpTextEdit#hold_refresh`), so a pasted text costs one drawing. A text that fits the box sits where the game puts a name; a longer one, such as a mod's name or a world id, scrolls, showing its end with the cursor after it and a dim `...` in the first cell while its start is out of sight. The description (`:area`) gets a box `AREA_ROWS` (4) lines tall that breaks the text at words (`MGQ_MpUi.wrap_spans` over cells of one character) and shows its last lines, with the `...` before the first line shown while lines above it are out of sight. The game's own letters stay hidden until a button is pressed in a frame no key went down, and show from the start when the game's window is not hooked or a form's text box asked for them. A key that types also moves the game's own buttons, such as Z for OK, so the letters ignore the buttons in a frame the keyboard was used. Cancel on the letters with nothing typed leaves.

**On disk.** `Patch/Multiplayer/Player.ini` keeps the player's key (`id`, which the DLL makes the first time and never hands to the scripts), chosen name, the last name Discord told, the parked mods' `rejoin` and `mods_back_restart`, bound hotkeys (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`), the interface's settings, the favourite worlds' ids (`favourite.<id>=1`) and the hidden worlds added (`added.<id>=1`), as flat `key=value` lines. The DLL keeps the two lists (`WorldLists`; the world screen's steps mark favourites and add or remove hidden worlds, and topic `worlds.added` lists them) and moves the entries of the `Favourites.ini` and `Added.ini` of earlier versions into Player.ini once, a setting it holds already winning, then deletes those files; while any of the three cannot be read it moves and deletes nothing and tries again at most every 60 frames. Each world entered has `Patch/Multiplayer/Worlds/<id>/` (`WorldCode.IdOf`), with `world.ini` (name, world code, the directory's id of the world, its `type`, `share`, `difficulty`, `squad` and `raid_bosses` once the directory told them, when it was made and last played) and `Save/`, which only the DLL reads and writes (`LocalWorlds`, `LocalWorld`; topics `worlds.local`, `worlds.found`, `worlds.describe`, `worlds.update`, and `worlds.latest_save`; the world screen's steps delete a folder): it reads the folders once until a world is written, made or deleted, writes a world described anew at once (`describe`, the seats going into its code through `WorldCode`), and finds the latest save by the files' times, the first in the folder's order of two written at once. `MGQ_MpWorld::World` is the scripts' copy of one world's fields, since the open world's are read every frame and the battle server wraps them. *Delete my saves of it* deletes the folder, and with it that this PC knows the password.

Only the DLL reads and writes these files (`IniFile.cs`). A write goes to a file next to the target (`.tmp`), is flushed to the disk and moved over the target in one step, never deleting the target first; a read takes UTF-8 with or without a byte order mark, UTF-16 by its byte order mark and anything else as Latin-1 (Notepad's ANSI), and tells a file that exists but cannot be read from a missing one. `PlayerIni` owns `Player.ini`: the name rule (the chosen name, cleaned as the scripts clean names, else Discord's of this session, else the last one Discord told), the key, and the settings. The scripts reach it through the topics `player.state`, `player.change` ({setting => value, nil to remove}; the key is refused), `player.discord`, `player.name` and `player.share`, each answering the settings as they then stand (`values` without the key, `readable`, `notice`, `name`), which `MGQ_Multiplayer::Player` keeps as its copy, since `setting` is read every frame (the hotkeys, the layout, the theme). Every other file goes through `ini.read` and `ini.write` (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Ini`). While `Player.ini` exists but cannot be read, the DLL never writes it: no key is made, so no world action, `store`, `forget` and `change` give false, it logs this once, the scripts ask again at most once a second (`Player::RETRY_SECONDS`), and the notification box tells it once a session (`take_notice`). `World.found` leaves a `world.ini` that cannot be read as it is.

**Raid Worlds.** A world is Classic or a Raid World, which its creator picks once (`type`, see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md)). `MGQ_MpWorld.raid?` is the one question every script asks (false outside worlds), and `MGQ_MpWorld.companion_sharing` tells how the open Raid World shares companions (`:off`, `:story` or `:all`; `:off` outside Raid Worlds), both from the open world's `World#raid?` and `World#companion_sharing`, which `world.ini` keeps. The details name the type, and for a Raid World how it shares companions.

**Its own saves.** Entering a world writes the player's own system save, then redirects every path below the game's `Save` folder to the world's (`Files`: the path-taking methods of `File` and `Dir`, and `Bitmap.new` for thumbnails), and loads the world's system save, or makes one (`System`): the Library, the system switches and the global system with the affection, which the game otherwise shares between all saves and loads once per start. The game names `Save/` in many places, its backups, thumbnails and temporary file included, so the paths are changed where they reach the files. Paths only move while a world is open, and a path such as `SaveData.ini` never does. The game's options live in each save, so they are unaffected.

- **Entering** loads the world's latest save, an autosave included, with the game's own `Scene_Load#on_load_success`. A world without saves goes back to the title screen, whose own `command_new_game` starts the new game, so everything the game and other mods do for one happens. Entering also takes a seat in the world's room (`mp_world_open`), which tells the directory the player is online.
- **Leaving** happens whenever the title screen starts other than for that new game: after *To Title*, a reset or a game over. It leaves the room, writes the world's system save and puts the player's own back.
- **PvP battles** are fought as duels in a world (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)); the PvP battle screen outside a world was removed on 2026-10-24, and the World overview's hotkey (F11) opens the World overview in a world and in single player.

## World story on the relay (Raid Worlds)

A Raid World's story is the relay's own, key by key, on its **story tracks** (`Relay/core/story_tracks.js`, the protocol in [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md#story-tracks)): the world's track, which every player who keeps up with the world plays, and a track of their own for each player behind. Every game keeps a copy of the keys its track holds in its own `$game_*`; the relay alone changes them. The game sends the keys the player's game writes as requests, applies them at once so the event that wrote them reads them, and writes what the relay hands back straight into its story: nothing is merged. The **story mapping** (`GET /v1/story-mapping`, see [Story mapping](#story-mapping)) tells which keys a track keeps; a key without a row is each player's own.

The DLL plays the protocol: `StoryTrack` of `MGQOnline.Game` (see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)) over the world room's text frames `track <JSON>` (`WorldSession.SendTrack`, `TakeTrackFrame`; every request of the game names `tracks` in `X-MGQ-Features`, beside `raid` and `keys`), behind the bridge topics of `StoryTopics`. The scripts' side is `story_keys.rbx` (the table and the write hook) and `world_story.rbx` (the rest). Released games (0.6.8 and before) keep playing the relay's **keyed story** (`Relay/core/story.js`, [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md#world-story)), which a world plays instead of the tracks until phase 6 moves it; of that story this game only reads and adds the **shared companions** (`RaidStory`, `mp_raid_story_fetch`, `mp_raid_story_headers`, `mp_raid_companions_add`). Classic worlds keep no story on the relay; their party's story shares the same write hook (see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)).

### Story mapping

The relay's one mapping for every Raid World (`Relay/core/story_mapping.js`, routes and rows in [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md#story-mapping)), filled by hand after review, never imported.

- **The derived index** (map events whose self switches are shared because they read or write a shared key, and the common events that do) is derived by the battle server from the rows of one `rowsRev` and the events database the mod ships (`MGQOnline.Game`'s `SelfSwitchIndex`, through the core's topic `story.index`, `BattleServer/lib/story_index.rb`; refused while the database differs from the server's game data in the maps or the common events, see [The battle server's core](#the-battle-servers-core-mgqonlineservercore)) and kept beside them; it goes stale with the next batch. The relay asks for it over its own connection to the battle server after every batch that changed rows and at start when it is missing or stale (`deriveStoryIndex`). The rule: a key of kind `shared`, `one_way` or `count` is shared; an event counts every page, its conditions and every branch, through the common events it calls at any depth, but not through the player's own common events (2, 3, 5, 114: every exit calls the transfer process); it reads a key in a branch, an operand, a place or troop by variable or a script (`$game_switches[n]`, `$game_variables[n]`), and writes one by Control Switches or Variables, Input Number, Select Key Item, Get Location Info or a script (`ListKeys`). The `events` rows for a whole map, a whole event or a whole common event (letter `*`) apply, a map's before an event's; a row for one letter stays for the game to apply as it reads the rows. With it come the names of the story's keys from the game's `System.rvdata2`, `MapInfos.rvdata2` (both folders of maps), maps and `Actors.rvdata2` (`StoryNames`, `GET /v1/admin/story/names`), the fields of the website's names file (`StoryNamesDto`: switches, variables, maps, events, companions), so the website can read them from the relay in place of the file `Admin__StoryNames__Path` names.
- **Fetched** by the DLL as the player's story starts (`story.enter`) and again once the relay reads requests by a newer revision than the one held (the `mapping` frame, or a `mapping` revision in a state or an answer): `GET /v1/story-mapping?known=<rev>` on a thread of its own (`DirectoryClient.GetStoryMapping`), tried again after 2, 5, 10 and 30 s when it failed, and after 30 s when it stayed the same while the relay names a newer one. `StoryMapping.Read` turns it into a `StoryTable`; a row this version cannot read is left out, its key each player's own. An answer with rows is kept in the relay mirror beside the events database (`story.db`, see [The events database](#the-events-database-mgqonlinedata)); while no table is held, the fetch first takes the mirror's table for the same relay and events database and names its revision as `known`, so a start after the first fetches nothing but `same`, and a relay that cannot be reached still leaves the table. Without a mirror (a folder the game may not write, logged once) every start fetches the whole mapping, as before. `StoryTopicsTests` start a second time over one mirror: it names the mirror's revision and keeps the table on `same`, keeps it while the relay cannot be reached, and fetches whole for another relay or without a mirror.
- **The table the scripts look writes up in** (topic `story.classes`, `StoryTopics.TableOf`): `switches` and `variables`, one `StoryClass` byte per id (binary strings; an id past the end is each player's own): 1 kept on a track, 2 one-way, 4 scene, 8 chest, 16 count, 32 the whole world's, 64 written by the defeat, 128 a Classic side switch; `self`, each `events` row's key (`ss<map>.<event>.<letter>`, `*` for every event or letter, `ce<id>.<letter>`) to its class; `index`, the indexed events as `map * 1,000,000 + event`; `local_steps`, each variable's steps that stay each player's own in places (`[variable, steps, shared as, [[map, from x], ...]]`); `gates`, each part's level; `self_default`, the class of a self switch no rule and no indexed event names. `MGQ_MpStoryKeys.class_of` finds a self switch's class as the relay does: its own rule, its event's, the common event's that wrote it, its map's letter's, its map's, else the index; `fixtures/story/classes.json` holds the kinds the relay's `TrackRules.kindOf` gives a mapping's keys, which `MGQOnline.Game.Tests` checks the C# table against and `story_keys_test.rb` the scripts' lookup over the bytes `StoryTopicsTests` pins.
- The scripts read the table anew once the status names another revision (`load_classes`). Until the first came, a Raid World's events sort by a Classic party's table, so they still sort as story meanwhile, and its writes wait unsorted (`@unsorted`), recorded by the mapping once it came.

### Story tracks

**The DLL's side** (`StoryTrack`, `StoryTopics`):

- **Placing the player** (`story.enter` `{world, fresh, level}`, staged by the scripts as the Raid World opens, a save loads or a new game starts, `fresh` for a new game): the DLL sends `sync` with the level once the world room is open, and again on every new connection (`WorldSession.Connections` counts the seats the relay gave). The relay answers the whole track (`state`), or `need` for a player it places by their story: the status says `need_seed`, the scripts hand the seed (`story.seed`: every switch and variable the mapping keeps or a chest's, and such self switches, at their values, those off or 0 left out) and the DLL sends `sync` with `fresh` and the seed. A sync waits for its answer before another goes; a refused one (`error`) lets the next go.
- **Requests** (`story.writes`, staged once a frame with the frame's writes, `[[key, value, site], ...]`): each write counts as the player's own until answered; the writes go as numbered batches of at most 4,000 (`seq` above the last batch the relay took, from the state's `seq`) on the player's track, the writes made before the player was placed waiting for it. A write the relay would refuse a whole batch for (a key it does not name, a value that does not fit, such as a float written as `fInfinity`, a site it does not take) is left out and logged. A batch the relay answers `sync` goes again after the next state; `seen` (taken before, as when an answer was lost over a reconnect) and `track` (the player is on another track now) ask for the whole track; `bad` drops it.
- **What comes back** waits in the DLL until the scripts take it (`story.take`, which they call once the player is free): a whole track (`state`) replaces everything that waited; pushes add their keys; the answer's own value of each request it did not keep as written (one-way, count, step, held, route, pinned, ended) too. A value for a key the player's own unanswered request wrote is dropped: the relay applied the batch that carried it before the player's, so the player's value stands unless the answer brings the relay's; a state names such keys in `held`, which keep the player's values. A push that skips a revision of the player's track asks for the whole track; a player behind takes the world's pushes as they come (the keys the whole world keeps, frozen fights, the summary). The refusals go to the scripts as the event `story.refused` (`[[key, code, value], ...]`).
- **Tellings and watching** (`story.tell` `{site, map}`, nil once it ended; `story.watch` seat or nil): the DLL names each telling with an id of its own and sends `tell` (again with the same id when the telling moves to another map), `told` at its end, and `watch` once the relay told of the watched teller's telling (`telling` frames, a state's `tellings`), again when that telling's id changes. A new connection sends the player's telling and what they watch again after the sync.
- **The level** (`story.level` `{best, cap}`): sent as `level` whenever it changed, and with every sync.
- **The part's end** (`story.leaves_part`, the list named as for `events.sort`, with its `values`): whether a story event, with the chain it starts across maps, would move the player's story past the end of their track's part, answered `{leaves, why}`, `needs` as `events.sort` does (with `ss`, self switches named `"map.event.letter"`), or `{unknown}` for a page the DLL's data lacks; see *Story held* under [Catch-up](#catch-up-world_catchuprbx).
- **A Classic world** (`story.mapping`, the world's id or nil): plays no story on tracks, but fetches the mapping from its relay for the rewards a party member catches up with (`story.rewards`, see [The party's story](#the-partys-story-coop_storyrbx)), once it opened and again every ten minutes with the revision held. The catch-up's topics read the same tables (`CatchupTopics`, `StoryTopics.Catchup`).
- **Status** (event `story.status`, posted once it changed): `synced`, `track`, `part`, `gate`, `rev` (the player's track's revision as the relay last told it), `taken` (the revision the last take brought the game to), `world` (the world's summary: `rev`, `part`, `route`, `done`, `open`, `checkpoints`, `start`), `pending`, `need_seed`, `classes` (the mapping's revision held), `tellings` (seat to `[telling, map, site]`), `told` (seat to the revision their last telling ended at), `await` (the seat whose ended telling the game has yet to take the track's revision of, while something waits), `telling` and `holder` (how the relay answered the player's own telling), `unanswered`.
- `StoryTrackRelayTests` plays two games' tracks against the relay's own `StoryTracks` in Node (`fixtures/story/track_harness.mjs`): the first game's seed starts the world, the second plays the world's track, a write reaches the other as a push, a one-way key lowered comes back as the relay's value, a key without a row stays the writer's, a watched telling reaches the viewer.

### Runs handed over by replay (C#)

Issue 73's model ("Issue 73 chest effects.md" in the workspace, "General model"): no effects are copied; a run whose event counts for others travels as an entry, and each receiver's own game plays that event again, silently. The C# side lives in `MGQOnline.Game/Story` and answers through `StoryTopics.Replay.cs`; the scripts' replayer is `story_replay.rbx`.

- **Who keeps each key** (`KeyScopes`, topic `story.replay_scopes {party}`): one scope per key from the one reviewed list, the story mapping. Work keys (the `work` option of a `personal` row, `StoryTable.Work`) are the game's; a chest's keys (`party_chests` self switches, `chest` switches) are the party's in a Classic party and each player's own in a Raid World, where the `chests` rows are the world's; any other key is the world's when its table keeps it (`wholeWorld` apart), else the player's. A Classic party takes its world keys from the Classic story's ranges until Classic moves onto the list. The answer is shaped as `story.classes` with a scope byte (0 player, 1 work, 2 party, 3 world, 4 whole world) and `keys`, the self switches named one by one, which go before the rules.
- **The entry** (`ReplayEntry`, topic `story.replay_ran`): a run that changed a party or world key logs its step; when its page gives, it makes an entry `{id, site, stream, scope, opener, keys (each with its value before), place, start, inputs, derived}`, every party and world key it changed being the step it hands over: into its story's stream when it changed a world key, a party chest's run too; into its party's stream otherwise. The entry is in a party's stream at once and in a story's when the player owns it (`owner`, a Classic leader); a member's step of the leader's story waits for the leader's entry, which claims it by site. `story.replay_reads {site, party}` names the world and work keys whose start values it carries: every page's conditions, the reads of each page's list and of the common events it calls, and the event's self switches (`PageMatch.ReadKeys`).
- **Rule 2** (`RunLog`, `PageMatch`, topic `story.replay_take`): an entry is done when the save's log holds a step it claimed before, when its place lies behind the save's start place, or when a standing step of its site is unclaimed in its stream (claimed now). Else the receiver's game picks the last page whose conditions hold, reading world and work keys at the entry's start values (`ReplayView`) and its own things as they are, asking for those the request lacks (`needs`): the run's page or a sibling that writes a key of the step is replayed, anything else waits. A waiting run is due again once one of the receiver's own things it read changed (`story.replay_due`); a reset that moves a step's key back ends the step and drops a waiting run (`story.replay_reset`), and a reset of a story marker lowers the start place. Opening a chest that waits for the player makes its run done. A run handed again while it waits, as the catch-up hands a Makina at each look, is answered as waiting without a replay until it is due.
- **The notice** (`ReplayNotice`, topic `story.replay_done`): one sentence from what the receiver held before and after the replay, such as "Ana opened a chest for the party: Blast Voice." and "You paid 180 G."; a replay that did not reach its step waits instead.
- **The save's log** (`story.replay_log {log, place}` at a load, `story.replay_log_save` for the save): steps by site with their claims by stream, the start place, the runs waiting for the player's own things and the runs handed to the player that wait to be taken (`story.replay_queue` as each is handed over, dropped once `story.replay_take` decides it, those `story.replay_due` names due again first) and the cursors, by stream the number of the last entry of a Raid World's track it took in (`RunLog.Cursors`, which each `sync` names to the relay, so it hands again what a save never kept), as a Ruby Hash of version 2; a load answers the runs to take.
- **Between games** (`ReplayWire`, topics `story.replay_wire` and `story.replay_unwire`): an entry crosses to another game as one text, JSON deflated in base64, which the scripts carry and never read; the receiver names its opener.
- **Entries with no opener run** (`ReplayEntry.Derive`, topic `story.replay_derive {site or chest, closes, party}`): a page the player's own history or a feature names (a Raid world chest the catch-up finds, Makina by level, the Classic key-item catch-up), with its id `derived:<site>`, its stream `derived`, its step the keys that close it and, of those, the world and work keys read as off from its start values. A chest's page is the one that closes it (`PageMatch.ChestPage`: events.db `chests` for a self switch, the first map page turning a switch on).
- **What gives** (events.db schema 3): the table `gives` lists each map page and common event that, itself or through a common event it calls, adds to what a player holds (gold, items, weapons, armors, party members, experience, levels, skills, classes, and script gains such as `gain_medal`); `choice_branches` holds each Show Choices answer's command range and what it does to holdings. `story_keys.rbx` logs every run that changed a party or world key with `story.replay_ran`, naming its stream: the Raid track (`world` or `t<n>`), or the Classic leader's `story:<id>`, and hands the entry on (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.hand_entry` for a Raid world entry, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.hand_entry` otherwise). The DLL makes the entry when the run changed a party key or its page gives (`PageMatch.Gives`: `gives`, or a written key whose live scope is the player's or a party's). At take time the replay answer's `inputs` leave out the answers of choices whose answers differ in the receiver's own keys or holdings (`PageMatch.IsReceivers`), so the replay asks them.
- Tests: `KeyScopesTests`, `PageMatchTests`, `RunLogTests`, `ReplayNoticeTests`, `SaveRepairTests`, `PartyRewardsTests` and `StoryReplayTopicsTests`.

### The write hook (`story_keys.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpStoryKeys` follows every write of `$game_switches`, `$game_variables` and `$game_self_switches` (after `[]=` of each, the game's own objects alone). The teller's scene mirroring (`MGQ_MpCoopScene.story_value`) takes each first; then the story that records (`recording`: `:raid` while a Raid World's story applies, `:classic` while the player leads a Classic party whose members follow their story) looks the key up, a byte of the table for a switch or variable, so a frame of about 1,900 writes (common event 154, the Final Chapter's reset, `MAX_BATCH` 8,000) costs no call of the DLL per write.

- **Recorded**: a Raid World's write of a key the mapping keeps or a chest's (`REQUESTED`), at the value a frozen raid boss fight holds it at (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.held_value`); a Classic leader's write of a key their party shares (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_key?`). Writes outside the game's events are requests too (the catch-up's raises, affection from gifts and battles, the found items' marks).
- **Where it came from** (`site_of`): the interpreter running a command (`executing`, before `Game_Interpreter#execute_command`, `executed` after), its common event by its list (`ce:<id>`), its map event's page by its list (`map:<map>:<event>:<page>`), the troop's battle event (`game:troop`), else `game:event`; outside every interpreter `game`, or `game:<what>` inside `as(what)`. Each interpreter keeps its own stack of running commands, swapped in around its `update` (`updating`), since a wait inside a command leaves the update with the command still running.
- **Never recorded**: the multiplayer's own writes (`applying`: the relay's values written into the story, a teller's values a viewer takes, the opening's setup, the difficulty's rates), and the writes while a lost battle's defeat runs (a novel scene, as after a lost story battle, or `MGQ_MpRaidFreeze.holding_writes?`), which sets back what its event set before the battle.
- **Runs** (`run_set_up` after `Game_Interpreter#setup`, `run_ended` after `#run`): each start of a map page or of a common event set up outside every command (never a troop's, nor a list a script makes) opens a run while the open world names its scopes (`scopes`, `story.replay_scopes`, a byte per switch and variable, then chests' self switches by name and the self rules as `class_of` finds them). Before each write the run notes a party, world or work key's value before its first write (`before_write`), and every open run notes a work or world key's value before anything wrote it, a value the relay brought included (its start value); a script's reads are noted while `command_355` or a script's branch runs (`reading_script`). Inputs are noted by command, `<site>@<index>` with the place `executing` kept: a choice's answer (the handler of `Game_Message#choice_proc=`), the extended choice, a number or item input by the command that set the message up to wait for it, a text before it too (`num_input_variable_id=`, `item_choice_variable_id=`), with the answer its window wrote once the message closes (`clear`), a present, `rand`, a key held, the keys `input_keys` read, the map at a place, a battle's outcome (the handler `BattleManager.event_proc=` sets, wrapped there, since command 301 waits for the battle inside itself), and what a branch or a variable reads of the timer or a character. Resets (a range of switches or variables written at once, `Game_SelfSwitches#delete`, a new `Game_SelfSwitches`) go to `story.replay_reset`. A run that closed a chest (it changed a party key, or a world chest's key the list names) goes to `story.replay_ran`, while the story's own runs go on by value until the story step, with the start values of the keys `story.replay_reads` names; a borrowing guest keeps the party keys it changed in their own story (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.keep_own`), and an entry the DLL answers goes to the party (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.hand_entry`). An end the DLL cannot log before the list came is tried again each second (`send_unsent`). A run also closes, with its interpreter's wait, once the map lets its interpreter go (`let_go` after `Game_Map#update`, over what `hold` noted at each setup holds an interpreter of the map: the map, a map event or a parallel common event): a loaded save brings its own map, a transfer makes the events and parallel common events anew, and a new page or a common event switched off drops an interpreter, whose list never goes on.
- **Once a frame** (`flush`, before `Graphics.update`): a Raid World's writes go to the DLL as one `story.writes`, a Classic leader's to `MGQ_MpCoopStory.recorded`. A loaded save or a new game drops what waited (`drop`). `request` adds a value of the player's own outside the game's writes, such as a frozen fight's key.

### The replayer (`story_replay.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpStoryReplay.play(entry, options)` plays a run handed over (Runs handed over by replay, above) in the receiver's own game. The entry names its site (`map:<map>:<event>:<page from 1>` or `ce:<id>`), the world and work keys' start values as the tracks write them and the opener's inputs `[at, occurrence, kind, value]`; the option `:view` tells which keys are world or work keys (the open world's kept keys by default), `:own_write` is called with each write of the receiver's own keys (a borrowing guest's `keep_own`, and in a Raid World a chest key's request to the relay, which keeps it with the player), and `:page` is the page the receiver's game picked. It answers a `Replay` that is done, failed or asking.

- **Interpreter** (`MGQ_MpStoryReplay::Interpreter < Game_Interpreter`): runs the page's real list (read with `load_data(DataManager.map_file_name(map))`) bound to its map and event: `setup` keeps the run's map, `same_map?` holds, `command_117` and a novel scene's common event (`call_novel_scene`) run in an interpreter bound alike, and a script that makes a `Game_Interpreter` makes this one (the constant is this class inside its scripts' scope). Every method of the game's interpreter a hook wraps runs the game's own body (`MGQ_MpHooks.plain`), so neither the story's write hook nor the party's events, gathering, gates, difficulty, freeze or telling see a replay.
- **The view**: while the replay's fiber runs, `$game_switches`, `$game_variables` and `$game_self_switches` are stand-ins (`Keys`); a world or work key goes to the view, which starts at the entry's start values and is dropped at the end, any other key to the receiver's own object. Nothing is put back: the receiver's own things keep what the replay did, a world value that arrives while the replay waits stays, and v57 set before a transfer never reaches the save. An object of keys the event makes anew (CE 153's `Game_SelfSwitches.new`) becomes the receiver's own, and `reset_selfswitch_map` resets both layers. The replay notes the receiver's own keys it read (`reads`) and every write (`writes`, with whether the view took it).
- **Closed**: the screen and the message are scratch objects; the player, the followers, the map's events and the timer are stand-ins that never move, show or start (a move route on them runs at once with only its switch commands and scripts taking effect); the map's display, scroll, tileset, parallax, battleback and refresh do nothing; `CLOSED_CODES` and `CLOSED_FUNCTIONS` do nothing (presentation and the opener's play: transfers, access settings, shops, name input, menus, saving, the title, vehicles boarded and the like); while a replay runs, the sound, `SceneManager`'s moves and `BattleManager.process_defeat` are shut (`CLOSED_CALLS`, through `core_hooks.rbx`). The medals the replay gains leave the popup for the notice (`medals`).
- **Inputs**: by the command that read them, `<site>@<index>` (`input_at`, the place the command started at), and their kind: a message's answer as the message window would give it (choice, extended choice, number, item, present), a random draw (`rand`), a key held, the keys `input_keys` read, the map at a place (285), a battle's outcome (301: 0 won), and reads of the player, an event, the timer or the map. A choice the record lacks is asked: the replay stops `asking` with the question's command, kind, labels, answers and lines, swaps the stand-ins out, and runs on with `answer`; a number, item or present the record lacks stops the replay (`stop`), which ends as a check that fails, so its run waits. A battle without a record counts as won; any other input the record lacks reads the receiver's own game.
- **Taking the runs handed to the player** (`receive`, `update` after the map's update): they wait in order and are taken one at a time while the player stands free on the map and no PvP battle runs. `story.replay_take` decides by rule 2, asking for the player's own values it lacks (`own_value`: keys, `item<id>`, `actor<id>`, `holdings`); a replay starts with what the player held before (`holdings`: items, gold, the party's companions and skills) and the step's keys, and `story.replay_done` answers the one notice from what they hold after, the medals the replay took off the popup included. A question the record lacks is shown on the choices' screen (`MGQ_MpCoopChoices.ask`) and the stream waits for the answer.
