Every two seconds `story.replay_due` names the waiting runs due again; `holdings` sums up what the player holds in one number, since a script's check of the party, such as a chest's lock (`unlock_level < 1`), reads no key. The save keeps the DLL's log in `$game_system` (`saving` before `make_save_contents`, `loaded` after `extract_save_contents`, `new_game`) with the runs that wait to be taken, so a loaded save takes back its own, and a new game or a world that closes (`forget`, called by `world.rbx`) none.
- **Gate** (generator, `Build/ReplayChannels.cs`): every command code and every script root (`Build/ScriptRoots.cs`: a function of the interpreter, a global or constant with the member it uses, an instance variable) of the map pages and common events is classed by the channels it touches in the game's scripts (State, Presentation, the opener's Play, Input), or the build fails; the keys a closed command writes (`ClosedWrites`: the transfer's v57, script 148) must be work keys of the reviewed list (`Problems`). `story_replay_test.rb` checks that `CLOSED_CODES` and `CLOSED_FUNCTIONS` are what the gate classes as presentation or play alone.
- **All or nothing** (`committing`, `finish`): a replay runs against the view (world and work keys) and a stage for the receiver's own things: own switches, variables and self switches it writes are staged and logged in order, and the party, actors, library and map vehicles are swapped for one copy of all four, made the way a save copies them. It commits only when it ends done and wrote one of the entry's keys (an entry without keys once done): the logged key writes go through the real objects, `own_write` per write, and the holdings merge field by field (untouched fields keep the receiver's value, numbers both sides changed add the replay's difference, id lists that changed size take the replay's additions and removals, other changed fields take the replay's value). A replay that stops, fails, raises or never reaches its step leaves the save unchanged; `story.replay_done` then reports `reached` false and the DLL holds the run pending for a fresh retry. The notice compares holdings sampled just before and after the commit. A number, item or present the record lacks is asked through `MGQ_MpCoopChoices.ask_input(opener, lines, kind, spec, done)` with spec `{digits, value}`, `{items: [[id, name, count]]}` or `{actor, items}`, listed as the game's windows list them; without it the replay stops.
- Tests: `story_replay_test.rb` over an interpreter that runs its commands as scripts 042, 148 and 167 do; `hooks_test.rb` for `plain`; `DerivedRowsTests` and `RealGameTests` for the gate.

### In the game (`world_story.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpWorldStory` follows the story after the map's update (`tick`), behind menus and battles too, while a Raid World the directory knows is open (`active?`); the world's id is its `directory_id`.

- **Entering**: a new world, a loaded save or a new game stage `story.enter`; until the relay's first track was written into the player's story the player's story events wait, ten seconds at most (`awaits_world?`, `AWAIT_WORLD_FRAMES`; `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.awaits_world_story`). The scripts hand the seed the relay asks for (`send_seed`, again after `SEED_FRAMES` while it still asks).
- **Writing what the relay handed** (`take_pending`, `apply`): once the player is free (`quiet?`: no event of their own, battle, PvP battle or transfer) and no lost raid boss battle's defeat runs (`holding?`), the scripts take what waits (`story.take`). A new game's opening that a whole track is past runs its setup first (`world_opening.rbx`, `before_take` with the track's 1001: `:wait` until it ran). A whole track is written over every key the mapping keeps (`lay_over`): each kept switch and variable at the track's value, off or 0 where the track never wrote one, every self switch the mapping keeps alike, the keys in `held` left as they are, then the player's chest flags. Then the keys pushed or answered, in order (`put_key`): a switch or self switch past the game's own handling, a variable through `[]=` (the game writes some through to its global system, as affection), each only when it differs; a key a frozen raid boss fight holds at its held value; a local step the player plays where it stays theirs (the Queen's Cup's tournament steps in its waiting room, `local_step?`) stays theirs; a key the whole world keeps going only up whose own value is higher (affection, `kept_higher?`) stays, and goes to the relay again, since the relay keeps the highest; an unlock the player holds that the track hands off (the second accessory slot 445, `MGQ_MpStoryState::UNLOCK_SWITCHES`, `kept_unlock?`) stays on and goes to the relay too, as a world an earlier build kept it each player's own in (`set_raw` refits every character). Frozen fights' keys go to `world_freeze.rbx` (`take_keys`).
- **Following it**: a whole track tells the mode (`:world` on the world's track, `:behind` on one of the player's own; "You play the world's story." once). The catch-up hears of a move on (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.moved`) and what the story carried the player past (`carried`, with the story before) while the mode is still the one the player came from, then of a fall behind (`behind`, the notice once per world). Then: the scene switches left on while no scene runs go off (`end_left_scenes`, the mapping's scene switches, not while a teller's scene shows them); the choices the story carried the player past and the Great Decision's route (`followed`, `MGQ_MpCoopChoices`); the personal half of the world's return to the Great Decision when the world finished and left the route the player was on (`returned`, played once free by `play_queued`, out of a scene of the player's own first, `MGQ_MpCoopScene::Own.leave`, such as their route's ending walked in a temporary party); and the route another player took first (a route variable's request refused `route`, `finished` or `closed`, `refused`): the companions the player's own branch of the decision took away come back (`restore_roster`), the notice names the world's route and the player teleports to the story.
- **The place's bans** (switches 100 and 94, each player's own): a teller's scene still sends both to its viewers, who stand where the teller does (`MGQ_MpCoopScene.place_ban?`), and one still on the way takes them on arrival (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.take_place_ban`). Whether event commands may move the world's story on, which a viewer's follow asks, reads the values that do (`moves_on?`: `PROGRESS_VARIABLES`, `PROGRESS_SWITCHES` and the mapping's one-way variables; `progress_now`, `progress_ahead?` up to `PROGRESS_CALL_DEPTH` common events deep).
- **Tellings**: the relay hears the player's telling from the DLL's telling machine (`TellingMachine` of `MGQOnline.Game`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)) as it starts (`tell` with the site of the map's interpreter, which a telling started by a write outside events lacks: then none), as it goes on on another map and as it ends. **Watching** (`note_teller`): the seat of the teller whose scene the player watches (`story.watch`). Once a telling on the player's track ended, the player's story events wait (`awaits_teller?`, the status's `await`: the teller's seat while the DLL holds the track's revision the telling ended at and the game has not taken it, `StoryTrack.AwaitingSeat`), since the scene would play again before, `AWAIT_TELLER_FRAMES` (10 s) at most from the status that named that telling, so a take that stays held never keeps them for good. The player's track goes in their state (`trk`, `state_fields`), which tells a teller who views; the tellings the relay told of on the track (`telling_of`) tell whom the player watches (see [Party or map](#party-or-map-coop_scoperbx)).
- **The level** (`send_level`, every `CHECK_FRAMES`): the highest base level among the player's characters and the highest level the game lets that character reach (`max_level(:base)`, another mod's cap), which the relay's level gates read.
- **The world's summary** (`world`): the status's `rev`, `part`, `route`, `done`, `open` and `checkpoints`, the shared companions (`comps`, fetched as the world opens, after a load or a new game, once a minute and on the relay's push of the keyed story's change, `story_rev`) and the level caps the player's story beat (`caps`, `MGQ_MpRaidPool.beaten_caps`, for the catch-up experience). The relay's tracks keep no story endpoint, so a teleport to the story goes where this game last saw it told (`endpoint` is nil, `MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_endpoint`).
- **The Great Decision** (`before_command`, `decision_list`, `decision_routes` by the summary's `open`, the routes the relay grants: the relay alone keeps the route rules), **the return** (`reset_list`, `route_credits?`, `return_list`, `return_commands`), the endings (`world_ending`, `Game_Interpreter#ssw`), and the collab scenario (`collab_refused?`, `leave_cleared_collab`) work as before: the first route a write takes locks the world's, the relay refuses another.
- **Saves** hold the story as it is.

### Catch-up (`world_catchup.rbx`)

**One look at everything** (`audit`): on entering the world, after a load or a new game (`forget` sets `@audit_due`) and on every level up (`level_up`, after `Game_Actor#level_up`), the next free check looks at everything the story owes the player once, whatever the rows' notes say: the story's skills Luka lacks (`missing_skills`; of the world's story only while the player plays it, so a player behind learns none past their part's checkpoint, never one waiting for its gate), the key items (`restock_key_items`), the story's companions missing from the roster of the joins whose event ran and no later removal took back, who wait for their gate (`missing_companions`, then `bring_waiting`), the story values the companions in the roster stand for (`companion_values`, also after every catch-up join in `bring_in`: the `companion_values` rule, Amira 540 with her information network, variable 1009, at 2 at least, since a companion the world shared comes without what their own join set, and her information in the Pocket Castle stays shut below it; the raised value goes to the relay as the player's request), the player's own values the story sets (`settle`), a warp ban left where warping is free (`lift_warp_ban`) and the vehicles (`check_vehicles`): one no event behind the story put on a map yet leaves it (`park_vehicle`, which leaves one the player rides until they got off, `got_off_early?`), and one the story gave that stands on no map comes back where a new game parks it, the start `$data_system` names for it (`return_vehicle`: Galda on Field map 2 at 186,1, the ship at 306,2; the boat has none and stays away), never one the player rides. `carried` also has the next check look at the vehicles, so a checkpoint or the world's story past Galda's event written into the player's brings it back. The skills, the values and the vehicles go by the world's story only while the player plays it (`played_world_story`); the key items and the companions go by it while the player is behind too.

**The player's own values the story sets** (`settle`, also on every review a story carried the player through): `SETTINGS` lists every setting of the game over's return point (1002) and the random encounters (command 136) by an event that moves the story on, beats a Level Cap milestone boss, as Adramelech's return point (19) whose game over would send the player into the Administrator's Tower, or plays a scene of the story by itself or on touch and says something (`raid_data.rb`'s `scene?`), as the arrival in Grangold Castle (33), which a player whose world's story ran it never runs, never by the game over's own map (154), the new game's setup first, each at its place, those of one step ordered by how far into it their marks lie (`depth`). Of each value, the latest row the player's story passed tells it, or the world's while the player plays it (never while they are behind); of the routes only the rows of the route under way count, none at the Great Decision, since a finished route's rows, which `passed?` counts for its rewards, would set its last return point; where the player still holds what an earlier row set, as after the opening's setup or a story written over theirs skipped the events, it takes the latest row's. Where that latest row lies on a step whose regions the world plays in an order of its own (the rules' `free_order`: the Chaos route's step 21 after the Pocket Castle's revival), the rows cannot tell which region came last, so the value stays as it is. A variable of the player's own, as the return point, is set only on the map of the event that sets it, never by a common event's row (`at_site?`), since a return point the world's story set elsewhere could send the player into another player's scene with no way out. A value of the player's own making, such as a church's return point, stays, and so do the encounters on a map whose events turn them on or off. The warp ban (switch 100) follows places instead, set on the way into dungeons and scenes and lifted on the way out: `WARP_FREE_MAPS` lists the maps the game sends the player to with it lifted more often than set, as the field, where `lift_warp_ban` lifts one left on at a look at everything; a transfer on a map from 1000 on names a map of its own thousand there (`raid_data.rb`'s `transfer_target`).

`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup` carries a player behind a Raid World's story along. What the story owes them is decided in the DLL by `CatchupEngine` of `MGQOnline.Game` (`CatchupLook`, whose methods are named as the script's were: `MissingSkills` for `missing_skills` below) over the catch-up's tables the relay keeps beside the story mapping (Relay/README.md "Catch-up tables", the rows that replaced the scripts' generated tables and the rules, counts and companion values that were constants of the script); the catch-up waits until the mapping came. The script asks once per revision of the mapping which keys, items and parts the engine reads (`keys`, `catchup.keys`); every `CHECK_FRAMES` while the player is free (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.quiet?`), after the map's update (`tick`), it hands a snapshot (`snapshot`: those switches, variables and self switches of the player's story and the world's last told one, the roster with base levels and caps, the counts of those items, the worn items, the job and race items, the map, the world's summary and sharing) to `catchup.check` (`look`, with `audit` or `vehicles` when due), and whenever `world_story.rbx` writes a story into the player's (`carried`, `look` "carried" with the story before). The answer is a list of actions the script carries out with the game's own grants in their order (`run`, `act`: give, learn, teach, class, join, raise, form, leave, change, set, switch, open, setting, vehicles, trim, makina, log, gifts), the companions on a visit, the side to ask, the orbs' event and the companions the world's list takes. The engine owns the save's notes (`ledger`): the script hands them after a load (`hand_ledger`, `loaded`) and keeps them in `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_catchup` whenever an answer brings them, so the save carries them.

- **Behind** (`behind`, from `world_story.rbx` as the relay puts the player on a track of their own): the relay keeps their part's checkpoint as that track, the world as it was right before the part's ending, which `world_story.rbx` writes into their story; the save notes the part (the notes' `checkpoint`), cleared once the world's story is written into theirs (`world_told`). While behind, the relay holds only what would move the player's story past the end of their part (`held`), and hands the world's keys the whole world keeps alone: the world's key items and chests come by the player's own track (`@world_told` is nil).
- **Story held** (`holds_story?`, asked by `coop_events.rbx`): while behind, and from a save's load or the world's entry until the relay's track came when the save notes the player behind in their part (`noted_behind?`), only a story event that would move the player's story past the end of their part is held (issue #48). Before it starts, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.leaving_part` asks the DLL (`story.leaves_part`, again per list and part only once the values the DLL named changed) whether its page, through the common events it calls and every branch counted, and the chain it starts across maps (`EventChain`: each map a Transfer Player names by a constant, in the folder of the map whose event runs it as the game resolves it (`GameDataPaths.TransferMap`), once, and the page each of its events that runs by itself would show there, the last whose conditions hold now or once the chain so far ran, on to the maps those pages name), sets a variable the part follows (1001 and the routes' 1141 to 1143) that the mapping keeps, to a value that puts the story in another part: the relay's `held` rule, in `PartEnd` of `MGQOnline.Game`. A value known only as the event runs, or a part the relay has yet to name, counts as leaving; a variable each player keeps as their own never does. So a part's ending on a cutscene map without exits, such as the Middle Chapter's epilogue on map 750, holds the event that starts its chain, Alice's final battle on map 138, on a map the player can leave. Such an event does not start and tells them "Reach level N to continue the story." (`refusal_text`, once per event in `REFUSE_FRAMES`, `held_behind?`); such story that runs by itself (a map event's autorun, a common event's) waits silently, so a teleport onto its map cannot loop; a talk turning into story ends there by the same rule (`guard`). Every other story event, such as Promestein's race machine or a companion's talk, and side events, talks, shops, chests and battles run.
- **Moving on** (`moved`, from `world_story.rbx` with the relay's `move`): the relay moves the player on by the level `world_story.rbx` tells it (its gate, or another mod's cap below it: `by` `cap`, which the gate's notices name as the level to reach, `level_to_reach`), to the next part the world played that has a checkpoint, or into the world's track; the notice says "Level N reached: catching up to ...". A move at a cap below the gate notes the gate in the notes' `capped` (`note_capped`) while the real level is below it, the carried review of the story the move writes into theirs then notes each `REWARDS` row it passes in `waits` (row key to gate) instead of giving it (`rewards_wait?`), and the next story written into theirs on the world's track clears `capped` (`drop_capped`). `give_waiting_rewards` gives the waiting rows at every look once the real level reaches their gate, so a later load does too. A player leaving a route the world finished first gets the personal half of the world's return to the Great Decision (`return_from_route`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.returned`). Only the gate counts a cap: companions and Makina wait for the real level (`reached?`, `gate_open?` for the Unlocks page).
- **Rows by story position** (`review`, `passed?`): a row counts once the story is in the row's part or a later one and the row's marks hold, so an optional event nobody played is never past; a route's rows also once the route is finished (its clear switch), whatever part the story is in, since the return clears its marks; side content by its marks alone. The row's side (before the Great Decision the side the player chose then, noted in the save's notes, `note_side`) and the choices' outcomes among its marks (`MGQ_MpCoopChoices.personal?`) are always read from the player's own story. Each row counts once per save (`ledger`, `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_catchup`, per world). A row the player's own game played gives nothing, since the game gave it; one a story written into theirs carried them past (`carried`: what was passed before is noted done first) gives what it gives, as long as its marks tell that its event ran (`carries?`, the row's `ran`; one only there to play stays the player's own to play, and is noted done once their own story is past it): the story's skills and items (`REWARDS`), with the prizes the game hands out once by a count outside the story's range (`PRIZE_VARIABLES`: the Reaper's Battle Challenge 87, the Colosseum 701-728, Survival 83; `raid_data.rb`'s `scan_prizes`; and the battlefucks' progress 801-858 and wins 907) or by a switch (the relay's `prize_switches`: the Medal King's one-of-a-kind exchanges 2324-2327 and 2336), rows of no part that also give when the player's own story got past one with none of it in their bag (`unclaimed_prize?`), as the world's count written into theirs before these rows existed; the companions a first playthrough loses for good (`REMOVALS` with `first`, variable 912 at 0) leave, unless a join of the same look comes after each of those removals or at the same place (`after?`: a later place, or later in the same event's list); temporary leaves only order the joins. Choices the story carried the player past go through the screen of `coop_choices.rbx` as before (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.followed`).
- **Key items and story chests for everyone** (`world_items`): every key item row (`KEY_ITEMS`, given or taken) and story chest (`CHESTS`) the world's last story is past (`@world_told`, the player's own story while they play the world's) comes to every player once. A story chest is played in the player's own game (action `replay`, `MGQ_MpStoryReplay.derive` with the chest's key, "Issue 73 chest effects.md"): the page that closes it runs from the player's own values with its key read as shut, so it gives what it gives them, medals and chest flags included, its lock checks their party, and a chest they cannot unlock waits until something of theirs changed; one the game shuts by a switch instead of a self switch, as the Land-Dweller's Sealed Orb (switch 2719), is keyed by that switch. The rows include the story's own pages that wait for a companion of the player's roster as well as the story's progress, such as Alice handing over the Pocket Castle once Lime joined (map 2, event 5), whose self switch the world's story carries, so a player who never had Lime gets the castle here. A row the story passed only takes what it takes; what the rows give comes from `restock_key_items`, every review: of each key item a row gives that the player's story or the world's is past, on the player's side, and that no later row took back, the most such a row gives, less what the player holds; so a row a newer table adds after the player's story passed it still gives, and a key item that went missing comes back. A row with `ran` false, whose event a global flag may use up for the world, counts the same. The ores (`[Material]` items: 151-158 and 943-947) are key items in the tables, since the blacksmiths and Papi's smithy only ask the player to hold one; a chest that also turns on chest flags (the Chaos forge's "Carrying Moonbow Crystal" 7102 to "Carrying Final Ore" 7104, each player's own) turns them on as it plays. A player in the world's story who then holds every orb of Part 2 (`ORBS`) below 1001 = 32 plays common event 330 once they are free (`check_orbs`, `play_orb_event`), which only an orb's own event calls.
- **Counts** (`check_counts`, every check while free): each variable of the `counts` rule is set to how many of its switches are on, the Randolphs found (1354) to the Randolph switches 3101-3180. Their events add 1 to the variable, so two players who each found one before either story reached the other kept one addition alone; the count laid right goes to the relay as the player's request, where a `count` row keeps the count itself.
- **Makina by level** (`check_makina`, every check while free): every Makina (`MAKINA`) comes once the real level `reached?` its level, the area level of the easiest place that gives it (the highest enemy level of the random encounters there, by the region on a field map, else of the nearest maps the place's transfers lead to, else of the fights there; at least the Level Cap's cap where a gift waits for the story, as San Ilia's laboratory's later gifts); a cap below it counts nothing. It comes by playing its chest (the row's `site`) in the player's own game, so the chest closes and 2045 comes along; a Makina the player holds plays none, and a chest whose lock their party cannot open waits.
- **Job and race items any player got** (`gained`, after `Game_Party#gain_item`): an item whose description starts `[Job]` or `[Race]` (`UNLOCK_ITEM_TAG`) a player of a Raid World gains, or holds on a look (`audit`, `note_held_items`), turns on the self switch `[FOUND_MAP, item, FOUND_LETTER]` (map 0, which no event reads), a request like any other write, which the mapping shares by its `ss0.*.F` row; every check gives each such item noted in the player's story or the world's last told one (`FoundIds`) that the player lacks, at once and wherever their story stands, a player behind included, and trims copies beyond the first, since a battle's drop finds one too and no run hands that over, while the replay of a run that found one gives it again. Their chests are world chests (`CHESTS`, which `raid_data.rb` fills with the chests of key items and of these items, the job orbs' pedestals of map 1650 among them), so one another player opened shows open.
- **Classes and races** (`missing_class_levels`): every `CLASS_LEVELS` row (a `set_class_level(actor, class, level, ...)` script of a story or quest event, such as Luka's Lowly Angel in the Forest of Spirits) the player's story or the world's is past gives its character the class at its level (`MGQ_MpStoryState.give_class`, the game's own `Game_Interpreter#set_class_level` with `change` false, so the class and race played stay) while the character is Luka or in the roster and below that level. Like the companion skills it goes by the character's levels, so earlier steps and later joins count; both run in `check_growth`, every `GROWTH_CHECKS` checks and at the next check after the roster changed, as the tables hold thousands of rows.
- **Companion skills** (`missing_lessons`): every `LESSONS` row (a skill a story, side or companion quest event teaches a companion; `raid_data.rb` leaves out a skill an event takes away again) the player's story or the world's is past teaches its companion the skill (`MGQ_MpStoryState.teach`) while the companion is in the roster and lacks it. It goes by what each companion knows, not by the notes, so a step passed before an update and a companion who joins later both count.
- **Power-ups** (`actor_changes`, `change_actors`, every review): every `ACTOR_CHANGES` row is what one story or quest event changes of one character by the game's script calls (`persona_change`, `level_adjust`, `change_actor_ability`, `force_change_equip`, `change_actor_equip_stone`, `set_actor_had`; `raid_data.rb` joins a script's continuation lines, so Ilias's abilities read whole), such as Alice's power-up end (map 1330, event 15: persona 7, level 300 in the game), Ilias's (map 1574, event 28: her abilities, level 312 in the game), Black Alice's tower (map 1382, event 4: level 200, six worn items and their stones) or Alipheese the 9th (map 1798, event 23: persona 741, level 212). Each call notes whether only a first playthrough runs it (inside the event's branch on variable 912 at 0), a level it sets is held at `MAX_GATE` (250, as every join's gate), and the row's gate is the highest of those levels. Luka's rows are left out, whose personas follow the scenes, and so are the calls of a choice the player makes. A row the player's own game played is noted done; one a story carried them past (`carries?`) waits in the notes with its gate (and the cap's gate after a move a cap opened), and runs once the real level `reached?` it: each call through a new `Game_Interpreter` (`ACTOR_CALLS` alone), the first playthrough's only while variable 912 is 0, none whose change the character shows already (`shows?`: the persona taken, the level reached, the item worn, the stones set, the abilities equipped), whether or not the character is in the roster, as the game's event does. Once per save, so a persona the player picks again later stays. Medals stay each player's own.
- **No gift notices** (`tell_gifts`): what the catch-up gives is logged, not told, as the Unlocks page shows it; the notices of the level gate, joins, leaves and held joins stay.
- **Companions by level** (`companions`, `note_join`, `bring_waiting`): a join the player's story is past waits for its gate (notes `c` carried, `o` held back) unless its companion is there or the player's own game left them out. Once the player's real level reaches the gate (`reached?`, a cap below it counts nothing) the companion joins as the game's own join does (`bring_in`: `persona_change`, `recover_all`, `add_actor`, standby by the game's own cut when the team is full, `add_stand_actor` while a story's temporary party plays), raised to the gate unless the join is a first one at the start level; a companion there already is raised to a carried join's gate and takes the join's persona when it is another than their main one (`take_form`), as Ilias of the roster takes her persona 27 at Ilias's power-up, which `add_actor_ex` changes in the game's own event; a join of the main persona keeps a persona an actor change gave before its gate (`keeps_form?`). Never twice (`in_roster?`; the roster is read once per look, `roster_view`). A removal passed after a join waited drops it. A story event of the player's own that hands out a companion during their telling before the gate of that companion's join in the player's part (`hold_join?`, around `Game_Party#add_actor` and `add_stand_actor`) is held back, notice "<name> joins you once you reach level N.", and the join waits for its gate.
- **Shared companions** (`MGQ_MpWorld.companion_sharing`): every companion new in the player's roster (`note_roster`) goes to the world's list (`mp_raid_companions_add`, at most one write per `SHARE_FRAMES`): the story's companions with *Story companions*, every one with *battle recruits* too. Each companion on the list (`comps` of `MGQ_MpWorldStory.world`) joins a player who lacks it on reaching its gate (`share_in`): a story companion at its first join's gate and persona, a battle recruit at its database start level; never one the player had (in the roster, or past one of their joins in the player's story that the catch-up carries, `joined?`; a talk the story only walked past, such as Pilate's, gave nothing), nor one their story took away for good (a lasting removal passed on a first playthrough, `taken_for_good`). The notes keep `SHARE_SETTLED` per companion once it joined or never will; an earlier build's `true` is looked at once more.
- **History before the log** (`CatchupLook.BeforeLog`): the rows give only the history before the world's log: a row gives when the world's story where its log starts was past it by its marks, side content without a step too, and a world chest when its opened key was on there (the relay's state hands those keys as `log`, the ones that differ from the track's; `world_story.rbx` keeps the story as `log_story` and the snapshot hands the DLL its values as "log"; where the world's story started, or where it stood when the relay first kept run entries). Later history comes by replaying the world's track. Once a player's story finished a route, every story companion the roster lacks has their recruit page replayed once at the join's gate (`CatchupLook.MissedCompanions`, a fight counts as won), unless the companion setting is Off. The new build ignores chest flags; the relay still serves them to released builds.
- Notices go to the notification box (`MGQ_MpNotices.message`). A loaded save or new game forgets the session's requests (`loaded`); the notes stay in the save.

### Catch-up experience (`world_catchup_exp.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpWorldCatchupExp` wraps `Game_Actor#gain_exp(exp, cexp)`, which the game's `BattleManager.gain_exp` calls for each character and EXP mods such as EXP Overlord call with their own share, so it grows after them. Experience given another way (items, events) stays. The rule is `CatchupExperience` in `MGQOnline.Game` (see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)); the script asks it through the topics `catchup.target`, `catchup.grow` and `catchup.factor` (`RuleTopics` in the DLL). In a Raid World's story (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.active?`) the target is the second highest of `BaseCap` (5) and the `caps` of `MGQ_MpWorldStory.world`, those the player's own story beat; a character below it gains `min(MaxFactor, 1 + (MaxFactor - 1) * (target - base_level) / ((1 - FullShare) * target))` times its base and its job and race experience, rounded half away from zero as Ruby's `Float#round` (`MaxFactor` 10, `FullShare` 0.25: tenfold in the lowest quarter of the target, then an even fade to 1 at it), each character by its own base level. The fade follows the target, since a fixed number of levels per step kept a late world's characters at tenfold for 159 levels and barely helped an early one. Each grown gain is logged. The Unlocks page's head lists the formation's factors (`exp_cells` in `world_overview_pages.rbx`), rounded to hundredths as Ruby 3.3's `Float#round(2)`.

### Affection

The game keeps affection (variable 3000 + companion, `NWConst::Var::ACTOR_REL_BASE`) in its global system, the highest of all saves. In a Raid World each companion's affection is a `one_way` key with `wholeWorld` of the story mapping: the highest any player of the world reached, players behind included. Every write of it is the player's request, from gifts and battles too, which raise it outside events; the relay keeps the highest, and a value it hands below the player's own stays the player's and goes to the relay again (`kept_higher?`), so the world converges on each companion's highest. Written into the story through `$game_variables[]=`, it reaches the global system as the game's own raise does. Gift steps ("Presents", 4000 + companion) stay each player's own.

## Raid World difficulty (`world_difficulty.rbx`)

A Raid World takes no player's choice: its create form leaves out *Player's choice*, `Directory.create` never sends it, the relay refuses it (400, code `raid_start`), and `MGQ_MpSaveDistribution.ask_start?` says no for a Raid World even when its directory entry still names one. It may start from a shared save as a Classic world does, see [Starting save](#starting-save-world_save_distributionrbx). Instead the world sets a **difficulty** for every player, the game's own value of variable 902 (-2 VERY EASY, -1 EASY, 0 NORMAL, 1 HARD, 2 VERY HARD, 3 HELL, 4 PARADOX; `WorldDifficulty` in the DLL, which the scripts read through the topic `world.rules`: `MGQ_MpWorld.difficulties` and `difficulty_names`). The relay keeps it as the world's `difficulty`, set at create and changed by the creator or an admin with an edit; a Raid World made without one, and every Classic world, sets none, and its players keep their own. The list tells it at the end of the world's line, `World#describe` keeps it in world.ini as the world is entered, and `MGQ_MpWorld.difficulty` tells it for the open Raid World.

**Applying it.** Common event 112 works the enemies' rates (variables 41-49) out from variable 902, and the start of every battle runs it again (common event 4), after the troop's enemies were made, so `apply` sets 902 and runs 112 at once in an interpreter of its own, as the game's own choice (common event 110) does. It runs after `DataManager.load_game_without_rescue` and `setup_new_game`, and `tick` (after `Game_Map#update`, every 30 frames) puts the game back on the world's difficulty whenever 902 differs while the map is quiet and no battle runs: after the relay's push, and after a fight put back the difficulty it kept.

**The fights with values of their own.** The game sets values of its own for some fights, the same for every player, and keeps the difficulty they replace in variable 908: the Colosseum during a match (switches 28 and 87, common events 158 and 159: 908 = 902, 902 = NORMAL, and back; a defeat there, common event 3000, puts 902 back and turns 87 off but leaves 28 on until the next win or leave, so 28 counts only with 87), the special bosses on EASY and below (switch 23, common events 10 and 11), the Labyrinth of Chaos (switch 41: common event 112 calls 900, which works the rates out from the Labyrinth's level in 149 alone) and the final battles' fixed NORMAL (switch 507). While any of them is on, nothing here writes 902 or 908 or runs common event 112; the game's own restore (common events 11, 159, 3000) puts the difficulty back, and the next look then finds the world's. The Colosseum's survival course writes the rates itself between rounds, also under switch 28.

**The game's own change.** `before_command` replaces a call of common event 110 (the new game's choice, the choice after a defeat, and the Reaper's difficulty change in Hades, common event 146, which calls it and then erases the Reaper's picture 5, so it runs as the game has it) in a Raid World with a difficulty by setting 902 to the world's and calling 112, with the notice *The world sets the difficulty: HARD.*; while a fight has its own values set, by a comment only. A Raid World without a difficulty and every Classic world keep the game's choice. The Ruler Ruler (common event 100, variables 92-99, EASY and below only) stays each player's own.

**Live after an edit.** The relay answers an edit that names a difficulty with the world room's text frame `difficulty <n>` (`difficultyText`); this DLL (`WorldSession.TakeDifficulty`) hands it to the game script as the inbox entry `kind=difficulty` with `value=<n>`, released DLLs log it as unknown and drop it. `overworld_sync.rbx` hands it to the route of `DIFFICULTY_FIELD`, marked `:relay`; `pushed` keeps it in world.ini, says *The world plays on HELL now.* and the next look applies it. Players who are not in the world read it from the list as they enter.

**The story.** A Raid World's story would carry one player's values to everyone, so `MGQ_MpStoryState` makes them each Raid World player's own (`RAID_PERSONAL_SWITCHES`, `RAID_PERSONAL_VARIABLES`): switches 3 (the refusal of every recruit, on from the new game until Lime joins and during the Pocket Castle's collab scenario, which one player's collab would otherwise hand everyone), 23, 28, 41, 87 and 507, 2013 and 2015 (the Pocket Castle's tour on a player's first visit, which would otherwise count as story and take every viewer along into the castle), and variables 41-45 and 49 (the rates), 92-99 (the Ruler Ruler's), 121, 123, 149 and 151 (the Labyrinth's floors, area, level and tier), 902, 903 and 906 (the difficulty, the highest cleared and its check) and 908. Switch 506, the eased scaling the story turns on and off, stays the world's. So are the scene switches (`scene_switch?`, `MGQ_MpWorldCatchupData::SCENE_SWITCHES`, which `raid_data.rb` lists): story switches every event turns off again once it turned them on, such as "White Rabbit Emerged" (2029), which shows a character only while its scene runs; a story written while one ran would leave that character standing for everyone. A teller still sends them to the scene's viewers (`MGQ_MpCoopScene.scene_value?`), who put them back once they no longer see the scene (`restore`), and a take turns any left on off while no scene runs and the player watches none (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.end_left_scenes`). A Classic party's story is unchanged.

**Battles a player joins.** A guest builds the host's troop in its own game, whose enemies read the rates 41-45 and 48 (HP, attack, defense, agility, instant death resistance, MP) as their parameters are asked, so in a Raid World the host sends its own with the invite, the late invite and the answer `hot_ok` (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.rates_text`, field `rates`), and the guest fights with them (`borrow_rates`, before the troop is set up, also for a rival's or a followed battle) until the battle ends: `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.restore` gives them back (`give_back_rates`), with the level sync's stats, in an after hook on `BattleManager.battle_end`, since the game's victory saves its autosave right after `battle_end` and before `Scene_Battle#terminate`; `ended` calls it again, which puts nothing back twice, and `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.fight_own` gives them back when its own encounter starts alone, also when the wait for the running battle's roster ended as its host left or broke it off (`own_ending?`: `:late`, `:gone`, `:broken`). A special boss's NORMAL, the Colosseum's and the host's Labyrinth level are thus the same in every game of the battle. The rates of experience, job experience and gold (46, 49, 47) stay each player's own.

## Raid boss pools on the relay

Each story boss of a Raid World is one fight the whole world shares. Its pool at the relay keeps how much of the boss's HP is left, as a share of its raid max HP; every battle against the boss reports what it dealt and takes its HP from the pool, so the boss falls only once the world wore the pool down. The pool fills up again by 3% of its max a minute, also while battles fight it, and the battle that empties it defeats the boss for the world until an admin resets it. The relay side is `Relay/core/bosses.js` (the admin routes `Relay/core/raid_admin.js`), the DLL side `RaidBosses` (`Network/World/RaidBosses.cs`, `DirectoryClient.Bosses.cs`) behind the `mp_raid_boss*` exports (`Exports/Exports.RaidBosses.cs`). Classic worlds keep none.

This replaces the earlier pools in kills, where a won battle took a share of a kill off and the boss fell back, its event and the story put back, until the pool was empty. Nothing of that is left: no story, scene or party is put back, no event or autosave waits for the relay, and the relay answers an old body with 400.

**Where it is kept.** Beside the story in the world room's own Durable Object: one storage value per boss (`boss:<key>`), written only by a report that changes the pool and by an admin's reset; regeneration costs no writes, since every read computes the share left from the last report. Access is checked as for the story (`/internal/member`, kept by the world room, whose answer also names the player, `name`, for `by`), the admin routes ask the directory (`/internal/raid-admin`); deleting the world deletes its pools. The Node relay keeps them in memory or in the SQLite table `boss_pools`, as JSON.

**Pool.** Kept as `key`; `max`, the raid max HP the first report named, rounded to a whole number of at least 1 (each game's difficulty and mods set the boss's own max HP, so the first report's stands for the world); `left`, the share of `max` left at the last report, 0 to 1; `at`, that report's time; `defeated`; `by`, the name the world's members knew the player whose report emptied it by, and `byPlayer`, their id, both null while the boss stands; `battles`, the last 32 battles with each one's last `seq`, newest last; and `reports`. The share left now is `defeated ? 0 : min(1, left + 0.03 * (now - at) / 60000)` (`leftOf`, `BOSS_LIMITS.regenPerMinute`). Every answer's `left` is that share at the moment of the answer, so a game adds the regeneration from when the answer came and never reads `at`, which keeps the clocks of relay and game out of it. The key is the one the game sends (see *Keys* below), 1 to 72 characters without control characters or spaces at either end, `#` allowed, percent-encoded in the address (`#` as `%23`). A world keeps at most 500 pools; a record left from the pools in kills reads as never reported, counts toward none and is overwritten by the next report.

### Routes

The player routes take the player's key in `X-MGQ-Player` and the auth key in `X-MGQ-Auth`, with the story routes' refusals (404 no world, 400 missing key, 401 wrong auth key, 403 removed player, 404 code `classic`). Each answers JSON.

| Request | Body | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses` | | 200 `{"bosses": [*pool*, ...], "regenPerMinute": 0.03}`, the pools a report touched, by key |
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses/<key>` | | 200 *pool*; a pool never reported is full, `{"key": <key>, "left": 1, "max": null, "defeated": false, "at": null, "by": null}`; 400 for a key that is none |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/bosses/<key>/report` | `{"battle": "<id>", "seq": <n>, "delta": <share>, "max": <hp>, "killed": <bool>}`, at most 4 KB; `battle` 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`; `seq` a safe whole number of 0 or more, rising within the battle; `delta` the share of the max lost since the battle's last report, -1 to 1, negative for healing; `max` the reporting game's raid max HP, above 0 and at most 1e18; `killed` true, false, 0, 1 or left out | 200 `{"boss": *pool*, "repeat": <bool>}`; 400 for wrong fields, an old `{battle, dealt}` body too; 413 for a longer body and with code `full` past 500 pools; 429 with code `rate` past 30 reports at once, then two a second, per player |

**Pool** as answered: `key`, `left` (the share left now), `max`, `defeated`, `at` (the last report) and `by`.

**A report.** A `seq` at or below the battle's last one is a repeat: it answers the pool with `repeat: true` and writes nothing, so a report sent again after a lost answer counts once; once more than 32 newer battles reported on the pool, a resend from an older battle counts again. A report on a defeated boss, and one on an existing pool that takes nothing off (`delta` 0, no kill), change and write nothing either. Otherwise the first report makes the pool, full, with its `max`; `killed` empties it, any other report takes `delta` off the share left now, clamped to 0 to 1. A pool whose HP shows as 0 (`left * max` below 0.5) is empty: the report that empties it sets `defeated`, `by` and `byPlayer`, and the boss stays defeated until an admin resets it.

**Admin routes** (`ADMINS`): the admin's key in `X-MGQ-Player`, else `player` in the address (GET) or the JSON body (POST, at most 4 KB); 403 for anyone else, the creator included, 404 for no world, 404 code `classic` for a Classic world.

| Request | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET /v1/worlds/<id>/raid` | 200 `{"story": <state without blob>, "bosses": [*pool* with "hp", "reports", "byPlayer"], "regenPerMinute": 0.03}` (see the story's *state*; `hp` is the HP left now, `round(left * max)`) |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/raid/bosses/<key>/reset` | 200 the full *pool* (`left` 1, `max` null) with `reset` (whether there was one); the pool is deleted, so it is full and undefeated, and the next report sets its max again |
| `POST /v1/worlds/<id>/raid/route/clear` | 200 the story's *state* without `blob`, with `cleared` (whether a route was locked); a cleared lock counts the revision up and pushes `story <rev>`. 409 with the *state* (no `blob`) and `code` `started` while the world's story is on the locked route (`activeRoute(counters)` is the route); that lock ends by itself once the world returns from the route |

### Push

After every report that writes and every reset of an existing pool the relay sends the text frame `boss <left> <max> <defeated> <key>` to every game in the world room (`bossText`): the share left now with up to 6 decimals and no trailing zeros, at least `0.000001` for a pool that stands, so its last HP never read as empty; `max` as a whole number; `1` when defeated, else `0`; and the key last, since it may hold spaces and `#`. A reset sends `boss 1 <the old max> 0 <key>`. Released DLLs drop it as an unknown text. This DLL (`WorldSession.TakeBoss`) hands it to `RaidBosses.Heard` alone, whose ledger (`PoolLedger`, see *Raid pools* under [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)) keeps it as the pool's newest word, keeps the name of a defeater it knew, since the frame names none, and tells the scripts a defeat or a reset once; a malformed frame is logged and dropped.

### Exports

Text arguments are UTF-8 and null-terminated; `world` is the world's folder id (`WorldCode.IdOf`), and the world must be the open one.

| Export | Arguments | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_raid_bosses_fetch` | `'p'` world | 1 started; 0 while a fetch runs, without a player, outside that world |
| `mp_raid_boss_report` | `'pppp'` world, pool key, battle id, the report as `seq\tdelta\tmax\tkilled`, such as `3\t0.0125\t5000000\t0` (a whole number from 0, the share lost from -1 to 1 with a point for decimals, the raid max HP above 0 and at most 1e18, `0` or `1`) | 1 queued; 0 without a player, outside that world, while 1000 reports wait, or for a key (1 to 64 characters, `#` allowed), battle id or report the relay would refuse |
| `mp_raid_boss_state` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) buffer, size | the length, negated when the buffer is too small; 0 before anything was asked or heard |

- **Fetching** runs on a thread of its own, one at a time; a relay that cannot be reached, answers 429 or fails itself (5xx) is asked again after 2, 5 and 10 s, then the fetch fails. It takes `regenPerMinute` (0.03 when missing) and the pools: a pool the game knew before the fetch started and the relay no longer lists is forgotten, one the world room told while it ran is kept.
- **Reporting** queues the report and returns; one thread sends the queue in order. A relay that cannot be reached, answers 429 or fails itself gets the same report, with the same `seq`, again after 2, 5 and 10 s, then every 10 s, up to `RaidBosses.MaxReportRetries` (13) tries again, about two minutes. After them the report is dropped with a log line naming its battle and `seq`, and the next one goes out, since the queue goes in order and one the relay never takes would hold back every later one; a dropped report's damage never reaches the pool. Any other refusal (4xx) drops that report at once (`report=failed` with its code and error). Reports are never merged: each is a request of its own.
- **The ledger.** `RaidBosses` keeps the pools in `Ledger`, the `PoolLedger` the scripts read through `raid.pools`: every report it queues counts there at once (`Own`) and waits for its answer, which settles it (`Settled`), as does a report dropped. **The newest word wins:** the relay tells the world room of a change before it answers, and the two travel apart, so every pool carries a rising stamp, and an answer to a request sent before the pool was last heard is not taken; it only adds a defeater's name the world room's word lacked. A defeat is thus lifted only by the world room telling the pool stands again (a reset) or by an answer to a request sent after the defeat was heard. Word of more HP than the game counts while one of its own reports of that pool waits is older than that report and not taken either; a defeat, a full pool and a pool that was defeated always are.

**`mp_raid_boss_state`** is a message (header lines, a blank line, the text) of the world the last request or word was for, empty before either; a request for another world forgets the pools before:

- `world` and `regen` (the share a minute).
- Once a fetch ran: `fetch` (`busy`, `done`, `failed`), `fetch_code` (the relay's code: `classic`, `rate`, `full`, …) and `fetch_error` (why it failed, for the player).
- Once a report was queued: `report` (`busy` while any report waits, else `done` or `failed` of the last), `report_queued` (how many wait), `report_code` and `report_error`; and `report_key`, `report_battle` and `report_seq` of the last report the relay answered.
- After the headers one line per pool, by key, as the ledger has it now: `key`, tab, the share left now (filled up at `regen` since the ledger took it, at most 1, up to 6 decimals, 0 when defeated), tab, `max` as a whole number, tab, `1` when defeated else `0`, tab, who defeated it (empty while it stands). The scripts no longer read it; they read `raid.pools`.

### In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpRaidPool` makes the story boss battles of a Raid World one fight against their pools. Classic worlds, and a player whose story is in an earlier part than the world's (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.mode` other than `:world`, `active?`), are never touched.

- **Which battles fight a pool** (`set_up`, after `BattleManager.setup`; `kind_of`, `refusal`): an event's battle (`:event`): the map's interpreter runs a map event (`in_map`, around `Game_Map#update_interpreter`; an event id above 0, so a common event run on its own never counts) whose battle command started the battle (`at_command`, around `command_301`), the troop is a boss's (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.boss?`: a story boss of the table or one of `OPTIONAL`, the one-time Chaos Domain bosses) and the last phase of its fight (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.last_phase?`; an `OPTIONAL` boss has one phase) and not in `UNPOOLED` (the two Great Decision fights, which set the world's route), neither a Hades rematch (switch 97, or anything on the rematch hall's map 576, `hades?`) nor the Colosseum (switch 87) runs, and the story has not beaten the milestone (`BEATEN`: the variables and switches the story writes after each milestone's last fight, alternatives split by `|`, conditions by `&`). The event may have moved the story or put the party together anew before its battle; nothing of it is put back. A co-op guest's battle fights the pools its host's invite names (`:guest`, see *Invites*), a side battle those of its troop (`:side`, see *Side battles*), and the game's retry of a lost battle (`retrying`, around `BattleManager.retry_battle`; `retried`) fights as the lost one did, under a new battle id, its HP taken from the pool, not restored. A random encounter never fights one.
- **Keys** (`key_of`, `member_keys`, `troop_keys`): a fight a milestone lets the player take in any order has a pool of its own under its boss's name (`FIGHTS`: Sisel, EX-Kyubi and the other Chaos route quartet, the five Deadly Sins vessels, Goddess, Demon and Fiend); every other pool is its milestone's (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.milestone_key`), with ` (cap <n>)` where milestones of several caps share a name, such as `Tamamo (cap 61)`, or an `OPTIONAL` boss's name. A boss's key holds no `#` and at most 60 characters (`MAX_KEY_LENGTH`). A troop whose setup makes several enemies (`data_own`: its members whose enemy the database has, such as Kagetsumugi and her dolls) has a pool per enemy, `<key>#<index>` by the enemy's place in the troop from 0; enemies another mod adds mid-battle come after them and fight no pool (`own_count`, `own_enemies`). The two routes' Doppel Lukas share one, so on the second route its pool is defeated already.
- **Tiers** (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses::TIERS`, `TIER_BOSSES`, `tier`, `factor`): every boss has a tier from 0, the strongest, to 9, set by hand per milestone name in `TIER_BOSSES` (the level caps play no part) or with its `OPTIONAL` entry: 0 is Chaos alone, 1 and 2 the Chaos route with its Phenomena of Ruin, 3 the two routes' final bosses, 4 the routes' other bosses (the Archangels and the Six Ancestors among them), 5 to 9 the story before them, the first bosses last. A tier names a factor per stat of the game's (`:mhp`, `:mmp`, `:atk`, `:def`, `:mat`, `:mdf`, `:agi`, `:luk`); a stat it leaves out stays. Now max HP 50 times at tier 0, 5 less per tier down to 5 times at tier 9, and defense and magic defense 5 times at tier 0, falling evenly to the game's own from tier 6 on.
- **Raid stats** (`MGQ_MpRaidPool::Stats`, `scale_enemies`): the battle's own enemies have their tier's factors on the stats the game computes, through one wrap of `Game_Enemy#mhp` and of each other stat a tier names (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.tier_stats`, wrapped as the game starts), which `battles_raid_tiers.rbx` shares. The tier's factors are kept for the battle (`Stats.scale`, `take_tier`; `Stats.forget` as a battle is set up and at the title screen) and each enemy is looked up by its object, so a stat call reads no tier table; the factor is applied on every call and never stored, since the game computes them anew from the difficulty and the states: so a factor always comes after the world's difficulty. So the game's own rules on a share of HP, such as an enemy's HP conditions and the ero offer, read the raised max. An enemy keeps its share of HP as its max is raised, then takes its pool's (`take_on`); a troop a guest builds anew from its host's (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_troop`) is raised at once (`rescale`), since the enemies' own max would cut the host's HP, and where this game reports, takes its pools' HP. A report's base belongs to the enemy object it was taken of (`rebase`), so an enemy built anew since (`rebuilt?`) reports nothing and takes its pool's HP at the next sync: its full HP never counts as healing the pool. An enemy's HP for a share is `hp_of`: `ceil(share * max)`, at least 1 while any is left.
- **Each command phase** (`command_phase`, around `Scene_Battle#start_party_command_selection`; by load order inside `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`'s wrap and outside `battles_sync_live.rbx`'s, so it runs before the host records what it sends the guests): the host, or a player who fights alone or took the battle over (`reporter?`: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.role` not `:guest`), reports first and then sets each own enemy's HP from its pool: the share left now of the enemy's raised max. The script hands C# the battle's own enemies (`turn`, topic `raid.fight.turn`: `place:hp:mhp:dead:id` each, the id its object's, which tells an enemy built anew apart) at each moment of the battle; `RaidFight` (see *Raid pools* under [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)) reports and hands back the HP to set, 0 for a kill, which the script applies. An empty pool kills the enemy (`kill`: HP 0 and its collapse), and once every enemy is dead `BattleManager.judge_win_loss` wins the battle as the game's own battles win. Reporting first keeps what the turn's end and the battle's start dealt by themselves (slip damage, automatic skills, troop events). A dead enemy keeps its death, one whose pool the relay told nothing of keeps its HP. A co-op guest never reports and never sets HP from the pools (`Fight#guest`, as C# last told it), not even a kill, since the stream gives it the host's values; once it takes the battle over (C# finds it the reporter at its next moment, the role compared every frame while the battle waits for commands, or `taken_over`), its `base` starts from its enemies' HP at that moment, so the turn its host reported already is not reported again. As a turn ends (`turn_ended`, before `Scene_Battle#turn_end`) only the enemies whose pool is empty die, which the game's turn end then counts.
- **While the battle waits for commands** (`waiting_for_commands`, after `Scene_Battle#update_basic` while `BattleManager`'s phase is `:input`): every 5 seconds (`RaidFight.WaitSyncMs`; the script compares the time C# named, `@sync_due`, before it asks) the own enemies take their pools' HP again, so the bar shows the other battles' blows and the regeneration while the players choose; nothing of the battle's own runs then, so it undoes none of its damage. A pool emptied meanwhile kills its enemy, and the host wins once all are dead, as at the command phase; while the live battle's host waits for the other players' commands (`waiting?`, `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Waiting.active?`, set by `Waiting.wait_for`), it leaves the victory to that wait, which runs inside `Scene_Battle#turn_start`: `Live.host_commands` and `host_guests_commands` answer `:won` once the troop is all dead, and `end_early` wins the battle after the collapse (`Live.win`), so the turn never starts on the finished battle. Should the battle end any other way during the wait, the `turn_start` hook leaves the turn out too (`Live.ended_in_wait?`).
- **Reports** (`report`, at each command phase and as the battle ends, `battle_ended` before `BattleManager.battle_end`, and as an action ends, `action_done` after `Scene_Battle#process_action`, at most every 2 seconds (`RaidFight.ReportMs`; the script compares the time C# named, `@report_due`) so a long turn's damage reaches the other games while the relay's two reports a second per player hold for a boss of up to four enemies; the turn's own enemies take the pools' HP only at the command phase, never mid-turn): per own enemy not reported dead, the share of its raised max HP it lost since its HP was last taken or reported (`base`; negative for healing, at most 1 either way), as `seq\tdelta\tmax\tkilled` with the battle's next `seq`, under the battle's id (`new_battle_id`), through the host's way to the pools (`IRaidRelay`: the DLL's `RaidBosses.Report`, the battle server's `PoolOutbox`, whose reports the server's scripts send through the relay's connection after each moment, `Online.send_reports`). An enemy whose HP did not change sends nothing; one that died reports `killed` 1, once. Nothing waits for the relay: a local kill is a kill, the battle wins and its event runs on at once while the DLL sends the queue in the background. Each report taken counts in the ledger at once (`PoolLedger.Own`) and the script reads the pools again after reporting, so a command phase before the relay's answer keeps the damage. A report the host refuses leaves the enemy `held`: its HP is not set from the pool until a report goes, which then carries what both turns dealt. A report's `base` counts at most the enemy's max HP now, so a max HP the battle lowered reads as no damage. A guest that takes the battle over reports from its next command phase on, from the HP its enemies had as it took over (see *Each command phase*).
- **What the game knows of the pools** (`@pools`, `Pool`, `read_state`): the ledger's pools as they stood at the last read (`POOLS_TOPIC`, `raid.pools`: the world, the share a minute and each pool's key, share left with every bit, max, defeat and who defeated it), read every `STATE_FRAMES`, after each report and as a defeat is told, and only while its `world` is the open world's; `left_now` adds the regeneration since the read at the ledger's share a minute. The rules of what counts (the newest word, the player's own reports before the relay answers, a standing pool's least share `PoolLedger.MinLeft`) are the ledger's, see *Raid pools* under [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame).
- **Fetching** (`fetch_on_move`, from `tick`): `mp_raid_bosses_fetch` as the player enters the world's story and on each map change; a fetch the DLL did not start is tried again after `FETCH_RETRY_FRAMES`. Entering another world forgets the pools first.
- **A pool empty already**: a battle against it starts with its enemies dead and is won at once, and its host invites nobody (`won_already?`, from `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.invitees` and `pools_won?`), not even the host of a waiting invite (`with_waiting_host`). So does a story battle against a boss whose milestone the world's story beat already (`story_won?`, `win_at_once`): it fights no pool, its own enemies start dead, and its first command phase wins it.
- **Defeat** is the game's own, and what the battle dealt stays in the pool; the lost battle is kept for the game's retry (`@lost`). What the defeat changed of the story goes back once it ended, and a fight against the pools waits frozen (see [Frozen fights](#frozen-fights-battles_raid_freezerbx-world_freezerbx-ui_raid_freezerbx)).
- **The boss falls for everyone**: once a pool is empty, every battle fighting it kills that enemy at its next command phase or turn end and wins once all its own enemies are dead, with the game's own rewards. Once every pool of a boss is empty, the chat log and the notification box say "\<boss> is defeated for the whole world." once (`announce`, from the ledger's event `raid.boss_defeated`, which a push's defeat posts once per pool, `told_defeated`; `back_up` allows it again after a reset, `raid.boss_back`, `told_back`).
- **The story**: the teller's event runs on after its battle as after any battle, and the story moves on for the world once, through the story's normal write (see [In the game (`world_story.rbx`)](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)).
- **Invites** (`invite_fields`, `note_invite`): a battle that fights pools adds `pool`, how many own enemies its troop has, and for a side battle `side` = 1, to its invite, its late invite (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.invite_fields`) and its retry's invite (`retry_of?`). The guest notes it through `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.note_invite`, also as it follows or yields to a rival's battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_pool`, which runs `set_up` for the troop taken), and derives the keys from the troop as its host did, so it fights the same raised max HP and can report should it take the battle over. A boss battle also invites the players watching the story its host tells (see *Who joins* under [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)).
- **Side battles** (`state_fields`, `side_of`, `start_side`): the host of a boss battle, or a player who fights one alone, tells `rpb` in its state (`STATE_FIELD`): the troop and its enemy rates (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.rates_text`), such as `71;100,100,0`, while its battle scene runs and the boss's pools are not all empty, else empty. Near such a battle that takes the player in no more, `battles_coop_join.rbx` offers a side battle on the same hotkey (see *Joining by choice* under [Co-op battles](#co-op-battles-battles_cooprbx)). `start_side` borrows the host's enemy rates (`borrow_rates`), sets the same troop up with escaping and losing off (`BattleManager.setup(troop, false, false)`) and calls the battle scene: a battle of the player's own (`:side`) against the same pools, which starts at once and invites and holds nobody (`side_battle?(troop)`, the public check `battles_coop.rbx` asks to skip gathering and invites), though other players near it join it by choice; it runs no map event, none of its troop's battle events (`without_pages`, which empties the troop's pages while the battle runs) and writes no story. It wins once its own enemies die, by its own blows or as the pool empties; a defeat is the game's, as for the boss's own battle, since a defeat back on the map at 1 HP let a player start the next side battle at once while each one's damage stayed in the pools. Its EXP, gold and drops come only with the first side battle won against that boss (`note_rewarded`); a later one gives none (`withhold_rewards?`, around `BattleManager.enable_get_exp?`, `enable_get_gold?` and `enable_get_drop?`, `REWARD_CHECKS`), for the players who join it too, whose invite tells `side`; the boss's own story battle always gives them. The save keeps the bosses rewarded in `$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_raid_rewards` (`REWARDS_FIELD`): `{"world" => <world id>, "keys" => {<boss key> => true}}`, started anew in another world. It never starts for a player behind the world's story, against a boss their story beat, or on an empty pool.

### Frozen fights (`battles_raid_freeze.rbx`, `world_freeze.rbx`, `ui_raid_freeze.rbx`)

A lost story battle against a raid boss keeps the story at the battle. The game's own interpreter cannot resume there (`marshal_dump` saves the index after the battle command and drops the common events it runs inside), so `MGQ_MpRaidFreeze` keeps its own record. Where the story fights each raid boss troop and the tables around it (`SITES`, `CALLERS`, `ALTERNATIVES`, `SCENES`, `SCENE_MAPS`, `DEFEAT_EVENTS`, `DEFEAT_WRITES`, `EXCLUDED_MAPS`, `PREAMBLE_CODES`) come from the DLL's events database once per session for every troop of `MGQ_MpRaidBosses` (`tables`, topic `raid.sites`, asked again a second later while the DLL gives no answer); a table the database cannot tell, as while the game data differs from it, is left out and its default stands in (no sites, so no fight freezes). Whether a page shows only during a scene (`scene_page?`) or runs nothing (`runs_nothing?`) the database tells for a page it holds as the game does (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.page_flags`, topic `events.pages`, one call per map), the scripts' own rule for any other.

- **The run and the frames** (`note_run`, after `Game_Interpreter#setup`; `call_common`, around `command_117`; `battle_command`, around `command_301`): as the map's event starts, a mark of the story's write journal (S_start, see below); while the map's interpreter runs (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.in_map?`), a stack of the common events it calls, each with its list, the call's index and `@branch`; and the battle command with its index, indent and branches. The page is found by the list that runs (`mgq_mp_pages`), never by the page the event shows, since Sonya's page 1 turns page 2 on before its battle.
- **The record** (`battle_set_up`, after `BattleManager.setup`, after `MGQ_MpRaidPool.set_up`): only for a battle that fights pools as an event's (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.set_up_kind == :event`), not a side battle, whose innermost site key is in `SITES` and whose map is no Hades hall; a fight on a cutscene map (`SCENE_MAPS`) needs its `SCENES` row. Every refusal is logged with its reason. The record (`$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_raid_freeze`, `{"world", "origin", "records"}` by troop, core types alone, the screen and the party as `Marshal` texts) holds the frames (outermost first), the preamble's start (the 132, 133, 283 and switch 22 commands right before the battle command at its indent), the player's spot and look, the followers, the camera, the screen, the map's look, the music, every event's place and look, the party's temporary members and split parties, the values the event changed before the battle (`start` at S_start, `pre` at the battle), and the tableau: for a field fight the boss and the events only its scene shows (`scene_events`: those that showed nothing as the event started, `@run[:shown]`, those turned since to a page whose conditions name a `DEFEAT_WRITES` switch, such as the scene switches 31-39, and those the scene turned to another page or moved on a page that does not move by itself, whose page now runs nothing), each standing for itself, while the map's other events stay live; the boss is the fight's event when it shows, else the scene's large sprite ("$") nearest the player, else its nearest character, else its nearest event, else the same choice among the map's shown figures whose page runs nothing (never one to talk to, never a "!" object), else an unseen row; the boss, chosen alike, alone on the field map the cutscene was entered from (`origin`, else the first `SCENES` source) for a cutscene fight.
- **The undo** (`defeated`, before `BattleManager.process_defeat`; `undo_due` in `on_map`, after `Scene_Map#update`): every lost raid boss battle of a Raid World (an event's, pooled or not, a guest's, a side battle, a served battle's local defeat) takes the mark S1 before the defeat; once its chain ended (the map, no interpreter, no transfer, no message), every key that changed since S1 goes back, but `DEFEAT_WRITES` and the player's own keys (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.personal?`): to its S_start value when the event changed it before the battle, else to its S1 value; items whose count changed come back. Never for Hades (switch 97, `EXCLUDED_MAPS`), the Colosseum (switch 87), Library replays, battles the event may lose, or an event whose start values are unknown, as after a load or when the journal lost them. While it waits (`holding_writes?`) `world_story.rbx` writes nothing the relay handed into the story, `story_keys.rbx` records no write and `world_catchup.rbx` waits. A player who pressed from a vehicle gets off it on arrival, the vehicle left where they pressed (`leave_vehicle`), and the vehicles on the map stand where the record has them (`place_vehicles`, the record's `vehicles`). The game's retry drops the pending undo and keeps the first battle's values and mark. A record then waits `frozen` and the world is told (`MGQ_MpWorldFreeze.note`); no message tells the player, the stand-in does, and the log says it.
- **The write journal** (`writing`, before `Game_Switches#[]=`, `Game_Variables#[]=` and `Game_SelfSwitches#[]=` of the game's own objects; `StoryJournal` and `JournalTopics` of `MGQOnline.Game`, topics `freeze.journal` and `freeze.since`): while a mark lives (the run's, a defeat's), every write is noted with the key's value before it (`[seq, kind, key, before]`; nil, true, false and Integers as they are, any other value as its `Marshal` dump in an Array of one) and handed to the DLL once a frame (`hand_journal`, before `Graphics.update`) and before every question, with the oldest live mark, so the DLL drops what no mark needs. A mark is the number of the last write noted, which costs no call. `changed_since` asks the DLL for each key written since a mark, with its value before its first write since it, and keeps what the old whole-story diff kept: the value then (a resume's record's `start` values standing for the story at its start, as the resumed run's mark is taken after `pre` went back), the value now, the same ones left out (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.same?`), switches and variables by id, self switches in the game's Hash order. The DLL keeps at most 100,000 writes; a mark whose writes it dropped answers `overflow`, which counts as unknown start values, and an undo then puts back nothing of the story (`lost_undo`, the items still come back, the record goes). Writes the DLL refuses stay with the scripts for the next hand-over (`held_back`, logged at the first refusal and every 600 after), those no live mark needs dropped and at most 32,000 kept; a mark before a write dropped so, or asked while the DLL refuses, counts as lost too. The relay's story (`world_story.rbx`'s `apply`, inside `unjournaled`) stays out of the journal, every kind of it: it lands only while no event of the player's runs (`quiet?`) and no defeat is put back (`holding?`), and it is the world's, never an event's or a defeat's to put back. The freeze's own restores (`write_value`) pass the game's `[]=` and are not seen either.
- **The resume** (`resume`, from the stand-in's press or J): refused while another player tells the story here, while a defeat is put back or a resume runs, or while the player is not free. With a record: the party comes back, the player is transferred onto the recorded spot, and on arrival (after `Game_Player#perform_transfer`, the map's starts held meanwhile) the values at the battle, the events, the player, the followers, the camera, the screen (copied into the live objects), the map's look and the music come back; the map's interpreter is set up as story (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.as_story`) at the outer call, or the preamble, with its branches, and each common event of the chain is handed its index, branches and id (`hand_over`). A recorded command that moved is looked for nearby; else the event starts from its first command. Without a record, the world's entry starts the event from its first command (`start_fresh`): on a field map where the player stands, or on the cutscene map of the player's side after a transfer onto its entry. While a resume runs, `coop_npcs.rbx` moves none of the map's events, and a battle against a boss whose side battle gave its rewards gives none (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.withhold_rewards?`).
- **After a won battle**: the record goes, the troop counts as won (`won?`), which ends the world's entry at once, and a guest or side battler without a record of their own is transferred onto the starter's spot (`teleport_joiner`) when on its map. Records of bosses the player's story beat are dropped every `CHECK_FRAMES`, and a frozen record the world's list lacks after the title is told again once (`share_records`).
- **The world's list** (`MGQ_MpWorldFreeze`): each frozen fight is one key of the relay's story tracks by its troop, `f<troop>`, which the whole world keeps, players behind included; its value is the entry's fields after the troop (`troop|site|outer|scene|map|rows|blocked|spot|held` as `encode_entry` writes it, every free text percent-escaped, read defensively and never through `Marshal`), or `0` once the fight ended (`ENDED`). The player's own fight goes to the relay as their request (`note`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.write_freeze`, site `game:freeze`); the world's come with every track and push (`take_keys`), merged as a union by troop. An entry ends once the player won its battle, every pool of its boss is known and defeated, or its `BEATEN` marker is met in the player's story or the world's last told one, and so does the fight the other side plays in its stead (the freeze's `ALTERNATIVES`, see above: the same map page, each in a branch of another side switch, as Sonya Mazda and Mainyu), since a player of the other side is past the fight without meeting its own end. Its held keys are shared at their held value: each write of one carries it (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.held_value`), and each value the relay hands of one is written as it (`put_key`). An end this game saw (`note_ended`) goes to the relay as `0` for the fight and the other side's, which the relay keeps in place of the fight for every game. An end is final for the world (`@ended`, `final?`): `take_keys` drops the fight, the player's own too, `note` and `merge` refuse it, the engine takes no record for it (`freeze_refusal`), and the end of one `ALTERNATIVES` fight ends the other. An entry longer than 65,536 characters (`MAX_ENTRY_LENGTH`, the relay's `maxFreezeLength`) is never written.
- **The stand-ins** (`MGQ_MpRaidFreezeUi`): built from the player's own frozen records and the world's entries of troops they keep no record of, on the tableau's map, in a Raid World's story outside battle, never while that fight resumes; looked at every `SCAN_FRAMES` (30) and anew on every map and after a load. A `Game_MpFreezeStandIn` (a `Game_Character`, nothing in the save) per row with a `Sprite_Character` in the spriteset (`@mgq_mp_raid_freeze`); the live events of rows with `hide` 1 hide, the boss's always and any other only while its page runs nothing (`hidden?`), and neither the tableau's blocked events nor an event whose page, or a common event it calls, writes a story value the fight holds (`DEFEAT_WRITES` left out; `page_writes`, the DLL's command index of the page, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.page_writes`, kept per list) can start (`refuse_start?`, around `Game_Event#start`, which reads the sources afresh once a frame, so a world's entry blocks in the frame it arrives; `block_starts` before `Game_Map#setup_starting_map_event` as a backstop). The stand-ins step aside, the live events showing, while the player watches another player's story scene, another player tells the story on the map, or a blocked event runs all the same. The action press facing the boss's stand-in, across a counter or under the player, resumes the fight (around `Game_Player#check_action_event`); so does the player pressing or touching one of the tableau's blocked events (`leads_in?`, `lead_in`: the field event that leads into a cutscene fight, another caller of its common event, a field fight's own event; never one that runs by itself or in parallel, and not while the stand-ins step aside), once per touch, no other event starting in that frame; `battles_coop_join.rbx` offers it on J within `JOIN_DISTANCE` after a joinable battle and a side battle, and `ui_raid_bar.rbx` draws the bar above it.

### Raid bosses outside Raid Worlds (`battles_raid_tiers.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpRaidTiers` gives the story bosses of a Classic world whose creator ticked *Raid Bosses*, and of single player while the player's *MGQ Online SP → Raid Bosses* is on, their tier's stats in every battle anew. It never acts in a Raid World (`active?` and `kind_of` ask `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?` first), and it asks the DLL nothing at all (`Player.ini` is read in Ruby), so single player needs no connection.

- **The setting** (`active?`): in a world, `MGQ_MpWorld.raid_bosses?` (the list's and `world.ini`'s `raid_bosses`, a Classic world's only, changed live by the relay's `raid_bosses` push through `MGQ_MpOverworldSync::RAID_BOSSES_FIELD`, `pushed` and `MGQ_MpWorld.take_raid_bosses`); outside one, `Player.ini`'s `sp_raid_bosses` (1 on, off by default; the Mod Config row `OPTION` in *MGQ Online SP*, which `ui_single_player.rbx` adds).
