- **Which battles** (`set_up` after `BattleManager.setup`, `kind_of`, `refusal`): the same as a Raid World's pools, by `MGQ_MpRaidPool`'s rules and the player's own story: a map event's battle command (own wraps of `Game_Map#update_interpreter` and `command_301`) against the last phase of a boss of `MGQ_MpRaidBosses` with a tier, not in `MGQ_MpRaidPool::UNPOOLED`, no Hades rematch (`hades?`), no Colosseum, and a milestone the story has not beaten (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.beaten?`); the game's retry of such a lost battle (`retrying`, around `BattleManager.retry_battle`); and the battle of an invite or a member's request to lead that says so (`invite_fields` adds `tiers` = 1 to `battles_coop.rbx`'s Classic invites and `lead` requests, `note_invite` from `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.accept` and `lead`), so the host decides for every game in it.
- **Stats** (`scale`, `rescale`): the pools' raid stats wrap (`MGQ_MpRaidPool::Stats`), around `Game_Enemy#mhp` and each stat a tier names, applied on every call; the own enemies keep their share of HP as they are raised, so they start full, and a troop a guest builds anew from its host's is raised at once (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_troop`). Nothing reports, syncs or regenerates.

### Single player (`ui_single_player.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpSinglePlayer.add_options` adds the four rows of *MGQ Online SP* as `:mod_stored` rows (`rows`: each one's key, name and help from its script, `MGQ_MpLeaderHud.sp_shown?`/`show_sp`, `MGQ_MpRaidBar.shown?`/`show_bar`, `MGQ_MpRaidTiers.single_player?`/`choose`).

- **The offer** (`changed`, `settle`, `on_map`): a row changed while the player plays (`@playing`, from `Scene_Map#start` until `Scene_Title#start`) outside a world counts while any changed row differs from its value at the last offer (`@before`, `@now`), so a row turned back counts as none. The map (after `Scene_Map#update_scene`, unless the scene changes) asks once through `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm` (`RESTART_QUESTION`, *Restart* / *Later*) while no box is open and `quiet?`: outside a world, no event, message or transfer, and the game's own saves allowed (`saving_allowed?`: not `$game_system.save_disabled`, no Library replay, switch 443). *Restart* goes on only while the map is still quiet (`answered`), else the offer comes again, since a save in the middle of an event would skip its command. It needs the DLL (`MGQ_Multiplayer.available?`) for the restart and logs when it is missing. Messages go to the notification box (`MGQ_MpNotices.message`), which shows outside worlds.
- **Restart** (`restart`, `save_resume`): the game's own `DataManager.save_game_without_rescue` writes `RESUME_FILE` (`Patch\Multiplayer\Resume.rvdata2`) while `with_resume_file` has `DataManager.make_filename` (wrapped at `SceneManager.run`, after the translation's plugins) name it, so no save slot is touched; the slot last used goes to `Player.ini`'s `resume_slot` (`SLOT_SETTING`) for the Save and Load screens' cursor, a file already there goes aside first (`keep_aside`, `KEPT_FILE`), and the save count stays (one less before the game's `on_before_save`, as its autosave does). The system save is written as the game closes, as on any exit. Then the Mod Framework starts the game again (`Mod_Framework::Mods.restart` with the action `multiplayer.resume`, `MGQ_Multiplayer::Stages::RESUME_ACTION`), whose title enters the game, which loads this script, and goes on from the file; without the framework the file goes and the game plays on. A save that fails restarts nothing.
- **Resume** (`resume`, after `Scene_Title#update`, once per visit, waiting while `title_busy?`: a world open or starting its new game, or the update offerle screen puts back): with `RESUME_FILE` there, `DataManager.load_game_without_rescue(resume_slot)` through the same redirect, then `Scene_Load.new.on_load_success`, as Continue finishes a load, and only then the file is removed. A file that fails to load goes aside (`KEPT_FILE`), and a message box names it.

### HP bars (`ui_raid_bar.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpRaidBar` and `Sprite_MpRaidBar` draw the raid bosses' HP.

- **The bar** (`draw_bar`): the boss's title in the preset's title color (`TITLE_COLOR`, gold in Monarch; `MGQ_MpRaidBosses.title`: the group the game's enemy library names it in, such as *Archangel*, *Six Ancestors*, *Phenomenon of Ruin* or, from the Chaos Domain's events, *Chaos' Phantom*; else *Tier \<n> Raid Boss*) centered across the bar, below it a row of gauges, one per enemy of the boss (`Bar#gauges`, each a `Gauge` with the enemy's name, HP and max), centered: the row's width (on the strip 65% of the screen's width, at most the strip's less its margins, `strip_row_width`; the overhead bar's width inside its frame) is shared among them with `GAUGE_GAP` (6) between, and each enemy's name in white is centered over its gauge; each line of text on the style's plate (`Style::PLATE`) as wide as its text plus `Style::PAD` on either side (`draw_plate`), the title's capped to the bar's width and a name's to its gauge's, the plates stacked onto each other and onto the gauges' frames without a gap. The names of a boss of several enemies share one font size, the largest from the layout's down to `MIN_NAME_SIZE` (14) at which every name fits its gauge (`name_size`), a name still too long cut with an ellipsis, a shrunken name drawn in its row's full height so it stays centered over its gauge; a boss of one enemy draws as a single gauge the row wide, its name and plate centered across the bar as the title (`name_box`). Every text is drawn through the shared style (`MGQ_MpUi::Style.use_font`, `draw_outlined`, `draw_plate`): the game's outline in solid black (`Style::OUTLINE`, the game's own is half see-through) widened by a copy above, below and beside it (`Style::OUTLINE_REACH` 1, since more fills the small letters in), never bold, which smears VL Gothic; the strip's title line names the party box hotkey that makes it small at its right (`BARS_HINT`). The gauge sits in a black frame with a thin edge in the preset's `EDGE` (gold in Monarch); its filled part is a maroon gradient (`FILL_TOP`, `FILL_MIDDLE`, `FILL_BOTTOM`) over the preset's empty part (`EMPTY`, `Style::WELL`). The HP inside the gauge (`draw_numbers`) is shortened by `MGQ_MpRaidBar.short_numbers`: the largest unit of `UNITS` (K, M, B, T, Qa, Qi) that leaves the max at least `MIN_DIGITS` (7) digits, the HP rounded up so a boss with HP left never reads 0, such as "236,167 B / 1,009,500 B", but never past the max, which rounds down; then the share left ("68.3%", "<0.1%" while any is left) when it fits, else the HP alone, else the share alone.
- **The damage trail** (`follow_trails`, a `Trail` per gauge): every change of a gauge's share left is noted with until when the enemy had the HP before it; the gauge shows, past its filled part, the most of the max HP the enemy had within the last `TRAIL_FRAMES` (120) in a darker maroon (`TRAIL_TOP`, `TRAIL_BOTTOM`), which then drains down to the HP left by `TRAIL_EASE` of the way a frame (at least `TRAIL_MIN_STEP`), drawn every `TRAIL_REDRAW_FRAMES` (2) meanwhile; healing and the regeneration rise at once, a hit on one gauge leaves the others' trails alone, and a bar whose names differ from the last shown starts every trail anew. A bar is drawn again only when its title, a gauge's name or numbers or a trail changed, numbers at most every `REDRAW_FRAMES` (6).
- **In battle** (`update_battle`, after `Spriteset_Battle#update`): while the battle fights pools, the strip, across the top of the screen until the player moves or narrows it (`place_strip`, the layout box `:raid_bar`, see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)), 640x86 at most (`STRIP_HEIGHT`, `STRIP_LAYOUT`: title 20, name 24, a 30 px gauge 65% of the screen wide with its numbers in 21) at z 90 (`BATTLE_Z`: under the game's windows, which start at 100, over the battlers and pictures). It has a gauge per own enemy of the battle (`battle_bar`), named as the game names it (`Game_Enemy#name`, which letters enemies of one name *A*, *B*, ...), with the enemy's share of its raised max times its pool's max once the relay told one, else its raised max. It hides while the game's message at the top shows (`top_message?`), which draws over it. The party box's hotkey (`MGQ_MpRaidBar.on_battle`, Tab unless bound) crops it until only its gauges show (`crop`: its `src_rect` steps down over `Style::SLIDE_FRAMES` (8) by `STRIP_HEIGHT - SMALL_STRIP_HEIGHT`, the strip staying where the player placed it) and back again, kept in `Player.ini` (`raid_bar_small`), a new battle's strip starting where it stands. While it shows, the game's own HP bar of a boss (Basic Enemy HP Bars Lite: the sprite's `@hp_bar`, the `MGQ_MpGame` field `hp_bar`) moves down below it, following the crop (`push_down`, after `Sprite_Battler#update_hp_bar`, by `battle_bottom`), only while the strip lies at the top of the screen.
- **On the map** (`MapBars`, after `Spriteset_Map#update`): every `SCAN_FRAMES` (30) and on each new map the map's events are looked through (`boss_events`): an event whose page starts a battle by troop id, directly or in the common events it calls, every branch counted, but never in one that turns the Colosseum's switch 87 on, whose fights reuse the bosses' troops, as the receptionist's common events 158 and 160 (the DLL's command index, `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.page_troops`: one `events.index` call for the pages that changed since the last look, `look_up_troops`, and one more for those its database holds otherwise; `troops_of`, kept per page), against a troop that shows a bar (`shown_troop?`: a last phase not in `UNPOOLED` with pool keys, never during a Hades rematch or in its hall, `MGQ_MpRaidPool.hades?`, whose pool the game knows or whose milestone the player's story has not beaten). Above each such event that shows (`shows?`: a character's or a tile's picture, markers such as `!Flame` included, neither transparent nor hidden) a 52 px high bar, 240 wide (`OVERHEAD_WIDTH`) for a boss of one enemy and 480 (`OVERHEAD_WIDE_WIDTH`) for one of several, so the names still read, its picture remade when the width it needs changes (`fit`; `OVERHEAD_LAYOUT`: title 16, names 18 and thin 8 px gauges without numbers), under the event's feet when the screen has no room above it (`place_over`). While no such event shows, the first boss's bar is the battle's strip, numbers included, where the player placed it (the same `:raid_bar` box as in battle), and while it lies at the top of the screen `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` tells its bottom, which the notification box and the party box move down by while at their default places. The bar (`map_bar`) has a gauge per pool key of the troop (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.troop_keys`, in the troop's order), named by the database's enemy, a name that repeats within the troop lettered as in battle (`map_names`, with `Game_Troop#letter_table`), each pool's share left now times its max; a pool the relay told no max of counts the enemy's own (`raid_max`: the enemy's max HP at the player's difficulty, `Game_Enemy.new(0, id).mhp`, kept per enemy and variable 41, times its tier's max HP factor), full before any report. A side battle's prompt also counts the steps to these events (`distance_to_boss`).
- **Outside Raid Worlds** (while `MGQ_MpRaidPool.active?` is false): the battle's strip shows the fight of `MGQ_MpRaidTiers` with each own enemy's HP and raised max (`tier_battle_bar`), and the map the bosses `MGQ_MpRaidTiers.shown_troop?` names (`tier_map?`) with a full gauge per enemy at `raid_max` (`tier_map_bar`), since nothing keeps their HP. Each shows unless the player turned it off in *MGQ Online SP* (`shown?`, `Player.ini`'s `raid_bar_map` and `raid_bar_battle`, 0 for off); a Raid World never reads them.
- **In C#** (`MGQOnline.Game/Hud/RaidBars.cs` behind `hud.battle_bar` and `hud.map_bar` of `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/HudTopics.cs`), for `ui_raid_bar.rbx` to read once binding 18 drew its screen on the framework: the battle's bar from its enemies (name, HP, max HP, fallen, the key of the pool each fights, none at a tier) against each pool's max HP once the ledger knows one; the map's bar of a troop from the keys of its pools, each read from the `PoolLedger` (read only, its regain included), the enemy's raid max HP (its own at the difficulty, which the script still finds, times its tier's factor, handed by the script until 7.2c) for a pool without a max, or at a tier a full gauge per enemy; the names lettered as the game letters them; each gauge comes with its short numbers (`ShortNumbers`, the largest unit that leaves the max 7 digits, the HP rounded up but never past the max) and its share left (`Percent`, a tenth of a percent from the share's exact binary value with ties to the even tenth, as Ruby's `format` writes it, `<0.1%` for less). The golden cases, `fixtures/hud/bars.json`, were recorded from the script of 3ee61503 by `ruby fixtures/hud/make_bars_fixtures.rb`; `RaidBarsTests` replays them.

## Starting save (`world_save_distribution.rbx`)

A world's creator may start every new player from one of their own saves instead of the opening, or let each new player choose (*Player's choice*, the directory's `choose` flag). Both are chosen once, when the world is made, and never change.

- **Picking.** `Scene_MpStartSave` is the game's own `Scene_Load`, whose choice is handed back instead of loaded. `files_of` writes the system save first, then names the save, its thumbnail when it has one, and the newer of the game's two system saves (`SaveSystemData.load_filename`), under the names a new player gets them by: `Save01.rvdata2`, `Save01.png` and `SystemSave.rvdata2`. So the starting save is the world's first save, and the world's system save (Library, medals, affection) is the creator's.
- **The creator** gets the files copied into their folder of the new world (`place`) and enters it from them, with nothing fetched back.
- **A new player** fetches them (`WorldDirectory.FetchStart` from the DLL's steps, a directory action of kind `start`) when entering a world whose list entry says `ready` and whose folder holds no save yet, but only after taking them: since a save can run code, the world screen first shows `EnterRules.StartWarning` with *Take*, *Start at the beginning* and *Back*, and nothing is fetched before *Take*. *Start at the beginning* starts a new game, *Back* goes back to the list. The world's creator, on a PC whose folder holds no save of it, fetches without the warning (`EnterRules.StartOf`). `check` first walks both files without building anything (`ClassCheck`, the battle server's `SaveCheck` for the game's Ruby, held to the same verdicts by `save_distribution_test.rb`) and throws them away with a plain message when they name a class of Ruby's own or of its libraries, such as a known gadget, before Marshal reads any of it; it then loads every object of the save and the system save before the world opens, since the game quits when a system save fails to load. A class the game lacks, from a mod only the creator has, is named in the message, and the files are thrown away, so the next entry fetches them again.
- **Player's choice.** Entering a `choose` Classic world whose folder holds no save yet asks first (`EnterRules.Where`): *From the creator's save* (only when it is `ready`) shows the same warning and fetches as above, *At the beginning* starts a new game, and *From one of my saves* opens `Scene_MpStartSave` again and copies that save with `files_of` and `place`, as the creator's own copy is made (the steps `save_screen` and `copy_own`, answered by `own_save` and `own_copied`). The copy waits for the world screen's next frame (`@own_start`), since entering loads the map, which must not happen while the scene starts. The creator is asked too when they made the world without their own save. Once the world holds a save, nobody is asked again.
- A world whose creator is still uploading (`pending`) cannot be entered.
- **A Raid World** from a starting save starts its story there, and nothing in the story is special to it: the relay starts the world's track from the first player's story (the seed it asks for), so the first game in the world starts the world's story in the save's part instead of being taken back to the beginning. Every later player from the same save is in the world's part, so the relay places them on the world's track, never behind. The catch-up's notes start empty in a save from outside the world, and `carried` reviews the player's own story first, so the rows the save played give nothing and take no companion. A new game in such a world is behind the world's part (Part 1 against the save's): the relay places it on a track of its own at Part 1's checkpoint, or its own story where the world keeps none of that part, its story events held, until its level reaches the part's gate.

## World mods (`world_mods.rbx`)

A world's required (`!`) mods must be the same in every game that enters it. The relay keeps a catalog of the admins' mods, which only they fill in the World Admin tool (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md), mod catalog): each mod's current version, the versions seen before, and a SHA-256 per file, `.rb` files without carriage returns (`ModHash`), so a checkout with Windows line ends and the release asset hash the same.

- **Kinds.** `link`: a script of a release on the admins' GitHub. `zip`: a link to a zip of a release, laid out as in `Patch`. `upload`: a mod of several files an admin uploaded, which the relay hands out as a zip. The DLL tells the script `zip` for a link whose release file is one (`CatalogMod.ScriptKind`).
- **The check.** The catalog comes with the list, and the DLL checks against it (`ModCheck` in `MGQOnline.Game`, through the topic `mods.check` of `ModCheckTopics`): the world's mods and the creator's hashes in, each required mod this game lacks or has in another version out as a row (`name`, `yours`: the version the hashes are, "unknown", "not installed" or "differs", `worlds`, `key` of the catalog mod to download, `target`, `text`, `downloadable`), with the world screen's `summary`; naming the world (`MGQ_MpWorldMods.differing(listed, name)`, as `on_enter` does) has the DLL log the check. It hashes this game's copies (`ModHash`) by the UTF-8 paths `PatchScripts.List` lists, each file once until `mods.forget` (`forget_installed`, as the world screen opens and after an install), and makes the check anew once the catalog changed, which marks `WorldChanges.Mods`. A mod outside the catalog is compared with the creator's hash (`modHashes` of the world, sent with create and edit, written by `mods.creator_hashes`), and a copy that differs can only be fetched from its author. Without a catalog or a creator's hash, being installed is enough, as before; while Patch cannot be read every mod counts as installed. While the list is fetched again the DLL still checks against the catalog it had; a link the relay could not read yet (no version, no files) is listed for admins only and left out (`ModTopics.Readable`), so it counts as a mod outside the catalog. The creator's hashes leave out names longer than 100 characters, as many as the relay takes, and stop at its 64,000 characters, both counted by code point as Ruby counts them. `fixtures/mods/check.json` holds the golden cases, recorded from `world_mods.rbx` by `ruby fixtures/mods/make_mod_check.rb` before the check left it, the summary's offer changed by hand since, once the download needed no restart; `MGQ_MpWorldMods.personal_keys` still reads the catalog's personal mods (`mods.catalog`) for the parking.
- **Download.** *Download* has the scripts install every differing catalog mod through the Mod Framework (`WorldEntering` hands them the step `install` with each downloadable row's name and catalog key, `MGQ_MpWorldMods.install`): each goes by the id the framework knows it by (`framework_id`: the installed mod of its key, else the website's or the relay's mod `Mod_Framework::Mods.check` finds for its name, else `relay:<key>`), and `Mods.install` downloads them, checks every file against the lists and writes them together into `Patch`, so a failure changes nothing. The scripts answer `installed` (`ok`, `text`, `restart`); the DLL forgets what Patch held and goes on entering the world, whose new mods load as the player enters the game, without a restart. Only a mod installed that runs already (`Mod_Framework::Mods.loaded`, such as a Title mod's title part) loads anew only in a new game session: the step `restart` has the framework start the game again (`Mods.restart` with the action `multiplayer.rejoin` and the world's id, `MGQ_Multiplayer::Stages::REJOIN_ACTION`), whose title opens the world screen and enters that world again (`MGQ_MpWorldMods.restarted_for`), checking anew. That step waits until the world's lock is open: a world this PC has no folder of yet is opened first, with its password or a Discord invite's code, and its folder keeps the world code (`world.ini`, as for every world entered), so the game that starts again enters it from that folder and asks for no password; no password or invite is written anywhere else. Once a mod that runs was installed, every world entered in that game starts it again first (`_restartFirst`). An outdated game offers the update on the title screen instead (`MGQ_MpWorld.offer_update`), as a Discord invite into a world does. Updates thus reach a player only on their next entry.
- **Settings.** The world's `settings`, Mod Config values of every mod it names, required or listed (no key bindings, no `:personal` rows), are applied whenever a save of the world is loaded or started (`load_game_without_rescue`, which the newest translation's replaced `load_game` still calls), and Mod Config Remake shows them greyed out (`ModConfigRemake.world_keys`) until the player leaves the world. The creator's own stay unlocked: in their own world, the row *Shared Mod Settings* that `world_mods.rbx` adds to Mod Config Remake's rows turns sharing on or off, and while it is on the creator's options of the world's mods become its settings as a save loads and as the options screen closes, at most 64,000 characters, as many as the relay takes (options past them are left out, logged and named in the notice). Creating and editing a world send no settings but the marker; the relay lets the creator and its admins replace them. A game applies only the settings of options a world may set (`world_option?`) in a mod's group of its own Mod Config, never the game's own options, key bindings or personal rows (`apply_values`).
- **Who decides.** `SharedSettingsSync` in the DLL keeps the world being entered and decides everything else; the rules and texts are `SharedSettings` and `SettingNotices` in `MGQOnline.Game`, with golden cases in `fixtures/mods/settings.json` (`ruby fixtures/mods/make_settings.rb`, recorded from `world_mods.rbx` before they left it). The script keeps the settings' codec (`encode`, `decode`, `settings_from`, which the battle server uses too), reading Mod Config (`menu_groups`, `option_pairs`, `options_table`), applying and locking, the row, and routing the world room's messages. It calls `settings.enter` (the world as the list has it, the player's id, whether the list is fresh), `settings.forget`, `settings.options` (this game's Mod Config groups, once a session, for the notices and the details' `settings.details`), `settings.loaded` (the options it applied and, in the creator's own world, their options of the world's mods and every option key), `settings.want` (`options` as the options screen closes, `switch` from the row), `settings.live`, `settings.observe`, `settings.told`, `settings.leave` and `settings.mods`; `settings.of`, `settings.confirmed`, `settings.shared` and `settings.marked` serve the world screen's forms. Every one of these answers the world as it stands (`id`, `name`, the creator, `mods`, `text`, its `crc`, `own`, `shared`, `latest_shared`), which the script keeps as `@world`, and the steps it is to play: `notify` (the notice already broken into the box's lines, 46 characters each, at most 3), `apply` or `unlock`, `apply_live` (whose options set go back through `settings.applied` for their notice), `broadcast` and `tell` (sent through `MGQ_MpOverworldSync.tell`), and `row`. Steps that come up in a frame of the DLL's own (an answer of the relay, a timer) mark `WorldChanges.Settings`, and `MGQ_MpWorldMods.tick` then takes them through `settings.step`.
- **The relay's latest settings win.** Before the creator's game sends settings, and before it tells another player whose state names other settings than its own (unless it read them less than 600 frames ago), the DLL reads the one world's settings on the relay (`WorldDirectory.ReadSettings`, `GET /v1/worlds/<id>` on a thread of its own, outside the actions). Settings someone else set there, such as an admin, become the world's, the creator's options and every player's; settings the creator's game wants meanwhile keep only what the creator changed, joined to the relay's (`SharedSettings.Merged`), and only the creator's own switch turns sharing on or off. The DLL keeps one directory action's answer for the world screen and the world's settings alike, so each claims the action it starts (`DirectoryTickets`, which only the DLL's own machines use) and takes only its own answer; a request starts only once nobody waits for the last answer, settings whose answer gave way to another request are sent again, settings that cannot be sent are tried again after 120 frames (the creator told once), and settings the relay never answers within 1,800 frames are named as failed. A world's settings are noted only as the world opens (`start_world`), so an entry refused for its data, a full world or a password leaves nothing behind, and the edit form starts from the settings the relay took from this game last while the list may be older (`settings.of`). The script asks no question of the world itself: a world noted counts as open.
- **Options for the catalog.** Mods build their Mod Config rows as they load, often from constants and method calls, so only a running game knows them. Whenever the world screen reads the list and the player is an admin, `report_options` asks the DLL which mods to send (topic `mods.report`, once a session per mod's current version: each catalog mod this game has installed, whatever its version, unless the relay holds the current version's options already or those of the version this copy is, the mod's `options_version`: the version its copy's hashes are, or an empty one for a copy that matches no version the catalog knows, such as a build of the admin's own; the relay keeps any version's options until the current version's arrive). The options are the same rows a world may set (`world_entries`), each with its name without the `[Mod]` prefix, its type and default from `NWConst::Config::DEFAULT`, and its choices from its `:values` (an array or a proc) or else `DATA`, named by `DATA_TEXT`, as tab-separated lines through the topic `mods.options`. A mod that offers no such rows sends an empty list, so the relay knows it offers none. The DLL posts them on a thread of their own (`SendModOptions`), outside the actions, and only logs the answer. The World Admin tool lists them in its mod settings dialog.

## Turn off other mods

A world whose `otherModsOff` is true is played with the mods it names and nothing else: as the player enters it, the Mod Framework loads only the mods of the world's session, those the world names (required `!`, essential `?` or only listed), those the relay's catalog marks personal and those every player has (`SessionMods.SharedKeys`: `patch`, `multiplayer` and `0modconfigremake`), besides the mods that run from the start of the game: the Framework mods, a Title mod that loads whole and a script the game's loader ran before the framework (`Mod_Framework::Mods.stays_on`), which nothing can take out again. Mods.ini does not change, and the session's mods stay as they are until the game starts again, which leaving the world for the title does under the framework. Nothing moves in the Patch folder any more.

**The setting.** The relay keeps it as the world's `otherModsOff` (see [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md)): sent with create (`WorldDirectory.Create`'s `otherModsOff`; the create form's *Turn off other mods* follows the type, on for a Raid World, until the player ticks it), changed by the creator or an admin with an edit (`WorldDirectory.Edit`'s `otherModsOff`, left out to leave it), listed as field 22 of the DLL's world line, which the DLL fills by type when the relay leaves it out (`ListedWorld#other_mods_off`). The details show *Other mods: turned off (2 of yours)* or *Other mods: allowed*, and the mods box adds *Your mods turned off here*, one line per mod with its scripts counted when it has several (`SessionMods.TurnedOff` over the scripts of the Patch folder, `PatchScripts`, each grouped by its mod: the folder right under `Patch`, else the script's own name).

**Personal mods.** The catalog may mark any mod personal, and may hold mods by their name alone (kind `name`, nothing to download), which exist only to be personal, such as someone else's cosmetic mod. The relay's list body carries every personal mod in `personal`; the DLL keeps them in `ModCatalog.Personal` and reads their keys and their names' keys (`ModTopics.PersonalKeys`, made by `ModKey`).

**Entering.** The DLL's steps into a world (`WorldEntering`) keep a game out of such a world while the catalog is unknown, since only the catalog tells which mods are personal ("The mod catalog has not loaded yet. Try again in a moment.", `CatalogAllows`). Once the steps start the world, the world screen (`start_world`) notes the mods of the world's session (`MGQ_MpWorldMods.note_session`), which `MGQ_MpWorldMods.enter_game` enters the game with through the framework right before the world's save loads or at the title right before its New Game: for a world that turns other mods off it asks `mods.session` with the world's name and mods, the mods that are on (`Mod_Framework::Mods.plan`) and those that run from the start (`stays_on`), and hands the answer's `only` to `Mods.enter` (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Stages.enter`); `SessionMods.Of` keeps the mods named, personal or every player's, in the framework's order. The notification box then tells the answer's `text` (`SessionMods.Entered`): "\<world> plays without your other mods: Invasions stays off until you leave it. Wide Text is part of the framework and stays on." While the catalog is unknown `mods.session` answers nil and every mod that is on loads. Once they loaded the DLL takes the Mod Config groups again (`settings.options`), those mods' options among them, and the world's settings apply as its save loads or its new game starts, so they reach those options.

**The rule changing.** An edit that changes `otherModsOff` or the mods makes the relay send the world room `mods_rule <0|1>` + tab + the mods (`modsRuleText`; Cloudflare through the world room's `/internal/mods-rule`, Node through `tellWorld`). The DLL (`WorldSession.TakeModsRule`) hands it to the script as the inbox entry `kind=mods_rule` with `value` and `mods`, ignoring and logging a value other than 0 or 1 or mods past 65,536 characters (`MaxModsRuleLength`). `overworld_sync.rbx` routes it to `MODS_RULE_FIELD`, marked `:relay`, and `MGQ_MpWorldMods.rule_pushed` takes the mods (`take_mods`) and asks `mods.now_off` with the session's mods, but those that run from the start (`SessionMods.NowTurnedOff`). When the world now turns off a mod the session runs, the notification box says "\<world> now turns off other mods. The game starts again and enters it without them." and after `KICK_FRAMES` (5 s), once the map is free (`map_free?`), the framework starts the game again into the world (`restart_for`, the action `multiplayer.rejoin`), which enters it with the session's mods.

**Mods an older release parked.** Until the Mod Framework, such a world moved the player's other scripts into the game folder's `Parked mods` and back. As the DLL starts (`mp_start`), `ParkedLeftovers.PutBack` moves any file an older release left below `Parked mods/Patch` back into `Patch` where no other file took its place, logs those that stay, and removes the journal and the folders once nothing is left; `Parked mods/Conflicts` stays for the player.

**Tests.** `SessionModsTests` covers the session's mods, the mods turned off for the details, the mods a changed rule turns off and the texts; `ModCheckTopicsTests` the topics `mods.session` and `mods.now_off`; `WorldEnteringTests` the wait for the catalog; `PatchScriptsTests` the scripts listed and the mods put back; `world_mods_test.rb` the script's entering with the session and the restart after a changed rule.

## On the map

`overworld_sync.rbx` is the scripts' side of the world room the DLL keeps (see [The world room](#the-world-room-worldroomtopics)); `overworld.rbx` shows what the player sees of it. Every frame in every scene, before `Graphics.update`, the sync hands the DLL the player's frame (`before_frame`); after it, the bridge's drain brings the room's events, which fill the sync's mirror of the other players (`Peers`), and the sync's tick lets the scripts follow (`on_tick`), so nothing piles up during a battle or in a menu.

- **What each game tells** (`Me`): one message of `key=value` lines with the player's id and name, the party leader's sprite and index (the vehicle's while in one), map, tile, facing, speed (`real_move_speed`, dashing included), whether the player is hidden, and what the player does (`Me.scene`): `battle`; `event` (a message or a running event on the map, or a novel scene, which requests and defeat scenes are); `away` while the game's window is not in front, unless in a battle or an event; a menu screen by its class name (`SCREENS`: `items`, `equip`, `shop`, `casino`, `library`, else `menu`); `sailing` or `flying` in a vehicle; or `map`. The DLL adds the player's id and name, when they entered their map, the ping it tells and the party's fields, and sends it to everyone whenever any of it changed, and only then, so a player standing still sends nothing: every incoming message counts against the relay's free plan.
- **The mirror** (`Peers`): a `Peer` per seat (`seat`, `state`, `ghost`, `member`, `away`), as the DLL's events tell: `world.peer` makes or updates it and calls `on_observe`, `world.peer_away` marks it away (its ghost hidden), `world.peer_moved` moves it to its new seat, `world.peer_displaced` keeps it aside once another player tells from its seat while it is away, as when the relay deals the seats anew (the DLL keeps it for the rest of its grace, the same player once it tells from another seat; `Peers.kept_with_id` finds a kept player by id), `world.peer_left` forgets it and calls `on_leave`; each change of a player's presence goes to the scripts' `on_presence` (`:away`, `:back`, `:moved`, `:gone`), the one source the battles follow; the party's view sets `member`, `world.labels` the names that tell players apart (`labeled`, `label_of`, `named`). `Peers.present` lists those whose connection stands, `Peers.with_id` finds one, `Peers.on_this_map?` tells one on the player's map. On the battle server, which sits in no world's room, the battle channel tells the players' presence: `Peers.wait_for` keeps one away for `REJOIN_FRAMES`, `Peers.come_back` takes one back, and `Peers.tick` forgets who stayed away. A routed message (`world.route`) goes to the script registered for its field (`hand_over`), with its body under `:payload` and `:relay` for the relay's push, which no player's message can carry; notices of the DLL (`world.notice`) show as the scripts' do.
- **Ghosts** (`Game_MpGhost`, a `Game_Character` with `@through`): one per player on the current map, made where they stand. Every frame on the map (`Game_Map#update`) it takes their sprite, speed and visibility, and walks one tile toward where they stand, along the longer axis first, at their speed; more than `CATCH_UP_TILES` (3) away, or after a map change, it moves there at once. It triggers nothing, since only the player and events check triggers.
- **Sprites** (`Spriteset_Map#update`): a `Sprite_Character` and a `Sprite_MpGhostLabel` per ghost, made and freed as ghosts come and go. The label shows the name, and before it an icon of the game's icon set for what the player does (`STATE_ICONS`): battle 451 (crossed swords), event 4 (speech bubble), typing 4 (the same; `MGQ_MpActions.scene` says so while the chat box is open), menu 183 (open book), items 3059 (potion), equip 3905 (hammer), shop 3874 (coin), casino 220 (cards), library 3240 (red book), sailing 4069 (anchor), flying 3836 (wing), away 6 (Zzz); none while walking. `Sprite_MpWorldStatus` at the bottom left shows the last notices for 4 s each and, from the DLL's `world.status`, "Reconnecting . . .", "Connecting . . ." or why the connection failed.
- **Pings** (`Ping`): the DLL's `world.status` brings the player's own ping, shown right above their head (`Sprite_MpOwnPing`), and after the name on each ghost's label the ping its player told; the DLL tells the others only a ping that moved noticeably, so jitter sends nothing. Colors: up to 100 ms green, up to 200 ms yellow, then red (`PING_COLORS`). `ui_actions.rbx` keeps `HEAD_ROOM` free above the head for it. Labels of characters on one tile stack upwards (`Sprite_MpGhostLabel#show` takes the room the labels before it took and returns its own), above the player's own ping on the player's tile, so two players standing together no longer draw their names over each other.
- **Registry**: the scripts that load after the sync join it at load time, each in a `begin` block before its hooks: `route(field, gate)` for their messages (the DLL gets every route with its gate), `state_fields` to add to the state message, `busy_scene` for a state of their own (the chat's `typing`), `on_tick` for every frame, `on_observe` and `on_leave` to hear of every state and every player leaving, `on_presence` for each change of a player's presence, and `label_line` for the line above a ghost's name. A failing block is logged once and left out; the others still run. Every state carries the marker field `state=1` (`STATE_FIELD`), which no route may be named like: a message with it is a state, any other goes to its route, so a state field may share a route's name. No message carries two marker fields, so the order of the routes decides nothing.

## The world never pauses (`core_async.rbx`)

In single player the map stops whenever another screen is in front: a menu, a shop, a battle or a story (novel) scene. In a world it goes on, so what others see of the player and what the player comes back to never waits for them.

- **Behind which screens** (`behind?`): every screen but the map itself, the title and the game over, while a world is open and a map waits on `SceneManager`'s stack. The map clears that stack whenever it starts, so a map on it is the one the player left and returns to; screens reached from the title never qualify.
- **What runs** (`tick`, from a `Scene_Base#update_basic` hook, which every screen's frame calls, battles and novel scenes included): `$game_map.update(false)`, so events move, background (parallel) events and common events run, ghosts walk (`overworld.rbx` hooks the same method), and the map's screen effects go on; and `$game_timer`, except in a battle, which runs it itself. The player does not move, and the map's main event, which drives whatever the player is in, never runs here.
- **What waits** (`hold?`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook): while the tick runs, a command in `HELD_CODES` yields its interpreter's fiber until the map runs it again: messages, choices and inputs (101-105), party changes (129), transfers and vehicles (201, 206), music, sounds and movies (241-246, 249, 261), everything in 301-399 (battles, shops, name input, menu, save, game over, title, and actors' changes that would reach into a running battle), and scripts (355), which may do any of these. An event that starts by touching the player, or autoruns, only marks itself as starting; the main event picks it up once the player is back on the map. Events outside the tick, such as a battle's own, never wait.
- **Field music**: the game's map refresh plays the field's music when a switch changed it (`need_refresh_autoplay_field`), which would cut into a battle's, so the tick leaves that refresh to the map.
- **The live map** (`Spriteset_MpLiveMap`): a menu whose background is the picture the map took on leaving (`SceneManager.background_bitmap`) shows a `Spriteset_Map` instead, updated every frame, its viewports below everything the menu draws (z -300 to -200) and dimmed like the picture. Menus that make their background themselves, such as the casino and the warp screen, keep theirs, and so do the save and load screens with their subclasses (`PICTURE_SCENES`), which build a window and a thumbnail per save file. A menu that closes for another menu hands its live map over (`leave_live_map`), so going from one menu to the next builds no second `Spriteset_Map`, and one the next screen does not take is freed once it started (`Scene_Base#post_start`). A new live map is built only while at least `LIVE_MAP_FREE_MB` (768) of the game's address space is free, as `mp_memory_usage` tells; with less, the menu keeps its picture, since a game of 2 GB address space closed in RGSS301.dll as menus built live maps with about 600 MB left. The mod's HUD (boxes, logs, wheels, prompts and bars) leaves the live map out (`Spriteset_Map#mgq_mp_hud?`, false for `Spriteset_MpLiveMap`), while players, characters and chat bubbles show.
- **No freeze between the map and menus** (`instant_switch?`, `leave`): leaving a screen freezes its picture (`Graphics.freeze` in `Scene_Base#terminate`), and the next screen fades in from it (`Graphics.transition` in `post_start`, 10 frames, the map 15). Nothing updates meanwhile, and even a transition of no frames takes about 117 ms (measured in game), so the world stood still for a moment at every menu. While a world is open, switching between the map and menus skips both: the screen keeps its last picture until the next screen draws its first. Battles, story scenes and the title keep their fades. `leave` logs every switch with the memory and the handles the game uses (`memory_usage`, `mp_memory_usage`), since a crash in the game's native code ends it unlogged.

## Parties (`coop.rbx`)

**The party** (`Party`) is the DLL's (see [The world room](#the-world-room-worldroomtopics)); `MGQ_MpCoop::Party` reads the view the DLL posts (`take_view`) many times a frame and asks the DLL for the player's actions (`invite`, `stop_inviting`, `join`, `decline`, `leave`, `remove`), each answering the view. Every state message carries the player's party id (`party`), whom its leader admitted (`party_members`) and whether they invite (`invite`, `invite_to` for players named from afar), so a party needs no message of its own. Inviting lasts 15 s from the wheel in `ui_actions.rbx`, or a minute once it names a player from afar (from the World overview), who sees it in their notification box (`ui_notices.rbx`), and makes a party of one with a new id, which the invite line above the player's own head (`Sprite_MpOwnLine`, its seconds from the view's `invite_ends` and the bridge's frame) and above their ghost on the others' screens shows. Accepting takes the inviter's id; the leader admits a player who names it only while the invite reaches them, and every member takes the leader's list. Ghosts outside the party are see-through in a Classic world (`STRANGER_OPACITY`) and solid in a Raid World, where everyone on the map shares it (`ghost_opacity`, `SOLID_OPACITY`); party members' names are green in both. `Party.members` (`member?` by the view's `member_ids`), `Party.leader` and `Party.invited_by?` (the view's `inviters`) are what the party scripts below build on.

**The party's messages** (`route`, `tell`). The party scripts (`coop_events.rbx`, `coop_npcs.rbx`, `coop_story.rbx` and `battles_coop.rbx`) register their fields with `MGQ_MpCoop.route`, which registers the same field with `overworld_sync.rbx` behind the DLL's party gate: a message is handed on only from a member of the party and with the party's own id; the scripts keep only checks of their own, such as who the Map Owner or the leader is. `MGQ_MpCoop.tell(seat, field, value, fields)` adds the party's id to what they send, in Ruby, since the battle server sends through it too.

A party holds at most four players (`MAX_PLAYERS`), one per place of the Frontline; the wheel refuses to invite into a full party or to accept the invite of one (`Party.full?`, from the view's `parties`), and the DLL refuses the join and turns away whoever joins one too many. Duels and trades keep their own invites in `MGQ_MpCoop::Invite` (frames and targets), whose `Invite.reaches_me?` is their near-or-named check with `Party.near?`. Only the leader invites more players (`Party.may_invite?`), and only the leader removes a member (`Party.remove`, from the World overview).

**Teleports between members** (`MGQOnline.Game/Teleports/TeleportRequests.cs` behind `TeleportTopics`, read by `coop_teleport.rbx`, Classic and Raid Worlds alike). *Teleport to \<leader>* (`MGQ_MpCoopTeleport.option`, offered by `MGQ_MpCoop::Offers.teleport_option` on the wheel in place of the invite) and *Ask to teleport to \<name>* in each member's row of the World overview ask through the player's state: `tpask=<member id>:<request number>` (`tp.ask` with the member's seat; a newer request replaces the one before). The leader's DLL allows a member's request in the frame it sees it (`AllowWaiting`, each drain); any other member allows or declines it in the notification box ("\<name> asks to teleport to you", *Allow*) or with *Allow teleport* in the asker's row (`tp.allow` with the seat and the script's `place_fields`, `tp.decline`). The answer goes through the party's gate as `ptp=allow` with the place (`map`, `x`, `y`, `d`, `warp_ban`) or `ptp=deny`, each with the request's `nonce` and each request answered once; the asker's DLL takes it through its own route (`WorldRoom.Take`), ignores an answer to another or older request, and posts `tp.allowed` ({name, map, x, y, d, warp_ban}), on which the script calls `MGQ_MpCoopGather.come_to` (which waits up to a minute for a busy asker), unless the player stands within 3 tiles of the place already (`GatherTiles`); its notices come as `tp.notice`. A request lapses after a minute of the bridge's frames (`AskFrames`, "\<name> did not answer your teleport."), ends once its member left the party, *Stop asking to teleport* on the player's own row takes it back (`tp.stop`), and a loaded save (`tp.forget` from the `extract_save_contents` hook) or the world's close forgets it with the requests answered. Each frame in a world the script hands the player's place with `tp.view` and keeps the view it answers once it changed (nil while it did not; every action answers it too): `tpask` for the state, the request's doing (`ends`, the frame it lapses at), the own row's choice and each member's texts and request. The golden cases, `fixtures/teleport/cases.json`, were recorded from the script of eae042b7 by `ruby fixtures/teleport/make_fixtures.rb` and are replayed through the room's party gate by `TeleportRequestsTests`.

## Party or map (`coop_scope.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpCoop::Scope` tells `coop_npcs.rbx`, `coop_castle.rbx`, `coop_events.rbx`, `coop_gather.rbx` and `coop_scene.rbx` whom the player shares the map and the story with. Each question goes to `Scope::Classic` or `Scope::Raid`, by `MGQ_MpWorld.raid?` (`Scope.raid?`, false while `world.rbx` cannot tell, outside worlds and in Classic worlds), so Classic worlds behave as before.

| | Classic | Raid |
|---|---|---|
| `peers_here` | party members on the player's map | everyone on the player's map |
| `sync_host` (Map Owner of `coop_npcs.rbx`) | lowest `[since, id]` of the player and `peers_here`; the castle's maps: the party's leader (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.owner`) | lowest `[since, id]` of the player and `peers_here`, the castle's maps too |
| `teller` | the party's leader | the player while telling; else of the players whose telling the relay told of on the player's track (`Raid.tellers`, the DLL's status `tellings`, see [Story tracks](#story-tracks)), told on the player's map (or the map the story takes the player to), the one whose story takes the player along (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.followed_id`) or whose pages the player follows (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.heard_from`); else the one whose story the player follows to where it moved them, on that map, whether or not the relay tells of their telling (`Raid.followed_on_the_way`, from `MGQ_MpCoopGather.follow_taken`); else the lowest `[since, id]` |
| `viewers` | the synced members (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.synced_members`) | the players on the map, and those the story took along who have not arrived yet (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.taken_along`), whose state's `trk` names the player's track (`same_track?`) |
| `story_from?(peer)` | `peer` is the leader | `peer` is the teller |
| `watches?(peer)` | `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.following?` | the relay told of `peer`'s telling, which it does for the tellings on the player's track alone |
| `telling?(peer)` | `peer`'s state says `telling=1` | the relay told of `peer`'s telling on the player's track |
| `follows_teller?` / `leads_story?` | `follows_leader?` / `leading_synced?` of `coop_story.rbx` | another player is the teller / nobody else is |
| `sharing?` | `MGQ_MpCoop.in_party?` | a world is open |
| message gate | `route` (same party) | `route_map` (same map) |

- **The map's gate** (`MGQ_MpCoop.route_map`): a script registers its field, and the DLL's map gate listens on `map_<field>`, so the party's gate and the map's never take each other's messages. A message is handed on (under its plain field as well) only from a known player who is on the player's map by their state, or whose message names the player's map, or the map the story takes the player to (`story_map` of the player's frame), in `mmap`; a message whose `mto` names other players' ids alone is dropped. `npcs`, `pevent` and `pscene` are registered with both gates; the party chat, removing a member, invites and duels keep the party's gate in both.
- **Sends** (`Scope.tell`, `Scope.tell_each`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map`, `MGQ_MpCoop.tell_map_to`): in a Raid World a message to everyone (`-1`) goes out once to the whole world while anyone else is on the player's map, and the map's gate of every game sorts it; a message to several players (the story's `say`, `done`, `pscene` and `follow`) goes out once too, naming their ids in `mto`, to one player to their seat. The relay ends a connection that sends more than 60 messages a second, which one send per seat ran past on a busy map. It carries `map_<field>=<value>`, `mmap=<map id>` (the player's map unless the sender names another, as the story's `follow` does with the map it left) and no party id. A Classic world's `Scope.tell_each` sends a lone member a message to their seat and several one `MGQ_MpCoop.tell(-1, ...)` through the party's gate, which a member it is not for ignores (`story_from?`, `watches?`), since a party of four sent every page and scene change three times.
- **The story in a Raid World** (`coop_events.rbx`): a story event starts only while no other player on the map tells the story (`hand_over`, notice "\<name> is telling the story here.") and never while the player follows a teller's story to where it moved them (`held_following`, see *Gathering in a Raid World* below); a talk or travel turning into story ends the same way (`guard`). A player telling the story says `telling=1` in their state, alone on the map too; who sees it is the relay's word: their pages (`say`, `done`) and scene changes (`pscene`) go to `viewers` alone, the players on the map who play their track (`trk` in every state, from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.state_fields`), and only a player whom the relay told of the telling takes them (`watches?`) and stands still while the scene plays on their map (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.blocked?`), but not while the teller fights the story's battle (their state's `scene` is `battle`): a player who came after its invites would otherwise neither move nor join it by choice until it ended. A player whose story differs sees the NPCs move (the Map Owner's events on other pages stay their own) but not the scene. Who tells on a map is the relay's telling lock, one per map and track (Relay/README.md "Telling locks"): a new telling asks for it as its event starts (`start_telling`, the topic `telling.start` of the DLL's `TellingMachine`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)), and the event waits at its first command until the relay answered (`lock_holding?`, `telling.command`, before every command of the map's interpreter, after `guard`, so a talk that turns into story waits at the command that moves the story on): granted, it runs; refused, since another game tells there already, it ends before it changed anything with "\<name> is telling the story here.". A refusal that names no teller (as before the relay placed the player on a track), no answer within `TellingMachine.LockHoldFrames` (3 s) or a closed world room lets the event run without the lock (`unlocked`), so a story event never waits for good. The other games on the track hear of the telling before the teller hears its grant, so they know the teller before its first page. A telling goes on at the story's next event in two cases, with its start and `taken_along`, which the machine tells `start_telling` (`continued`): an event that starts while the telling ran as the last frame ended, and, when the telling moved its teller onto another map (`moving`, before `Game_Player#perform_transfer`, `telling.moving`), an event there that runs by itself (an autorun map event or a common event a switch starts, `by_itself?`) within `TellingMachine.ContinueFrames` (5 s) of the move, since the game's transfer process, the player's own, runs between them and the next event may wait for the screen to fade in; a story event the player starts there is a new telling. The lock moves to the new map as the transfer lands (`arrived`, after `Game_Player#perform_transfer`, `telling.arrived`), while the telling's event may still run there, so the next event goes on without a hold. The lock goes back once the telling's event ended (`finished`, `telling.end`) and no event may go on with it any more. Any other telling is new. While telling, `state_fields` also says `twb`, whether warping is banned where the player stands. Chests stay personal (`shares_chests?`): a Raid World's parties share no loot, and a `chest` message is ignored there. The pages a player follows are forgotten once another player tells (`forget_former_leader`, by `page_source_id`), and a page ends once its teller left or the relay ended their telling (`page_teller`, `page_done?`; a Classic leader's 2 s after their state stopped saying they tell, `STALE_FRAMES`, since a state and pages travel apart). In any world, a screen that stays fully dark for `DARK_SCREEN_FRAMES` (5 s), or `WATCHED_DARK_FRAMES` (10 s) while the player watches another player's scene, while the player stands free on the map (no event running or starting, no message, no transfer, no change of a watched scene that reached the screen a moment ago, `MGQ_MpCoopScene.just_changed?`, no opening's setup, and no event running alongside the map that fades in itself, `stuck_dark?`) fades in (`lift_dark_screen`), as after a game over that returned the player to an earlier point whose arrival scene another player's game had run.
- **Gathering in a Raid World** (`coop_gather.rbx`): nobody is gathered (`gathered?` is always true): whoever is on the map watches. A transfer of the teller's story calls everyone who was on the map, and those an earlier transfer of the same telling took who have not arrived yet (`after_transfer`: `pevent=follow` with `mmap` the map it left and `moves`, 0 for a telling that does not move the world's story on; `take_raid` takes only `follow`). The teller notes the values that move the world's story on as the telling starts (`note_story_progress`, `MGQ_MpWorldStory.progress_now`); the telling moves it on (`moves_story?`) once one of them changed, or while the commands its interpreter has yet to run may set one (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.progress_ahead?`, through common events up to `PROGRESS_CALL_DEPTH` deep), since a talk that sets values of its own and moves its teller, as a minigame does, is sorted as story too. A receiver follows at once only while it watches that story (`Scope.watches?(teller)`), is not behind the world's story (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.behind?`) and the telling moves the world's story on, a follow without `moves` counting as one that does, or its game keeps its own copy of the telling's story event that runs by itself from starting (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.keeps_autorun_of?`, noted by `hand_over` within the last `KEPT_AUTORUN_FRAMES`), since that copy would start and play the scene again the moment the teller left; every other receiver gets an offer in the notification box (`MGQ_MpCoopGather::Offers`, key `[:story_follow, seat]`): "\<name>'s story moved on to \<place or map id>. Follow?", which Y takes through `followed` and N dismisses. The offer drops once the teller's telling ends, after `GatherCalls.FollowOfferFrames` (60 s), once the player is on that map, or when the world closes. A follow, taken at once or accepted, waits `GatherCalls.AskedLapseFrames` (60 s) for a busy player, since the story calls once and the teller's pages, a battle or a menu keep a player busy longer than `GatherCalls.CallLapseFrames`. While the player is on the way, and for `CallLapseFrames` after they arrived (`GatherCalls.FollowTaken`), `follow_taken` names the follow: `followed_id` and `story_map?` read it, so the teller's pages from the new map reach the player, `Scope::Raid.teller` keeps that teller while their state says they tell nothing for a moment (between the story's events, while the game's transfer process runs, `followed_on_the_way`), and `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.hand_over` keeps the player's own story events from starting meanwhile (`held_following`), so their own copy of the story starts neither on the map they leave nor just after they arrived. Until they show up on the teller's map, or the telling ends, they stay among the `viewers` (`taken_along`, forgotten when a new telling starts, not by the story's next event), so the scene changes and pages told meanwhile reach them. *Teleport to the story* on the player's own row of the World overview (`MGQ_MpCoop::Offers.story_option`) goes to `MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_endpoint` once the player is free (`join_story`); it is closed to a player behind the world's story (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.holds_story?`), who could not play it there: the endpoint the relay keeps of the world's story (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.endpoint`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)), whose warp ban this game does not know, so the player keeps their own; while the relay keeps none, where this game last saw the story told (`note_story_place`, forgotten when the world closes), with the warp ban there (`story_warp_ban`): the player's own `warp_ban?` for their own telling, the teller's `twb` for another's; without `twb` the player keeps their own.
- **The party's story** (`coop_story.rbx`) is off in a Raid World: `MGQ_MpCoopStory.leader` is nil, `follows_leader?` false and `synced_members` empty, so no member borrows a leader's story and no leader tells one; a member who borrowed one gets their own back. The world's story takes its place (`world_story.rbx`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)), built on the same state model (`story_state.rbx`, `MGQ_MpStoryState`): `mix` (which takes the chests to keep), `story_lists`, `pack`, `unpack`, `decode_full`, `raw_state` and `set_raw`.

## Squads (`coop_squad.rbx`)

A party shares one single-player team: each player keeps a **squad** of their first characters, and the rest are cut. `share(position, count, max)` splits the Frontline's 4 places and the Backline's `max - 4` (`max` is the player's own `party_member_max`, 8 plus variable 56) between the players, one more each for the first ones while some are left over; the players are ordered the same way on every game (`ranked`): the leader first, then by player id, compared byte by byte. Outside a party, or alone in one, nothing is cut. The math of this section is C#'s (`SquadMath` in `MGQOnline.Game/Squads/`), since the battle server arranges its players too: the script asks the topics both hosts answer (`squad.shares`, `squad.rank`, `squad.raid`, `squad.arrange`, `squad.past_cap`, `squad.default`, see `SquadTopics`) and keeps each answer by its request (`ask`, up to `KEPT_ANSWERS`), since the game's hooks ask for the squad many times a frame; it reads the game's objects and applies the answers itself. `fixtures/squad/squad.json` holds what the script's own math answered before it left. **The squad view** (`SquadView` in `MGQOnline.Game/Squads/`, behind `SquadViewTopics`, the DLL alone since it reads the party): `tick` asks `squad.view` once a frame in a world with the player's `party_member_max`, their own choice of followers, whether a Raid World is open, whether the battle is a raid and the world's squad rules, and keeps the answer, which `own_share`, `mode` and `squads?` read (and through `own_share` `place_at`, `place_of` and the World overview's Frontline level): the share (`SquadMath.RaidShare` in a Raid World, else the player's place among the party's players ranked as above, nil outside a party), the followers' mode (the leader's state's `follow`, the player's own while they lead or play outside a party) and whether squads split. Outside a world the view is none, which cuts nothing. `SquadViewTests` covers it; `coop_squad_test.rb` reads it from the stand-in of `party_support.rb`. Nothing here changes the game's party: a battle the player fights alone brings their whole team, and `party_member_max` and `max_battle_members` stay the game's, except in a Raid World (below). Variable 56 is the player's own in `story_state.rbx`, since the game cuts its party list down to `party_member_max` on every change, so a leader's smaller value would drop a member's characters.

- **Followers** (`Game_Follower#visible?`, `follower_shown?`): in a party only the player's share of the Frontline (a follower's `@member_index` below the share; the trail sent to the others lists only those shown), or nobody in a party whose leader chose *Leaders Only* (`mode`, the option *Party Followers* in `$game_system.conf[:mp_party_followers]`, registered in the Mod Config Remake's `MOD_CONTENTS` or the game's `CONTENTS`; members read the leader's from their state's `follow` and may not change it). `tick` keeps the share and the mode of the frame's view, which `follower_shown?` reads many times a frame without the bridge, and refreshes `$game_player` once either changed.
- **Ghosts' followers**: every state carries `trail`, the looks of the followers the player shows (`name*index|...`); `Game_MpGhost` in `overworld.rbx` keeps a `Game_MpGhostFollower` per look while the player is a party member, each stepping onto where the one before it stood, before the ghost steps, as the game's followers do, and gathered when the ghost jumps.
- **The formation screens** (`place_of`, `place_at`): both hooks wrap the game's own `draw_item`, so the game's and the translation's layouts stay, and only change how a row draws by its place in the squad. The menu's formation list (`Window_MenuStatus`) draws the squad's Frontline faces opaque and its Backline's translucent, as the game draws the Backline (`draw_actor_face`), and the cut characters monochrome: faces and icons through `monochrome` (a grey copy per face and icon, made once per session, since `get_pixel`/`set_pixel` over every pixel is slow) and texts and gauges through `text_color` and `grey`. The party edit screen (`Foo::PTEdit::Window_PartyMember`) colors rows through `change_color`: Frontline normal, Backline the system color, cut grey. Both draw a line above the first cut row.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`, from `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) every player has a squad, in a party or not (`squads?`), sized by the world's squad rules (`rules`: `MGQ_MpWorld.squad`, `default_rules` (`squad.default`) when the world names none; `:luka`, `:formation` 4..8 or `ORIGINAL` 0, `:front`, `:raid_front`). The team splits (`SquadMath.Layout`): the Frontline the rules give for the fight (`raid_fight?`: in battle against a troop of `MGQ_MpRaidBosses.boss?`), the Backline what is left but one cut place, which exists only while a place is left over and never with `ORIGINAL`. `raid_share(max, raid)` is that split for the player's `party_member_max` (`raid_sizes`, `squad.raid`); `own_share` is it, so the formation screens draw the line above the cut place and it monochrome. Co-op battles take it through `SquadMath.BattleShare`: the battle keeps `FRONTLINE` (4) places split between up to `RAID_PLAYERS` (4) players, each player's Frontline is the smaller of their rules' Frontline and their split (at least 1, so everyone's first character fights), and the rest of their squad goes to the Backline; `own_build` sends `largest_squad` (the larger squad of a regular fight and a raid), and the host's `arrange` cuts it. `share` stays as it is for Classic worlds and team duels, though a team duel takes only the squad (`team_build`, `raid_team`). Nobody follows on the map (`follower_shown?` is false for every follower, so the `trail` is empty). The relay's `squad` push (`MGQ_MpOverworldSync::SQUAD_FIELD`, see `pushed`) changes the rules live, with a notice, and the next `tick` refits the team.
  - **The team** (`fit_team`, `party_member_max`, `team_cap`): the game cuts its party list (a `Game_Party::BattleMember`) down to `party_member_max` on every change; a cut companion stays in `include_actors` and so waits on standby (the Pocket Castle). `party_member_max` is capped at the Formation size (never with `ORIGINAL`) only for the `$game_party` that `tick` fitted to the current rules (`@capped`, `@capped_rules`): on entering a Raid World, when the rules change, and for every new game or loaded save there (a new `Game_Party`). Fitting lifts the cap first, brings Luka back from standby unless he may leave (`luka_back`), sends the companions past the Formation size to standby (`add_stand_actor`, then `move_stand_actor`, Luka kept unless he may leave, `past_cap`), and swaps Luka into the squad's last place when he stood past it (`luka_into_squad`, again only while he must stay), so nothing depends on the order of the game's own cut. A team in a battle waits. A story's temporary party (`temp_actors_use?`) is its own standby list, so the cap never holds for it (a cut character would leave the story) and is lifted while it plays (`lift_cap`); the team is fitted again once it ends. Outside a Raid World the cap is lifted. A companion joining the full team, a recruit or `$game_party.add_actor`, lands on standby by the game's own cut (a recruit after the game's "The party is full" choice); items raising variable 56 change nothing below the cap.
  - **Battles of the player's own and duels** (`raid_battle?`: in battle, no co-op party formed, no team duel): `max_battle_members` is the fight's Frontline and `bench_members` the Backline, both kept to the squad's places of the team (`in_squad`, since the game skips hidden members such as an eaten one; `own_order` is the existing members among the same places, so `raid_team` never brings the cut place either), so the cut character never fights, not even as a reserve, and a skill reaching the Backline too (`item_target_members`) reaches only the squad. The game's Backline window and swap count from `max_battle_members`, which is why the Frontline is narrowed there. This covers a player left alone in a co-op battle (`go_solo`, `take_over`), who fights on with their own squad. A duel's team (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Team.build`) is the squad, `raid_team`, and its Frontline is the regular fight's (`pvp_front`), so without the Backline only those fight. Outside a battle `max_battle_members` stays 4.
  - **Luka** (`install_luka`): while `luka_free?`, the party edit screen enables Luka's row (`free_luka_rows`) and sends him to standby like anyone else (`move_luka`). In every Raid World the game never swaps him to the team's first place: `command_216` (followers on or off, which story and defeat scenes run) runs with `Game_Party#swap_order` held back (`followers_command`, `holding_order?`), except for a story's temporary party, and `BattleManager.can_giveup?` is false unless Luka leads (`luka_leads?`), since Give Up swaps him to the front.

## NPCs in a party (`coop_npcs.rbx`)

In a Classic world the party members on the same map share its NPCs, and players outside a party share none: each game moves its own, as in single player. In a Raid World everyone on the map shares them, party or not (see [Party or map](#party-or-map-coop_scoperbx)). Of those sharing a map, one game moves the map's events and the others follow it. Who shares the map and who its Map Owner is are the script's; what the Map Owner sent and to whom, and what a follower takes, are the DLL's (`MGQOnline.Game/Npcs/NpcSync.cs` behind `NpcTopics`), which the script tells each frame while anyone shares the map, and once more when nobody does: `npcs.update` with the map, the Map Owner's seat (-1 for the player, nil for nobody) and, for the Map Owner, when each player sharing it came onto it (`arrivals`, seat to `since`), answered with whether the DLL needs the map's events now (`snapshot`) and the revision of the events followed.

- **The Map Owner** (`owner`): of the players sharing a map (`peers_here`, `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.peers_here`: the party members on it in a Classic world, everyone on it in a Raid World), the one who entered it first (`Scope.sync_host`). Every state message carries `since`, when the player entered their map (`Me.map_since`, milliseconds by that computer's clock), so every game finds the same Map Owner without asking: the earliest `since`, the lower player id among equals. On the Pocket Castle's maps of a Classic world it is the party's leader while they are there (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.owner`, see The Pocket Castle under [Events in a party](#events-in-a-party-coop_eventsrbx)). It starts anew once the player left the world, loaded a save or started a new game (`Me.reset_map_since`), so a returning player never takes the Map Owner at once; a reconnect keeps it. On the title screen, which keeps the previous game's map, and while a world's new game waits there (`before_map?`), the game tells nobody a state. A player whose connection is down counts no longer (`peers_here` leaves them out), so they never hold the events still. It changes as players come and go. It has nothing to do with who created the world.
- **The Map Owner's game** moves events as the game does; when the DLL asks, the script hands it every event of the map (`send_events`, `npcs.snapshot`, `id:x,y,d,page;...`), and the DLL sends, at most every 15 frames (`SendFrames`, counted by the frames the script tells) and only when something changed, each changed event's x, y, facing and page index (`-1` for none, as when erased): `npcs=<map id>`, `full=0`, `events=<id>:<x>,<y>,<d>,<page>;...` to everyone through the scope's gate (`Scope.tell`): in a Classic world the party's, with `party=<party id>`; in a Raid World the map's, as `map_npcs=<map id>` with `mmap` (in a Raid World a message to everyone goes out only while anyone else is on the map). A player who comes onto the map gets every event (`full=1`) to their seat at once (`SendWhole`), noted by their `since` once it went out; one that failed is sent again on the beat until it does, logged once per arrival, since the follower moves no event before it. The events whose page changed in what went to everyone come back (`turned`), and the script logs them by their names. The others block events walking on their own there (`Game_Event#collide_with_characters?`), never a forced route nor any event while the story this game tells plays (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.telling?`), since a story waits for the routes it moves its events on and a player in the way would hold it up for good, and events that approach the player go for the nearest of them (`near_the_player?`, `move_toward_player`). Touching another player's ghost starts nothing, since only the player starts touch events.
- **The others' games**: the DLL takes the Map Owner's messages through its own routes (`WorldRoom.Take`, `npcs` past the party's gate, `map_npcs` past the map's), drops them at once while the player's game moves its own events, and checks the rest in the next frame against the map and the Map Owner the script names, after a new map started anew, so a Map Owner who changed in the same frame counts and a whole map that came with the move is kept; the script reads the events to follow when their revision changed (`npcs.targets`, `{id => [x, y, d, page]}`, and whether a whole map came). The script (`follow`) stops events moving on their own (`update_self_movement`), and walk each event toward its place, one step at a time within 3 tiles (`MGQ_MpOverworld::CATCH_UP_TILES`), at once farther away or after 60 frames stuck (`STUCK_FRAMES`); a full picture places every event at once. Every map the player enters starts anew, as a new part does (`start_anew` in the script, the DLL's own on a new map), and a follower takes no part of a map before its full picture (the DLL's whole map): the last map's places on the new map's events of the same ids put Hades' exit into a wall once. An event talking to the player, moved by the player's own events, or on another page than the Map Owner's, which means another story state, stays where it is. While the map's interpreter runs an event the player plays in their own game (`playing?`: their own story or an autorun, not another player's story they watch), the follower moves no event, since that event's move routes wait for events the Map Owner's places would hold elsewhere for good; once it ended, every event moves to its place at once (`snap`). An event walking into the player there starts its touch event there.
- It runs from a `Game_Map#update` hook, so behind menus and battles too (see [The world never pauses](#the-world-never-pauses-core_asyncrbx)).
- **Tests:** the golden cases, `fixtures/npcs/cases.json`, were recorded from the script of eae042b7 by `ruby fixtures/npcs/make_fixtures.rb` (who shares the map and its Map Owner given per step) and are replayed through the room's gates by `NpcSyncTests`; `coop_npcs_test.rb` covers the script's side on the stand-ins of `party_support.rb`.

## The party's story (`coop_story.rbx`)

The party's story plays in Classic worlds alone, built on the story's state model, `story_state.rbx` (`MGQ_MpStoryState`), which a Raid World's story shares: the story read and written as raw data, what is each player's own (by the table of `story_keys.rbx`), a story joined with the player's own values, written for a message and read back, and the story's skills, companions and items granted. Outside a party every player plays their own story in their own world save. In a party, the members synced with the leader play the leader's: the player who made the party (`Party.leader`: the member whose id starts the party's id, else the lowest id while they are gone).

- **Who follows the leader's story** (`sync_state`, `check_sync`, `follows_leader?`, `synced_members`): every player tells how far their own story is in their state (`sm`: variables 1001, 1141, 1142, 1143; a member who borrows the story of a leader they are ahead of tells their own). Before the Great Decision (1001 below 40, `GREAT_DECISION`) two players are synced while at most 2 apart in 1001 (`SYNC_STEPS`); past it, while both are at 40 on the same route (the first of 1141-1143 above 0; the Great Decision itself sets 1143 to 1) at most 5 apart on it (`ROUTE_SYNC_STEPS`). Anything else is not synced: too far apart, one on each side of the Great Decision (as a player reset to it against one on a route), or different routes. The member decides after every map update, again whenever either side's progress changes, and tells the leader whose story they follow in their state (`ssync`: the leader's id, empty for none); a member who keeps the leader's story stays synced, as their story moves with the leader's. A member not synced plays their own story completely: no borrowing, catch-up or gifts (the leader's story messages are ignored, nothing waits), their own story events run (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.following?`), and the leader's pages, scene changes (sent to `synced_members` alone) and gathering never reach them. Chests, the map, co-op battles, chat, trading, duels and the castle are shared all the same. Notices: "You and \<leader> are too far apart in the story; each of you plays your own.", "... are on different sides of the Great Decision; each of you plays your own story.", "... are on different routes; each of you plays your own story." for the member and the same with the member's name for the leader, who also reads "\<member> follows your story while in the party."; a member who starts following reads the borrowing notice "You follow \<leader>'s story while in the party."

- **What is borrowed** (`MGQ_MpStoryState`): the story as the game keeps it, `$game_switches`, `$game_variables` and `$game_self_switches`, read and written as raw data (`data_of`, `MGQ_MpGame.get` and `set` of `:data`). The game's own `[]=` would run its side effects: a switch in 1001-2000 adds or removes that companion, an awakening switch refreshes an actor, an affection variable writes through to `$game_global_system`.
- **What stays the player's own** (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_switch?`, `personal_variable?`, by a Classic party's table: `ClassicStoryTable` of `MGQOnline.Game`, which the DLL builds from the game's counts of switches, variables and actors (a companion each) in its events database and the chest flags of the story mapping it holds, the reviewed `chest_flags` rows (the events database's `switches.chest_flag` before one came), topic `story.classic_table` with no request, asked once the bridge is open and again once the rewards changed (`MGQ_MpStoryState.rewards_serial`), held by `MGQ_MpStoryKeys.classic_table`, none without an events database; the member's own choices, their map's chests and the choices' lines stay the scripts' rules): the side they chose (switches 4 Alice Chosen and 5 Ilias Chosen, `SIDE_SWITCHES`) until the Great Decision, which sets the side anew with the route, so after it they follow the leader's (`personal_switch?` takes the story's variables), configuration mirrored in switches (95, 446, 447, 502), the second accessory slot outside a Raid World (445, `UNLOCK_SWITCHES`; a Raid World's story mapping shares it, and `MGQ_MpWorldStory.kept_unlock?` keeps it on under a track that hands it off, `set_raw` refitting every character), party membership (switches 1001-2000), awakening (switches 6000-6999, `AWAKENING_SWITCHES` to `AWAKENING_LAST`;
