the game's switches from 7000 tell which story bosses were defeated and belong to the story), the chest flags (`chest_flag?`, switches only chests turn on, such as "Initial Machine" 2045 and the Chaos route's carried ores 7102-7104, and the switches the Final Chapter's chests note themselves opened in instead of a self switch, such as "Treasure Chest: Monster Realm Grangold Castle 1" 2655, see `switch_chest?` of `story_rewards.rb`), the small and large medals handed to the Medal Queen and King (variables 55 and 59, whose prizes go by them), where a game over returns them (variable 1002), monsters' friendliness (variables 2000-2999) and affection (variables from 3000, one per companion). Every join of a story with the player's own values (`mix`) keeps these.
- **Borrowing**: a member asks the leader (`story=ask`, every 2 s until answered, again whenever the leader changes), who answers with the whole story (`story=full`: `s` and `sf` the switches on and off, `v` the variables set, zero included, `ss` and `ssf` the self switches on and off; what the story never set stays out). The lists travel packed in one field `z` (`pack`: deflated and Base64), and a story still above `MAX_FULL_BYTES` (200 KB, the DLL's frame is 256 KB) leaves out what it turned off or set to zero and says so (`part=1`), so a member who catches up keeps none of their own values in its gaps. The member keeps their own story aside (`@own`) the first time and plays the leader's with their own values. The leader then sends what their game wrote, at most every 15 frames (`story=delta`), to the whole party: while members follow the leader's story, `story_keys.rbx` records each write of a key the party shares (`recording` `:classic`) and hands the frame's writes to `MGQ_MpCoopStory.recorded`, which keeps the last value of each key (`@writes`) for the next delta (`delta_of`), so no comparison of the whole story runs. Variables carry only numbers, strings and true or false (`encode_value`), so no message can make objects in the other game.
- **Getting it back** (`restore`): leaving the party, the leader leaving or changing, or becoming the leader gives the member their own story back, with their own values as they are by then.
- **Playing along** (`same_story?`, `behind?`, `@keeps`, `@recorded`, `own_story`): a member whose main story and route progress (`STORY_MARKERS`: variables 1001, 1141-1143) are no further than the leader's when they first borrow keeps what they play together. One as far along has every change of the leader's story they take recorded and laid over their own story when they get it back and in every save; one behind keeps the leader's story as they played it (`caught_up_story`), with their own value of every switch, variable and self switch the leader's story never set, such as a side quest only they played, and their own chests on every map (`MGQ_MpStoryState.all_chest_keys`, the relay's `party_chests`), so a chest only the leader looted stays shut for them. A member ahead gets their own story back unchanged. A companion who joins the leader's roster (`Game_Party#add_stand_actor`) while the leader's main event plays story on the map (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.telling?`, `telling_party?`) is told to the party (`story=recruit`, `actor`), one the leader has already too, and joins the roster of every member who keeps the story and lacks them, unless only the other side gets them; one recruited outside the story, as after a battle, stays the leader's. What changes in a shop, a synthesis or a menu the story opens is never told, since those scenes are not the map.
- **The rewards' tables** (`MGQ_MpStoryState.rewards`, `Rewards`): the story's skills, companions and items, the events where the sides differ, every chest and the chest flags are the catch-up tables the relay keeps beside the story mapping, which a Classic world has the DLL fetch too (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.follow_classic`, topic `story.mapping` with the world's id, nil once it closed; asked again every ten minutes, as no track tells a new revision there). The scripts read them through `story.rewards` (`PartyRewards` of `MGQOnline.Game`, in the shapes the constants of `coop_story_rewards.rbx` had), at every call until they came and then every `REWARDS_FRAMES` with the revision held. The whole story waits for them on both sides (`rewards_came?`): a leader leaves an ask unanswered and a member asks nothing until they came, since without them a member would catch up with nothing and with both sides' rewards.
- **Catching up** (`holdings`, `catch_up`, `MGQ_MpStoryState.rewards`): the leader tells what of the story's rewards they hold with their story (`story=full`) and whenever it changes (`story=hold`, rebuilt and compared every 15 frames): `sk` the story skills Luka knows, his abilities included (`knows?`), `ac` the story companions in the roster, `it` the story items and key items with their counts, `side` "a" or "i". On first following the story, a member who was behind gets what the leader holds and they lack: the skills and companions, and the items, as many as the leader holds and one list of the story's events gives at most, summed over the lists (key items as many as the leader holds; equipment worn at the castle counts, `story_count`). A key item a party chest gave the leader comes by playing that chest in the member's own game instead (`replay_key_chests`, topic `story.replay_key_chests`, `PartyRewards.KeyItemChests`: each party chest open in the leader's story, `@leader_story`, whose closing page gives it), as a run of the member's history, so the whole chest comes along, the Makina with its chest closed and 2045 on, one copy. After that only the story's own messages bring more: companions (`story=recruit`), items (`story=gain`) and skills (`story=learn`, which the leader sends when Luka learns a story skill while `telling_party?`, hooked on `Game_Actor#learn_skill`), so what the leader gets outside the story stays the leader's. The past catch-up covers the companions and skills the leader got from optional side events too, by design: the member takes over the leader's flags, so those events count as done for them. What waited before the whole story came (`drop_covered`) is forgotten where the story covers it: older holdings always, and for a member who just caught up the story's gifts, companions and skills, which they would get twice. Companions are read from the player's own roster (`roster_ids`, the party's `@include_actors`), past a story's temporary party, by their main persona (`main_id`, as the game's roster keeps every persona), and the game's roster never holds a companion twice; the game's switches 1001-2000 already read whether a companion is there.
- **Sides** (the rewards' `sided` and `groups`, `due_groups`): a skill or companion only the events where the sides differ give before the Great Decision (`sided?`) never comes as it is, unless the Final Chapter gives it too (`ROUTE`, such as the companions the Great Decision brings with the route), which comes as the leader holds it once the story is past the decision; one the story gives while the sides still differ waits until then (`@route_waiting`, `take_route_rewards`), since the Great Decision brings its companions before the route's progress is set. A reward some event gives without a side, such as Morrigan at the Strategy Conference, comes as it is. Each such event the leader's story got past (`played?`) gives the member their own side's rewards once (`@granted_groups`), such as Angel Dance in place of Demon Decapitation at the first camp, or Eden at the Snow Shrine for Ilias's side where Alice's side fights her. An event counts as got past when everything one of its pages leaves in the story holds (`marked?`: the story variables it sets, else the switches and self switches it turns on, neither scratch, the player's own nor a Library Flag), or when the leader holds a reward only that event gives, to either side (`only_from?`), as for the camps, which leave nothing; either side counts, since the Great Decision sets the leader's side anew. The member's side here is the one they chose before the Great Decision (`story_side`, `@own_side`), past it the leader's for one who never chose, so a member holds one side's rewards whenever they joined. A member as far along leaves out the events the story had got past when they joined (`@baseline_groups`). After the Great Decision the side switches follow the leader's route (`follow_route_side`, also when the route's progress arrives alone in a delta, which `apply` takes before the switches), and route companions come as the leader's story gives them. Run `ruby Tools\story_rewards.rb "<game folder>\Data"` in MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker to write the table again; it leaves out the Final Chapter's events (the Great Decision, common events from 9000, lists that read a route's progress, every map from 1000 on) but for `ROUTE`, counts gold-only chests as chests, and companions who join by their switch (1001-2000) or a party set by `set_actors(...)` as joining. It reads the maps from 1000 on where the game keeps them (`map_folders`, as `DataManager.map_file_name` finds them: `Data/Map/Data` for 1000-1999, `Data/Map2/Data` for 2000-2999, each with its own `MapInfos.rvdata2`), such as the Chaos route's snow shrine where Puruel and Inuel join.
- **Choosing a side** (`ask_side`, `choose_side`): only the leader plays the Iliasville scene where the player takes Alice or Ilias along, so a member without a side gets the same choice in their own game once the leader chose and the member is free on the map outside the leader's story scene; the answer sets their own switch 4 or 5, brings in the companion that choice brings (`:choice` events: Alice or Ilias), and catches them up with their side's rewards.
- **The Pocket Castle's way out** (variables 21-23, each player's own, `MGQ_MpCoopCastle.arriving`): the castle's item notes where it was used in variables 21-23, and its way out ("Leaving the Pocket Castle") transfers there. They are each player's own, so the leader's never send a member elsewhere, and a member the party brings into the castle (its front gate, map 126, or a resident's map) from outside, by a story's call, its follow or a teleport to a member, has them set to where they stood, as the item would. Before, such a member left the castle for a place of an old save, such as Iliasville.
- **Lent key items** (`lend_keys`, `return_lent`, `without_lent`): a member ahead of the leader gets none of the items and gold the leader's story gives (`story=gain`), but borrows the leader's key items (`RPG::Item#key_item?`) they lack, such as the one that opens the basement of Lily's mansion, so they can play the story along. The leader tells the key items they hold (`key_items`, `k=<id>:<count>,...`) with their story (`story=full`) and whenever they change (`story=keys`, checked every 15 frames); the member's game lends the difference to what they hold themselves (`@lent`) and takes back what the leader no longer holds, in one notice ("<Leader> lent you <item> for the story.", `key_list`). Once they get their own story back (`restore`), the lent items go back too, and a save made meanwhile, the autosave and a duel's snapshot included, leaves them out of its party (a copy of `Game_Party`). Since no save holds them, a member who crashed, loaded a save or came back from a duel borrows them again with the leader's story. A member who keeps the story gets key items to keep instead (see Catching up).
- **Held back** (`held_back?`, `hold_back`, `take_held`): what the leader's story gives or takes (`hold`, and the leader's run entries) waits while a PvP battle runs, whose end puts the game back as it was before it, and until the member plays the leader's story again, as after a load or a duel; it is taken once they do (after the map's update), only from the leader who sent it, since another leader's story differs, and forgotten once the party is over. Chests other members open during a PvP battle wait the same way (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.take_held_chests`). Gifts never count as what a chest the player opens at that moment gives (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.granting`).
- **Saving** (`DataManager.make_save_contents`, `own_with_values`): while a member plays the leader's story, every save, the autosave included, holds their own story with their own values as they are. Loading a save or starting a new game (`extract_save_contents`, `create_game_objects`) brings its own story, so the member asks the leader again.
- **By the reviewed list and by replay** (`KeyScopes.Classic`, `classic_world?`): a Classic party keeps its keys by the reviewed story key list; only world keys follow the leader's story by value, a chest's keys are each member's own copy. A following member's writes of world keys go to the leader as `req` messages (`request`, every 15 frames); the leader keeps them by the list's merge kinds (`take_requests`, `merged`: a count and a lowered one-way value are refused) and sends back what it keeps. The leader's runs reach every following member as entries of the stream `story:<leader id>` (`coop_events.rbx` `hand_entry`, `take_entry`) and are replayed there. A following member's run of the leader's story goes to the leader with the next `req` as its entry (`request_run`, the `runs` field, entries joined by commas); the leader keeps it in that stream when the member's requests changed a key of its step or the step holds a party key (`keep_runs`), hands it to the party named by the member (`hand_kept`, the `opener` field) and replays it in its own game too, and the member's log claims its own step by site; the gain, recruit, learn and depart messages are gone, and `hold` only updates what the leader holds and their side. A member behind catches up once on first follow: frozen rows (`PartyRewards`), side rewards of events the leader passed, and derived entries for the leader's closures of keys the player keeps (`derive_closures`), their choices answered by `DerivedAnswers`. Where the bullets above name a Classic party's table or the gain, recruit, learn and depart messages, this bullet holds.

## The story's choices (`coop_choices.rbx`)

A member makes the story's choices for themselves, whatever the leader chose. The choices, their outcomes and the rules below are the DLL's (`ChoiceTable` and `StoryChoices` of `MGQOnline.Game`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)), which the script asks through the topics of `ChoiceTopics` (`choices.table`, `choices.passed`, `choices.prompts`, `choices.missed`, `choices.offered`, `choices.endings`, `choices.note`, `choices.outcome`, `choices.personal`, `choices.skip`, `choices.offer_refusal` and `choices.lapsed`, each request carrying the values the rules read of the story played, `values_of`): the script reads the game for them, writes the outcomes and shows the screen. The choices (by the main story's progress 1001 from which each can be made): Alice or Ilias (7, switches 4/5), Magistea Village (17: defeat Lily, switches 2096/7017/2095, Lucia 167 joins, or defeat Lucia, 2097/7016/2094, Lily 163 joins; 2137/2138 and 1029 = 6), the Navy or the Pirates (22: the Navy, 2148/7001, 1050 = 9, Tatsuko 203 and Stacy 204 join, the ship at Field 334,240; or the Pirates, 2149/2169/2150, 1050 = 6, 1051 = 5, Ashel 201 and Bonnie 202 join, the ship at Field 21,211, the treasure 709:6 and Ashel's 376:53 self switches on; both 2168, 7068, 1053 = 0 and party places 56 at least 2, `:least`), Plansect (24: the Priestess's side, Queen Bee defeated 7003, 1061 = 1, the Priestess 241 joins, or the Queen Bee's side, 2154/7002, 1061 = 2, Miria 245 joins; 1056 = 6), Succubus Village (27: Natasha's side, 7007, Natasha 288 joins with Lily, 2210, else 2211; or the mayor's side, 7008, the mayor 287 joins with Lucia, 2212, else 2213; 1065 = 3), the Spider Princess (30: 2280 and she, 334, joins, or the Queen Ants, 2281), the Sphinx (31: challenge her, 2090, or invite her, 153 joins) and the Great Decision (40).

- **What is asked** (`passed`, `made?`): every choice whose progress a catch-up or the shared story carries the member past, unless their own story made it. Amira in Iliasburg is no choice: asked her name or killed, she joins later all the same, so her switches (2127, 2108) and her join follow the story. The third way of the Great Decision only when the member's own save cleared both endings (`ssw(:ed1) && ssw(:ed2)`, as common event 380 asks).
- **Kept the member's own** (`personal?`, `MGQ_MpStoryState.choice_key?`): the switches and variables of every choice the member's own story made are personal while they play the leader's story, like the side switches, so the leader's full story and deltas never overwrite them. The DLL names these keys (`choices.personal`, `StoryChoices.Personal`), asked once per request and kept (`personal_keys`): `{raid, own}` in, where `own` holds the values of the member's own story kept aside (nil while they play their own); `s` and `v` (the switches and variables), `maps` and `self_switches` out. In a Raid World they are every choice's switches and variables, and `maps` and `self_switches` name the Navy's and the Pirates' lines (`personal_self_switch?`); in a Classic party those of the choices the member's own story made, none for the leader. The choices' companions (`ChoiceTable.ChoiceCompanions`) never come with the catch-up or a `story=recruit`, only with the member's own outcome (`skip_reason`).
- **The leader's offer** (`offer`, `Offers`): the leader's World overview menu of a member who is behind (no progress beyond the leader's) and does not follow the leader's story offers *Sync story*. The offer stands in the leader's state (`ssoffer`: member id and number) for a minute, until the member follows or leaves; the member answers it in the notification box. Declining tells the leader (`choices=declined`). Accepting opens the screen of choices; *Bring me there* lays the outcomes over the member's own story first (`apply`), then the member follows the leader by the offer (`offer_with?`, before the rule of `check_sync`), catches up as a member behind, and `borrowed` ends the offer.
- **The Great Decision**: the same route as the leader's catches up as usual. Another route (`start_route`): the member keeps the leader's story up to the decision (1001 = 39, 1140-1143 = 0, `MGQ_MpCoopStory.keep_and_leave`), catches up with nothing only the leader's route gives (the rewards' `final`), and once free plays `decision_commands`: common event 154 (the game's own reset of the Final Chapter), the outcome's part of common event 380 (from 1002 = 111 or 112 to the transfer, with its party changes when 912 = 0), switches 4/5 and the route's variable = 1, and the transfer to map 430 or 431 (25, 19, facing up), whose event plays the route's start. The third way sets 7091, 7092, 92, 1001 = 40 and 1143 = 1 and goes to map 437.
- **Synced play** (`story_passed`): a delta that carries the shared story past a choice, or resolves one, which the member has not made asks their own outcome on the same screen with one row once they are free on the map.
- **The screen** (`Scene_MpStoryChoices`, `Window_MpStoryChoices`): a `Window_MpWorldForm` with a switch per choice (`MGQ_MpWorld::Form::Field` of kind `:switch`, `Form#turn`) and the line of what the picked outcome brings below it (Succubus Village follows the Magistea answer), then *Bring me there* and *Cancel* (a prompt: *Confirm*). Left/Right or Enter turn a switch, a click turns it or presses a button; the lines at the top name the choice and the keys. Every turn and the final choices are logged.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`) every choice's outcome is each player's own (`personal?`), and nobody offers a story sync (`Offers.peer_option` is nil). A choice the world's story carries the player past (`story_passed`, called by `world_story.rbx`) asks their own outcome, the side included, and so does a choice a later build added (`ChoiceTable.LaterBranches`, the Navy or the Pirates) that the save is past without its outcome, once with the first world's story after entering, a load or a new game (`missed`). A player whose own play took a side at Navy Headquarters (`ChoiceTable.NavySides`) is offered that side alone (`StoryChoices.NavySides`, `OutcomeIndex`). The self switches of the Navy's and the Pirates' lines (`ChoiceTable.NavyMaps`, `NavySelfSwitches`) are each player's own (`personal_self_switch?`, through `MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_self_switch?` and `mix`), so one pirate's treasure leaves the next pirate's chest full. An outcome puts the ship where its side leaves it (`place_ship`) and has the catch-up follow it as a story carrying the player past it (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.carried`), which gives its items, such as the Dark Saber, and its return point; the screen stays while no leader is followed, and what waits (choices to pick, a Raid World's route to confirm, the queued Great Decision, the accepted offer) is forgotten when a save loads, a new game starts or the world closes (`forget`). The Great Decision's labels are `decision_routes` (the world's route after `raid_decision`, else `MGQ_MpWorldStory.decision_routes`), the third way by the world's clears (`both_endings?` reads 7096 and 7097), and its note says the whole world plays the route (`choices.note`). Confirmed on the screen titled "The world's route", only the player's own half plays (`take_raid_decision`, `raid_decision_commands`, see [In the game](#in-the-game-world_storyrbx)).
- **Asked on the replay** (`StoryChoices.Logged`): a choice whose run a stream hands the player (its point past the main story's progress where that stream began: a Raid World's log start, the summary's `start`, or where a Classic member started keeping the leader's story, `MGQ_MpCoopStory.follow_place`) is asked while the run replays, through `MGQ_MpCoopChoices.ask_input(opener, lines, kind, spec, done)` with kind `choice`, `number`, `item` or `present`, once the player stands free on the map (`Scene_MpStoryQuestion`). The model (digits, rows, scrolling, cancel answer, texts) is `ChoiceInputs` in `MGQOnline.Game` behind `choices.input_open` and `choices.input_step`; the scene only draws it and forwards keys, pad and mouse. The `ChoiceOutcome` rows in `ChoiceTable` are frozen history: only choices up to where the stream began (or with no stream) still get them through the sync story and own-outcome screens.

## Events in a party (`coop_events.rbx`)

**Where it is sorted:** in the DLL, by `MGQOnline.Game`'s `EventSorter` (see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame), Event sorter) through the topics `events.sort` and `events.kinds` (`MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/EventTopics.cs`), over the events database the mod ships (see In the DLL in [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata)), whose fingerprint gate the scripts open once (`open_events`). The scripts name the list: a map event's page (map, event, page from 0) or a common event (found by its list, `common_id`, with the running map and event), each with its list's length and shape (`size_and_shape`, kept per list object); a list the database does not hold as the game shows it (another length or shape, such as a translation's changed branch, or a list of the scripts or of another mod) they hand over whole (`inline_request`, each command's plain parameters, with the running map and event). The first sort of a list answers `needs`: the switches and variables its branches read and the switches and variables it sets whose being the player's own decides (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_switch?` and `personal_variable?`); the scripts keep them per list and send their values with every later sort (`values_of`), so a sort is one call, and take the last answer while those values stay (`sorting`). Whether a list is a Pocket Castle resident's the scripts tell (`resident`). A map's chests (`chest_keys`, kept per map and revision of the relay's rewards) are the map's chests among every chest the relay keeps (`party_chests`, the reviewed rows, which hold the chests behind a branch a new game skips and leave out on purpose pages the database names as chests, such as a world chest every player shares). Before the relay's rewards came they are the self switches the events database names for the pages it holds as the game does (`chest_letters`, one `events.chests` call: a new game's chests, false for any other page) and those the DLL sorts as chests among the pages it holds otherwise (`events.kinds` in one call, the pages it answers none for in one more with their commands, `page_kinds`). A sort the DLL cannot give plays as a talk (`UNSORTED`), logged once per list.

**What an event page is** (`kind`; `kind_of` for a list of commands): read from its commands when it starts, following called common events up to 5 deep. Only the commands that can run now count (`Runnable`): a conditional branch on a switch or a variable (111) counts only when it holds now (`Holds`), its else only when it does not, and a branch on anything else counts whole. A branch on the variable a preceding `unlimited_choices` script writes the player's answer into (`ChoiceScript`, `ChoiceVariables`) counts whole, since the choice is not made yet, and in `MarksOf` its sides count as a choice's branches (`IsChoiceBranch`), so story behind it is `choice_story` and caught at the choice like any other: Alice's recap at the Strategy Conference and Iliasville's village chief keep their sets there. A scene that runs by itself (trigger 3) and hides its story behind such a choice is `:story` (`kind`), since a member's copy ended at the story would start over and over. Called common events count the same way (`CommonMarks`, once per common event and depth in a sort), except the player's own (`OwnCommonEvents`, 2, 3, 5 and 114, also the scripts' `OWN_COMMON_EVENTS`): called through command 117 they add nothing to the caller's marks, so an event that calls them is sorted by the rest of its list, so a shop calling a side quest's common event, as Vanilla's supply quest in Magistea and the other towns (common events 274-288), is a talk while that quest's step cannot run. What a choice's branch (402, 403) would make story counts as `:choice_story` instead: the page stays a talk that may turn into story (`may_tell?`), such as a blacksmith whose "Talk" moves a side quest on; a choice that gives items and sets the event's own switch stays a chest. While such a talk runs on the map, each command is watched before it runs (`guard`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook): at the first that moves the story on (`story_command?`), a member's game ends the event there (on the list's closing empty command) with "Only <leader> can move the story on.", and the leader's game tells the party the story from there (`@telling`), without gathering. Exits hold the story's warnings in such branches (Iliasville's: "I need to be on time for my baptism", the village chief, the crying slime), which would make them story long after the warnings passed. Since the answer depends on the game's state, a page is sorted anew each time it starts.

- **Battlefucks** first (`BattlefuckEvents` in MGQOnline.Game's `Events`, the same rows `story_rewards.rb` finds from the common events that count a win or a loss, 6999 and 7000, and writes into `coop_story_rewards.rbx`): a page that can now call one of them is `:own`: it plays in the game of whoever starts it, with no telling, gathering or hold, and what it sets goes to the story like any other change.
- **The Pocket Castle** next (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.resident?` in coop_castle.rbx, maps 227-230 and 268-278, not the side stories' copies of it): a companion's page (`companion_talk?`: its first speaker's name shows their affection variable, `\V[3000+n]`) and a page that calls one of the castle's services (`SERVICES`: Vanilla's shop, Papi's smithy, the maids' party saves, the item storage, Teeny's inn) or the coin shop (`@goods`) is a talk, whatever it sets: each player talks to them in their own game. The companions' scripts (`actor_label_jump`, presents, novel scenes) appear in the castle's story events too, so only the speaker tells them apart. This makes 728 of the castle's 745 story pages talks; the 17 left are its story events, such as the Sphinx's orb, the Elf Princess's room and Papi joining.
- **The leader's residents** (coop_castle.rbx): on the castle's maps the party's leader is the Map Owner of `coop_npcs.rbx` while there (`MGQ_MpCoopCastle.owner`), so the castle shows the leader's residents. A resident the member's own game shows nothing of (its event shows no page there, or one without a look) while the leader's page shows it, such as a companion only the leader recruited, stands as a see-through ghost (`Game_MpResident`, as see-through as players outside the party, `MGQ_MpOverworld::STRANGER_OPACITY`) with the look of the leader's page, walks where the leader's walks and starts nothing, since it is no event of the member's map (`update`, `ghost_of?`, drawn by a `Spriteset_Map` hook). Residents the member has stay their own to talk to; a resident whose page differs only (both show it) stays where the member's game puts it. Decided by the user on 2026-10-03.
- **Exits** next (`exit?`): a page the player starts (trigger 0-2) that transfers them without dialogue, battle, items, its own self switch or a story script is `:travel`, even when it sets switches or variables on the way. These are bookkeeping: the warp and library flags of a town's entrance, "Warp OFF", "Encounter OFF", or a flag such as Iliasville's north exit noting Sonya. The leader's game sets the same when it passes, so a member takes the exit in their own game instead of handing it to the leader. This turns 370 map pages from story into travel, 128 of them the world map's entrances.
- `:battle`: the page starts a battle (301) and shows no dialogue itself (101, 102, 105). The wandering monsters call common events before and after their battle that set variables, so the page alone decides.
- `:story`: it sets a switch or variable that is neither scratch (named like "General", "temp", "System Only") nor the player's own (see `MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_switch?`); party membership switches 1001-2000 always count. Or it sets its own self switch along with anything but dialogue (dialogue and the self switch alone is a one-time talk, `one_time_talk?`: a signpost or a villager who speaks once, which each player reads in their own game; the self switch follows the leader's story like any other), fights with dialogue, changes the party (129), uses vehicles (206), asks for a name (303), ends the game (353, 354), or runs a script that is not a talk's or travel's. The flags the game toggles around its fights (22, 73, 97) and the event variant it picks before a call (variable 129) are each player's own (`MGQ_MpStoryState`), so clearing them keeps a battle page a battle and a trader's talk a talk.
- `:chest`: it gives items or gold (125-128) and sets its own self switch, with no story behind a choice (`choice_story`); the chest flags it turns on, switches only chests turn on (`MGQ_MpStoryState.chest_flags`, the relay's `chest_flags` table, such as "Initial Machine" 2045 of the first Makina chests), are each player's own (`MGQ_MpStoryState.chest_flag?`), so they set no story. A script a talk may run keeps a page a chest, as the `gain_medal` of a weapon's pedestal; `story_rewards.rb` keeps the same `TALK_SCRIPTS` (and `TRAVEL_SCRIPTS`, a test compares them), so such pedestals are in the relay's `party_chests` and each player's own. A chest that notes itself opened in such a switch alone, with no self switch, is a talk, which each player opens in their own game. A page that sells story behind one, as Amira's paid tip in Iliasburg (map 29, event 33), is a talk that may turn into story, or story when it sets its own switch outside the choice, so the story value it sets reaches the world's.
- `:travel`: it transfers the player (201, or the scripts in `TRAVEL_SCRIPTS`).

A talk or travel page with story only behind a choice (`may_tell?`, `WATCHED_KINDS`) is watched while it runs (`guard`): at the first command that moves the story on, a command 117 whose common event holds such a choice included, the leader's game tells it from there as story and a member's ends it. Travel counts since the Great Decision (map 407, events 39-41, common event 380) and 216 other pages, such as the Orb of Guidance at the Gold Coast, hold their transfer behind the same choice as their story; so the decision's transfer takes the players on the map along.
- `:talk`: everything else: conversations, choices, shops, and the scripts in `TALK_SCRIPTS` (companions' own lines, choices, presents and affection, medals, the job change, synthesis, casino and party menus, and shops that list their goods in a script, `goods` or `@goods`, as the Casino's coin sellers, and the cosmetic scripts: custom move routes, waits, character zooms and angles, map zooms, the mini-games' key input and picture checks).

Over the game's 107,478 pages (every map event page and common event, the maps from 1000 on included) this gives, in a new game on 2026-10-11, about 17,600 story, 70,600 talk, 13,700 travel, 5,200 chest and 400 battle pages. The event checker next to this repository (MGQ-Paradox-Event-Checker) measures what every page does in the game. Its `Tools\sorting_check.rb` compares those effects with the sorting the events database generator's `sort` writes outside the game, see Generator in [Events database](#events-database-mgqonlinedata).

**Chests** are the player's own, and in a Classic party opening one opens it for the whole party by replay ("Issue 73 chest effects.md"): the run that opened it, bracketed by `story_keys.rbx`, changed a party key (a `party_chests` self switch or a `chest` switch), so the DLL logs it and answers its entry, which `hand_entry` sends the party as `replay=<text>` (`MGQ_MpStoryReplay.wire`). A member takes it (`take_entry`, never in a Raid World nor from someone outside the party), names its opener and hands it to `MGQ_MpStoryReplay.receive`, whose own game plays the chest again once they stand free on the map and no PvP battle runs: what the chest does for them it does, the one notice tells what they got or paid, and a chest they looted already, alone or at the same time, gives nothing more. A run of a page a party key closes tells the party no story gain (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.telling_party?`, `MGQ_MpStoryKeys.party_run?`), so a chest page that plays as story gives once. A member playing the leader's story sees and plays their own chests: every party chest's self switch is the player's own copy in every join of stories (`MGQ_MpStoryState.personal_self_switch?`, `Rewards#chest?`), so a replay reads their own on any map, and those of the map are set again whenever a map is set up (`map_entered`), and the party keys a run or a replay wrote stay in their own story kept aside (`MGQ_MpCoopStory.keep_own`).

**Story scenes together** (coop_gather.rbx, `MGQ_MpCoopGather`, to which `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.take_party` hands `gather` and `follow`). Party event messages carry `pevent=<kind>`, the party's id, and the leader's place: `map`, `x`, `y`, `d`. Only the leader's are taken.

- Members do not follow the leader to other maps; only story scenes bring them over.
- **Teleports between members**: see *Teleports between members* under [Parties](#parties-cooprbx).
- `gather`: when the leader's main event starts a story scene (`scene?`: story with its own dialogue, or a novel scene) and a member stands away (`gathered?`: on the leader's map within `GATHER_TILES`, 3), the scene waits at its first command (`holding?`, a `Game_Interpreter#execute_command` hook that yields the interpreter's fiber, asking the DLL's `gather.holding`), so the leader can neither move nor open the menu, and calls the members, again every 3 s (`GatherCalls.CallFrames`). A member called (`called`) gets 5 s (`GatherCalls.GatherFrames`, counted in frames from the call, a countdown above their head) to finish what they do, then is brought to the leader once free (`come?`, `update`, which asks the DLL's `gather.frame` and `gather.take`): on the map, after a battle, menu, event or transfer of their own. The call carries whether warping is banned where the leader stands (`warp_ban`, the game's switch 100, `MGQ_MpStoryState::WARP_BAN`), and the member takes it over on arrival (`take_warp_ban`): the game sets the ban in the events a player walks through to enter or leave a cave, which a member brought over never touches, so a Harpy Feather would otherwise stay unusable. The switch is each player's own (`PERSONAL_SWITCHES`), since it tells of the place a player stands in, not of the story. While called (`coming?`) the member meets no random encounter (`Game_Player#encounter` starts none and draws new steps once they ran out) and turns co-op battle invites down, so no new battle holds them up. Once everyone stands near, or after 30 s (`GatherCalls.HoldFrames`) without those still away (`missing`), the scene plays and the leader's state tells `telling=1` (`story_playing?`); meanwhile every member on the leader's map stands still and cannot open the menu (`blocked?`, through `Game_Player#movable?` and `Scene_Map#update_call_menu`). A member's call lapses once no call came for 6 s (`GatherCalls.CallLapseFrames`, `pending_call`), so one the scene started without is never brought over later; leader calls and a Classic follow keep that lapse, while a teleport the player asked for (`come_to`, from `coop_teleport.rbx` and `join_story`) and a Raid World's follow have their own, `GatherCalls.AskedLapseFrames` (60 s), after which the teleport says "You were too busy to teleport to \<name>.". A call or teleport to a map this game lacks says "Cannot teleport to \<name>: this game has no map \<id>." in place of the arrival. Whether this game has a map (`map_known?`) is read from the file where the game keeps it (`map_file`, the game's own `DataManager.map_file_name`: the maps from 1000 on lie in `Data/Map/Data`, `Data/Map2/Data` and so on), since `Data/Map1650.rvdata2` does not exist and every follow onto such a map was dropped. The calls, the hold and the offers with their frames are the DLL's (`GatherCalls`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)); the script reads them once per frame while a call, a follow or the hold may stand (`state`, `gather.frame`, with the map settled and the members missing; `wake` as one comes, until the DLL answers `idle`) and asks anew after each call it took (`refresh`). A loaded save sets `Graphics.frame_count` back, so a moment before it counts as long past (`within?`, kept for coop_teleport.rbx), and a loaded save or the world closing forgets the calls, a teleport the player asked for and the hold (`forget`).
- `follow`: when the leader's story moves the leader elsewhere (a transfer while `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.telling?`, noted around `Game_Player#perform_transfer`: `before_transfer`, `after_transfer`), such as a theater show's stage and the "Stage End" common event's way back to the Grand Theater (map 624), or a story's own teleport, the members who stood near the leader's old place are told the new one and come at once, without the five seconds (`followed`). Until they arrive, the leader's pages of that map reach them (`story_map?`), so none is missed. A follow that lapses tells "<Leader>'s story went on without you." (`lapse_notice`). The leader's other transfers take nobody along.
- A PvP battle keeps calls out (`pvp_running?`), and when a duel puts its snapshot of the game back (`MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Battle.restore`) a call that waited through it is dropped (`drop_call`), so the member answers the leader's next call; a member is only moved on a settled map (`settled?`: no PvP battle, no Library battle, `Graphics.brightness` 255). A call that waited through a duel and moved the player as the map started again left a lasting black screen in the field test of 2026-10-04.
- Gathering, calls, arrivals, follows and calls that lapsed each write a line to the in-game log (`co-op gather:`).
- A member is moved once they are free (`free?`: on the map, with no event, message or transfer of their own in the way), checked after every map update, so a member in a menu or battle follows when they are back. Another map means a transfer, the same map a plain `moveto`.

**Story in the leader's game.** A member's game never plays story itself:

- A story event the member starts (`Game_Map#setup_starting_map_event`, which looks at every starting event, so one left to the leader never keeps the member's other events from starting) does not start, in the member's game or the leader's: only the leader starts story, in their own game (`hand_over`). The member hears "Only <Leader> can move the story on.", once per event in 3 s (`refuse_story`, `REFUSE_FRAMES`), since an event the player stands on starts again with every step, with "Bring them here to go on." while the leader is elsewhere.
- Story events and story common events that run by themselves (autorun) are left to the leader's game (`leave_to_leader?` in `setup_autorun_common_event`), which runs them on its own map.
- While the leader's main event plays story (`@telling`, which ends with the event, `finished`, since a loaded save or the game put back after a PvP battle runs the map's last event's list once more), every message page and its choices (`show`, caught in `Game_Interpreter#wait_for_message`, since the Yanfly plugin writes its own `command_101` after the Patch folder loads) go to the party (`pevent=say`: face, index, background, position, and the lines and choices in Base64). The DLL names, paces and keeps the pages (`StoryPages`, through `pages.show`, `pages.passed`, `pages.flush`; the script sends what it answers). Each page carries an id (a session tag and a count, so a page of an earlier session is never taken for one done), and once the leader moved past it the leader's game says so (`shown`, after `wait_for_message`: `pevent=done`). While the leader holds the game's skip (`skip_held?`), which passes a page every frame, the leader's game tells at most one page every `StoryPages.SkipSendFrames` (10 frames): a later page within them replaces the one kept (`@kept_say` holds where it goes), which is never told, and `flush_say`, after the map's update, tells the one kept, and its `done` if the leader passed it, once the frames passed, the skip is let go, the event ended or another scene was called, so the members end on the last page; `done` goes out only for pages told. Two messages per page every frame ran past the relay's limit per connection and closed it. Members on the leader's map hand each page to the DLL (`hear`, `pages.hear`, at most `StoryPages.MaxHeard`, 30, kept) and show it in their own message window once free (`show_heard`, `pages.next`), the choices as its last line ("<Leader> chooses: Yes / No"), and leave out the pages the leader moved past meanwhile while more than `StoryPages.CatchUpPages` (3) wait, so a member back from a menu or battle joins near the leader's page. Only the leader moves it on: `Window_Message#input_pause` waits on such a page (`mirroring?`) for its `done` and its reading time (`StoryPages.ReadingFrames`) instead of the player's buttons or the game's skip key (`page_done?`, `pages.over`), and ends it anyway once the leader is gone or stopped telling the story for 2 s (`StoryPages.StaleFrames`, since the leader's state and their pages travel apart). The window asks every frame, but the DLL only once what it reads changed (`page_held?`): another teller, telling, wait or input, a page heard, passed or shown, or the frames after which time alone may end the page (`wait` of `pages.over`). A page that fails to hold (`hold_page`) waits for the player's buttons as the game's own pages do, since an error there would end the game. A page too long for the window shows its rest after 2.5 s (`OVERFLOW_FRAMES`); `process_input` marks the pause at a page's end, after which the player's own messages wait for their buttons again (`page_ended`). Escape codes such as `\V[n]` are read in the member's game, which plays the leader's story. Novel scenes are not shown to members yet.

**The story's scene** (coop_scene.rbx, `MGQ_MpCoopScene`). While the leader's story plays for the party (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.story_playing?`), each change the story makes besides its dialogue goes to the party as `pscene=<kind>` with the map and the arguments as one `MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Wire` line (the changes of a frame are kept, `send_change`, and go out after the map's update, `flush_changes`; see below): a picture of the map's screen (`picture.show`, `move`, `rotate`, `start_tone_change`, `erase`), such as a story's CG, the map's screen (`screen.start_fadeout`, `start_fadein`, `start_tone_change`, `start_flash`, `start_shake`, and the game's `od_fadein`/`od_fadeout`), the leader's character hidden or shown (`player.transparent`), as on a theater show's stage, and what the story does to the map's events: a move route (`event.route`, hooked after `Game_Character#force_move_route`: the event's id, whether it repeats or skips, and its commands up to code 44 with a sound as its file, volume and pitch, never a script, 45), an animation or balloon (`event.animation`, `event.balloon`, after the writers of `Game_CharacterBase`; -1 for the player's character, which a viewer sees on their own) and an erased event (`event.erase`). In a Raid World the story's switches, variables (whole numbers only) and self switches go too, gathered over the frame and sent once after the map's update (`story_value`, `send_values`, `story.values`, each as `[kind, key, value]`, what `MGQ_MpWorldStory.personal?` names each player's own left out): the world's story reaches the viewers only once the telling ended, so without them a viewer's game showed the scene's characters on no page (`coop_npcs.rbx`: "stays the player's own: page -1 here"), and the Map Owner's moves of them were dropped. A Classic party's members get the leader's values from `coop_story.rbx` every quarter second instead. A viewer's pages give way to whatever waits for the player to be free and lapses meanwhile (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.give_way_to`, `giving_way?`: the story's follow through `MGQ_MpCoopGather.due?`, a battle's invite or lead request through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.waiting_for_player`): no page starts, a shown page the teller moved past ends at once (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.page_done?`), the passed pages are left out (`StoryPages.Next`), and the rest show after the arrival, since the follow waits for a free player and the pages kept them busy for good in the field test of 2026-10-11. The player's character's move routes go to the viewers too (`event.route` with -1), so their own character walks the route and the camera, which follows it, moves as the teller's; while it walks one, `walks_player?` keeps `Game_Player#update_nonmoving` and `check_event_trigger_touch` from starting what it steps on or bumps into and from meeting enemies. A route the viewer takes always skips a blocked step (`apply_route`), since their characters stand elsewhere than the teller's and a step that never succeeds would hold the character for good. The story's camera goes as its place, `map.camera` (`send_camera`: the teller's `display_x` and `display_y` in 32nds of a tile, once it moved and `CAMERA_FRAMES` after the last at most; the viewer's camera jumps there from more than `CAMERA_JUMP` tiles and eases a `CAMERA_EASE`-th of the way each frame nearer, after `Game_Player#update`, only on the teller's map, and comes back onto the viewer's character with `restore`), since a scroll moves the camera from where the viewer's own character stands, the story's sounds as `audio.<call>` (`audio`, before each of the game's `Audio` calls in `AUDIO_CALLS`: music, background sounds, fanfares and sound effects played with the file named without its folder, volume, pitch and the music's position, those the story left out as the game's defaults, `AUDIO_DEFAULTS`, stopped and faded; a viewer plays them through `RPG::BGM`, `RPG::BGS`, `RPG::ME` and `RPG::SE` on the teller's map screen alone, a name with a path left out and a file it cannot play rescued; away from it, as in a battle or a menu, whose end brings their own music back, or on the way to the teller's map, whose autoplay would replace it, the last music and background sound wait until the viewer is there, `hold_audio`, `play_held`, and the fanfares and sound effects are left out), the map's look as `map.tileset`, `map.battleback` and `map.parallax` (`map_look`, before `Game_Map#change_tileset`, `change_battleback` and `change_parallax`; on the teller's map alone, a tileset of `$data_tilesets` and pictures this game has), and `map.zoom` (after the game's `map_zoom`, kept to 100-2000 and put back by `restore`). Only the story's own changes count (`story_change?`, `map_turn?`): those made inside the update of the map's main interpreter, which a common event it calls runs inside too, noted around every `Game_Interpreter#update` (`updating`, `updated`); a parallel event, such as the map's own display, changes pictures every frame and stays the leader's own. The update tells interpreters apart rather than the command, since a command may wait frames before it runs, as while the party gathers. A member on the leader's map, or on the way there (`MGQ_MpCoopGather.story_map?`, since the screen keeps its pictures and tint through a transfer), makes the same change (`apply`) once the DLL passed it (`SceneChecks`, through `scene.take`): a picture only by a number of 1-100 and a file name that may hold dots but no path, of a picture this game has (`map_graphics?`; one it lacks erases the picture's place instead, since the game's sprites load it without a rescue); a change of a picture or the screen only with the arguments it takes (as many as the game's method takes, places, zooms, opacities and turning speeds finite and within ±100,000, `SceneChecks.MostAmount`, durations whole and within 0-36,000 frames, `MostFrames`, a shake's power and speed within 0-9, `MostShake`, a whole origin, a blend type of 0-2 and a `Tone` or `Color` where the game takes one), since the game keeps them and a wrong one raises or hangs in its update each frame; an event's change only on the teller's map itself, since another map's events share its ids; a story value only for an id this game has, never one that is the viewer's own, taken as the world's (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.mirrored` sets it in the story the viewer built on, so it is not written as their own change); a move route without a command it cannot run (`route_command`: a graphic this game has, `MGQ_MpOverworld.graphic?`, a sound file in `Audio/SE`, whole numbers otherwise, a name without a path), never waited for. It notes what to put back, with the screen's tint before the story's first screen change. Once the member no longer sees the story (`watching?`: the relay ended the teller's telling, a Classic leader's state stopped saying they tell it, or either left the map) and, in a Classic world, no change came for 2 s (`STATE_GRACE_FRAMES`, since the leader's state that says the story plays travels apart from the changes), the story's pictures are erased, flash and shake cleared, the member's own tint comes back over 30 frames (`RESTORE_FRAMES`) on the map they watched from (on a map the story brought them to, no tint, `NO_TONE`), the brightness comes back, the member's own character shows as before on the map they watched from (on a map the story brought them to, shown, since the teller's story may go on in their own game there, as an ending does) and a teller's route their character still walks ends (`end_player_route`) (`restore`); a loaded save forgets it (`forget`). The player's own scene is followed too (`MGQ_MpCoopScene::Own`): the state of the screen, the player's character and the party (`state_now`: temporary party, hidden character, followers, tint, music) as the first event of the map's interpreter began and as its latest began (`started`), and the pictures its events showed (`picture`), until no event of it runs and nothing it changed stands (`standing?`: a hidden character, hidden followers or a temporary party, as an ending's walk leaves them). Where the mod ends one of its events early (`MGQ_MpCoopEvents.end_event`), `cut` takes back what that event changed, its scene's pictures included; where it moves the player away (a call, follow or teleport of coop_gather.rbx, the world's return to the Great Decision, the end of a collab scenario the world's story cleared), `leave` takes the player out of the whole scene, a dark screen fading in either way.

The scene's changes wait in the DLL (`SceneQueue`, through `scene.change`) and go out once a frame, after the map's update (`flush_changes`, `scene.flush`), in their order and grouped by the map they happened on: a lone change as `pscene=<kind>`, which earlier builds take, several as one `pscene=batch` whose `args` is a `Wire` line of `[kind, args]` pairs, at most `SceneQueue.MaxBatch` (256) and within `SceneQueue.MaxBatchBytes` (240 KB, below the 262,103 bytes the DLL sends of a world message, its 256 KB frame less the cipher, with room for the other fields), split into more messages past either; a lone change longer than that goes out alone. Earlier builds ignore a batch as an unknown change. A viewer has the DLL check each pair as a single change (`scene.take`, `take_change`) and drops a batch of more than `MaxBatch` pairs or of anything but pairs whole. While the teller holds the game's skip, the changes go out only `SceneQueue.SkipSendFrames` (10) frames after the last went out, or once the skip is let go, the story stopped playing or another scene was called (`SceneQueue.Due`). Kept changes are merged (`SceneQueue.Add`): a picture's move replaces its last move that nothing else of the picture followed, the camera's place its last one, while skipping a sound effect the last one, and the story values waiting are merged into one `story.values`, the newer value of each kept; a move route is never left out, since a viewer's character may have walked part of it. The viewers are noted with the changes, since a Raid World's viewers are nobody once its telling ended, and in a Raid World each message names the map its changes happened on in `mmap`, since the teller may have moved on meanwhile.

## Shared UI (`ui.rbx`)

What every flat sprite box of the mod draws with, so no widget declares a color, a font size or a timing of its own; the mod's `Window_Base` windows wear the preset's window skin instead (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)).

- **The tick** (`MGQ_MpUi::Tick`): the models of the interface live in C# (`MGQOnline.Game`'s `Ui/`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)), the drawing, the game's `Input`, the sounds and the capture stay here. A model's owner puts the model's input where it read the keys before (`put(model, input)`, or `collect { }` for one read every frame), and `send_frame`, at the end of the `ui` after-hook of `Graphics.update`, stages the frame's inputs as one `ui.tick`; that hook runs before `Multiplayer.rb`'s, which registers once the scripts loaded, so the same frame's batch carries it and its drain brings the models' events back. A frame in which no model put input stages nothing. `play(event)` plays the sounds an event names under `cues` (`CUES`), `buttons` reads the game's buttons past the capture once a frame as the models take them (`BUTTONS`: went down, went down or repeats, held). The owner acts on an event where it reads the keys, a frame after the input, never inside the drain, which runs after the frame was drawn.
- **Cutting and breaking texts** (`MGQ_MpUi.cut`, `wrap`, `spans` in `ui_text_box.rbx`, and `MGQ_MpUi::Glyphs`): RGSS measures a text in the game's font (VL Gothic) as the sum of its characters' widths on every size the mod uses (probe 0.1d of the refactor), so C# cuts and breaks texts by a table of widths (`ui.text.cut`, `ui.text.wrap`, `ui.text.spans`) instead of the many `Bitmap#text_size` calls a cut or a wrap made, each 20 to 30 µs. `Glyphs.key_of(measure)` finds the font a picture or a window's contents draws in by its name, size and boldness; the first use measures printable ASCII (`FIRST`), checks that `SAMPLES` measure as the sum of their characters and defines the font in C# (`ui.glyphs`), and every later text hands over the widths of the characters C# lacks with its own request (`new_widths`), each measured once. A font that fails the check, such as a font with kerning that a translation or another mod sets, is logged once and measured here from then on (`cut_measured`, `wrap_measured`, `wrap_spans` with a measuring block), as is every font while the bridge is closed and a text that is no valid UTF-8. `spans(nil, ...)` breaks in the font of cells, for a box that counts characters (the text screen). The characters a model meets that were never measured, such as those typed into a text box, come back as `ui.glyphs.wanted`, which `measure_wanted` measures on a small picture in that font and stages.

- **Style** (`MGQ_MpUi::Style`): the colors (`BACK` of every box, `PLATE` behind one line on a busy picture, `PICKED` for the picked row or choice, `FIELD` of a text field, `OUTLINE` solid black, `TEXT`, `DIM`, `GOOD`, `WARN`, `BAD`, `GOLD`, `HEADING`, `OWN` yellow for the player, `MEMBER` green for a party member, the same object as `GOOD`; `BACK`, `PLATE`, `PICKED`, `FIELD`, `HEADING`, `EDGE` the thin frame, `RIM` the line around it, `TITLE`, `WELL` a gauge's empty part and `TAB` an unpicked tab are the preset's, set in place by `ui_theme.rbx`), `DIM_OPACITY` of an icon that cannot be taken, the font sizes (`TITLE_SIZE` 20, `TEXT_SIZE` 18, `SMALL_SIZE` 16, `TINY_SIZE` 14), `PAD` (6), `GAP` (4, between columns and tabs), `HINT_GAP` (three spaces between a hint's parts) and the timings (`FADE_FRAMES` 12, `SLIDE_FRAMES` 8, `LINGER_FRAMES` 180 for a transient line). `use_font(bitmap, size, color)` sets a bitmap's font (never bold, solid outline), `draw_outlined` draws a text with outline copies as far as `OUTLINE_REACH` (the corners left out), keeping a copy of the font's color, since RGSS's `Font#color` hands out the font's own color, which `Font#color=` overwrites, and with the font's own outline off meanwhile, which each copy would grow a pixel further, `draw_plate` fills the plate behind a text as wide as the text plus `PAD` on either side, `draw_plated` both. `close_hint(*also)` words what closes a screen in one order everywhere ("Esc, Numpad 0 or F11: close", "Esc or Numpad 0: close"), which the wheels (`MGQ_MpWheel.draw_hint`), the list box, the overview and the chat use. `faded(color, frames)` lowers a color's alpha over the last `FADE_FRAMES` frames of a line about to go. `draw_box` fills a box with `BACK` inside its frame, `draw_frame` draws the frame alone (`RIM` outermost while it is not see-through, then `EDGE`; `frame_width` is 1 or 2), `draw_line_box` one outline. A new box draws through `draw_box` and `Style.use_font`, cuts names with `MGQ_MpUi.cut` and names no color of its own; a script's color of a meaning the style has is the style's: the formation's cut line (`MGQ_MpCoopSquad::CUT_COLOR`) and the Raid Worlds' marker (`MGQ_MpWorld::RAID_COLOR`) are `BAD`, the featured worlds' `GOLD`, a ghost's outline its member's `MEMBER` or `TEXT`, and every request and offer, the invites, challenges, trade offers, story offers, teleport asks and the join prompt, `MGQ_MpActions::LINE_COLOR` (`OWN`).
- **Motion** (`MGQ_MpUi::Motion`, one per sprite, owned by the sprite's owner, which calls `update` every frame from where it updates the sprite): `show(dx, dy)` makes a hidden sprite visible at an offset from its resting place with opacity 0 and heads for the place and full opacity, a slide with a fade; from a fade out it only turns the opacity back, so nothing jumps; `hide` heads for opacity 0 and sets `visible` false on arrival; `place(x, y)` sets the resting place without a slide (boxes that follow `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`), `slide_to(x, y)` slides there from where the sprite stands; `moving?`, `shown?` (target opacity 255), `hidden?`. Linear steps through `Motion.step_towards`, no easing, which the raid bar's own crop shares. An owner never sets `visible` or `opacity` itself once it has a Motion, and does not redraw while `!shown?`, so the last picture fades out; the owner disposes the sprite and drops the Motion with it.
- **PlayerColor** (`MGQ_MpUi::PlayerColor`): `of(who, member = nil, own = nil)` is `Style::OWN` for the player themself (`:me`, or an id equal to `MGQ_MpOverworldSync::Me.id`), `Style::MEMBER` for a party member (a Peer's `member`, a state Hash through `MGQ_MpCoop::Party.member?`), else `Style::TEXT`, also for a `who` it cannot read, never raising. `label` names the color for the log ("own", "member", "plain"). `who` is a Peer, a state Hash, an id String or `:me`. ui.rbx loads before overworld_sync.rbx and coop.rbx, so the derivations reach them at run time, guarded with `defined?`. Used by the labels above heads, the party and map box, the World overview's rows, the co-op status window and the edge arrows.
- **Confirm** (`MGQ_MpUi::Confirm`, `Sprite_MpConfirmBox`): one yes/no box at a time over any scene. `ask(question, [yes, no]) { |answer| }` opens it (refused and logged while one is open or the question is empty), wraps the question to `Sprite_MpConfirmBox::WIDTH` (400), draws the two choices with the no choice picked first and the hint `HINT` ("Leave: Y   Stay: N, Esc or Numpad 0   Enter: the picked one", the hotkeys by `MGQ_MpHotkeys.label`, drawn whole and smaller when long labels make it wide), at `Z[:confirm]` without a viewport, so it lasts across scenes, and takes the buttons (`Capture.start(SCREEN)`), so the trade scene's windows and the map stay quiet under it; the hotkeys are held by the gate in `MGQ_MpHotkeys.pressed?`. `tick` runs from the `Scene_Base#update` after-hook every frame and hands C# the frame's keys (`put_keys`, the model `confirm`, `ConfirmModel` in [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)): the accept and decline hotkeys (`Key.triggered?`, watched anew on every `ask`), `MGQ_MpUi.cancel?`, confirm (`Capture.trigger?(:C)` takes the picked choice) and left or right (moves the pick), none while `$mgq_text_input`; C# reads none in the first tick after `ask` (a caller asking from a window's own `update` without an `Input.update` would otherwise answer with the press that chose) and answers with the event `ui.confirm` (the pick, or the answer and what gave it, with its sound), which `take_event` takes. An answer takes two ticks: the frame whose keys answer it (or `close`, or the scene no longer being the one `ask` saw) stores it, plays the sound and disposes the sprite at its end, leaving `open?` true and the capture on, and the next tick, after `Input.update` ended the press, stops the capture and calls the block once; so the Enter or Esc that answered never reaches the overview's menu or the trade's windows in the same frame. The block gets false for a close from outside and a scene change. `leave_scene`, from the `Scene_Base#start` after-hook, answers no and finishes at once, since a scene whose start waits in a loop of its own (a co-op battle's `battle_start`) would never tick a box left open, and a start reads no key. The callers: the overview's *Remove from party* and *Leave the party* (`MGQ_MpCoop::Offers`, `REMOVE_CHOICES`, `LEAVE_CHOICES`), the trade screen's cancel (`Scene_MpTrade#on_cancel_trade`); the overview skips its own `update` while `Confirm.open?`.

## Interface presets and layout (`ui_theme.rbx`, `ui_layout.rbx`)

**Presets** (`MGQ_MpUi::Theme`). `PRESETS` holds Monarch, the default (the raid boss bar's maroon `36, 10, 14` with its gold edge `200, 164, 96` in a black rim, gold titles `255, 224, 140`), and Legacy (the chat's black glass, grey edge, no rim, white titles, the mod's look before the presets and the values `ui.rbx` starts the style's colors with), each a color per style constant from `BACK` to `TAB`. `apply(name, why)` copies the preset's values into those `Color` objects in place, so every alias of them elsewhere follows (`CLOSED_SHADE = Style::PLATE`, the raid bar's `EDGE`, `EMPTY` and `TITLE_COLOR`), and counts `serial`, which every picture drawn in the colors keeps in its redraw key; a `Color.new` copy of a style color would not follow. `load` applies `Player.ini`'s `ui_preset` as the script loads, `choose` applies and keeps it. The third preset, Game's Default (`GAME_LOOK`, `game?`), has no colors of its own: `game_colors` works them out from the game's `Window` skin in the game's window color (`$game_system.window_tone`, which the game's *Window Color* option and event command 138 set and every save keeps): `BACK` is the skin's background averaged (`SAMPLE_STEP`) and toned as a window's tone does (`toned`: gray, then add), at the game windows' `GAME_BACK_OPACITY` 192, the shades and `TRACK` mixed from it, `RIM` and `EDGE` the outermost and the brightest pixel down the frame's top border (`frame_line`), `PICKED` the background `PICKED_LIFT` towards the cursor's color, `HEADING` and `TITLE` the palette's system and normal text colors. `follow_game`, after every `Graphics.update`, works them out anew once the window color differs from the one they came from, so the options, a save loaded or an event recolor the boxes on the next frame. The red HP fill and the damage trail of the raid bar, the text colors, the chat's colors and `PlayerColor` are the same in every preset. The row *Interface Look* of Mod Config Remake picks it (`MGQ_MpUi.add_option`: a `:mod_stored` row, so the menu reads and keeps nothing in the save; only a Mod Config Remake that knows such rows gets it).

**Window skin.** `skin` paints a 128x128 window skin in the preset (`paint_skin`, made once per preset): the background in `BACK` made opaque (the window's `back_opacity` carries the alpha), no pattern, a box's frame along the frame square's borders (`SKIN_BORDER`, its middle keeps the scroll arrows) and the cursor as a picked row in its frame; the rest (arrows, pause sign, text colors) is the game's own `Window` skin. Every window, the game's and the mod's, is dressed by the after-hooks of `Window_Base#initialize` and `Window_Base#update` (`dress`): the skin once per `serial` (the preset's, or the game's for Game's Default), and on every update no tone for a preset, since the game's `update_tone` sets the window color on every update and would tint it; with Game's Default the game's tone stays. A game window wears the game's skin and tone too while the row *Look on Game Windows* is Off (`game_windows?`, `Player.ini`'s `ui_game_windows` 1 or 0, on until turned off; `dress_game_windows`); a window re-dresses once `serial` or what it wears changed. A game window keeps its own `back_opacity`. `Skinned` marks the mod's window classes (`Window_MpInfo`, the world screen's, the trade screen's, `Window_PvpLobbyCommand`, `Window_MpStoryChoices` through `Window_MpWorldForm`, `Window_MpWaiting`), which also take `BACK`'s alpha as their `back_opacity`. `ui.rbx` declares the empty module, since `Window_MpInfo` includes it before `ui_theme.rbx` loads; a game window a mod scene makes, the trade screen's help window, is extended with it (`Scene_MpTrade#wear_theme`).

**Layout** (`MGQ_MpUi::Layout`, its rules in C#: `LayoutModel`, see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)). A floating box registers once (`register(id, name, min_width, min_height, resize)`, resize `:both`, `:width` with its height following what it shows, or `:none`; `ui.layout.register` hands C# its place in `Player.ini` too), asks `rect(id, default)` every frame for where to draw (the drag's place while dragged, else `Player.ini`'s `ui_<id>` as `x,y,width,height`, else its default, fitted to its least size and wholly on the screen; C# answers once per default and screen, `ui.layout.rect`, and its events keep the answer up to date, so a quiet frame asks nothing) and `offer`s the rect it drew while it shows, with its depth. `tick`, a collector of the frame's tick, reads the left Alt (`ALT_KEY`, 0xA4; the right Alt is AltGr) and the left button and hands C# the frame while Alt is held or a drag runs, and one frame more after, with the boxes offered since the tick before; C# keeps the rules and posts `ui.layout` (places changed, `Player.ini` to keep or forget, which the script writes, the drag, the box under the mouse, the log): Alt with the button going down on the topmost box under the mouse starts a `Drag`, which moves the box, or resizes the sides within `GRAB` (8) of the mouse; the sides stop at the least size and the screen's edges (`span`); letting go keeps the place (`finish`) and logs it. A second click within `DOUBLE_CLICK_FRAMES` puts the box back (`reset`). While Alt is held an overlay above everything (`Z[:layout]`) outlines every offered box with its name, washes the one under the mouse, draws a grip into a resizable box's corner and names the controls at the bottom (`HINT`). `MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse.clicked?` and `held?` answer false while `arranging?`, so no box takes a click meant for the layout. The row *Interface Positions* says *Moved* while `any_moved?` and puts every box back with *Default* (`reset_all`). The boxes of `ui.rbx` register from its `Scene_Base#start` hook too, since the layout loads after it and *Default* must reach a box not shown yet.

**The boxes.** Each box keeps today's place as its default and works out its least size from its own content, so it always shows everything it holds:

| Box | Id | Resizes | Least size |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chat on the map, in battle, in the shops, the casino and the trade screen | `:chat`, `:battle_chat`, `:menu_chat` | both | 400 wide (tabs and hints), 5 rows (the whisper names) |
| Leader HUD | `:leader_hud` | none | the Small size's (170 wide), so each size keeps its own place at the screen's edges; registered anew after a preset change, whose frame sets the height (`register_layout`) |
| Notification box | `:notices` | both | the hotkey column plus a name (298), 2 rows (`capped` shows nothing in one) |
| Party box and a Raid World's map box | `:party_box` | width | `WIDTH` (296); small mode keeps `SMALL_WIDTH` at the box's right edge |
| World status lines | `:world_status` | width | 266, 4 rows |
| World overview | `:overview` | both | the Players and World pages' columns (560), title, tabs, hint and 3 rows or the longest menu |
| Raid boss bar (map and battle strip) | `:raid_bar` | width | the wider of the title line with the hint at both sides and three gauges with their share (`min_strip_width`), measured at load |
| Waiting box | `:waiting_box` | width | its text and the leave offer, up to 360 |
| Confirm box, message box | `:confirm_box`, `:message_box` | width | the confirm box's choices and either box's whole hint, up to the default width |
| List box of the world screen | `:list_box` | both | the item and button columns (460), a view's note and hint, 3 rows |

A box at its default moves down by `MGQ_MpUi.top_room`; one the player moved stays where they put it, and `top_room` counts the raid strip only while it lies at the top.

**The Leader HUD** (`ui_leader_hud.rbx`, `Sprite_MpLeaderHud` in the map's `@viewport3`, which darkens with the screen's brightness). It draws `$game_party.leader` from a redraw key of its values and `Theme.serial`: in a square as high as the name and both bars, the face in the Large size (`Cache.face`, `stretch_blt`) or in the Small size the map sprite's figure standing and facing down (`draw_figure`: the smallest rectangle of pixels that are not see-through in the frame, kept per character by `figure`, grown by the largest whole multiple that fits or shrunk into the square), the name and `base_level`, red HP and blue MP bars with `Vocab.hp_a`/`mp_a` and the numbers in the Large size, and three bars of `MGQ_MpLeaderHud.exp_rate` for `:base`, `:class` and `:tribe` (`base_exp`/`class_exp`/`tribe_exp` against `current_level_exp`/`next_level_exp`, full at `max_level?`). `Player.ini` keeps `leader_hud` (0 for off inside a world), `sp_leader_hud` (0 for off outside one, the *MGQ Online SP* row, `leader_hud`'s value until the player sets it; `on?` picks by `MGQ_MpOverworldSync.in_world?`) and `leader_hud_size` (Large or Small), set by the `:mod_stored` rows *Leader HUD* and *Leader HUD Size*; the hotkey `:leader_hud` (H) switches the size on the map (`on_map`, from `Scene_Map#update_scene`), unless the HUD is off, the chat box or a wheel is open. It hides while the game's top message shows. At its default it sets `MGQ_MpLeaderHud.room` (its margin, height and the 4 pixels of `BELOW`), which the notification box's default adds below `top_room` in place of its own margin, so the box sits 4 pixels under the HUD, and `lower_windows` moves the map scene's `@map_name_window` and `@gain_medal_window` (through `MGQ_MpGame`) below it, back to the game's place once it hides or is moved. Labels, bubbles, wheels, the overhead raid bar, the edge arrows and the join prompt follow a character and only take the preset's colors. The DLL's `AltMenuGuard` swallows `SC_KEYMENU` for Alt pressed alone and while a text screen takes the keyboard, so letting Alt go never opens the window's menu, in which the game would wait.

## Actions (`ui_actions.rbx`)

**The action wheel** (`Wheel`, `Sprite_MpActionWheel`). Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`, B by default, read with `GetAsyncKeyState`, since neither the game's `Input` nor its gamepad plugin reads it) opens it on the map while no event or message runs, and closes it again. Its choices (`wheel_options`, rebuilt every frame from what the scripts registered) are a ring of up to eight, spread over the arrows' directions in their order (`wheel_places`), and one over the player, the World overview: a globe (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview::WORLD_ICON`, the game's icon 3988) in a square box narrower than the player's sprite, so it never covers the boxes beside it. `Sprite_MpActionWheel.layout` lays the ring out from its directions: the side and diagonal boxes in a column left and right of the player, up and down above and below all of them, and the picture is sized to fit. It opens on the middle; the arrows held point at a choice, which stays picked once they are let go, and a direction without a choice picks the middle. The game's confirm button takes it, and cancel closes the wheel. The wheel's rules are C#'s (`WheelModel` "wheel", see [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame)): `on_map` hands over the frame (`Wheel.follow`: the hotkey, and while open cancel, confirm and the arrows held through `MGQ_MpWheel.put`; or, while the map does not leave it free, why it closes), `Wheel.take_event` opens and closes the capture and logs, and the choice confirm took is run in the next frame's `on_map` (`take_pending`), whose choice closes the wheel (`Wheel.close`, `ui.wheel.close`). `MGQ_MpWheel.places` asks C# once per count (`ui.wheel.places`). A choice without `run` is grey, and taking it shows its `refusal` as a notice. While the wheel is open it holds the buttons (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Capture`, which has the guarded `Input` report none to the game, while the wheel reads them past it), so the player stands still and the game's menu and map keys stay shut. Leaving the map, an event or a message closes it.

The six choices sit where `MGQ_MpWheel.places(6)` puts them:

- Middle: the World overview (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview.open`).
- Up: as a party's member, teleport to the leader (`MGQ_MpCoop::Offers.teleport_option`); else accept the invite of a player nearby (`MGQ_MpCoop::NEAR_TILES`, 2, on the same map) who is outside the party, else invite the players nearby who are outside it.
- Up-right: accept the challenge of a player nearby, else challenge the players nearby, or stop challenging (`MGQ_MpBattlesDuel`).
- Down-right: accept the trade offer of a player nearby, else offer the players nearby a trade, or stop offering (`MGQ_MpTrade`).
- Down: leave the party, or stop an invite nobody took.
- Down-left: chat, grey while the keyboard cannot reach the game (`Background.running?`).
- Up-left: *Report a Bug* (`MGQ_MpBugReport`).
- Left and Right hold no choice and point to the middle.

While the World overview is open, the wheel stays shut and the wheel's hotkey closes the overview instead.

**The line above the player's own head** (`own_lines`, `own_line`, drawn by the Spriteset hook): the first line an `own_line_from` block names wins; else the doings the `own_doing_from` blocks name, joined with ", " and " . . .". `own_lines` gives the line with the seconds and without them: the sprite draws the first while it fits its width, else cuts the second, so a cut never drops a countdown that fits. A doing is `nil`, a String ("Inviting to a party"), or `[String, Time]` with the moment it lapses, which the line writes as "Inviting to a party (12 s)" (`TIME_LEFT`, `doing_text`: the seconds rounded up, the text alone once lapsed or when the lapse is no Time, so a frame count by mistake breaks nothing). Timers of the mod count frames, so a caller converts with `MGQ_MpActions.lapse_in(frames)`, or `lapse_at(since, frames)` for a moment counted from a `Graphics.frame_count`; `MGQ_MpCoop::Invite#frames_left` and `MGQ_MpCoopGather.frames_left(since, frames)` give them. One `Time.now` per frame, read after the blocks ran. Each block runs through `named_by`: one that raises is logged once and left out, so the line and the wheel keep showing. The log compares the texts without the seconds, so a countdown logs once. The doings today: the party invite (coop.rbx), a teleport request to a member (coop_teleport.rbx), the leader's gathering with up to `GatherCalls.NamedMissing` (2) missing members and "+n", and a member's time before coming over (coop_gather.rbx, from the DLL's `gather.frame`), a challenge, the wait for an accepted duel and a team duel's readiness (battles_duel.rbx), a trade offer and the wait for an accepted trade (trade.rbx).

## Chat (`ui_chat.rbx`)

**The chat box.** The chat's hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:chat`, T by default) or the wheel's chat choice opens the chat box, which takes the keyboard through `mp_typing` and the text box in C# (`MGQ_MpUi::TextEdit`) like the world screen's text box, and holds the buttons (`Capture`), so keys that type move nobody. It opens while the map is quiet, or while the player only waits for the party's story (`map_open?`, `MGQ_MpCoopGather.waiting?`: gathering, called, held or reading the leader's pages), and a box already open stays when an event starts, whose messages then wait for it. It opens in a battle too (after `Scene_Battle#update_basic`, which runs in the battle's waits), and in the scenes of menus `MGQ_MpChat.follow_scene` names: the game's shops (`Scene_Shop`), its casino (`Scene_CasinoBase` with the slot machines, `Scene_Poker`, `Scene_CasinoPrize`, each looked up by name and left out when the game lacks it) and the trade screen (`Scene_MpTrade`), after each `update_basic`, their waits included, with the log as the layout box `:menu_chat` above the windows; there it waits while another box of the mod, such as a question, holds the buttons, and the menus offer only the whisper for a player's name. The character of the key that opened it arrives after it opened, since the key is read from Windows before the game's window gets the character, and is left out (the box's `:echo`, within `KEY_ECHO_FRAMES`). The chat acts on what typing did a frame after it was typed, as the box reports it; a command and a space are taken out of the box then, after the frame's typing.
