Enter sends, Escape or the numpad's 0 closes, as the numpad's 0 closes the game's windows (`MGQ_MpUi.numpad_cancel?`, see below, read from Windows; its 0 arrives as the number row's, so what came in its frame is dropped, and a later one finds the typing off), and a line has at most 120 characters (`MAX_LENGTH`). The box has a cursor that blinks every half second (`TextEditor.BlinkTicks` in C#) and shows at once after a change. Typing and Backspace work at the cursor. Left and right move it (`Capture.repeat?`, so a held arrow repeats), Home and End jump to either end, and Delete removes the character after it. None of these keys types a character, so they are read as the game's buttons or from Windows (`Key`). The text moves left as far as the cursor needs to stay in sight. A line goes to the chosen chat (see below) as its own message: the global chat's `chat=<line>` and `name=<sender>` to everyone (`overworld_sync.rbx` tells a state from any other message by its `state` field alone), the map chat's as one message to every game too, with `ch=say` (its name before, which released games read) and the sender's `map` (`tell_map`), and a receiver takes a `ch=say` line into the map chat and drops one said on another map than its own (`receive`); the name covers a line that arrives before its sender's first state. Every line, the player's own included:

- joins the chat log (`Sprite_MpChatLog`, font 16) at the bottom left, above the status line, for 10 s (`LOG_FRAMES`); the open chat box shows the kept lines (`KEPT`), wrapped, with the box on the row below, and scrolls them: up and down a row, Page Up and Page Down a page (`PAGE_ROWS`, the page keys read from Windows, since the game's L and R are Q and W too). `MGQ_MpChat.scroll` counts the rows below the last one shown; the sprite breaks the lines into rows (`rows_of`, once per text), keeps the scroll above the oldest row (`limit_scroll`), scrolls on by the rows of lines that come in meanwhile so the rows shown stay put (`keep_place`), and draws an arrow at the right while older rows lie above or newer ones below. Closing the box shows the newest rows again. The log lies on a viewport of its own, which rises above everything while the box is open (`MGQ_MpUi::Z[:typing]`) and otherwise rests just under the map's topmost viewport (`@viewport3.z - 1`), whose wheels and World overview lie above it, or in a battle above its windows (`BATTLE_ROOM`, `Z[:wheels]`); and T and typing work through `Scene_Battle#update_basic` (`on_battle`), which runs in the battle's waits too;
- shows for 6 s (`BUBBLE_FRAMES`) in a bubble (`Sprite_MpChatBubble`) above the sender, while their ghost is on the player's map, placed from the character's `screen_x`/`screen_y`, so it needs no sprite of `overworld.rbx`.

**Chats, tabs and colors.** A line goes to the global chat (everyone in the world), the map chat (the players on the same map), the party chat or the whisper chat (one player). `/g`, `/m` or `/p` (also `/global`, `/map`, `/party`, `COMMANDS`) at a line's start chooses the chat for it and every line after it (`take_command`; with a space after it the command is taken out of the box as it is typed, and the box names the chosen chat before the text); the chat starts in the global chat on the All tab, also in every world after the first (`reset`, which puts back whatever tab was picked), and a chosen chat holds until the player chooses another; `/s`, which chose it once, is plain text now. `/w <name>` or `/whisper <name>` chooses the whisper chat and whom it goes to: while the box holds the command and the start of a name, a list above the box offers the names of the world's players that fit (`suggestions`, at most `SUGGESTIONS`), Up and Down move the pick, and Enter or Tab (a typed `\t`, or the Tab key read from Windows) takes it and empties the box (`take_suggestion`); a line sent with the command finds the longest name that starts it (`named_in`). A whisper is `whisper=<line>` and `name=<sender>` to that player's seat alone (`tell_whisper`, routed to `receive_whisper`); the sender's log shows `[To <name>] `, the receiver's `[Whisper] `, and Multiplayer InGame.log leaves its text out. `/help` opens a help tab at the far right of the tabs (`open_help`), which shows `HELP` and nothing else, takes no typing but Escape, and closes once another tab is picked or the box closes (`leave_help`). The empty box names scrolling, Alt and `/help` (`BOX_HINT`), and the tab and resize controls while Alt is held (`ALT_HINT`). A click on another player's name in the open box's log (each line keeps its sender's id, the sprite where their name lies, `@name_spots`) opens their menu above the name (`open_menu`, drawn by `draw_menu` on a sprite of its own): `MGQ_MpActions.peer_options`, which the World overview shows too, and Whisper. Pointing or Up and Down pick a choice, a click or Enter takes it through `MGQ_MpActions.choose` (one that leaves, such as accepting a duel, closes the box too), and Escape, the numpad's 0 or a click outside closes the menu alone; it closes once the player leaves the world. The party chat sends `pchat=<line>` through `MGQ_MpCoop.tell`, so it carries the party's id and the party's gate (`coop.rbx` routes `pchat` to `receive_party`, since `ui_chat.rbx` loads before the gate) drops it for everyone else; outside a party it is refused with a notice. The open box shows tabs above the log (`TABS`: All, Global, Map, Party, Whisper; `tabs` leaves the Whisper tab out until the player whispered, got a whisper or chose the whisper chat, `whisper_tab?`, set in `choose` and `add` and cleared with the whisper chat in `reset` when the world closes), picked with a click or the left Alt with left or right (`alt_keys`, the arrows read from Windows every frame, the editor's keys left alone while Alt is held; the right Alt is AltGr, which types). What arrives while the left Alt is held and for `ALT_CODE_FRAMES` after is dropped (the box's `:alt_codes`): Alt with the numpad's arrows, which are its digits, types an Alt code once Alt is let go, a symbol the game's font lacks; a chat's tab also chooses that chat, a command chosen on a chat's tab moves to its tab, and All keeps the chat. A tab shows its chat's lines and the game's own (`Line#shown_in?`). The log is a layout box, `:chat` on the map and `:battle_chat` in battle (`Sprite_MpChatLog::BOXES`), which the left Alt and the mouse move and resize (see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)): its height gives its rows (snapped to whole rows, drawn at the rect's bottom), at least `MIN_WIDTH` (400, which the tabs and the hints need) and `MIN_ROWS` (5, the whisper names above the box); `chat_size` in Player.ini, which the chat's own resize kept before, is read only as the default size. The open chat and each tab have a thin outline in the preset's edge (`OUTLINE`, `Style::EDGE`, `draw_frame`), the chat's white while Alt is held (`RESIZE_OUTLINE`). The picked tab is filled with its chat's color under dark text (`TAB_INK`). A line of any chat, the whispers too (`UNREAD_CHATS`), that comes while neither its tab nor All is picked lets that tab glow yellow at its top right corner, fading toward its middle and opaque at the corner (`UNREAD_GLOW`, `draw_unread_glow`), until its tab or All is picked (`see_tab`, every frame). Letting Alt go never opens the window's menu, in which the game would wait (`AltMenuGuard`, see [Interface presets and layout](#interface-presets-and-layout-ui_themerbx-ui_layoutrbx)). The log keeps each line as a `Line` (name, text, `who`, channel, frames left) and draws its head in color (`draw_row`): the tag (`[Map] `, `[Party] `, the global chat none; `TAGS`) in its chat's color (`CHANNEL_COLORS`, World of Warcraft's: the global chat white as its say chat, the map chat its zone pink 255, 192, 192, the party chat its party blue 170, 170, 255, whispers its whisper pink 255, 128, 255), then the sender's name by `who` (`NAME_COLORS`: the player's own yellow, a member of the player's party green as their label on the map, anyone else white, an admin's gold as the featured worlds, the game's own lines grey), then the text, in the chat's color for the map chat and the whispers (`COLORED_TEXT`) and white otherwise. A bubble is filled with its chat's color, white for the global chat, at 85 % opacity (`Sprite_MpChatBubble::OPACITY`). `log_lines` writes the lines as text, as the tests read them.

**Sent lines, replies and whispers heard.** Every line sent is kept as typed, commands included, in `history` (`remember_sent`: the last `HISTORY_SIZE` (20), no repeat of the last, for the session only, cleared when the world closes); the left Alt with up or down (`UP_KEY`, `DOWN_KEY`, read from Windows with the tab arrows in `alt_keys`) walks through them (`walk_history`: the draft being typed is kept and comes back past the newest; not on the help tab). `/r` (`REPLY_COMMAND`) answers the last player who whispered (`reply_to`, noted in `whispered` with their name and id): it chooses the whisper chat for them as `/w` does, refused while nobody whispered or once they left (`player_of`: by id, else by name for a sender without a state yet; a known id never falls back to the name). A whisper that arrives while the box is closed plays `WHISPER_SOUND` (`Sound.play_recovery`, which nothing on the map plays by itself), once a frame. `UNREAD_CHATS` is every chat, the whispers too, so the Whisper tab glows; while the box is closed, a tab has unread lines and the log still shows lines, the log draws the tab row dimmed above itself (`draw_tabs(chat, true)`, `CLOSED_SHADE` over each tab, the glow over the shade), so the glow is seen and the row goes with the last line; an unread mark alone would keep the row for good on a tab the player never picks. A character the box refuses buzzes once a frame (`@buzzed`), so a paste longer than the box takes buzzes once; Ctrl+V pastes through the DLL's typing path, as the world screen's text boxes do. `ALT_HINT` names the sent lines, `HELP` the sent lines, `/r` and Ctrl+V.

**The admins' chat.** Every line of the global chat is also mirrored to the relay (`MGQ_MpChat.send_line`, the DLL's `chat.send`), which keeps a world's last lines for its admins; the map and the party chat are not. A line an admin says in the World Admin tool arrives as a `chat` inbox entry with the admin's `name`, which the DLL hands to the chat's route as `chat=<line>`, `name=<admin>` and the Symbol key `:relay`, which no player's message can carry, since those are parsed into strings; the log shows it as `[Admin] <name>: <line>` in gold, without a bubble, since no seat said it. A line named `MGQ Online` (`MGQ_MpChat::NOTICE_NAME`) is the relay's own instead, its update notice or an admin's announcement (`POST /v1/admin/announce`): the log shows it red as `// <line> //` in every tab (`MGQ_MpChat.notice`, `Sprite_MpChatLog::NOTICE_COLOR`), and the notification box shows it for ten seconds, broken into as many rows as the box's width needs, in place of the announcement before (`MGQ_MpNotices.announce`).

While the chat box is open, `ui_actions.rbx` leaves the wheel's hotkey to it, and while the wheel is open, the chat's hotkey does nothing.

**The model in C#** (`MGQOnline.Game/Chat/ChatModel.cs` behind `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/ChatTopics.cs`) holds these rules as the script keeps them today, for `ui_chat.rbx` to read once binding 18 drew its screen on the framework: the chosen chat and tab, the Whisper and help tabs, the unread tabs, the commands (`.NET` expressions pinned to Ruby 1.9's ASCII classes, `/i` written out as `[wW]`, `LineBreakers` the script's `LINE_BREAKERS`), the names offered after `/w` and the pick, whispers and `/r` by player id over the room's `Labels`, the lines sent brought back with Alt, sending through `chat.send`'s own way out, receiving with the map's check, the admin's lines and the relay's notices (a route of the room's registry that knows the relay pushed it), the lines' and bubbles' lifetimes on the drain's frame and the reset as the world closes; the box's text is the model's `Box`, which typing hands in (`chat.typed`) and the model edits. Its topics (`chat.open`, `chat.close`, `chat.typed`, `chat.enter`, `chat.escape`, `chat.refused`, `chat.alt`, `chat.pick`, `chat.take_pick`, `chat.tab`, `chat.whisper`, `chat.game_line`) answer the view, the staged `chat.scene` starts the chat's frame, which posts `chat.view` once it changed, and the events `chat.sound` and `chat.announce` play the sounds and fill the notification box; a refusal is the room's notice. `ChatModel.Listen` takes the `chat` and `whisper` routes and the party's `pchat` past the party's gate; until the wiring the scripts keep those routes. The log's wrap, scroll and clicks, a player's menu and the notice queue go to the framework. The golden cases, `fixtures/chat/cases.json`, were recorded from the scripts of 3ee61503 by `ruby fixtures/chat/make_fixtures.rb`; `ChatModelTests` replays them through the room's routes.

## Emotes (`ui_emotes.rbx`)

The emote wheel's hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:emotes`, E by default, which neither the game's `Input` nor its gamepad plugin reads) opens a ring of eight emotes around the player (`Sprite_MpEmoteWheel`): a jump, then the game's balloons (`EMOTES`: surprise, question, music, heart, anger, sweat and idea, rows 1-6 and 9 of `Graphics/System/Balloon.png`, drawn from its full frame). It opens only on a quiet map with nothing holding the player, no action wheel, no chat box and no World overview (`free?`), holds the buttons (`Capture`) and covers the action wheel (`MGQ_MpActions.cover`). The arrows held point at an emote like a joystick (C#'s `WheelModel` "emotes", the emotes placed by `MGQ_MpWheel.places`; the emote confirm took plays in the next frame's `on_map`): the wheel opens pointing at none, keeps the last emote pointed at while no arrow is held, and confirm with none pointed at closes it without an emote; confirm plays the emote pointed at on the player's character (`balloon_id`, or `jump(0, 0)`) and tells every game `emote=<place>` with the map (`play_own`), at most one every half second (`COOLDOWN_FRAMES`, ended too by a loaded save or a new game, which set the frame count back); cancel or the hotkey closes it. Another player's emote plays on their ghost while they are on the player's map (`take`), only one the wheel has.

## Edge arrows (`ui_edge_arrows.rbx`)

`MGQ_MpEdgeArrows` points at the players on the map whose ghost is off the screen: a filled triangle (`LENGTH` 14, `WIDTH` 12, `fill_triangle` row by row, since a Bitmap draws only rects, in the player's `PlayerColor` with outline copies in `Style::OUTLINE`) where the line from the player's sprite to the ghost crosses the screen's edge (`point`: the edge rect is the screen less `MARGIN` (14) on each side and `MGQ_MpUi.top_room` at the top; `axis_reach` finds the crossing), and on the side away from the edge a plate with the name (`MGQ_MpOverworld.name_tag`, cut to `NAME_WIDTH` 90) and the tiles away (`LABEL` "%s %d"; `plate_distance` keeps the plate `LABEL_GAP` clear of the triangle). It hooks `Spriteset_Map#update` and `#dispose` on its own through `MGQ_MpHooks`, with a `Pool` of `Sprite_MpEdgeArrow`s on the map's topmost viewport at `Z[:arrows]` (190, under every line and box).

- **Whom** (`targets`): party members always, everyone in a Raid World (`MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`), on the player's map with a ghost, whose tile's middle lies off the screen (`target_of`, `off_screen?`); the nearest `MAX_ARROWS` (8) by tiles (`distance_x_from`/`distance_y_from`, so a looping map counts the short way), ties by id. A player in a menu or a battle keeps their arrow, since it is them the others look for.
- **When not** (`hidden_reason`): outside a world, while a screen of the mod lies over the map (`MGQ_MpActions.covered?`: the action wheel, or any screen registered with `cover`, the emote wheel, the World overview and the chat box), and while a message shows; then every arrow fades out. Each arrow fades in and out with a `Motion` (`SLIDE_PIXELS` 10 along its angle); the pool keeps up to `LIMIT` (16) sprites, so an arrow fading out can be given to a new player, and finds one by the player's id first, so an arrow follows its player without a new fade.
- **Drawing** (`Sprite_MpEdgeArrow#show`, `draw`): the picture is drawn anew only when the name, the tiles, the color or the angle stepped to `ANGLE_STEP` (10 degrees) changed; the sprite's `ox`/`oy` put the triangle's center at the sprite's place, which `Motion#place` sets every frame.
- **Log** (`note`, `gone_reason`): an arrow that appears is logged with the player, the tiles and the color's name; one that goes with why (left the world, connection down, left the map, not in the party, on the screen, others nearer); a reason that hides them all once when it starts and ends. A frame that fails is logged once and fades every arrow out (`Pool#update`).
- **In C#** (`MGQOnline.Game/Hud/EdgeArrows.cs` behind `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/HudTopics.cs`), for `ui_edge_arrows.rbx` to read once binding 18 drew its screen on the framework: `hud.arrows` takes the frame's reason, the screen, the top strip, the player's sprite and the ghosts on the map (`{seat => [screen x, screen y, tiles, color's name]}`, the tiles counted by the ghost, so a looping map counts the short way) and answers each arrow's seat, place and stepped angle, nearest first, ties by id, logging as `note` does; the room tells who is present, a party member and on the player's map, and whether the world is a Raid World. `hud.arrow_picture` lays out a picture for a stepped angle and the plate's measured width: its rect around the triangle's center, the plate's place, the slide and the triangle's rows, the outline's four passes first. Halves round away from zero, as Ruby's `Float#round`. The golden cases, `fixtures/hud/arrows.json`, were recorded from the script of 3ee61503 by `ruby fixtures/hud/make_arrows_fixtures.rb`; `EdgeArrowsTests` replays them.

## PvP battles (`battles_pvp.rbx`)

Two games swap their teams' builds, then fight the same battle live (see [Live battles](#live-battles-battles_syncrbx)), each player commanding their own team; on the host, the friend's characters are rebuilt as real characters and take the friend's commands.

**Flow.** A duel or a team duel in a world starts a PvP battle (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)). The PvP battle screen outside a world, with its hosting, join codes and Discord invites, was removed on 2026-10-24 (`battles_pvp_lobby.rbx` and the map's and title screen's hooks of `battles_pvp.rbx`); the DLL's exchange below is no longer called by any script.

**Exchange (DLL, `Network/Pvp/`).** `Session.Current` holds the state; threads of an earlier start (a generation) change nothing.
- The host makes a **join code** (`JoinCode`): `mgqmp2;<token>;<relay>`. The token is 16 characters (about 79 bits); the relay is an id of `Relays`, the one this version hosts on (`Relays.Current`). The games never connect to each other directly, so the code holds no address. The host enters its **relay room** (`Serve`) and only then offers the code: on the clipboard (`Clipboard`) and, while hosting, in the activity as party and join secret (through the Discord bridge, see below); until then the PvP battle screen says it is reaching the relay. When the relay cannot be reached at first, hosting fails (`RelayUnreachable`); when it turns the game away as too old (426, `RelayUpdateNeededException`), hosting fails at once, before or after the code is out, with the text the relay names in `X-MGQ-Refusal`, else `Relays.UpdateNeeded`. Once the code is out, the host keeps the connection open with `ping`. When the relay turns it away or was lost, the host tries again, since a friend may hold the code already: after 10 s the first time (`RelayFirstRetry`), since the relay may still hold its last connection, then every 30 s (`RelayRetry`). Hosting stops with a message (`NobodyJoined`) once nobody joined for 30 minutes (`HostWaitLimit`) or the relay closes the lone host for waiting too long (close code 4004, `RelayFrameChannel.WaitedTooLong`). The first guest swaps teams, which cuts the wait (`StopWaiting`). A team too large for a frame fails hosting and joining at once (`TeamTooLarge`). `mp_cancel` turns a waiting Discord invite down, except once a team arrived: ending a battle's link keeps an invite the player accepted during the battle.
- The guest (`Reach`) enters the room the code's token leads to and waits there at most 10 s for the host (`RelayPairTimeout`). The game says whether the relay found no host in the room (`NotHosting`), could not be reached (`RelayUnreachable`), turned the game away as too old (the relay's text, as for the host) or is unknown to this version (`UnknownRelay`). A code that starts like one of any mod version (`mgqmp`, `JoinCode.IsOfAnyVersion`) but does not read is from another version (`OtherModVersion`), which the game tells apart from no code at all; an invite with such a code waits like any other, so joining it can say so. Each side sends one **frame** of at most 256 KB (`IFrameChannel.MaxFrameBytes`; four late-game builds take about 35 KB) over the **frame channel** (`IFrameChannel`; `RelayFrameChannel` carries each frame as one binary WebSocket message through the relay). Every body is **encrypted** (`FrameCipher`): AES-GCM with a key derived from the join code's token and two salts of 16 random bytes each, in the clear (HKDF-SHA256): the guest sends its own first and the host answers with its own, so every connection has a key of its own even when a guest tries the same code again or a relay replays an earlier guest; a counter per direction ahead of each frame and in its nonce, so a frame of another token, or changed, repeated, left out or sent back, fails the check. Inside is a `Message`: `key=value` headers (`game`, `player`, or `refused`), an empty line, the team, in UTF-8. The token itself never travels: a guest's first frame that decrypts proves its join code, and the host turns any other guest away without a message and goes on hosting. The host then checks the `game` fingerprint and answers with its team. The connection then stays open as the **link** (`Link`) that a live battle will need: `kind=game` frames carry the game script's own text both ways (`mp_send`, and `mp_receive`, which the game script polls), `kind=ping` goes out after 2 s without another frame, and `kind=bye` says goodbye; the link goes on with the exchange's cipher. A reader and a writer thread of its own keep the game from ever waiting on the network; only the writer encrypts, pings included, so frames go out in the order of their counters. The link counts as `closed` after a goodbye and as `dropped` when the connection breaks or stays silent for 10 s, which the pings of a game still running never let happen; `mp_state` reports it as `link`. Cancelling or starting anew closes it, so the PvP battle without a live battle still ends it at once.
- **The relay** passes frames between the two games, which only connect out to it, so it works where a home connection cannot be reached from outside (DS-Lite, CGNAT, no IPv6, no forwarded port). The room is named after the token by HKDF with a label of its own (`Relays.RoomOf`), so the relay learns neither the token nor the frames' key and only ever sees encrypted frames. A join code names its relay by id, and `Relays` maps each id to an address; moving to another relay is a new id beside the old one, never a new address for an old id, since a guest always takes the relay the host's code names. The server, its protocol and the move to a rented machine: [Relay/README.md](../Relay/README.md).
- Discord invites and the profile: see [Discord](#discord-optional).

**Team (`Team`, on `MGQ_MpActors::Builds` and `MGQ_MpActors::Items` in `core_actors.rbx`).** `Team` writes and reads the whole team through `Builds`, the Frontline first, then the Backline, at most `MOST_MEMBERS`, below a first line `backline=1` or `backline=0`, the sender's *PvP Backline* option. The host's line holds for the battle (`Team.sent_backline?` on the host, `Team.backline?` of the host's team on the guest); without the Backline, `Battle.start` takes the first 4 members only. A line per member, the **build** rather than its results: `member=` then, split by `;`, the actor id; personal level; job; race; every job and race level (`id:level`); the eight stat growths from items; the skills learned; the abilities learned and the abilities set (`skill type:id.id`); the equipment per slot; the eight stats plus hit, evasion and critical in per mille as the sender sees them, for the self-check; then what of the sender's *save* the character reads: the counters `Builds::COUNTERS` (battles fought, the character's Library counts, affection) and the switches its battle start states wait for that are on. Equipment (`Items`): `w<id>`/`a<id>` for a database item, `s…` for a socket item with its gems, `e…` for an enchanted item with its rolls (rarity, upgrade, sockets, variance, enchantments, stat rolls, trait rolls, gems), which the receiver turns into its own copy and lets the game remake the traits from (`reset_data`, as on loading a save). Only numbers leave the game, never the save format, since loading someone else's Marshal data can run code. The receiver takes only ids its own data has, and logs a line or item it cannot read. `Builds.game` is the build format (`Builds::FORMAT`) plus the sizes of the databases, which must match, since ids and fields differ between versions. The format was checked by writing and reading back real Part 1 and late-game parties (socket and enchanted equipment, 1,136 skills, 182 job and race levels) outside the game.

**Rebuilt characters (`Game_MpActor` in `core_actors.rbx`, `Opponent`).** Each friend's character is a `Game_MpActor`, a `Game_Actor` subclass made for its actor id and given the build (`rebuild`), with its owner's affection, save counters and battle start switches, the extra accessory slot and enchanted equipment its owner's game allowed, and items from its owner's bag; `Opponent` builds on it for the enemy side. The build: job, race, levels (and the matching EXP), stat growth, skills, abilities, equipment, then the game's own `refresh` and `recover_all`. As a real character it has every trait of its equipment, gems, abilities, job and race: pre-battle spells and the other automatic skills, passives, and the stat boosts only a battle applies. It answers for the enemy side where the game assumes the player's party (audited: the battle scripts' references to `$game_party`, `$game_actors`, `actor?`/`enemy?` and Luka):
- allies `$game_troop`, enemies `$game_party`, its index in the troop, always a battle member;
- drawn as a sprite (`use_sprite?`), picture by `Pictures`, placed side by side at the screen's bottom;
- `luca?` false, so Luka's binding, giving up and ero offers stay with the player's Luka;
- `booster_actor_exist_param` counts its own side, where the game counts `$game_party.members`;
- the hit effect of a monster (blink) instead of shaking the screen, and on defeat a monster's flash and sound (`:whiten`) instead of its collapse: it stays on the battlefield and turns grey and see-through once that flash ends (`Sprite_Battler#update`: the game marks it `dead?` when the hit lands, before the battle log tells of the defeat, so a `:whiten` effect while dead starts the silhouette; the opacity is set every frame, since every sprite effect makes it opaque again when it starts), since its team can revive it. It stays dead to the game, so every target choice (`Game_Unit#random_target`, the enemy window, all-enemy scopes) skips it as it skips dead monsters;
- the monster data the battle's code reads of the enemy side (`enemy`, `enemy_id`, `friend`, `lose_event_id`, `escape_level`, `escape_not_count?`, `steal_list`, `boss?`, `hide_name`, `lefx`), with no rewards or recruiting; any other method only monsters have is answered by the monster stand-in (`method_missing`) and logged once, so a call the audit missed does not end the game mid-battle;
- the extra accessory slot when the sender had it (6 slots sent), where the game reads a switch of the receiver's save;
- enchanted equipment even while the receiver's save has enchanting off (`NWConst::Sw::ENCHANT_OFF`, which `equippable?` reads), since the game's `refresh` would take it off: `equippable?` turns the switch off for the check in the switches' own entries (`MGQ_MpGame` `:data`), since the game's setter takes every enchanted item off the player's party when it turns on;
- the sender's save where the game reads the receiver's for a character (fields 12 and 13): the damage boosts of `booster_ex_count` (battles fought in the save, the character's Library counts of carries, defeated enemies, times down, orgasms and steals, its affection), `love` itself, which a won battle never raises in the receiver's save (`love=` does nothing), and the battle start states that wait for a switch (`auto_state_with_switch`), which `on_battle_start` adds or removes to match the switches that were on in the sender's save;
- actions from the game's own actor auto-battle (`make_auto_battle_actions`), which targets its `opponents_unit` and checks what is usable;
- no action without a target: skills aimed at one sex only, or at Luka only while they bind (`ext_scope`), find none in a team without one, which the game never had to handle since only monsters use them on the party, and the battle log then names a target that is not there (`replace_ext_character` fails on `nil`). A `Scene_Battle#use_item` hook shows the game's own "is watching the situation" line instead (`Battle.targetless?`), for both teams;
- the conditions of its automatic skills (pre-battle spells, counters, turn start and end) from its own side (`firing_auto_skills` through a `BattleManager._auto_skill_per` hook): the game checks `$game_party` for "an ally of these ids" and "an ally has this state". Which automatic skill fires is random (`rand < per`, and a `sample` among skills of the same priority).

What stays as the player's side is harmless in a PvP battle or undone by the snapshot: gold and items spent on skills, Library counters, the local actor of the same id that `Game_Actor#refresh` touches. `Battle.check` logs where a rebuilt character's stats or rates differ from the sender's; both measure outside of battle.

**Battle (`Opponents`, `Battle`).** An empty troop is appended to `$data_troops` for the battle only; after `BattleManager.setup`, the rebuilt characters are put into `$game_troop`'s `@enemies` and the escape ratio is made again. Picture: the full picture the Library shows (`NWConst::Library::ACTOR_IMAGE`, 640 × 480 in Graphics/Pictures), at full size, cut to the box around its visible pixels (sampled every 4th pixel, reading them all is too slow) down to the picture's bottom edge, which stands at the screen's bottom like the game's own full-size monsters; the face, enlarged, when there is none. The Library's pictures are the same files as the monsters' battle pictures and the largest the game has; shrunk, they turn jagged, since RGSS scales without smoothing. Drawn by `Pictures.stand_in_for` through `Sprite_Battler#update_bitmap`; their HP bars through `Sprite_Battler#update`, since the HP bar plugin only draws for `Game_Enemy`. The battle runs in the game's own battle replay mode (`$game_temp.in_memory_battle`: no EXP, gold, drops, recruiting or autosave) with *can lose* (defeat revives and returns to the map); `Game_Troop`'s EXP, gold and drop totals are 0 for other mods such as a victory screen. It also turns on the rules every multiplayer battle shares (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:pvp)`): the switch that removes the battle's *Party* command (`NWConst::Sw::FORBID_BATTLE_SHIFT_CHANGE`, 27) unless the battle has the Backline (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:pvp, backline)`), and the switch *No Seduction* (86), which ends common event 6 before it runs a monster's **ero offer** (the low-HP offer of an H-scene for giving up, common event 2000 + enemy id) or conversation. Before it, `Battle.start` Marshals the save contents plus `$game_library`, `$game_system_switches` and `$game_global_system`; the `Scene_Map#start` hook extracts them again and removes the troop, the way the game's own Retry restores a battle. This also undoes whatever rebuilding the characters touched.

**Nothing reaches the disk.** The game writes the system save (Library, system switches, affection) at every scene change (`Scene_Base#pre_terminate` → `SaveSystemData.auto_save`) and when it closes, so the battle scene's end would write the battle's changes before the map puts the snapshot back. While a PvP battle runs, hooks on `DataManager.save_system` (`Battle.as_before_for_system`) and on `save_game_without_rescue`, `auto_save_game_without_rescue` and `save_game_backup_without_rescue` (`Battle.as_before_for_save`) swap in the snapshot for the write and swap back after it. A reset (F12) during a battle goes to the title screen, which makes the save's objects anew but keeps the shared ones; `Battle.forget` puts those back and calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.finish`, which closes a live battle's link, so the friend's game wins instead of waiting. The restore also drops the notices of medals earned in the battle (`$game_temp`'s `@gain_medals`, the medals themselves are in the Library) and the game's Retry data of the battle (`BattleManager`'s `@retry_data`). The feature caches (`CacheActorFeatures`, `CacheUniq`) are keyed by object, not by actor id, so a rebuilt character never shares an entry with the player's character of the same id.

## Backline (`battles_pvp_backline.rbx`)

A PvP battle between two players has the Backline when its host's *PvP Backline* option says so (`Backline.wanted?`, `$game_system.conf[:mp_pvp_backline]`, on by default); a duel's challenger hosts, and tells the option in their state (`challenge_backline`), which the challenge's lines show. Team duels never have it. `Battle.start` then rebuilds every member of the other team: the first 4 stand in the troop, the others wait in `Backline.reserve`, and the balance fills the HP of both Backlines too.

- **The player's own swap** is the game's own shift change (`Scene_Battle#bench_member_ok`, `Game_Party#swap_order`), so it stands at once on the player's screen. `Backline.round_front` reads the Frontline once per round, keyed by the troop's turn count, which only changes as a turn starts; a character standing in a place another held then was swapped in (`swapped_in?`), gives no commands (`Game_Actor#inputable?`) and has no actions (`Game_Actor#make_actions`, which the game runs anew after a shift change). Swapped back within the same command phase, it costs nothing.
- **Telling the other game** (`Backline::Mode`): a guest's commands carry its Frontline's actor ids (`own_order`), which the host puts in its troop before the commands (`take_order`, `take_front`). Once the commands came, the host takes away any actions of the characters swapped in and posts its own Frontline as `pvp_front` after a flush, before the turn's events (`share_order`, `settle_round`); the guest takes it in the stream's order (`stream_kinds`, `take`). Whether a Frontline the other game told fits the team (as many characters as the troop, each of the team once) and whether a game tells its own anew are C#'s (`BacklineFront`, topic `live.backline`, started by `Backline.begin` with the team's ids, ended by `finish`); the troop's order, `stand`, the redraw and taking actions away stay the script's. So both games reorder between the same two sends, and the stream's places (`a0`, `e0`) mean the same characters on both. `take_front` leaves out a Frontline that names a character the team lacks, names one twice or has another size, and draws the troop's sprites anew (`Spriteset_Battle#dispose_enemies`, `create_enemies`). A sprite made for a dead character starts as the silhouette (`Sprite_Battler#update` in `battles_pvp.rbx`).
- **Targets** are places: the game finds a single target by its index when the action runs, so an attack hits whoever stands there by then.
- **Outside both Frontlines**: the stream names the host's own Backline `b<actor id>` and the other side's `r<actor id>` (`Mode.reserve_ref`, `Mode.reserve`), and records their values and names with the battlers' (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.party_side`, `troop_side`), since skills that reach the Backline change them. `Game_Troop#item_target_members` gives such skills the other side's reserve, as `Game_Party`'s gives the player's.
- **Defeat** is the game's: a side loses once its whole Frontline is down, whoever waits behind it.

## Duels (`battles_duel.rbx`)

A duel is `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp`'s PvP battle between two players of a world, carried over the world's room instead of the PvP link. The handshake is the DLL's (`MGQOnline.Game/Duels/DuelHandshake`, topics in `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/DuelTopics.cs`): the challenge, accepting, the start on both sides, declines, call-offs and a team duel's gathering, with their messages, notices and log lines as the script had them (`fixtures/duel`). The script hands it what only the game knows and starts what it hands back from the map:

- **The player's frame** (`before_frame`, before every `Graphics.update`): `duel.frame` with whether the player is free on the map (`free?`: no event, message, transfer, menu or other multiplayer battle) and the game's fingerprint (`Team.game`), staged once either changed. The handshake's timers count the drain's frames.
- **Messages**: the routes `duel` and `duel_call` (the party's gate) hand each message to `duel.take` (`forward`); until the world room takes C# routes (phase 7.0) they pass through the scripts.
- **Actions**: `duel.challenge` (`invite`, with the Backline for the log), `duel.stop`, `duel.accept` (`accept`, with the team, `Team.build` under the fingerprint, while free), `duel.decline` (`decline`, from the notification box). Each answers the view.
- **The view** (`duel.view`, posted after the frame's step and after an action once it changed, so an older view the scripts take after an action's answer never stays): the player's challenge (`inviting`, `targets`, `invite_ends`), what the line above their head names with the frame it lapses at (`doing`), and each player who challenges by seat (`challengers`: near, naming the player, a team duel). `inviting?`, `targets`, `challenged_by?`, `team_duel?`, `state_fields`, `label_line`, `own_doing` and `Offers` read it, never the bridge.
- **Needs** (`duel.need_team`, answered with `duel.team`, `answer_need`): what only the game's data can tell, read when the handshake gets there: how many members another player's team holds (`Team.parse`, a team of none is declined or called off with `team`), the player's own team (`pvp`, with `Team.sent_backline?`) or Frontline (`coop`, `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.team_build`), and how many characters each Frontline of a team duel brings (`Builds.parse`).
- **Notices** (`duel.notice`): shown with `Status.show`; the DLL logs them as the scripts did.
- **Challenge**: like a party invite, for 15 s (`PartyInvite.Frames`), to the players nearby, and to a player anywhere named by the World overview for a minute. The state tells `challenge=1` and `challenge_to` (ids); never a field `duel`, which marks the duel's messages and would turn every state into one. Anyone may duel, in a party or not. The line above the challenger's ghost says "Challenges you to a duel (B)" to the players it reaches, and the challenger's own line "Challenging to a duel (15 s) . . ." (also "Waiting for \<name\>'s duel" after accepting, "Getting the sides ready" for a team duel's challenger and "Ready for the duel" for a called player, each with its seconds).
- **Accept**: only while free on the map. It stops a challenge of the player's own, since they cannot take two duels, sends `duel=accept` to the challenger with the game's fingerprint and team and waits 6 s (`AnswerFrames`) for the answer. While its team is read, the challenge takes no other accept.
- **Start** (`duel.start`, `take_start`, `on_map`): the challenger, still challenging and free, answers `duel=start` with a battle id (`RandomId`) and its own team, and hosts; the handshake hands each side the duel (role, id, the other player's seat and name, their team, the Backline, a team duel's sides), and on the map's next frame `duel.begin` lets it start: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(role, id, [seat], name, :pvp)` and `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Battle.start`, or `duel.withdraw` once the battle did not start. Whichever side cannot start any more (busy, a `start` it no longer expects, a team of another version or none, or an event that came first on the map) calls the duel off: `duel=cancel` with the id drops the other side's duel still waiting to start, and `battle=broken` ends a battle already waiting.
- **Decline**: `duel=decline` with a reason (busy, stopped challenging, another version of the game, an unreadable team, declined), shown as a notice.
- **Over the world's room**: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync` already speaks it for co-op battles; in PvP mode the seats are the other player's, a player who leaves the world ends the battle (`world_gone?`), and `finish` leaves the PvP link alone. Discord and the ghosts need nothing new: `Battle.running?` feeds `pvp_battle_with`, and the state's scene is `battle`.
## Team duels (`battles_team.rbx`)

A duel becomes a team duel when either player leads a party of two or more, unless both play in the same party (`DuelHandshake`, which the view tells per challenger as `team_duel?`). Each side is that party, or the single player; party members who do not lead still duel one against one.

- **Gathering** (in the DLL's `DuelHandshake`): on the accept, the challenger sends `duel=team` to its own party and to the player who accepted. A call counts only from the challenger the player accepted or from the leader of the player's party. The player who accepted, leading a party, passes the call on through the party's gate (`duel_call`) and tells the challenger whom (`duel=side`), who then count on the other side. Each answers `duel=ready` with their game's fingerprint, their Frontline (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.team_build`, the places the duel counts) and their `party_member_max` once free on the map. After everyone answered, or 10 s (`ReadyFrames`), the challenger arranges both sides as a co-op battle's party (`SquadMath.Arrange`: the leader first, each player's share of the Frontline, a single player's whole Frontline) and sends `duel=start` with `team=1` and both sides to those ready, `duel=cancel` (`late`) to the others; without anyone of the other side ready the duel is off, and so it is once the challenger gets busy. A player who left meanwhile keeps their place under the name "?". A player busy by then sends `battle=leave`, which counts them as having left.
- **The battle**: `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(..., :pvp, true)` (`team?`). Every game has its own side as its party, through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.form` (`MGQ_MpBattlesTeam.form`), and the other side as its troop, rebuilt as `MGQ_MpBattlesPvp::Opponent`s that carry their owner's seat (`opponents`, through `Battle.start`'s block). The challenger hosts. A guest on the host's side has the host's party and troop (`same_side?`, like a co-op guest); one on the other side has them swapped (like a one-against-one guest). The host takes commands from every guest (`several?`), each for their own characters (`Commands.commanded_by?`): a guest of its side in its party, a guest of the other side in its troop, by the same places. The Backline stays out of a team duel, whatever the players' options say.
- **Leaving**: a guest who escapes leaves the duel (`leave`) and returns to the map; their characters stay. At the next command phase the host hands them to the first player of their side still in it (`settle`, `heir_of`), told as `team_heirs`, so that player commands them (`Game_MpAlly#inputable?`). The sides' seats and who commands whose characters are C#'s (`TeamSides`, topic `live.team`: `prepare`, `settle`, `take_heirs`, and `other_side_gone?`); the script keeps a copy of the heirs for `commands?`, which the command windows ask every frame, and names them in the log (`heirs_text`). The host wins once the other side is empty; a host who leaves or gives up ends the duel: lost for its side, won for the other (`guest_end`, `end_early`).

## Trades (`trade.rbx`)

A trade moves items and gold between two players on the same map. Key items stay out (`Items.tradeable?`: `key_item?`, enchant stones excepted); only the bag counts, not equipment or the storehouse. The DLL keeps the trade itself (`MGQOnline.Game`'s `Trade/`: `TradeMachine`, `TradeSession`, `TradeOffers`, `TradeTexts`, through `TradeTopics`); the script keeps the offer to trade, the items, the bag, the save and the screen, and reads the trade from the DLL's view.

- **Offer and accept**: like a duel challenge (`MGQ_MpCoop::Invite`, in the script), told as `trading=1` and `trading_to`, never a field `trade`, which marks the trade's messages; the world room hands those to the DLL's route (`WorldRoom.Take`), never to a script. It reaches only players on the same map (`same_map?`). The player who accepts stops an offer of their own and the DLL sends `trade=accept` (`trade.accept` with the seat, after the script found the player free, `busy_reason`); the one who offered, still offering and free, answers `trade=open` with a 24-character id the DLL makes up (`RandomId`, never the game's `rand`), and both games open `Scene_MpTrade` from the map (`trade.open_screen`, `on_map`). Each frame the script hands the DLL, once it changed, why the player is busy besides the trade (`trade.tick`: `busy`, `MAP_NOT_FREE` while the map is not free), its offer (`inviting`, `targets`) and the world's directory id. The line above the player's head counts the offer down ("Offering a trade (15 s)") and the wait for the trade to open ("Waiting for \<name\>'s trade", `TradeMachine.AnswerFrames`, the view's `ends` against `Bridge.frame_number`), through `own_doing`. The trade screen's cancel asks first through `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm` (`Scene_MpTrade#on_cancel_trade`, `CANCEL_QUESTION`, `CANCEL_CHOICES`): yes cancels (`trade.cancel`), no activates the commands again, unless the trade ended under the box, whose windows are disposed by then.
- **The view**: the DLL posts `trade.view` whenever it changed and answers it after every action: `session` (`id`, `partner`, `name`, `stage` `open` or `committing`, `my_rev`, `their_rev`, `confirmed`, `their_confirmed`, `relay_id`, `leaving`, `unsent`, and `refusal`, why the player cannot confirm as far as the trade goes) and `accepted` (`id`, `name`, `ends`). The script keeps it as `Session` with both offers as its items (`mine`, `theirs`), so the screen and the game's hooks read it without the bridge. The other events: `trade.their_offer` (`rev`, `text`, read with `Items.read_offer`), `trade.note` (a message for the screen, `NOTE_FRAMES`), `trade.closed`, `trade.stop_offering` and `trade.declined` (the notification box lets the offer go, a "no" drops the player from `targets`).
- **Offers**: the script clamps a change to the bag, the party's gold and `MAX_AMOUNT` (`set_amount`, `set_gold`) and hands the offer as text to the DLL (`trade.offer`, `Items.write_offer`: `g<gold>` and `<token>*<amount>` separated by semicolons), which refuses a text no game writes (`TradeOffers.IsValid`: `g` and 1 to 10 digits, then amounts of 1 to 99, 1 for a `u` token), bumps the revision and sends `trade=offer` with it. A token is `i`, `w` or `a` and the id, or `u`, an enchanted or socketed copy as `MGQ_MpActors::Items` writes it, and after `~` its name prefix as the enchantment's id, `r` or `p` and its place in that list (`prefix_ref`). Every change takes both confirmations back.
- **Confirm**: `trade.confirm` hands why this game cannot take the other's offer (`Items.receive_refusal`, nil while it can); the DLL's own reasons come first (`ConfirmRefusal`: no trade, the relay deciding, both offers empty, both offers over 32 KB, `TradeOffers.MaxOffersBytes`, the one limit `TradeSeal` and `WorldTrades.Commit` keep too, alike in both games, which build the same text, or the other's offer not one a game writes). `trade=confirm` names the revisions of both offers (`mine`, `yours`). A game whose own confirmation and the other's name the offers as they stand (`TradeSession.Agreed`) hands both offers to the relay (`ITradeRelay`, which `WorldTrades` is): `WorldTrades.Commit` with an id of the trade's id and both revisions as `%04x` of `rev % 65536`, ordered by player id ordinally (`TradeOffers.RelayId`), so both games name it alike and a later confirmation is a trade of its own, and the text `<id>=<offer>|<id>=<offer>` ordered the same way (`TradeOffers.AgreedText`). The relay commits once both games sent the same text. The own offer is `g0` (`TradeOffers.Empty`) until the script hands one and the other's empty until they send one, as the script kept them, so a one-sided gift goes back once as `differ` (the receiving game writes its untouched offer as `g0`, the giving game as empty) and the reopening sends it.
- **Commit**: while the relay decides (`committing`) nothing changes; the DLL reads how `WorldTrades` follows the commit each frame (`CommitNow`), so the script asks the relay nothing. `committed` posts `trade.apply` (the relay's id, the other's name, both offers), and the script applies the trade (`Items.apply`, inside `MGQ_MpCoopEvents.granting`, so a leader's story does not pass it on), notes the relay's id in the save (`$game_system`'s `@mgq_mp_trades`, the last 50), saves (`Saving`: the slot last loaded or saved in the world, else the first free one) and, once a save holds it, tells the relay through the DLL (`trade.done`). `cancelled` opens the trade again unconfirmed under a new revision; an offer or unconfirm from the other player cancels a commit the relay has not decided; a failed commit is cancelled at the relay and asks again for trades to recover.
- **Away and back**: the DLL counts the frames the other player is away from the world room's players (`Peer.Present`, gone once the room forgot them) and ends the trade after `AwayFrames`. A message that cannot go out is logged as not sent. Once the other player is back while the trade is open, the DLL sends the own offer's last revision (an equal revision is ignored there) and `confirm` or `unconfirm` again, so a lost unconfirm never lets the other game commit alone. A trade message that cannot go out also marks the trade (`unsent`): while the trade is open and the other player is in the world, the same goes out again every `PollFrames` until both messages go out, which clears the mark; only the first failed try logs its messages, and nothing is tried again while the relay decides. A game that gets an offer, confirm or unconfirm of a trade it does not have, or no longer has, answers with `trade=cancel` and `reason=off`, which closes that trade on the other side; a stray cancel is never answered.
- **Recovery** (`TradeRecovery` in the DLL, `Recovery` in the script): once per save loaded in a world (the script hands `trade.tick`'s `save`, its `Game_System`'s object id, and `on_map`), on the map and outside a trade, the DLL fetches the committed trades the player has not marked done (`WorldTrades.FetchPending`, followed through `PendingNow`), again every `RetryFrames` (5 s) while it cannot, and posts `trade.recover` with the save it asked for and each trade's id, the other player and both offers apart (`TradeOffers.Sides`, which leaves out a trade not naming the player). The script keeps them (`apply_waiting`, from the map's own frame in `on_map`) until the player is free on the map, since they may come in a battle, a menu or a trade, and drops them once another save was loaded meanwhile, which the DLL asks the relay for anew. One the save lacks is then applied (`Recovery.apply_once`, noted in the save first) and saved; each is then marked done (`trade.done`).
- **Tests**: `MGQOnline.Game.Tests` replays `fixtures/trade/cases.json`, the trades `fixtures/trade/make_fixtures.rb` played through `trade.rbx` of commit eae042b7 before its rules moved (it reads that script through git), frame by frame through a world room; `trade_test.rb` covers the script's side on the fake bridge.

## World overview (`world_overview.rbx`)

A box over the map (`Sprite_MpWorldOverview`), not a scene, so the world runs on behind it; it holds the buttons (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Capture`) while open. Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys`, F11 by default) or the wheel's middle opens it, in a world and in single player; its hotkey, cancel, the wheel's hotkey, a click outside the box, leaving the map, an event, a message or the chat box close it.

- **Single player** (`SOLO_PAGES`, `SOLO_TITLE`): outside a world the hotkey opens it too, titled *Single player* (`world_name`, an empty `title_tail`), with only the Quests page and the pages of other mods; it remembers whether it opened in a world (`@in_world`) and closes once a world opens or closes under it (`tick`, `openable_while_open?`).
- **Pages** (`PAGES`, `pages`, `page`, `turn_to`, `turn`, `turn_by_keys`): *Players*, *World*, *Quests* and in a Raid World *Unlocks*, switched with left and right (`Capture.repeat?`) or a click on the tabs drawn under the title (`Sprite_MpWorldOverview.tab_at`, `TAB_WIDTH`, the style's `GAP` between them); turning closes a player's menu and has a page with a head, a list and a pane read again (`forget_model`). While another page shows and a player's call waits on the Players page (`view[:calls]`), the Players tab carries a gold mark (`CALL_MARK`), since the notification box hides behind the overview. The title (`draw_title`) is the world's name with its type and a Raid World's difficulty after it (`title_tail`: " | Raid | Hard", `WORLD_TYPES`, `difficulty_name` from `MGQ_MpWorldDifficulty.value`), cut before the party's size on the right. `update` returns at its top while `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.open?`; `page_lines` and `page_view` build the page's lines and view, `hint_of` the hint at the bottom (`PLAYERS_HINT`, `PAGE_HINT`, `MOD_HINT`, `MOD_PANE_HINT` in a mod's pane). The Players page draws a scrollbar (`Style.draw_scrollbar`) and the mouse wheel moves its pick.
- **Pages with a head, a list and a pane** (`world_overview_pages.rbx`, `SPLIT_PAGES`): each page has a `Model` (`model`, read every `READ_FRAMES` and on turning to it, kept as the same object while nothing changed, which the drawing's signature goes by): a `Head` (a portrait, `Looks`, or an icon in a `PORTRAIT` box, a title with a note at the right, and lines or a grid), the list's rows (`[:none, text]`, `[:fold, Fold]`, `[:entry, entry]`) and what can be picked (the head's entry first, then the groups and their entries). Every group is a `Fold` (`add_group`) whose state the page keeps per key until the session ends (`fold_open?`, `toggle`). `row_view` tells what a row shows (`RowView`: label, text, note, dimmed, gauge), `pane_of` what the pane shows (`Pane`: title, portrait, lines of `[:text]`, `[:item, PaneItem]` with an icon or portrait, `[:gauge]` and `[:gap]`; `pane_layout` sets consecutive items in rows of as many columns as the widest fits, `item_rows`, `PANE_COLUMNS`, `COLUMN_GAP`). State per page (`split_state`: pick, list scroll, pane scroll, kept within the rows as the box's height changes); `update_split` picks with up and down, opens or folds a group with confirm (`toggle`), scrolls the pane with page up and down (`:L`, `:R`); `mouse_split` picks by a click (`entry_at`), and the mouse wheel (`wheel`, `MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse.wheel`) scrolls the list a row a notch or the pane `WHEEL_LINES` (`area_at`). The drawing (`draw_split`, `split_layout`, `LIST_SHARE`, `SPLIT_DIVIDE`) caches fitted texts (`fitted`, `FITTED_MOST`) and draws a scrollbar for the list and the pane (`Style.draw_scrollbar`).
- **Pages of other mods** (`world_overview_mods.rbx`): a mod adds an object to `ModPages` (`add`, `all`), defined by whichever mod loads first so neither names the other. While its `shown?` holds, `mod_tabs` lists it after the own pages (at most `MOD_TABS`, two, which `TABS_WIDTH` makes room for) under a key `mod_<n>` (`MOD_PAGE`, `mod_page?`, `mod_page`). `mod_tabs` is asked once a frame, since the overview asks for its pages many times a frame. `mod_model` lays it out from its `head` (title, note, lines, and the looks of the portrait beside it), `groups` (a `Fold` each) and per entry its `row(key)`, kept in a `ModEntry` so a change redraws. Only the picked entry's `pane(key)` is read (`mod_pane`, every `READ_FRAMES` or after `forget_model`): title (in line with the portrait's top), looks as `[character_name, character_index, face_name, face_index]`, lines `[:text, s]`, `[:item, name, note, dim]`, `[:control, id, name, note, tone, less, more]` (a `Control`), `[:gap]`, a line under the title (`Pane#subtitle`, the whole width under the buttons), and `menu(key, [])` as the buttons beside its title (`Pane#buttons`). Confirm, or a second click on the entry picked, moves the cursor into the pane (`focus_pane`, `@focus` over `focus_targets`: the buttons, then the controls); Up and Down step, Left and Right run `adjust(key, id, step)` on a control (nil once changed, else the refusal, which buzzes and goes to the notification box) or step between buttons, Ctrl or cancel go back to the list (`update_focus`, `leave_or_focus_pane`); clicks press a button or change a control by its arrows (`click_pane`, `Sprite_MpWorldOverview.pane_target_at`). A button opens its menu (`press_button`, `mod_menu`, read once per path: `menu(key, path)` lists the choices after the button and those in `path` as `[text, enabled]`); a choice with choices after it opens them, the last runs `choose(key, path)`, whose text goes to the notification box, closes the menu and reads the page again (`choose_in_mod_menu`); cancel steps back (`back_in_mod_menu`); pointing and clicks follow `mouse_mod_menu`. The menu hangs under the pane's buttons (`menu_origin` with a `:focused` view); a control's tone (`:good`, `:warn`, `:bad`, `:dim`) colors it (`TONES`), its arrows are bright while Left or Right may change it. The hint is `MOD_HINT`, `MOD_PANE_HINT` while the cursor is in the pane.
- **The World page** (`world_model`): its head is the world's icon (`WORLD_ICON`) with its name, its type and difficulty as the note (`world_note`), its players online (`PLAYERS_LINE`, `PLAYERS_OF`) and in a Raid World its story (`STORY_LINE`, `world_story_text`: the world's part from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.world` and the route by the number of its variable, *Not told yet* before one is told), else `CLASSIC_STORY`. The list holds the objective (a `Note` of the main quest's messages, see the Quests page) and in a Raid World every raid boss as a `BossEntry` in a group per section with its count (`BOSS_SECTION`; `boss_sections`: the sections of `MGQ_MpRaidBosses.sections` in story order, each boss once in the order the story meets it, a pool of a fight with its own name, such as Sisel's, after its section's bosses; only the sections the world's story reached, `reached?` by `SECTION_REACH` from `MGQ_MpWorldStory.part_of` and the routes in `done`, and those holding a boss the relay told a pool of; the pools whose bosses the tables lack last under `OTHER_SECTION`; `tier_of` and `section_of` find a pool's tier and section by name, else by `MGQ_MpRaidPool.troop_of_key`; `boss_of` averages the share left of a boss's pools from `MGQ_MpRaidPool.pools` and `left_now`, `UNTOUCHED` for one never reported, `defeated` once all are, and `by` of the pool hurt last). A boss's row shows its share (`share_text`, rounded, at least 1% while anything is left) over a thin gauge (`ROW_GAUGE`), or `DEFEATED`; its pane its section, its tier with what `MGQ_MpRaidBosses::TIERS` gives its enemies (`TIER_LINE`), a gauge (`PANE_GAUGE`) and who last hurt it (`LAST_HURT`). Without bosses the list says `NO_BOSSES`, in a Classic world `CLASSIC_BOSSES`.
- **The Quests page** (`quest_model`, in every world): `walk` runs through an event list as the game would run it now without running it (branches on switches, variables, party members, items, and the interpreter's question methods with whole numbers through `check_holds?`; first choice; label jumps; calls up to `OBJECTIVE_DEPTH`; an exit ends it). The main quest (`main_quest`) is every message of Amira's event (`OBJECTIVE_EVENT`, CE 108) in a branch, her network's branch passed (variable 1009, `NETWORK_VARIABLE`), her side quest lists (`SIDE_EVENTS`) skipped; `plain_message` joins a message's lines without the speaker (`SPEAKER`, `speaker_of`) and the text codes (`TEXT_CODES`). Side quests (`side_quests`): CE 131, or CE 135 from Chaos route step 21 (`CHAOS_ROUTE`, `CHAOS_SIDE_STEP`). Companion quests (`companion_quests`): the Pocket Castle's bulletin board, map 228 event 37 (`BOARD_MAP`, `BOARD_EVENT`, read once a session by `board_list`). In both lists an entry starts where it sets variable 11 to 1 (`QUEST_COUNT`, `quest_mark?`) and keeps the messages of its block by its first speaker (`quests_of`); the network counts there. Each is a `Quest` (kind, speaker, messages, a side quest's number, and a companion's `Looks` by name through `looks_of`: the party's character of that name first, else the first of `$data_actors` with a map sprite; a Japanese name in a translated game finds none) whose row shows its first sentence (`first_sentence`, `SENTENCE_END`, `ABBREVIATIONS`). The head is Amira (actor 540, `AMIRA`) with the main quest; behind the page `draw_backdrop` draws her Library picture (`NWConst::Library::ACTOR_IMAGE[540]`, `backdrop`) at `BACKDROP_OPACITY` 36. Portraits (`draw_portrait`) follow the Mod Config row *Quest Portraits* (`PORTRAIT_OPTION`, Player.ini `quest_portraits`, `portraits`): *Sprites* (`Sprite_MpLeaderHud.draw_figure`) or *Faces* (`Sprite_MpLeaderHud.draw_face`), on the box's background.
- **The Unlocks page** (`unlock_model`, only in a Raid World, `pages` leaves `RAID_PAGES` out elsewhere): its head is Luka (actor 1, `LUKA`) with the catch-up experience (`EXP_HEADING` with `MGQ_MpWorldCatchupExp.target`, `YOUR_LEVEL_NOTE` with `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.counted_level`) and a grid of the formation's members, two a line (`exp_cells`: name, `LEVEL_SHORT`, factor), or `NO_CATCH_UP`. The list holds a `Level` per level of `MGQ_MpWorldCatchup.unlocks`: those whose every unlock the player got in a group folded at first (`PASSED`), the others in an open one (`COMING_UP`); `NOTHING_UNLOCKED` without any. A level's row names its unlocks and says `DUE`; its pane (`level_pane`) lists what it brings: the gains of moving on from a part (`MGQ_MpWorldCatchup::Gain`, from `gains_of`: the key items and the story's and side quests' rewards of the next part the world's story passed, and of the player's own part those their story has not passed yet), key items and items that unlock a job or a race (`UNLOCK_ITEM_TAG`) first, then moving on (`MOVING_ON`), the companions by their portrait, the skills, the Makina and the other items by their icons, each `DONE` and dimmed once the player has it or `DUE`, a group each half a line apart; a note every unlock shares goes once into the title (`TITLE_JOIN`).
- **Story and title texts** (`OverviewText` in `MGQOnline.Game`, `OverviewTopics`): the scripts read the game and the DLL writes the texts. `story_code` reads the progress (1001), the routes' variables (1141 to 1143) and the sides' switches (4 and 5) by their ids (`PROGRESS`, `ROUTES`, `SIDES`) and hands them to `overview.story` (`{"p"=>..., "routes"=>{"chaos"=>..., "destroyer"=>..., "judgment"=>...}, "sides"=>{"ilias"=>..., "alice"=>...}}`), which answers the code: the part by the progress alone, in Part 3 the first route under way (chaos, destroyer, judgment), else the first side chosen. `overview.story_text` writes a code (*Part 2: Alice side*, empty while unknown), `overview.world_story` the world's part as `story.part` finds it (`code` and `text`; the routes `ad`, `mr` and `chaos` of variables 1141 to 1143 are the Destroyer, Judgment and Chaos routes), and `overview.title` (`raid`, `difficulty`) the title's `tail` (*\| Raid \| Hard*, the difficulty's name from `WorldDifficulty` in plain case, ASCII only as Ruby 1.9's `capitalize`) and the World page's `note` (*Raid \| Per player*). Each answer is kept by its request (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview.cached`, at most `TEXTS_KEPT` of a kind), since the title and the rows are drawn every frame. The golden cases, `fixtures/overview/story.json`, were recorded from `world_overview.rbx` by `ruby fixtures/overview/make_fixtures.rb` before the texts left it. The raid boss summary stays Ruby: it reads the boss tables of `battles_raid_bosses.rbx`, which move with the Raid Designer's model, and `reached?` reads the world's story, which phase 3 moves.
- **What every state tells** (`state_fields`): `place` (the map's shown name, `display_name`, empty on a map the game names nowhere), `lv` (the highest base level of `battle_members`, Luka included, `formation_level`, kept as it was during a co-op battle) and `progress` (`part`, `part:side` or `part:route`, such as `2:alice` or `3:destroyer`). Level and story are read every 3 s (`READ_FRAMES`). The story is read by name in `$data_system`, the translation's or the untranslated game's (`PROGRESS`, `SIDES`, `ROUTES`): Part 2 from progress 19, Part 3 from 34 (`MGQ_MpWorldStory.part_of`, as the DLL's `StoryParts` and the relay count them); the route only in Part 3, else the side. In a party the members borrow the leader's story, so they show the leader's. In a Raid World it also tells `flv`, the highest base level of the player's share of the Frontline, Luka included (`front_level`: `battle_members` cut to `MGQ_MpCoopSquad.own_share`'s Frontline share, else the first character); a Classic world never sends it.
- **The list** (`rows`, `lines`, `own_row`, `row_of`): every player online and the player, grouped by place (the player's first, then by name), each group by name with the player first. A row shows what they do (the ghost's icon), a crown for a party's leader, the name in the player's color (`PlayerColor.of(row.player, row.member)`, cut to its column), the party's size, "Lv n", the place in the story ("Part 2: Alice side") and the ping. A player whose party invite or duel challenge reaches the player shows it in place of their story (`call_of`). The title shows the world's name and the player's party ("Party 2 / 4").
- **The menu** (`menu_options`): for another player, accept their party invite (also one that names the player from afar, `Party.invited_by?`), invite them wherever they are (`Party.invite(id)`, told as `invite_to`), or remove them from the party as its leader (`Party.remove`, after the confirm box's "Remove \<name\> from the party?", `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm.ask`, `REMOVE_QUESTION`, `REMOVE_CHOICES`), and accept their challenge or challenge them (`MGQ_MpBattlesDuel`); on the player's own row, leave the party (after "Leave the party?", `LEAVE_QUESTION`; a party that dissolved while the box asked is not left, `party_options(asking)`; the wheel's *Leave the party* asks nothing, since picking a side and confirming is a deliberate pair already) and stop inviting or challenging. The arrows and confirm, or the mouse, pick and take; choices that cannot be taken are grey and say why.
- **The mouse** (`MGQ_Multiplayer::Mouse` in `Multiplayer.rb`): `GetCursorPos`, `ScreenToClient` on the window in front and `GetClientRect` scale the pointer to the game's screen while the game is in front; the left button is read like F11. Pointing picks a row or a choice once the mouse moved, a click opens or takes it.
- **The party box** (`Sprite_MpPartyBox`, `party_rows`): while the player is in a party of two or more and the overview is closed, a box in the top right corner of the map lists the party: the leader first with a crown, then by name, each with the icon of what they do (`row.icon`; `AWAY_ICON` and `Style::DIM` while their connection is down, `away?`), the name in its color (`color_of`: `PlayerColor.of(row.player, row.member)`, the player's own yellow, a member green; cut to the name column with `MGQ_MpUi.cut`), "Lv n" and the ping, each in a fixed column (`COLUMNS`, `LEVEL_WIDTH` fits "Lv 100", `MGQ_MpOverworld::PING_WIDTH` (64) the widest ping "999+ ms", drawn by `MGQ_MpOverworld.draw_ping` as in the overview, `WIDTH` is their sum, so level and ping never meet), and their place on a smaller line below (`draw_place`). The log names each listed player with whether they lead and their color (`color_label`). `fit_place` shortens a place to the box: the Pocket Castle's short name (`SHORT_PLACES`), then without its part in brackets, then cut with "..." before a floor, layer or area (`PLACE_FLOOR`) it keeps; the game's names run up to 45 letters. It builds rows for the party only, since it redraws every frame it changed. Its hotkey (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:party_box`, Tab by default, which the game does not read) makes it small and full again on the map, in a party, with no screen of the mod over the map (`MGQ_MpPartyBox.on_map`, `toggle`); the choice is kept in Player.ini (`party_box_small`), so it holds after a restart and in every world. Small, it lists a row per player with the crown, the icon, the name and the ping, without title, level or place, `SMALL_WIDTH` (198) wide (`draw_small`, through the same `draw_line`).
- **A Raid World's party** (`MGQ_MpPartyBox.sections`, `draw(party, others)`): in a party the box lists the party wherever its members are (`MGQ_MpWorldOverview.raid_party_rows`: `party_rows` with each player's "Lv n" from `flv`, the player's own from the Frontline), with their place lines, and below it under "Map n" (`draw_heading`) the players on the map outside the party, a line each, capped as below; small, the party's lines come first. A party member on another map stays listed, as in a Classic world.
- **The map box** (`draw_map`, `map_rows`): in a Raid World (`MGQ_MpPartyBox.map_box?`, `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) outside a party the box lists everyone on the player's map (`Scope.peers_here`) while someone else is on it: the party's members first and green, its leader first with the only crown, then the other players by name in their `PlayerColor`, each with their icon, "Lv n" from their `flv` and the ping, and no place line. The title says "Map n", the number of players on the map. The box grows with its players up to `MAP_LINES` (10) lines; past them it lists 9 and a line "+n more" (`capped`, through `MGQ_MpUi.capped` and `MORE_TEXT`, keeping the player's own line), small too. Its hotkey works while someone else is on the map, or while the player is in a party.
- **Models in the DLL** (`MGQOnline.Game/Overview`, `OverviewTopics.RegisterRoom` from `WorldRoomTopics.Register`): `OverviewState` keeps what the player tells the others (`lv`, `flv` in a Raid World, progress, place); the DLL asks the game to read every 180 drain frames through the `overview.read` event and keeps the levels from before a co-op battle while it runs (`overview.tell`, `overview.fields`). `OverviewRows` builds the Players page, the party box and the map box from the `WorldRoom`; `OverviewFeed` hands them to the scripts once per change through `overview.rows` (serial). `OverviewPages` lays out the World, Quests and Unlocks pages from what Ruby hands (sections and tiers, quest texts, unlocks) and the `PoolLedger` (`overview.world`, `overview.quests`, `overview.unlocks`); folds persist per session (`overview.fold`). The scripts are wired to these topics with binding 18. Goldens: `fixtures/overview/make_models.rb` writes `state.json`, `rows.json` and `pages.json` from the scripts' own methods; ties that Ruby's unstable `sort_by` leaves open are ordered by seat.

## Party size and leader

`MGQ_MpCoop.size_of(id)` and `leader_of(id)` read a party's size and leader from the party view's `parties`, as the DLL finds them (the player the party's id names, else the lowest id; a player who just joined counts only once the leader's list names them, so the leader turning them away reaches them), and `leads?` tells whether a player leads their party of two or more. Every party script asks `MGQ_MpCoop.party_leader` and `party_leading?` for the leader of the player's own party of two or more. Any party can be shown, not only the player's own: a ghost's label shows "2 / 4" after the name and a crown (`MGQ_MpOverworld::CROWN_ICON`, 226, the game's King's Proof) before it; the player's own line above their head (`Sprite_MpOwnPing`) shows the same next to their ping. A party holds at most four players (`Party::MAX_PLAYERS`).

## Co-op battles (`battles_coop.rbx`)

When a party member's game sets up a battle (`BattleManager.setup`, which random encounters, event battles and story bosses all go through), the party members on the same map who are walking it, reading an event's messages, or on it with their window in the background, which keeps running (their state's `scene` is `map`, `event` or `away`), are invited to it. When nobody is, the in-game log says what each party member does. The party's leader is the **host** and computes the battle, so the leader's game and mods decide it; the others are **guests** and play it back through `battles_sync.rbx`, now over the world's room. When the leader is not among the members invited, the game that set the battle up hosts it.

- **Leader leads** (`offer`, `await_leader`, `lead`, `refuse_lead`; in the DLL `AskLeader`, `LeaderWait`, `LeaderEnd`, `ServeRequest`): a member whose game sets up a battle while the leader is invited sends the leader `coop=lead` with a battle id it chose, the troop, escape, lose and the map, and becomes a guest of that battle (`join_world(:guest, ...)`). Its battle start (`Live` in `battles_sync.rbx`) waits up to 6 s (`LeaderFrames`, "Asking <leader> to lead the battle...") for the leader's `coop=invite` with that id. The leader, on the member's map, no other battle running and free within 3 s (`AcceptMilliseconds`), sets up the same troop and hosts it with the member's id, inviting the member though their state says `battle`; otherwise it answers `coop=no_lead`. A refusal, a leader gone or a silent one leaves the battle to the member, who hosts it (`host`) and drops a late invite for the id it asked with (`@dropped_bid`). A leader whose member did not join calls the battle off for everyone (`call_off`, `broken`), since the encounter was the member's.
- **The matchmaking in the DLL** (`MGQOnline.Game/Coop/CoopMatchmaking.cs` and `CoopMatchmaking.Invites.cs`, topics in `MGQParadox.Multiplayer/Topics/CoopRoomTopics.cs`): the offers, the held encounters, the stand-still, the encounter's steps, the players a Raid World's battle takes, the invites, the requests to lead and the rivals, below. The scripts hand what only the game knows (`facts`: the map and tile, the live battle, the battles' rules, a PvP battle or Library replay, the scene, the map's event, a trade, the leader's call, a transfer or step under way, the co-op party, a late join and its own troop, the running battle the player asks to join or gave up on, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.asking_battle` and `MGQ_MpBattlesJoin.gave_up_battle`, and whether the story that calls the player is the waiting invite's) with each call, and before every frame once they changed (`before_frame`, `coop.frame`), so the co-op messages the frame brings read them; `offer_request` adds the battle's: escape, lose, a side battle, empty pools, a boss, the enemies' rates, the raid bosses' invite fields, the story's readers, a member's battle the player leads, those who stay out. The DLL's routes take the party's `coop` and a Raid World's `map_coop` before the scripts: invites, requests to lead, the leader's refusal, `soon` and `freeze` it handles itself, every other kind (`off`, `hot`, `pick` and their answers) it hands over as the event `coop.message` (`take_message`, `take`), an `off` going back through `coop.take_off` once the running battles had their say. The scripts take the steps only the game can take (`act`: `join` makes the battle live, as host or as the guest who asked the leader, `ask_hot`, `fight_alone`, `start_held`, `accept` an invite, `lead` a member's battle, `follow` a battle the player's host yielded to, `miss`, `break_off`, `call_off_own`; `yield_to_rival`, `await_leader` and `decline_waiting` call their own topics); every answer and the event `coop.status` carry the status the scripts read (`holding`, `frozen`, `busy`, the invite that `waiting`, a `rival` kept, `asking` the leader, a `requested` lead and what `waits` for the player, kept by `take_status`). The DLL sends the co-op messages and logs them as the script did, and shows its notices through the events `coop.notice` and `coop.drop_notice`. Its golden scenarios, `fixtures/coop/match.json` and `invites.json`, were recorded from the script of e2a51fe5; `GameScript/Tests/match_support.rb` stands in for it in the script tests, where `coop_deliver` hands a message over as the routes do, and replays them as it loads.
- **Stretched encounter steps** (`party_encounter_steps`, after `Game_Player#make_encounter_count`, installed at run time; `coop.steps` in hundredths): while another player who would fight the encounter stands within 20 tiles of the player (`EncounterPlayers`, `Near`: a party member in a Classic world, any player whose connection stands in a Raid World), the steps the game drew are multiplied by `EncounterStretch` (1.5) and a random factor of 1 ± `EncounterMargin` (0.25), drawn by the DLL, so the players' steps never fall into step; a player with nobody near keeps the game's draw. Stretching once per party member kept a party of four from meeting enemies at all, and counting the whole map kept players far apart on the field from meeting any.
- **Held encounters** (`encounter`, `Hold`, `Poll`, `coop.take_soon`): a random encounter (`Game_Player#encounter`) whose battle the game just set up waits up to 3 s (`HoldFrames`, counted by `coop.poll`, once per map frame) while party members on the map are busy (`BusyMembers`: any scene but `map`, `event`, `away` and `battle`, such as a menu, typing or a vehicle). `offer` holds it instead of inviting, `encounter` then returns false, the player stands still (`Game_Player#movable?`) and opens no menu (`Scene_Map#update_call_menu`), and each busy member is told `coop=soon`, which shows for a moment as a message in their notification box (`MGQ_MpNotices.message`). Once every one of them is back on the map, or the time ran out, the poll answers `start_held`, and the scripts offer the battle as usual and call the battle scene (`start_held`); an event that started meanwhile drops it, and so does the title screen (`drop`, `coop.drop`).
- **The party stands still** (`coop.freeze_party`, `coop=freeze`, which the DLL takes, `frozen?` from the status): whenever a member's encounter is held, hosted or asked of the leader, every party member is told `coop=freeze` with the map. A member on that map stands still and opens no menu (through `hold_player`) until they join that battle (`accept`), turn it down (`Decline`), set up a battle of their own (`offer`) or 10 s pass (`FreezeMilliseconds`, by the clock, since a member in a menu counts no map frames), and reads "Enemies! <Member> is in a battle." in the notification box for as long as the hold (`FreezeFrames`, keyed `[:soon, seat]`, which the `coop=soon` notice shares); every way the hold ends early goes through `Thaw(reason)`, which drops those notices with the reason.
