Before, only the member who met the enemies and the leader stood still while the others could walk off before their invite came.
- **Invite** (`host`, `coop.host`): the host makes the battle a live co-op battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(:host, ...)`), turns on the multiplayer rules (`MGQ_MpBattles.begin(:coop)`) and tells the party `coop=invite` with the battle's id, the troop, escape and lose, the invited seats and the map. Not while another multiplayer battle, a Library replay or no party is there.
- **Accept** (`on_map`, `accept`; in the DLL `TakeInvite`, `ServeInvite`): an invited member on the host's map joins once free on the map (no event or transfer of their own), within 3 s (`AcceptMilliseconds`, counted by the clock (`Expired`), since an invite that came outside the map waits for it), else turns it down (`battle=decline`). A member in a battle of their own turns it down at once. Joining sets up the same troop (without inviting anyone itself), becomes a guest (`join_world(:guest, ...)`) and calls the battle scene from the map, which plays the game's own battle music and transition. The leader's story dialogue on screen is cleared.
- **Gather** (`gather`, at the host's battle start; the DLL's `CoopJoins` takes the joins and declines from the live battle's session, `coop.gather`): the host waits up to 6 s (`JOIN_FRAMES`) for each invited member's `join` (`own_build`: the builds and HP and MP of as many characters as their squad holds with two players, the most any battle needs, and their `party_member_max`) or decline. Without anyone, the battle is the host's own (`stand_down`). Otherwise it sends `roster`: the troop (`troop_entries`: each enemy's id, screen x and y, and whether hidden) and every player (`arrange`): seat, name, builds, HP and MP, `party_member_max`, the order of their places, and their shares of the Frontline and the Backline (`MGQ_MpCoopSquad.share`), the party's leader first, then by player id. A guest (`join`) sends its `join` at its battle start and waits for the `roster`. A guest whose own troop differs, as through a mod of either game, fights the host's instead (`take_troop`): it rebuilds the enemies as `Game_Troop#setup` does, names them apart again (`make_unique_names` with `@names_count` emptied) and draws them anew (`Spriteset_Battle#dispose_enemies`, `create_enemies`), since the host names every enemy by its place. It pays out its own rewards for them. Only a host's enemy whose id is no enemy of this game's database keeps the guest's own troop.
- **The party** (`form`): every game builds the same party in the roster's order, each player's share of the Frontline (`Player#lines`; a player is a `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop::Player` record, which the messages carry as its fields in order). A player's own characters are their real ones; everyone else's are `Game_MpAlly`, a `Game_MpActor` on the party side with its owner's seat (`mp_seat`), HP and MP, which this game's player never commands (`inputable?`) and for which the host's computer chooses nothing while it waits for its owner's commands (`make_actions`, `awaits_commands?`). The party is named after the player's own characters (`Game_Party#name`, `party_name`), as when it runs away or is defeated, since its first character may be another player's. `Game_Party#battle_members` returns this party while the battle runs (hooked once the first scene starts, since plugins may define it anew), and `bench_members` the player's own share of the Backline (`own_bench`); `all_members` stays the player's own, so EXP goes to the player's own characters. Others' characters are rebuilt once per battle (`ally`), so one swapped out and back keeps what the battle did to it.
- **Swaps** (`swap`, `take_order`, `share_order`): the battle's *Party* command works, since `MGQ_MpBattles` forbids it only in PvP. In its shift change a player may pick only their own characters (`Window_BattleStatus#current_item_enabled?`), and `Scene_Battle#bench_member_ok` swaps within their squad's order, in their own party too, as the game's shift change does. A guest's commands carry their order, which the host takes before the commands (`Commands.apply`); once every guest's commands came, the host sends `coop_party` when any order changed, before the turn's events. A swap only changes who stands in a player's own places, so the others' commands still mean the places they chose. A player whose own Frontline is down still gets the command phase while their Backline may swap in (`Game_Party#inputable?`, `may_swap_in?`, the rules of the game's `can_shift_change?`): alone the game ends the battle there and never needs it, while in a co-op battle the others fight on.
- **Leaving** (`settle`, `reform`, `go_solo`, `take_over`): whoever gets away leaves the battle. A player who leaves a story's battle they cannot escape (`Live.leaving_loses?`) loses it alone, with a defeat scene of its enemies (`Live.forfeit`, `Live.pick_defeat_scene`: a guest always picks one of a random enemy whose scene this game has, since its own is nil or a former battle's, the host keeps its own), or leaves it as an escape when no enemy has one. At the next command phase, before it is recorded, the host takes the players who left out: the others fight on with the shares of the smaller party (`arrange` again), the roster carrying everyone's squad for two players for that; the host sends what it recorded so far, then `coop_party` with the players who stay, which a guest's playback takes between two sends (`Channel.take_first`, `Playback.next_event`), so every battler reference after it means the new party. Characters who stay keep what the battle did to them (`mp_seat`, `mp_place`). A host left alone, and a guest whose host got away or is gone, fight on as a battle of their own (`stand_alone`): their own full team, the game's own settings, no live battle; a guest starts a new command phase from the host's last values, with a defeat scene picked anew of its troop (`resume_alone`). Every player still in the battle hears of it at once (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync::Departures`): a `leave` goes to the whole room, so each game tells "<name> left the battle." when the sender is one of the battle's players (`player_seats`) in the battle log's popup at the top (`popup`, marked `POPUP_MARK` with its kind and seat, so the host's popup a guest's playback repeats shows once) and in the status line as it arrives, mid-turn or during command input; a battle player who leaves the world is told too (`on_leave`), each once per battle. A player who joins the running battle is told the same way, "<name> joined the battle." (`Departures.joined`, once per battle and player apart from their leaving): on the host as it takes them in (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_in`, `tell_joined`), on a guest as the host's `coop_party` names seats new to the party (`reform`).
- **Status names** (`install_status_names`, with `install_shift_change`, since plugins define `Window_BattleStatus` anew; `draw_status_name`, an `around` of `Window_BattleStatus#draw_actor_name`): while a co-op battle runs, the player's own characters are drawn in `Style::OWN` and a `Game_MpAlly` by its `base_name`, without its owner, in its owner's `PlayerColor` (the peer at its `mp_seat`, else its `owner` by name), cut to the width the game passes (100). A character below a quarter of its max HP is left to the game, which colors it (`hp_color`, which covers 0); so is every character outside a co-op battle, and a name the mod cannot draw, logged once. The battle's messages keep the full `name`.
- **Troop changes** (`share_troop`, `note_troop`): the host notes the troop its guests know (each enemy, what it is and whether it is hidden; an enemy the game hid as it was eaten counts as fallen, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.mark`, so the guests keep it among the defeated that pay out) with the roster, once a battle fought alone turns live (`go_live`) and whenever it tells them. Whenever its troop differs from that note, such as enemies another mod added mid-battle, an encounter taken in or an enemy that transformed, appeared or hid, it sends the guests still in the battle `coop_troop` with the troop and the names, once per change: right before each send of its stream (`Mode.before_send`, which `Recorder.flush` asks before it posts, a no-op in other modes), so a guest always knows an enemy before the stream names it, and as a command phase opens (`Mode.settle`). Hotjoining's own sends go the same way. A guest transforms, shows or hides its enemies as the host's first ones (`match_enemies`, each keeping its HP and states, their letters as the game's Enemy Transform and Enemy Appear give them) and adds the new ones after them; when the host's troop is shorter, it takes the troop whole, and when it holds an enemy this game lacks, it keeps its own. Never in a PvP battle or a duel, whose troop is the other side's characters.
- **End** (`restore`, after `BattleManager.battle_end`; `ended`, after `Scene_Battle#terminate`): what the battle lent the player's game that a save keeps, the host's enemy rates and the level sync's stats, goes back as `battle_end` ends (`restore`), since the game's victory saves its autosave right after it and before the scene terminates. The terminate hook then does the scene's cleanup: the player's own party again, the game's own settings back (`MGQ_MpBattles.finish`) and the live battle over, the world's room left open.
- **The rules in C#** (`MGQOnline.Game/Coop`, through `Topics/CoopTopics.cs`, which both hosts answer, so the battle server's host asks them as the player's game does): the host's intake of a request to join its running battle (`JoinIntake`, `coop.intake` with the battle's facts the scripts hand over, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.intake_facts`, and `coop.room` for the `rbn` of its state), the places of an encounter's enemies (`EnemyPlacement`, `coop.place`, the widths measured by the scripts), the enemies a request carries (`TroopEntries`, `coop.write_entries` and `coop.read_entries`), the battle's level (`CoopLevel`, `coop.level`, the scripts handing the leader's personal levels as `MGQ_MpActors::Builds` reads them), the roster and the party on the battle line codec (`CoopRoster`, `coop.write_roster`, `coop.write_party`, `coop.read_roster`, `coop.read_party`, `coop.read_troop`, reading another game's players as `Player.read` does, which the scripts keep for a team duel's sides and a served battle's setup), the host's troop repaired on a guest (`TroopRepair`; `coop.repair` for enemies a script read itself, which `plain_enemies` hands over as whole numbers and the class names of fields of another kind) and what the host does once players left (`CoopDepartures`, `coop.settle`). Their golden cases, `fixtures/coop/placement.json`, `entries.json`, `intake.json` and `roster.json`, were recorded from the scripts of e2a51fe5 (`ruby fixtures/coop/make_fixtures.rb <old tree>`); `GameScript/Tests/coop_support.rb` answers the topics in the script tests by a stand-in checked against them.
- **In a Raid World** (`raid?`, from `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.raid?`) a battle takes the players on the map, party or not; Classic worlds keep everything above.
  - **Who joins** (`raid_candidates`): the other players on the battle's map (`Scope.peers_on`) whose state's `scene` is `map` or `away` (`RAID_FREE_SCENES`: walking it, or on it with their window in the background, which keeps running, as a Classic world invites them; not `event`) who stand within `NEARBY_TILES` of the player (`near?`) and who are not busy (`raid_free?`: their state's `busy`, 1 while they trade or are about to teleport, `state_fields`, sent only in a Raid World), and for a boss battle (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.battle?`, `invitees(troop_id)`) also those whose `scene` is `event` because they watch the story the player tells (`watching?`: among `MGQ_MpCoop::Scope.viewers` and not busy), so the teller's watchers fight its boss with it; the party's members first, then by the steps (diagonals counted as one) from the party leader's tile when the leader is on the map, else from the player's own (`raid_center`, `distance`; positions from the states, not the ghosts), at most 3 with the player (`RAID_PLAYERS`). Outside a party too (`host_possible?`).
  - **The player who starts it hosts it** (`offer`): no leader is asked, a request to lead is refused at once (`TakeRequest`), and the host comes first in the battle's order (`arrange`). The invite and the hold (`freeze_party`) go to the players taken alone, through the map's gate (`tell_map`, `map_coop=<kind>`), which only a Raid World takes (the DLL's route of `map_coop`; a Classic world keeps the party's gate); a busy party member is still waited for (`hold`, `coop=soon`), and the players the hold froze whom the battle then leaves out get `coop=off` (`release`). Of two invites waiting for a player, the one whose battle hosts first (`HostsFirst`: an event's battle, else the host who entered the map first) stays and the other is turned down (`KeepsWaitingInvite`). The invite tells `enc`, 1 for a random encounter, else 0. A battle that begins while another player's invite waits for its player also invites that player, whose own battle gathers and so is no candidate (`WithWaitingHost`), so of two battles one yields. The host waits 10 s for them to join (`RAID_JOIN_FRAMES`, `join_frames`), a guest 4 s more for the roster (`roster_frames`). A guest who trades turns the invite down (`on_map`). No level sync (`MGQ_MpCoopLevelSync.level_for` is nil).
  - **Two battles started at the same moment** (in the DLL `IsRival`, `KeepRival`, `Yield`; `yield_to_rival`): a player whose random encounter (`offer`'s `encounter`, also a held one) still gathers its players and who is invited to another battle on the same map, of at most 4 players, that is an event's (`enc=0`) or whose host entered the map first (`Scope.key_of`, the lower `[since, id]`), keeps that invite instead of turning it down (`YieldsTo`). Its gathering ends, it tells the players it invited `coop=off` (`take_off`, `TakeOff`: a waiting invite is forgotten, a guest waiting for the roster follows or breaks off, and nobody stands still for it any more unless another host froze them or their invite waits, `UnfreezeFor`), takes the rival's troop (`adopt_troop`: `Game_Troop#setup`, the escape ratio, escape and lose, no first strike, its enemies drawn anew) and becomes the rival's guest: `Mode.host_start` returns `:guest`, and `Live.host_start` starts the battle as a guest. A rival's invite that waited as the player's own battle began counts too (`AnswerWaitingInvite`; the player then hosts even with nobody to invite, `WaitingRival`), any other waiting invite is turned down then; of two rivals the one that hosts second is turned down (`KeepRival`), and a rival whose `coop=off` comes is forgotten. The rival turns the other's invite down as usual. An event's battle never yields, since its event goes on by the battle's result. A guest waiting for the roster of a random encounter's battle (`awaited` in the DLL) keeps an invite to a battle it would yield to instead of turning it down (`Follows`); when its host's `coop=off` comes it takes that battle's troop and joins it (`Follow`, the scripts' `follow`; `join` sends its join anew), and when its own roster comes it turns that invite down.
  - **Hotjoining** (`battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin`): every game tells the battle it fights in its state, `rb` (the battle's id) and `rbh` (its host's seat), also a battle of its own (`fight_alone`: nobody to invite, nobody joined, or left alone), both empty outside a battle (`state_fields`, sent only in a Raid World), and told, with the state's `scene` `battle` (`busy_scene`), also while the battle's scene waits on the scene stack because its party command called the Library or Config (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.in_battle_scene?`), so the battle stays joinable meanwhile. A random encounter on a map where another player's state tells such a battle (the DLL's `CoopAsking`, `coop.ask`; `TargetBattle`: the one whose host entered the map first, whose own state tells the same battle, among those whose host stands within `NEARBY_TILES` and whose `rbn` says it takes players in, `RoomIn`, a state without `rbn` counting as one player) asks its host instead of hosting (`ask`, from `offer`, only while no invite waits, handing the troop's enemies and their widths): `coop=hot` through the map's gate with the battle's id, the troop, its enemies (`id:x:y:hidden`, `;` between them), the widths of their pictures as this game measures them (`widths`, `id:width`, `;` between them, `coop.write_widths`; a request of 0.6.9 or before tells none) and the seats of the free players it would have taken (`raid_candidates`, who stand still; none whose state tells a battle, no more than the battle's room besides the player, and none of the player's party members, who are asked instead, see *Party members and a battle outside the party*), and waits as that battle's guest up to 3 s (`AnswerFrames`, `await` from `Mode.before_start`, the DLL counting the frames, `coop.ask_wait`, and ending the wait, `coop.ask_end`, which takes the host's `coop=hot_ok` or `coop=hot_no` through its route). The host takes it in (`take_request`, `coop.intake`) when it hosts that battle or fights it alone, its battle no longer gathers, nobody left it to the computer, neither troop is a boss battle (`MGQ_MpRaidBosses.battle?` for its own, or the `boss=1` its host's invite carried for a battle it took over, `boss?` for the encounter's), and fewer than 4 players fight in it with those taken in before. Of the players the request brings it takes the asking one and as many others as it has room for (`JoinIntake.Fit`), and of the encounter's enemies those this game has that find places, as many as do, none when none do (`fitting_enemies`, `place_anew`), so a crowded battle or a full troop never keeps the asking player out (`EnemyPlacement`: at most 8 in the troop, `TroopLimit`, which counts every member of the troop, the fallen too, since the troop keeps them; a fallen enemy's place on the screen free, `fallen?`; on the screen where at most half of the narrower of two pictures, measured through `Cache.battler`, or as the request's player measured them (`EnemyWidths`, kept for the battle, so the battle server, which measures every picture as 32 pixels wide, places a joining encounter's enemies as the players see them; the server also keeps the widths of the troop the setup carries for its battle as it takes the battle, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.keep_widths`, topic `coop.keep_widths`), hides behind another, `MaxOverlap`, the place nearest the middle among equals; pictures as wide as the screen stand in its middle and count for no overlap, as the game's own troops stack them there). It answers `coop=hot_ok` with its escape and lose and the seats it took (`seats`; the asker lets the others walk on, `join_late`), invites the players who come along with `hot=1`, lets in their battle messages (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.expect`), and, fighting alone, hosts its battle without turning it live yet; otherwise `coop=hot_no`. A player another request brought along who asks with their own encounter, both requests sent at once, is taken out of that request first (`release_companion`), and ignores that request's invite to the battle it asks (`CoopAsking.Battle`). Taken in, the player joins as a late guest (`join_late`: the host's escape and lose, no first strike or surprise); refused or without an answer it hosts its encounter as usual, telling a silent host `coop=off`, and the players the request held in place walk on again (`release_companions`, `coop.ask_release`) unless the encounter invites them anew. A late guest (`late?`, also one invited with `hot=1`) waits up to 75 s for the roster (`ROSTER_FRAMES`, counted by the DLL, `coop.roster_wait`), "Joining <host>'s battle at its next turn..." with "Up to 75 s" on the box's names line (`UP_TO_PREFIX`, since the wait names nobody and the text line would clip) and the seconds waited below. The host takes them in as a command phase opens, before the live battle opens it (`settle`, first in `Mode.settle`): a host fighting alone turns its battle live first (`before_phase`, `go_live`, its hook wrapping `battles_sync_live.rbx`'s): the multiplayer rules, its squad as the party's first player, the stream. The new players' characters join the party (`arrange`, `form`), and the encounter's enemies the troop once its player joined (`add_enemies`: placed anew with the troop as it stands then, so they fill the places of enemies that fell since the request, around the places of the encounters still to join; when enemies added since leave no room for them all, only the first that still find a place join, never past `TroopLimit` (`place_anew`); started as the game starts a battle's, named apart, the escape ratio anew, drawn anew); the guests in the battle get `coop_party` and, through `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.share_troop` (see *Troop changes*), `coop_troop` (the troop and the names, which a guest adds after its own enemies or takes whole, `take_troop`, between two sends; a guest's `grow_troop` starts, discovers and rescales the enemies it adds the way the host's `add_enemies` does), the new players `roster` (the troop with the fallen enemies, eaten ones too, marked `2`, `late_entries`, which a guest rebuilds dead and unseen, `lay_down`, once the game lettered them, which it does only for the living, so the guest names every enemy as the host does, its escape ratio anew, the players, no level and the turn) and `ready` with the names, and then the command phase's stream. Players who turned the invite down or stopped waiting (`give_up`: any end of a late guest's wait without the roster sends `decline` and puts its encounter's own escape and lose back), left the battle or the world, or did not join within 60 s (`WAIT_SECONDS`, told `coop=off`) are forgotten (`expire`; the DLL keeps the list of the players taken in on the live battle's session, `CoopJoins`, `coop.pending`); a host fighting alone whom nobody joins keeps its battle its own (`stay_alone`), its Retry too should the battle end first. A host whose co-op battle goes on alone (`stand_alone`) calls off those taken in who did not join. The host's `coop=off` lets a late guest's encounter start on its own (`call_off_own`, `fight_own`, through `take_over`: its own escape and lose, from its start), and so does a roster that never comes; an invited late guest breaks off as any guest. The battle's end tells those taken in who did not join `coop=off` (`ended`, after `Scene_Battle#terminate`, though not while the battle's scene waits on the scene stack because the party command's Library or Config called another screen, `called_away?`). Every game pays out the whole troop it fought, as in any co-op battle. An event's battle never asks, since its event goes on by its own troop's result and pages.
  - **Joining by choice** (`battles_coop_join.rbx`, `MGQ_MpBattlesJoin`): the host of a battle, or a player who fights one alone, also tells `rbn` in its state: the players in it with those taken in, while it takes players in, else 0 (`state_fields`, `coop.room`, `JoinIntake.Closed`: no PvP battle, no longer gathering, nobody left it to the computer, not ending, fewer than 4). While the player is free on the map (`busy_reason`: the map the scene and quiet, no transfer, no multiplayer battle, not held, no chat box, wheel or World overview, neither trading nor about to teleport) and another player within 3 steps (`JOIN_DISTANCE`, diagonals counted as one, from the states' `x` and `y`) tells a battle whose host, on the map and telling the same battle, tells `rbn` 1 to 3 (the DLL's `JoinChoice`, `coop.join_frame` every map frame with the reasons only the game knows, `busy_reasons`; `OpenBattle`, the nearest first), a prompt below the player's character (`Sprite_MpJoinPrompt`) says "Press J to join battle" with the hotkey's current key (`MGQ_MpHotkeys` `:join_battle`, J by default; `on_map` finds the battle every frame, `prompt_text` hides it while the map is not free); never in a Classic world, and never near the battle the player was in last, whose host keeps a guest who left among its seats. The hotkey is read only in a Raid World, and not at all while another hotkey has its key (`reads_hotkey?`), since a key's press goes to the first who reads it in a frame. The hotkey sends the host `coop=pick` with the battle's id and the map through the map's gate (`JoinChoice.Request`), and the player stands still (`hold_player`, `asking?` from the status's `join_waits`) up to 3 s (`AnswerMilliseconds`, `Settle` with each map poll), "Asking <host> to join their battle..." in the notification box. The host takes them in (`take_request`, `coop.intake`: the battle open as `rbn` tells it, the same battle and map, the player neither in it before nor taken in already) as a player another encounter brings along, but without enemies (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_in_player`): `coop=invite` with `hot=1`, `boss=1` for a boss battle and `pool` for one that fights a raid boss's pools (`invite_fields`, which a late guest who takes the battle over keeps), so an event's battle and a boss battle take them too. The player joins it as any late guest; the event's result and the boss pool's report stay the host's, and the player's rewards are a co-op guest's. Otherwise the host answers `coop=pick_no` with `why` (`full`, `ending`, `gathering`, `pvp` or `closed`), which the player reads in the notification box ("Cannot join <host>'s battle: it is full."), as they read a host who stays silent ("No answer from <host>'s battle."). A player who stops waiting sends the host `coop=off` with the battle's id, which forgets them (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.take_off`), and ignores its invite should it come late (`JoinChoice.GaveUp`); an invite that came in time but waits for the player to be free keeps them waiting (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.invited_by?`). **Side battles** (`sides`, `JoinChoice.SideTarget`, `fight_alongside`): while no battle near the player takes them in and they are free, a raid boss battle on the map whose host tells `rpb` (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.side_of`: a boss the player's story has not beaten, its pools not all empty) with one of its players, or the boss's map event (`MGQ_MpRaidBar.distance_to_boss`), within `JOIN_DISTANCE` makes the prompt say "Press J to fight \<boss> alongside". The hotkey starts the player's own side battle against the same pools (`MGQ_MpRaidPool.start_side`, see [In the game (`battles_raid_pool.rbx`)](#in-the-game-battles_raid_poolrbx)), or says "Cannot fight \<boss> now." in the notification box when it cannot start. A side battle starts at once as the player's own: `offer` neither gathers, holds nor invites for it (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.side_battle?`, asking `MGQ_MpRaidPool.side_battle?`), and other players near it join it by choice; its host tells `rpb` as any raid boss battle's does, so next to a full side battle the hotkey offers yet another, and every player can fight a boss at once.
  - **Party members and a battle outside the party** (the DLL's `CoopAsking`: `PartyNear`, `OfferParty`, `PartyWait`, `PartyEnd`; `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.party_offers`, one `coop.party_offers` a bridge frame, and `Offers`): when a player's encounter asks a running battle whose host is outside their party, or the player asks to join one by choice (`JoinChoice.Request`, `OfferPartyJoin`), their party members near and free (`raid_free?`, `near?`) are not brought along but asked in the notification box, `coop=party_hot` with `mode`, the host's seat and the battle's id. Where the battle has room for the player and them all (`RoomIn`), the mode is `join`: "Join battle with \<player>?", whose accept sends the member's own `coop=pick` (`accept_party`, `coop.accept_party`, `Request` with `along` false), and the player goes on at once. Otherwise an encounter asks `start`: "Start battle with \<player>?"; the members stand still, the encounter waits up to 8 s before asking the host (`PartyAnswerFrames`, "Asking your party to start a battle..."), the members answer `coop=party_ans` with `ok`, and should one say yes the encounter starts as its own battle, which invites the players near but not the members who said no (`stays_out_seats` in `raid_candidates`); else the player asks the running battle alone and tells the members `coop=off` with its id. Joining by choice offers only `join`. No member near, the player joins alone. An offer lapses after 20 s (`PartyOfferMilliseconds`), 8 s for `start`, once the member is in a battle, the player fights another, the battle ended (`OfferEnd`) or the player's `coop=off` names it.

Each game ends the battle as the host's ended: a victory with its own EXP, gold and item drops (the guest's enemies carry the host's death states, which `exp_total`, `gold_total` and `make_drop_items` count), a defeat with its own defeat, an escape as an escape.

## Live battles (`battles_sync.rbx`)

**First version.** Both players command their own team. The host's game computes the battle and streams what it shows; the guest's game plays that back and sends its commands. Each game sees its own team as the party, so the two would draw their random numbers in a different order if both computed. A duel or a team duel calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.join_world(..., :pvp)` (see [Duels](#duels-battles_duelrbx)); `Battle.start` calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.battle_started` as it calls the battle scene, and `Battle.restore` calls `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.finish`. The live battle over the PvP link, which nothing started any more, was deleted on 2026-10-30.
- **Co-op** (`mode` `:coop`, `join_world`): the same system with several guests over the world's room. A message is `battle=<kind>`, `bid=<battle id>`, then the body (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.tell`), sent to each of the battle's other players (`player_seats`) and taken only for the running battle (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.take` through overworld_sync.rbx's routes); a guest takes only its host's, the host only its guests' (`Channel.receive`, `take_from`). The battle names its players by the world seats they held as it began (`Seats`, `pin_room`), each name kept with its player's id, so a reconnect that deals the seats anew changes nothing: `Seats.peer` finds the player on whatever seat they hold, `Seats.world` the seat a message goes to, none while they are away, and only the `:gone` of the players' presence takes one out (`Seats.presence`, `take_gone`); away is a pause the battle waits through. Each message to a player carries its number to them (`Sequence`, field `sq`): the sender keeps the last `KEPT_SENDS` (300) per player, whoever sees a player of the battle come back asks them for what came after the last number taken (`resync`), and the receiver takes each number once and in order, holding a later one until the gap is filled or the world's grace ran out (`let_gaps_go`); a game that never numbered a message is never asked. The sides stay as they are, since every game has the same party and troop (`Playback.battler`, `Names.setup`). A guest's commands cover only its own characters, and the host gives them to that guest's allies (`Commands.apply` by `mp_seat`). The host waits for every guest still in the battle (`all_ready?`, which starts each wait with nobody ready, and `host_guests_commands`); a guest who left (gone from the room, or `leave` once their escape succeeded, `Live.escaped`) does nothing more, their characters' actions taken away at once on the host (`guest_left`, `Mode#left`, `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.idle`; commands they sent before are left out), and taken out of the party at the next command phase (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.settle`). Every player's Escape is the game's own escape attempt (`escape_leaves?` is false in a co-op battle); a failed one spends that player's turn, since its empty commands take its characters' actions away. When the host gets away (its stream ends with `process_abort`) or is gone, each guest takes the battle over as its own (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.take_over`). Each game ends it as the host's ended (`guest_end`), never swapped. The world's room stays open afterwards (`finish`). A guest who never got into the battle, since it ended before the roster came (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.never_got`) or the host's party left them out (`left_out`), goes back to the map with a notice at the bottom left naming the host, or "The server" (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.miss`, `Departures.say`); a running battle's `off` tells the players it was taking in why (`why`: `over` or `closed`), which they are told before they fight on their own (`MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.fight_own`); the starter of a battle the server never started fights it as their own.
- **Session (C#):** the live battle's state is MGQOnline.Game's `LiveSession` in `Battles/Live`, which both hosts answer through `LiveTopics` (`live.join`, `live.change`, `live.players`, `live.admit`, `live.receive`, `live.take`, `live.pending`, `live.tick`, `live.end`, `live.phase`, `live.departure`): the role, kind, seats, those who left and those expected, the battle id, whether the host plays on alone, broke off, is served or runs, the messages that arrived until they are taken (`Channel.receive`, `take`, `take_first`, `take_from`), the battle id and seat filter with its logs once, the ending checks (`Channel.ending` asks `live.tick` with whether the player is in a world, whether a served battle's channel closed and the seats the world told are gone; every 60 checks it takes out the guests who left, whom the script hands to the mode with `departed`, and asks the mode only whether a team duel's other side left), the departures told (32 kept), the host's ready, answered and phase sets and the waiting lists (`live.phase`), and the endings (`LiveVerdicts`: `EndEarly`, `GuestEnd`, `FriendGone`, with their texts). It is "messages in, messages out": the transports stay `Channel.post` and `Channel.poll`, the game's endings and the mode's steps stay the script's (`end_with`, `departed`), and the script keeps the session's view in its own fields (`take_view`), which the hooks read every frame, refreshed by every reply; whether the guest is behind comes with every message taken in or out (`behind?`).
- **Messages** (`Channel`) are the game script's own: a kind (`ready`, `commands`, `events`, `forfeit`, `broken`) and the rest. Values travel as `Wire` lines, tab-separated typed tokens (`i5`, `sText` with `\t`, `\n` and `\` escaped, `@a0` for a battler, `k12` a skill, `t3` an item, `z2` a state, `w5`/`a7` a weapon or armor, `g` in front of one of these for an equipped or stolen item, `xcount` an action's kind, `[` … `]` an array, `{` … `}` an action result as field names and values), never as Ruby code or Marshal data. Since the other game could send anything, `Wire.parse` reads a line of at most 512 KB (`MAX_LINE_BYTES`, the relay's largest message) nested at most 8 deep (`MAX_DEPTH`), and gives nil for a line that fails to read instead of raising; `g` takes exactly one `k`/`t`/`z`/`w`/`a` token (`ITEM_KINDS`); a database id counts only within its table; a symbol (`:` and `x` tokens) is looked up among the symbols the game already has (`Wire.symbol`, a snapshot of `Symbol.all_symbols` taken at the first one read), never made of the peer's text; and an action result takes only the fields `Game_ActionResult` has (`result_fields`). The intake rescues too: `Commands.apply` keeps the computer's actions when a guest's commands fail (a command's id within its table, its target index -1 to 255), `Names.setup` shows the host's names unswapped, and the playback leaves out a stream line or a mode's message that fails (`Playback.take_message`), each logged once. A caller of `Wire.parse` checks for nil and for the kind of each value it needs: `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop::Player.read` takes a player's seat, party_member_max, places and shares only as numbers and its HP and MP only as a list, dropping a field of another kind (`dropped`, logged once per field and kind) and leaving out a player whose seat is no number (`read_all`); a guest keeps every enemy of the host's troop at its place, since the stream names an enemy by its place (`@e<n>`), replacing a screen x or y that is no number with that of its own enemy at the place, else 0, and a mark that is no number with 0, and keeps its own troop when an enemy's id is no enemy of its database (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.repaired_enemies`, logged once per field and kind) before it takes the troop (`take_troop` of `battles_coop.rbx` and `battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`), which, when the troops hold the same enemies, each shown or hidden alike, keeps its own and only moves them to the host's places (`same_enemies?`, `keep_troop`), since the battle server, without pictures, never corrects a large picture's place by its size, and makes any enemy it builds anew of the kind the troop's battle needs (`new_enemy`, such as a Challenge Battle's enemy that takes damage without falling); a team duel's ready answer counts a party_member_max that is no number as 0, a team duel start whose sides do not read leaves the duel as a busy player does, and a team duel guest keeps only pairs of seats of who commands the characters of who left (`take_heirs`). The guest's intake of the stream is C# (`GuestStream` in `MGQOnline.Game/Battles/Live`, topics `live.guest_setup` once per battle and `live.take_stream` once per send, both hosts through `LiveTopics`): it splits a send into its lines, counts and leaves out a line that does not read, lets through only the kinds the playback knows, the battle log's methods and the calls the guest's hooks list (`Playback.set_up` hands it the hooks' lists, `Hooks.log_calls`, whether this game has the name box and the animations' table size), audio calls whose file lies in the Audio folder at the game's volumes, pitches and fades (`AUDIO_FILE` there), pictures and changes of the screen as `coop_scene.rbx` takes the story's (`SceneChecks`), waits of at most 10 s (`MaxWait`, rewritten as whole frames) and animations this game has, logging each refusal once under the script's tag (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.log_lines`); it drops a choose again that names only other players. The script reads the lines left with its battlers (`Playback.take_send`) and plays them; `map_graphics?` stays the script's, a cut-in this game lacks erasing the picture's place.
- **Start:** both battle scenes send `ready` and wait for the other's (`Waiting`, "Waiting for <Player>..."; the host's box names the guests not ready yet, `NOT_READY`, `not_ready`). The host's `ready` carries its party's and troop's names, which the guest swaps for its own in every text that still arrives as text (`Names.setup` hands them with its own names to the DLL, whose `StreamNames` swaps them longest first: the host's characters are the guest's enemies, named with their owner), but not in a speaker's name box (`\n<Name>`) or the untranslated game's speaker line (`【Name】`). An untranslated guest has no name box, so it shows a translated host's as a speaker line (`StreamNames.Readable`). The host turns its skip settings off for the battle (`SKIP_SETTINGS`), since the guest sees only what the host's battle shows; they come back as `BattleManager.battle_end` ends (`Hooks.ending`), before the victory autosave writes them, and `finish` puts them back after a reset or an early end.
- **Host:** `Recorder` streams every event every 10 frames, with each battler's HP, MP, SP, states with the turns they have left, buffs and barriers when they changed (the buffer is C#: `RecorderBuffer`, topic `live.record`, which keeps the events, lets them go every 10 frames as the `frame` check of `live.tick` counts them, or once 200 wait, counts the turn's and the battle's events and sends, and keeps each battler's last values, so the script hands every battler's values in one call per `values` and writes nothing that did not change; the script writes the events of its game's objects, runs the mode's `before_send` before each send and posts it, and keeps `seeded`, `muted` and `call`): before and after every action, after every hit (`apply_item_effects`, which counters and reflections go through too) and before every sprite effect, so the guest has a hit's HP and a defeat's death before it shows them; at every command phase every battler's, which corrects whatever the guest missed. Sprite effects are recorded at the setter and after `perform_damage_effect` and `perform_collapse_effect` of `Game_Actor` and `Game_Enemy`, which write the field directly (`Recorder.sprite_effect`). When recording fails, the host breaks the battle off (`MGQ_MpBattlesSync.break_off`, which sends `broken`), since the guest sees nothing but the stream. In a PvP battle, a host's character that falls with an actor's collapse is the guest's enemy, so its collapse becomes the defeat flash its silhouette starts from (`Playback.sprite_effect`). The stream also carries a `commands` marker where the host's command phase starts, sent at once as `start_party_command_selection` begins (`Recorder.command_phase`, once per phase), since with none of the host's characters able to act (stunned, bound, frozen by time stop) the game goes straight on to `turn_start` and the host waits for the guest's commands within that call; and an `end` marker in `process_victory`, `process_defeat` or `process_abort`. At each turn's start the host waits for the guest's `commands` (`Live.host_commands`) and gives them to the friend's characters (`Commands.apply`: skill or item, id, target index; a troop is the other game's party in the same order, so an index means the same member). The DLL plans them (`CommandPlan`, topic `live.plan`, with each place's owner and the tables' sizes): which places the guest commands (its own, and in a team duel those it took over), and of each command whether it names a skill or item within its table and a target from -1 to 255 (`MaxTargetIndex`), with at most 16 skills chained after it (`MaxChain`), the chain cut at its first item or command it cannot read; the script then asks the game how many actions each character takes (`action_count`) and whether it may use each skill (`allowed?`), and makes the actions. A character the guest names no commands for (`nil`) keeps the computer's choice, an empty list takes its actions away, and a list counts only as many commands as the host's game gave the character actions, since the host rolls the extra ones. The friend's characters use items from the friend's bag, so `Opponent` checks no item count and uses none up; the host records an `item_used` event for a consumable one, which the guest's own game uses up (`Playback.use_up`).
- **Guest:** never runs battle logic. `battle_start` and `turn_start` send its `ready` or its `commands` (`Commands.build`) and play the stream (`Playback.run`) until the next `commands` marker or the end, and `judge_win_loss` never ends the battle on its own. Animations are only started on the guest's battlers, unless the guest holds its skip key, since the host's recorded waits that follow keep the pace and the game's own animation methods would wait a second time. When four or more sends of the stream wait (`BEHIND_SENDS`), the guest skips waits and animations until it caught up. A message goes into a box as soon as its last line arrived. An enemy whose collapse a co-op host starts counts as fallen in the guest's troop too (`Playback.note_fallen`, the game's `Game_Troop#dead_enemies`), so the guest's own victory offers a recruit from it, as every player's does. Values are set directly (`@hp`, `@states`, …), with no side effects of adding a state; once a character's states or buffs changed, the game's cache of its features (`CacheActorFeatures`, which only `Game_Actor#refresh` clears) is cleared (`features_changed`), or a state's extra actions, such as Division's (states 208-210, 663), and its counters would never count on the guest. A recorded call the guest does not make (`callable?` false) is logged once per method ("left out the host's log call ..."), since a call left out shows nothing, such as a hit's damage numbers; the host logs where a co-op guest's rebuilt character differs from what the guest's game showed (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.check`, `Game_MpActor#differences`), whose rates now include magic reflection and counter (`MGQ_MpActors::Builds::RATES`, xparams 0, 1, 2, 5 and 6). The host's end is turned around (`Live.guest_end`). After a played turn the guest ends its turn (`BattleManager.turn_end`) before the next command phase, since `BattleManager.input_start` makes new actions and starts at the first member only when the phase is not `:input` already. While it plays a turn, the guest keeps its party's status windows up (`@battle_actor_status_windows_show`), which the game shows only during its own turn logic. Time stop (the game's *over drive*, `Game_Party#od_turn` and `od_user`, which decide who may act) travels as a `time_stop` event whenever it changes (one whose character is no battler on the guest ends at once, `Playback.time_stop`, since the game asks that character for its observer), and its darkening and music fade as `Game_Screen#od_fadein`/`od_fadeout` and `Audio.start_over_drive`/`end_over_drive`.
- **Each game's language:** a translated and an untranslated game play together, so the host sends what happened rather than its texts wherever the game builds a line from data, and the guest writes the line with its own game's code and texts (`Recorder.call`, `Playback.call`). These are every `display_*` method of `Window_BattleLog` (the battle log's lines, built from the target's `Game_ActionResult`, the database and `Vocab`), `Scene_Battle#process_skill_word` (the line a character says with a skill, and its cut-in) and `#display_skill_name`. The host records the method, its arguments, the action results of the battlers among them and a seed; it draws its own random numbers from that seed during the call (`Recorder.seeded`), and so does the guest, so the guest's `.sample` picks the same one of a skill's lines. Everything the call does itself is left out of the recording (`Recorder.muted`), since the guest's call does it too: its log primitives, popups, messages, sounds, pictures, sprite effects (on the guest's own battlers, so a hit on the host's character is a hit on an enemy) and waits, which the guest skips while it catches up (`Playback.skip_wait?`). The battlers' values are recorded right before the call. The guest calls only the `display_*` methods its own hooks wrapped (`Hooks.log_calls`) and the battle's `SCENE_CALLS`, compared as text, never making a symbol of the host's. "X appears!" comes from `BattleManager.battle_start`, which the guest never runs, so the host records an `emerge` event instead of those lines, with whether its party struck first or was surprised, and the guest names its own troop, says who struck first and gives its first command phase the same start (`Playback.emerge`, `take_encounter`). A call with an argument `Wire` cannot carry is recorded as what it does, in the host's language, like every line built outside these methods.
- **Rules:** Escape ends the battle at once as a forfeit (`Live.forfeit`), which also sends `forfeit`; Give Up is off, as in every multiplayer battle (`MGQ_MpBattles`, `BattleManager.can_giveup?`). Both sides watch for the friend's `forfeit`, a `broken` and the other side being gone (`Channel.ending`) every frame of `Scene_Battle#update` (`Live.watch`) and while they wait (`Waiting.wait_for`, `Playback.run`), so a forfeit or a friend who leaves ends the battle while a player chooses commands too; `Live.end_early` ends it. No message holds either player up: the Battle Dialogue mod, when installed, shows them in boxes (see Dialogue boxes under [Discord (optional)](#discord-optional)), and one the message window shows moves on by itself after 90 frames, at confirm or cancel, or at once while the message skip is held (`Window_Message#input_pause`, `Live.pass_message`). During the victory's results the game's Skip Battle Results setting decides, as in a normal battle: Instant passes at once, Slow and Fast move on after 10 frames, and No Skip only on a press (the window's `message_skip_mode` and `message_pause_input`). When the other side is gone, the remaining player wins. A battle broken off has no winner (`process_abort`, "broke off" on the map). There is no turn timer: once a wait lasted 10 s (`Waiting::LEAVE_FRAMES`), the box offers to leave the battle with Cancel, which asks first (see *The waiting box*) and counts as a forfeit, so a friend whose game hangs with the link still open never holds the other player for good.
- **The waiting box** (`Waiting`): `wait_for(scene, text, details = nil, prefix = WAITING_PREFIX, back = false)` opens a `Window_MpWaiting` (a `Window_Base` in the preset's window skin and the layout box `:waiting_box`, `WIDTH` 360 at first) of `LINES` (3) lines (`HEIGHT` from `LINE_HEIGHT` 24 and `PADDING` 12 as the game's `Window_Base` has them, given to the window at `open`, since the contents are made once with the window) on the first frame without an answer: the text, the names line and the seconds. `details` is a lambda returning the names still waited for (asked every frame, so cheap; one that raises is logged once and the line stays empty, `names_of`), drawn after `prefix` ("Still choosing: ", "Not ready: ") only when they changed, cut to the window; the seconds line (`MGQ_MpUi::SECONDS_TEXT`) is drawn once every `REDRAW_FRAMES` (60), from "0 s", until `LEAVE_TEXT` takes it at `LEAVE_FRAMES` (`refresh`, from `leave?`). Cancel then asks before the player leaves (`choice`, `ask_to_leave` through `MGQ_MpUi::Confirm`, "no" picked first: `LEAVE_QUESTION`, or `LEAVE_LOSES_QUESTION` where leaving loses the battle, with `LEAVE_CHOICES`), since players press it to go back and choose again. With `back`, the host's waits for the guests' commands (`Live.host_commands`, `host_guests_commands`), Cancel goes back to the host's own commands at once (`:back`, `Live.go_back`), the guests' commands that came staying with their characters, and after `LEAVE_FRAMES` the line says `BACK_TEXT` and the question offers `BACK_CHOICES` (*Leave*, *Choose again*). `open`/`leave?`/`close` take a `Box` record for the playback's own wait. `WAITING_FOR` ("Waiting for %s...") words the wait for a player, which the live battle and the playback share. `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.seat_name(seat)` names a seat for the line: "you" (`OWN_NAME`), the peer's name, or "seat n" for one the game does not know, never raising (`peer_name`). The host waits through `Live.wait_telling`, which names the seats its block waits for and tells the guests those seats whenever they change (`tell_waiting`, the `waiting` message, `WAITING_KIND`, sorted seats joined by commas, nobody once answered; also everyone as a command phase opens, and those asked to choose again), so a guest's box names the same players: the playback's wait and the guest's start read them with `host_waits_for` (`Channel.take(WAITING_KIND)`, the player themself left out), taken as they come so none piles up; `forget_waiting` at each battle's start.

`Hooks.install` puts the hooks in once the first scene starts (a `SceneManager.run` hook), since the game's plugins load after the Patch folder and `Promestein2` defines `Scene_Battle#battle_start`, `Scene_Battle#turn_start` and `Window_BattleLog#add_text` anew, which drops a hook installed before it. `Hooks.wrap` keeps each wrapped method under a name of its own, since `turn_start` is wrapped twice. What the stream carries: the calls the guest makes itself (see *Each game's language*), and outside them the battle log's primitives (`add_text`, `replace_text`, `back_one`, `back_to`, `clear`, `clear_popup`), `PopupResults#push`, messages with their face and speaker (`process_skill_word` and `process_down_word` set it, since a line names its speaker only by name, which both teams can share), pictures of the troop screen (cut-ins; erases only of pictures that were shown), its shakes, flashes and tone changes, every `Audio` call (the log plays some sounds directly), animations as `show_animation` is asked for them, before it checks the skip key, sprite effects, the skill name, turns and the scene's waits.

**Battle modes (`MGQ_MpBattles::Mode`).** A kind's mode keeps only its steps on the scene (forming the sides, settling who fights on, taking its messages, the order of places, the characters outside the battle, taking a battle over); what every kind decides the same way the defaults ask the DLL (`MGQ_MpBattles.decided`, topic `live.mode`): whether a guest stands on the host's side, the players of both sides, who took over whose characters and whether the other side is gone, from a team duel's sides, and the kinds of the battle's own messages in the stream (`team_heirs` in a team duel, `pvp_front` while a duel has the Backline). The co-op mode answers these itself.

**Not yet in the live battle:** effects outside the battle log's calls turned around for the other side, a confirmation before Escape, and the computer taking over the host's team when the host leaves.

**Not covered yet:** the Backline in team duels and levelling the two teams.

## Battles on the battle server (`battles_served.rbx`)

A co-op battle of two or more players in a world goes to the relay's battle server where the relay has one (`Relay/README.md`, Server battles; the server is `BattleServer/`, see its README). The server hosts it with the game's own battle code and this mod's host (`battles_sync*.rbx`, `battles_coop*.rbx`); every player, the starter too, plays it back as a guest over the battle's channel, a WebSocket of its own beside the world room that carries that battle's messages to and from the server's seat (250, `SERVER_SEAT`) in plain text, so the server reads that battle alone and the world's token stays with the players. Solo battles stay on the player's game, and PvP battles and duels keep their own transports.

- **Serving** (`serve`, from `MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.gather` once the players are arranged): while a world is open and the DLL is there (`available?`), the starter hands the relay the battle's setup (`setup_text`: the script collects the facts and C# writes the `Wire` line, `served.setup.write`, a Float or Symbol among the facts crossing as `{"float" => text}` or `{"symbol" => name}` (`plain_fact`), since the bridge carries neither; see *Served battles* under [Game rules](#game-rules-mgqonlinegame): `ServedSetup.Format`, the fingerprints of its MGQ Online scripts, `scripts_fingerprint` (the script reads the scripts, C# sums them, `served.fingerprint`), and its game data, the troop, escape and lose, the map, the enemies' rates and entries, the arranged players, the level, the switches on and the variables set, the Raid flag, the raid boss fight `MGQ_MpRaidPool.served_fields` (written from the C# fight and ledger, `raid.fight.export`) or a Classic world's tier, its own seat, the world's squad rules, the world's mods it runs with its options of them, `MGQ_MpWorldMods.served_mods` and `served_settings`, and the widths of the troop's pictures as it measures them, `MGQ_MpBattlesHotjoin.widths_of`, which follow the other facts on the line only when the starter names them, so a setup without them keeps its bytes) and waits for the relay; the DLL refuses a serve the relay does not answer within 5 s itself (`BattleSession.ServeTimeout`, code `timeout`). Served, it tells the players `serve` with the battle's id over the world room and becomes a guest of the server (`become_guest`, `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.serve_over_channel`); a guest waiting for the roster that gets `serve` follows it there (`follow`). Refused (`no_server`, `busy`, `scripts`, `data`, `mods`, `core`, ...) or without an answer, the starter hosts the battle as before; a refusal of `BattleSession.LastingCodes` (no battle server, a version the relay serves no battles of, other scripts or game data on the server, a server without its core) keeps the DLL from asking again in this game session: it names it in `mp_battle_state`'s `lasting`, which `available?` reads, and refuses every later serve at once.
- **The channel** (`open`, `drain`, `take`): its entries come as the DLL writes them (`kind` `seat`, `message` or `closed` with its `code`); `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.Channel` posts over it while the battle is served (`post`, a guest's message to the server's seat alone, since the relay closes the channel of a player who sends to another) and `MGQ_MpBattlesSync.tell` sends a served battle's messages to single players through it too. A closed channel ends the battle for a guest (`closed?`, `served_gone?`), one that does not open within 10 s too: the DLL ends it then with a `closed` entry of the code `open_timeout` (`BattleSession.OpenTimeout`). On the battle server `mp_battle_state` names the seats that came onto the channel (`in`) and those that left it and are not back (`out`), as their entries pass to the scripts (`online.rb`, `note_seat`); `arrived` reads `in`. The server tells the other players of one who leaves (`pass_departure`, `departed`), since a player's `leave` reaches it alone.
- **The front:** the server sits in no world room, so the starter stands in for it there, through its DLL (`BattleSession`, the starter being the game whose serve the relay took and that opened its channel; `ServedTopics`). Its state names the battle as its own (`front_fields`: `rb`, `rbh` the starter's seat, and `rbn` as the server tells it in `front_state`, which the DLL keeps, `served.front_state`), requests to join it (`hot`, `pick`, `off` for the battle, `fronts?`, `served.fronts`) go to the server with their players' ids and names, which the script hands the DLL (`forward`, `served.forward`, as `front_in`), a player who left the world goes as `front_left` (`peer_left`, `served.left`), and the server's messages for the world room (`front_out`, the server's stand-in for `mp_world_send`) go there from the DLL at once, co-op messages alone, never reaching the script. The server takes such a request as the world room would have handed it over, takes the players in as a host does (`battles_coop_hotjoin.rbx`, `battles_coop_join.rbx`, its invites and `hot_ok` naming `served`, which sends the late player onto the channel) and has the relay let them onto the channel.
- **On the server** (`server?`): the game's recruit roll after a victory is left out (`served.rb`, around `BattleManager.process_follow`), since each player's game offers its own and one the server rolled would join a party nobody keeps. `host` takes the setup (the starter's switches and variables, the map, the rates, the troop with its boss fight, `MGQ_MpRaidPool.serve_fight`, which hands the starter's pools to the core's ledger through `raid.fight.import`, or its tier); `served.rb`'s `check` reads the setup through C# (`read_setup`, `served.setup.read`) and refuses one C# refuses (`refusal_of`, `served.setup.check`: another format, other scripts or game data, a troop without enemies in the server's database, which alone the script tells, no players, players not on distinct world seats from 0 to 31, a starter among none of them); a forked child gets the setup's mods before its sandbox and so before its core opens, so `server.rb`'s `mods_for` reads them in Ruby alone (`mods_in`); `host_gather` waits up to `ARRIVE_FRAMES` for the players to come onto the channel, then starts the battle as the starter would have (`MGQ_MpBattlesCoop.start_battle`). The server sits in no world room, so the channel tells its players' presence: a player who drops off it is kept away for the world's grace (`Peers.wait_for`) and one who comes back is taken back (`Peers.come_back`), while `served.rb` ticks `Peers` in place of the world's sync. While the battle waits for its players, `served.rb` updates the windows and sprites on every sixth frame only (`WAIT_DRAW_FRAMES`). Each served battle runs in a child process with the relay's battle lifetime as its CPU time (`SERVED_CPU_SECONDS`, plus `CPU_GRACE_SECONDS` before the hard limit); at the limit the child ends the battle as `failed`. The child logs its CPU seconds at the battle's end, and the parent logs each child's exit or signal with its battle id.

**The DLL** (`BattleSession`, `Exports.Battle.cs`), each export returning at once:

| Export | Arguments | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_battle_serve` | `'pppp'` world, battle id, the players' world seats separated by commas, setup | 1 started (POST to the relay, state `serving`, then `served` or `refused` with `code`); 0 outside that world, without a player, or for an id, seats or a setup the relay would refuse |
| `mp_battle_open` | `'pp'` world, battle id | 1 started (state `opening`, then `open` and a `seat` entry); 0 as above |
| `mp_battle_send` | `'lp'` seat, text | 1 when it goes out; 0 while the channel is not open, for a seat outside 0 to 254, or past 256 KB |
| `mp_battle_receive` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) | the oldest entry, see above; 0 while none waits |
| `mp_battle_state` | `'pl'` (READ_ARGUMENTS) | `state` (`idle`, `serving`, `served`, `refused`, `opening`, `open`, `closed`), `code` (`timeout` for a serve the relay did not answer in time, `open_timeout` for a channel that did not open in time) and `lasting` once a refusal ended every later serve of the session |
| `mp_battle_close` | `'v'` | 1; forgets the battle |

A broken channel is taken again after 1, 2 and 4 s; a refusal (403, 404 once the battle ended) or the relay's close (`closed <why>`, or its reason) ends it with that code, a channel that could not be taken again with `lost`. An idle channel pings every 5 s.
